**Definition (learn):** Nagara concentrates architectural emphasis on the sanctum spire in an open, wall-less terrace; Dravida distributes it across a walled axial enclosure with a pyramidal vimana and an increasingly dominant gateway (⚠️ owner 03 §13.2).
**Breakdown:**
- Spire: Nagara = curvilinear shikhara; Dravida = vimana.
- Enclosure: Nagara = often none (Khajuraho); Dravida = walled.
- Gateway: Dravida = gopuram.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Khajuraho vs Chola (Brihadishvara).
**Links:** ART-1085, ART-1086, ART-1074, ART-1091, ART-1114.
**Trap:** Nagara shikhara and Dravida vimana are not the same object (❌ owner 03 §9).
**Mains hook:** Nagara spire vs Dravida enclosure: where the architectural effort falls.
**Back:** *"Nagara heaven-spire; Dravida walled gate."*

### Flashcard ART-1089 — Khajuraho setting: Chandella heartland
**Front:** What were the names and dynastic frame of the Khajuraho region?
**Definition (learn):** The region was known as Vats in ancient times, Jejabhukti in the medieval period and Bundelkhand since the 14th century; the Chandela dynasty ruled central India between the 9th and 13th centuries AD with capital Kharjju-ravahaka (Khajuraho) (✅ owner 03 §4, PDF p. 79).
**Breakdown:**
- Vats (ancient) → Jejabhukti (medieval) → Bundelkhand (from 14th c).
- Contemporaries: Rashtrakutas, Pratiharas, Palas.
- Gupta-period centres nearby: Bhumara, Khoh, Nachna, Deogarh (PDF p. 79).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 9th–13th c; capital Khajuraho.
**Links:** ART-1090, ART-1091, ART-1100, ART-1106.
**Trap:** Chandela is the dynastic name; the spelling variant is handled in ART-1102.
**Mains hook:** Khajuraho as the culmination of a central-Indian tradition.
**Back:** *"Vats, Jejabhukti, Bundelkhand; Chandelas from Khajuraho."*

### Flashcard ART-1090 — Dhanga and the assertion of independence
**Front:** Why does Dhanga matter in Chandella history?
**Definition (learn):** Dhanga, the first fully independent Chandela king, took the title maharajadhiraja on the decline of the Pratiharas and Palas; several Khajuraho temples date to his reign (✅ owner 03 §4 & §13.6, PDF pp. 79–80).
**Breakdown:**
- Early rulers Jayashakti (Jejjaka/Jeja) and Vijayashakti expanded under Harsha and Yashovarman.
- Pala overlordship wording is not retained as settled chronology (owner 03 advanced §2).
- Temple building follows political self-assertion.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Title maharajadhiraja.
**Links:** ART-1089, ART-1091, ART-1106.
**Trap:** Do not attribute a named temple to Dhanga beyond "several temples during his reign" (❌ owner 03 §13.12).
**Mains hook:** Independence creates the surplus and the need for visible sovereignty.
**Back:** *"Dhanga: king of kings, temples follow."*

### Flashcard ART-1091 — Khajuraho architecture: jagati, sandstone, no wall, sunrise
**Front:** What are the defining architectural features of Khajuraho's mature temples?
**Definition (learn):** Nagara temples on raised jagati platforms without any enclosure wall, in fine-grained buff, pink and pale-yellow sandstone; all temples except the Chaturbhuja face sunrise (✅ owner 03 §4, PDF p. 80).
**Breakdown:**
- Platform: jagati; no enclosure.
- Material: fine sandstone; granite exceptions for Chausath Yogini, Brahma and Mahadeva (⚠️ ASI, owner 03).
- Orientation: east (sunrise), one exception (Chaturbhuja).
- Clustered urushringas (ART-1078).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Chaturbhuja exception.
**Links:** ART-1077, ART-1078, ART-1088, ART-1093.
**Trap:** Not all Khajuraho temples are uniformly oriented (❌ owner 03 §9).
**Mains hook:** The open terrace makes the exterior the display surface.
**Back:** *"Sandstone terrace, no wall, east-facing except one."*

### Flashcard ART-1092 — Khajuraho plural patronage: 6 Shaiva, 8 Vaishnava, 1 Ganesha, 1 Surya, 4 Jain
**Front:** How is Khajuraho's patronage distributed by dedication?
**Definition (learn):** Of the surviving temples, six are Shiva, eight Vishnu, one Ganesha, one Sun and four Jain Tirthankara temples, on one site and in one idiom (✅ owner 03, PDF p. 80).
**Breakdown:**
- Shaiva 6; Vaishnava 8; Jain 4; Surya 1; Ganesha 1.
- Strongest fact for a plural-patronage argument.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Totals 20 (owner's dedication count). UNESCO's page says roughly twenty survive; its longer synthesis says 23 temples in three clusters (fetched, WHC 240). ⚠️ Count conflict: route to topic 14.
**Links:** ART-1094, ART-1095, ART-1096, ART-1100.
**Trap:** Do not state a current count as today's figure (❌ owner 03 §13.12).
**Mains hook:** Shaiva, Vaishnava and Jain sects side by side in one architectural idiom.
**Back:** *"Six Shiva, eight Vishnu, four Jain, two others."*

### Flashcard ART-1093 — Chausath Yogini, Khajuraho: earliest building and shape conflict
**Front:** What is Khajuraho's Chausath Yogini and what is its shape?
**Definition (learn):** The earliest building at Khajuraho, an open-air granite enclosure reflecting Yogini/Tantric and Shaiva traditions; one of about 13 surviving Chausath Yogini temples in India (✅ owner 03, PDF p. 81). Its plan is the source conflict below.
**Breakdown:**
- Earliest group: Chausath-yogini, Brahma, Varaha, Lalgu.
- Book (PDF p. 81) calls it circular; owner 03 follows ASI and UNESCO in calling it rectangular.
- The fetched UNESCO page for Khajuraho does not mention this temple (⚠️).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** About 13 surviving; mostly 9th–11th c (owner 03 §13.12).
**Links:** ART-1104, ART-1105, ART-1092.
**Trap:** Do not merge Khajuraho's Chausath Yogini with Morena's (ART-1104).
**Mains hook:** Show the earliest tantric-shaiva shrine as a distinct granite form.
**Back:** *"Earliest Yogini: granite, open, shape disputed."*

### Flashcard ART-1094 — Lakshmana and Duladeo: the second sandstone group
**Front:** Which Khajuraho temples form the second, sandstone group?
**Definition (learn):** The second, entirely sandstone group comprises the Lakshmana and Duladeo temples and the Mahadeva temples (✅ owner 03, PDF p. 80).
**Breakdown:**
- Lakshmana: Vaishnava (ART-1092 count).
- Duladeo: sandstone group.
- Mahadeva temples: part of this group.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Second group after the earlier granite group.
**Links:** ART-1093, ART-1095, ART-1092.
**Trap:** Do not assume the earlier group is equally splendid; the source calls it "less splendid."
**Mains hook:** Scale and sculptural density rise from the earliest group to the sandstone group.
**Back:** *"Lakshmana and Duladeo: sandstone maturity."*

### Flashcard ART-1095 — Kandariya Mahadeva: the culminating temple
**Front:** Why is Kandariya Mahadeva the culminating Khajuraho monument?
**Definition (learn):** Dedicated to Shiva, the largest and loftiest monument at Khajuraho, marking "the peak of the architectural and sculptural efflorescence" (✅ owner 03, PDF pp. 80–81).
**Breakdown:**
- Largest and loftiest.
- Most sculpturally dense Shaiva example.
- Culmination of the axial chain (ART-1071).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Shaiva; Chandella.
**Links:** ART-1078, ART-1094, ART-1103.
**Trap:** Do not attribute it to a named ruler without source support (❌ owner 03 §9).
**Mains hook:** Use Kandariya as the named climax for the 2025 Chandella question.
**Back:** *"Kandariya: largest, loftiest, the peak."*

### Flashcard ART-1096 — Other named Khajuraho temples: Vishvanath, Parsvanath, Jagadambi, Chitragupta
**Front:** Which other Khajuraho temples are named and what does each represent?
**Definition (learn):** Vishvanath (major Shaiva temple); Parsvanath (principal Jain temple); Jagadambi (Shaiva); Chitragupta (Surya or Sun dedication, unusual for the region) (✅ owner 03, PDF pp. 80–81).
**Breakdown:**
- Vishvanath: major Shaiva.
- Parsvanath: principal Jain.
- Chitragupta: Surya temple.
- Each cited as a specific case.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 80–81.
**Links:** ART-1092, ART-1095, ART-1097.
**Trap:** Do not cite Khajuraho temples interchangeably as "generic Khajuraho temples" (⚠️ owner 03 advanced §4).
**Mains hook:** Each named temple supports a specific point: earliest, sectarian range or peak scale.
**Back:** *"Vishva Shiva, Parsva Jain, Chitra Sun."*

### Flashcard ART-1097 — Matangeshvara: the active worship temple
**Front:** Which Khajuraho temple remains an active worship site?
**Definition (learn):** Matangeshvara, a Shaiva temple, is the only temple still an active worship site at Khajuraho (✅ owner 03 §5 & §13.9A, PDF pp. 80–81).
**Breakdown:**
- Living monument.
- Conservation must accommodate ritual use (owner 03 §13.11).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Shaiva.
**Links:** ART-1100, ART-1095.
**Trap:** Do not assume all Khajuraho temples are in worship.
**Mains hook:** Living worship changes the conservation brief.
**Back:** *"Matangeshvara: still praying."*

### Flashcard ART-1098 — Khajuraho sculptural programme: breadth of life
**Front:** What does the Khajuraho sculptural programme include?
**Definition (learn):** A variety of artwork including, but not limited to, maithuna reliefs: deities, minor deities, worshippers, secular courtly life, dancers, musicians, teachers and disciples, Vyalas (hybrid lion-bodied animals) and Tantric/Shakti imagery (✅ owner 03, PDF p. 81).
**Breakdown:**
- Deities and Jain Tirthankaras.
- Secular and social registers (dancers, musicians, teachers).
- Vyalas on Vishnu-dedicated shrines (also found in other Indian temples).
- Maithuna: one register among many.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Do not state any percentage of erotic sculpture (❌ owner 03 §13.5).
**Links:** ART-1099, ART-1103, ART-1111, ART-1161.
**Trap:** Treating erotic imagery as the sole or defining content (❌ owner 03 §9).
**Mains hook:** "Breadth of life": the programme as one continuous register of lived and sacred experience.
**Back:** *"Gods, dancers, Vyalas, worshippers, and some intimacy too."*

### Flashcard ART-1099 — Erotic sculpture is not unique to Khajuraho
**Front:** Is Khajuraho's erotic imagery unique?
**Definition (learn):** No. The source states "like Khajuraho temples, the erotic element is also prominent in the temples of Konark and Bhubaneswar" (✅ owner 03, PDF p. 75); Konark's chariot walls carry erotic mithuna scenes (PDF p. 78).
**Breakdown:**
- Khajuraho: regional parallel, not a Chandella invention.
- Odisha: Konark, Bhubaneswar.
- Interpretations (Tantric, ritual, symbolic, didactic) are contested and must be stated as such.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 75, 78.
**Links:** ART-1098, ART-1085, ART-1080.
**Trap:** Presenting mithuna as unique to Khajuraho (❌ owner 03 advanced §9).
**Mains hook:** Use Konark and Bhubaneswar as the comparative point.
**Back:** *"Konark too: a regional parallel, not an exclusive."*

### Flashcard ART-1100 — Khajuraho UNESCO inscription and count caution
**Front:** When and why was Khajuraho inscribed, and what counts must be avoided?
**Definition (learn):** The Khajuraho Group of Monuments was inscribed on the UNESCO list in 1986 under criteria (i) and (iii) (✅ UNESCO WHC 240 fetched). The Ministry and book describe it as recognised for outstanding architecture, diversity of temple forms and testimony to the Chandela civilisation (✅ owner 03, PDF p. 83).
**Breakdown:**
- Chandella peak per UNESCO: 950–1050.
- Count conflict: UNESCO "roughly twenty" survive; the longer synthesis says 23 temples in three clusters across about 6 sq km.
- Owner 03 totals 20 by dedication.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1986; criteria (i), (iii).
**Links:** ART-1092, ART-1089, ART-1103, ART-1119.
**Trap:** Do not state a current count as ASI's current figure (❌ owner 03 §13.12). Do not treat the UNESCO inscription as cost-free (advanced owner 03 §11).
**Mains hook:** Closing line: the 1986 inscription plus a conservation-versus-access note.
**Back:** *"1986: architecture, forms, Chandela testimony."*

### Flashcard ART-1101 — Khajuraho's reception: Al-Biruni, Ibn Battuta, Burt and Little Khajuraho
**Front:** How was Khajuraho documented after its decline, and what idiom travelled?
**Definition (learn):** Al-Biruni (11th c) mentioned Khajuraho as the capital of Jajahuti; Ibn Battuta (14th c) called it "Kajarra"; the town sank from public consciousness after the 13th-century decline; T.S. Burt, a British engineer, rediscovered it in 1838; Alexander Cunningham documented the temples; the 10th-century Bhand Deva Temple in Rajasthan, "Little Khajuraho," shows the idiom travelling (✅ owner 03 advanced §13.5, PDF p. 82).
**Breakdown:**
- Al-Biruni: "Jajahuti."
- Ibn Battuta: "Kajarra."
- Burt 1838; Cunningham later.
- Bhand Deva: Rajasthan, 10th c.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1838; 10th c; Ibn Battuta 14th c.
**Links:** ART-1089, ART-1100.
**Trap:** Do not treat "Little Khajuraho" as a second Chandella capital.
**Mains hook:** External notice and later rediscovery show the site mattered and then was lost.
**Back:** *"Al-Biruni named it, Burt found it in 1838."*

### Flashcard ART-1102 — Chandela versus Chandella spelling
**Front:** Which spelling should be used for the dynasty?
**Definition (learn):** The source uses both "Chandela" (dynastic name) and "Chandella" (descriptive, PDF p. 82). The 2025 PYQ uses "Chandella," which must be preserved when quoting or answering it (✅ owner 03 §4 & §9).
**Breakdown:**
- Dynastic name: Chandela.
- PYQ wording: Chandella.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Spelling only.
**Links:** ART-1089, ART-1103.
**Trap:** Do not silently correct the PYQ spelling (❌ owner 03 §9).
**Mains hook:** Use the PYQ's spelling when answering that question.
**Back:** *"Chandela the dynasty, Chandella the PYQ."*

### Flashcard ART-1103 — 2025 Mains PYQ: Chandella sculpture, vigour and breadth of life
**Front:** How should the 2025 Q3 answer on the Chandella artform be built?
**Definition (learn):** "'The sculptors filled the Chandella artform with resilient vigor and breadth of life.' Elucidate. (Answer in 150 words)" (✅ UPSC Mains 2025 GS Paper 1, owner 03 §7).
**Breakdown:**
- Vigour = how figures are carved (posture, movement, depth).
- Breadth = what is carved (range of subjects).
- Name Khajuraho; show vigour through twisting postures; show breadth through deities, courtly life, musicians, Vyalas and mithuna; close with Kandariya and the 1986 inscription.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 10 marks, 150 words.
**Links:** ART-1098, ART-1099, ART-1095, ART-1100, ART-1106.
**Trap:** A paragraph only on erotic sculpture (owner 03 §13.1).
**Mains hook:** Vigour and breadth are one achievement: the carving method puts a whole world on one wall.
**Back:** *"Vigour is how, breadth is what."*

### Flashcard ART-1104 — Morena (Mitaoli) Chausath Yogini: CSE 2021
**Front:** What does the Morena Chausath Yogini temple show in the CSE 2021 item?
**Definition (learn):** A circular temple of the Kachchhapaghata dynasty near Morena, Madhya Pradesh. The reproduced CSE 2021 statements are: (1) circular, built under the Kachchhapaghata dynasty; (2) only circular temple in India; (3) meant to promote Vaishnava sect; (4) popular belief that it inspired the Indian Parliament building; the key gives item 35 as (c), "1 and 4" (✅ owner 03 §13.9C, PDF pp. 166–167, 185).
**Breakdown:**
- Statement 1 and 4 supported (at key level).
- Statement 2 and 3 not supported.
- Parliament link is a popular belief (ART-1105).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Kachchhapaghata; key (c).
**Links:** ART-1093, ART-1105, ART-1092.
**Trap:** Do not merge Morena and Khajuraho (❌ owner 03 §13.9C).
**Mains hook:** Objective item; the answer discriminator is the Morena versus Khajuraho distinction.
**Back:** *"Morena circles, one and four true."*

### Flashcard ART-1105 — Chausath Yogini and the Parliament building resemblance
**Front:** What is the status of the claim that the Parliament building was inspired by a Chausath Yogini temple?
**Definition (learn):** The book says the old (1927) Parliament building's construction "was influenced by Chausath Yogini Temple at Mitaoli" (✅ owner 03, PDF p. 156); topic 05 labels it a debated resemblance; CSE 2021 certifies only that a popular belief exists (✅ owner 03 §13.9C).
**Breakdown:**
- Popular belief: not a documented design source.
- Debated resemblance.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Parliament building (1927).
**Links:** ART-1104.
**Trap:** Do not state the influence as established (⚠️).
**Mains hook:** Distinguish belief from documented influence.
**Back:** *"A belief, not a blueprint."*

### Flashcard ART-1106 — Khajuraho as a Chandella cultural landscape (advanced thesis)
**Front:** How should a sophisticated answer frame Khajuraho?
**Definition (learn):** As a Chandella cultural landscape integrating political autonomy (Dhanga), mature Nagara morphology and an integrated sculptural programme, which asserts religious authority across Shaiva, Vaishnava and Jain patronage (⚠️ advanced owner 03 §1).
**Breakdown:**
- Political autonomy as enabling condition.
- Mature axial chain and clustered spires.
- Integrated multi-register sculpture.
- Plural patronage on one site.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Dhanga; 1986.
**Links:** ART-1090, ART-1091, ART-1098, ART-1103.
**Trap:** Reducing Khajuraho to "the erotic temples" (❌ owner 03 advanced §1).
**Mains hook:** "Open with Dhanga's autonomy, not with the erotic sculpture."
**Back:** *"Autonomy, morphology, programme: one landscape."*

### Flashcard ART-1107 — Pallava architecture: the four Longhurst styles
**Front:** What are A.H. Longhurst's four chronological Pallava styles?
**Definition (learn):** Mahendravarman (610–640), Mamalla (640–670, under Narasimhavarman I), Rajasimha (674–800) and Nandivarman (800–900) (✅ owner 03 §13.8, PDF p. 84). Temple architecture in South India began under Mahendravarman I (610–630 AD).
**Breakdown:**
- Stage 1: rock-cut pillared mandapas with garbhagriha.
- Stage 2: monolithic rathas.
- Stage 3: structural Shore Temple.
- Stage 4: Nandivarman style.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Longhurst scheme is a modern classification, not a Pallava self-description (⚠️ owner 03 §13.8).
**Links:** ART-1108, ART-1109, ART-1110, ART-1112.
**Trap:** Do not present the Longhurst scheme as a Pallava self-description.
**Mains hook:** Pallava as a transition, not a single style.
**Back:** *"Mahendra, Mamalla, Rajasimha, Nandi."*

### Flashcard ART-1108 — Mandagapattu and Mahendravarman I's rock-cut beginning
**Front:** What is the earliest Pallava rock-cut work and what is its inscription's claim?
**Definition (learn):** Mandagapattu cave temple, Villupuram (Mahendravarman I), the earliest Pallava rock-cut work; its inscription stresses that the temple was made without timber, metal or brick (✅ owner 03 §13.8, PDF pp. 86–87). Mamandur caves near Kanchipuram carry Mahendravarman I's titles including Vichitrachitta and Satrumalla.
**Breakdown:**
- Mahendravarman I: "first great patron of art and architecture."
- Titles: Vichitra Chitta, Chettakari, Chitrakarappuli.
- Mamandur: wall paintings survive.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 610–630 AD.
**Links:** ART-1107, ART-1111.
**Trap:** Do not confuse Mamandur with Mandagapattu.
**Mains hook:** Rock-cut beginnings and painting patronage.
**Back:** *"Mahendra cut caves, without wood or brick."*

### Flashcard ART-1109 — Pancha Rathas: experimentation in stone
**Front:** What are the Pancha Rathas and why do they matter?
**Definition (learn):** Five temples carved from a single rock at Mamallapuram, each named after the Pandavas and Draupadi, using known plan and elevation forms: Draupadi (hut-like), Arjuna (dvitala vimana with mukhamandapa), Bhima (rectangular, wagon-vaulted), Dharmaraja (tritala vimana), Nakula-Sahadeva (apsidal) (✅ owner 03 §13.8, PDF pp. 85–86).
**Breakdown:**
- Mamalla (Narasimhavarman I) monolithic rathas.
- "Indicates the experimental tendency of the architect."
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Mamalla style, 640–670.
**Links:** ART-1107, ART-1110.
**Trap:** Ratha here is a monolithic temple, not the wall projection (ART-1079).
**Mains hook:** Pancha Rathas as stone experimentation.
**Back:** *"Five from one rock: five forms, five heroes."*

### Flashcard ART-1110 — Shore Temple, Mamallapuram: the switch to structural building
**Front:** Why is the Shore Temple decisive for Pallava architecture?
**Definition (learn):** The Shore Temple complex overlooking the Bay of Bengal, assigned to Rajasimha (8th century): "During his reign, the rock-cut technique of temple was replaced by structural temples." It holds a Shiva linga, a Somaskanda and Vishnu on Ananta (✅ owner 03 §13.8, PDF p. 86; UNESCO Group of Monuments at Mahabalipuram, 1984, owner-stated).
**Breakdown:**
- Shift from excavation to construction.
- Three shrine areas.
- Marco Polo's "Seven Pagodas" reference (PDF pp. 86–87).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 8th century; UNESCO 1984 (owner-stated, ⚠️ not fetched).
**Links:** ART-1107, ART-1109, ART-1112.
**Trap:** Do not present Pallava structural temples as the end of the trajectory (owner 03 §13.8).
**Mains hook:** Pallava as initiating a trajectory, not completing it.
**Back:** *"Rajasimha: rock to structure, Shore Temple by the sea."*

### Flashcard ART-1111 — Pallava murals: Kailasanatha and Panamalai, and the survival caution
**Front:** What survives of Pallava painting?
**Definition (learn):** Narasimhavarman II Rajasimha (700–720 CE) patronised wall painting in the ambulatory of the Kailasanatha Temple at Kanchi, with Shiva-Parvati legends; Panamalai preserves Parvati watching Shiva's dance. These are "the only two surviving examples of the Pallava mural paintings" (✅ owner 03 §13.8, PDF pp. 88, 324).
**Breakdown:**
- Mahendravarman I's Mamandur paintings are earlier.
- Survival, not original extent.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 700–720 CE.
**Links:** ART-1107, ART-1108, ART-1112.
**Trap:** Survival is minimal; do not infer the original extent (❌ owner 03 §13.8).
**Mains hook:** Use the survival caveat when estimating the Pallava contribution.
**Back:** *"Two murals survive; Kanchi and Panamalai."*

### Flashcard ART-1112 — 2024 Mains PYQ: Pallava contribution to art and literature
**Front:** What is the 2024 Q2 Pallava demand and how should a 150-word answer run?
**Definition (learn):** "Estimate the contribution of Pallavas of Kanchi for the development of art and literature of South India. (Answer in 150 words)" (✅ UPSC Mains 2024 GS Paper I, owner 03 §7). Architecture first; literature jointly with topic 11.
**Breakdown:**
- Mahendravarman I rock-cut start (610–630 AD).
- Longhurst's four styles.
- Mahabalipuram Pancha Rathas and Shore Temple (UNESCO 1984).
- Kanchipuram: Dandin's *Avantisundari Katha*.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 10 marks, 150 words.
**Links:** ART-1107, ART-1109, ART-1110, ART-1111.
**Trap:** A list of monuments with no development logic (owner 03 §13.1).
**Mains hook:** Estimate as a transition, not a final style; the Cholas inherited it.
**Back:** *"Rock, rathas, Shore, Dandin: a transition."*

### Flashcard ART-1113 — Chola phased architecture: Narttamalai to Shembiyan Mahadevi
**Front:** What phases show the development of Chola temples?
**Definition (learn):** Earliest phase: Narttamalai Shiva temple by Vijayalaya (mid-9th c), vimana with ardha-mandapa and six parivaralayas; next phase under Aditya I and Parantaka I (late 9th/early 10th c): Brahmapureshvara, Nageshvaraswami and Koranganatha, antarala and mukhamandapa added; third phase: Shembiyan Mahadevi's patronage (✅ owner 03 §13.9 & PDF pp. 90–91).
**Breakdown:**
- Narttamalai: parivaralayas.
- Aditya I and Parantaka I: devakoshtha niches.
- Shembiyan Mahadevi: rebuilt brick temples in stone.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Mid-9th to early 10th c.
**Links:** ART-1082, ART-1114, ART-1116.
**Trap:** Do not present Brihadishvara as the first Chola temple.
**Mains hook:** Phased building shows development not a single monument.
**Back:** *"Narttamalai, Parantaka, Shembiyan: development in phases."*

### Flashcard ART-1114 — Brihadishvara, Thanjavur: the Chola climax
**Front:** What is the Brihadishvara temple at Thanjavur?
**Definition (learn):** Built by Rajaraja I in the early 11th century, also called Rajarajeshvara, Periya Kovil and Dakshina Meru; a huge vimana with a towering pyramidal shikhara; pillared porch, mukhamandapa, ardhamandapa, antarala and garbhagriha; a huge rectangular enclosure with large gopurams; a Nandi Mandapa with the second-largest monolithic Nandi in the country (✅ owner 03 §13.9, PDF p. 91).
**Breakdown:**
- Vimana (Dravida).
- Enclosure with gopurams.
- Nandi monolith (Lepakshi's is largest).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Early 11th c. UNESCO 1987 (Great Living Chola Temples, ART-1119).
**Links:** ART-1115, ART-1119, ART-1088, ART-1074, ART-1118.
**Trap:** Brihadishvara is one of three components of one serial World Heritage property (ART-1119).
**Mains hook:** Brihadishvara as technical and ceremonial climax, and a living temple.
**Back:** *"Thanjavur: Rajaraja's vimana, granite, Dakshina Meru."*

### Flashcard ART-1115 — Brihadishvara: first fully granite temple and its height
**Front:** What does the source claim about Brihadishvara's stone and height?
**Definition (learn):** The book calls it "the first fully granite temple in the world" and gives the vimana as about 60 m (✅ owner 03, PDF p. 91). UNESCO's component page gives the tower as about 59.82 m and consecration around 1009–1010 CE (✅ UNESCO WHC 250 fetched).
**Breakdown:**
- Height: "about 60 m" (book) / 59.82 m (UNESCO).
- Consecration: around 1009–1010 CE (UNESCO).
- "First fully granite" is the book's claim; flag as a claim, not verified.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Do not quote a decimal vimana height from the book (❌ owner 03 §7).
**Links:** ART-1114, ART-1119.
**Trap:** Quoting an invented decimal height; or the book's "world first" claim without a caveat (⚠️).
**Mains hook:** Use "about 60 m" and "early 11th century" in an answer.
**Back:** *"About sixty metres, consecrated near 1010."*

### Flashcard ART-1116 — Shembiyan Mahadevi: a queen as temple patron
**Front:** Who was Shembiyan Mahadevi and why does she matter?
**Definition (learn):** A Chola queen, patron during the reign of her husband Gandaraditya and her son Uttama I, under whom many brick temples were rebuilt in stone (✅ owner 03, PDF p. 91).
**Breakdown:**
- Elite women as major patrons.
- Chola patronage not exclusively male or royal-male.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gandaraditya; Uttama I.
**Links:** ART-1113, ART-1098.
**Trap:** Do not generalise to the condition of women (owner 03 §13.10 table).
**Mains hook:** Gender and patronage evidence from temple records.
**Back:** *"Shembiyan: a queen who turned brick into stone."*

### Flashcard ART-1117 — Gangaikondacholapuram: an incomplete successor
**Front:** Why can Gangaikondacholapuram not be compared with Thanjavur?
**Definition (learn):** Rajendra I's Brihadishvara at Gangaikondacholapuram is incomplete; it has a lower vimana and an inward-curving shikhara but more heavily sculpted walls (✅ owner 03, PDF p. 92). UNESCO's page places it in Perambalur district (✅ UNESCO WHC 250).
**Breakdown:**
- Lower vimana; inward-curving shikhara.
- Heavier wall sculpture.
- Incomplete.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Rajendra I; Perambalur district.
**Links:** ART-1114, ART-1119, ART-1122.
**Trap:** Do not compare its completed scale with Thanjavur (❌ owner 03 §13.9).
**Mains hook:** Incompleteness is a limit in a "comment" answer.
**Back:** *"Gangaikonda: unfinished, lower, heavier."*

### Flashcard ART-1118 — Airavatesvara, Darasuram: Rajaraja II's temple
**Front:** What is Airavatesvara at Darasuram?
**Definition (learn):** A 12th-century temple built under Rajaraja II, named after Indra's white elephant; it displays Vaishnava and Shakta traditions alongside Shaiva ones and legends of the 63 Nayanars; the Rajagambhira Thirumandapam courtyard has carved pillars and horse-drawn stone chariots (✅ owner 03, PDF p. 92; UNESCO WHC 250 names Darasuram).
**Breakdown:**
- Nayanar legends.
- Rajagambhira Thirumandapam.
- Tamil administration inscription (see the 2024 Q11 route, ART-1123).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 12th c; Rajaraja II.
**Links:** ART-1119, ART-1113, ART-1114.
**Trap:** UNESCO's OUV places it at Tanjavur while the description names Darasuram (⚠️ UNESCO internal wording).
**Mains hook:** Inclusive iconography as the temple's own claim to comprehensiveness.
**Back:** *"Indra's elephant: Shiva, Vishnu, Shakta, Nayanars."*

### Flashcard ART-1119 — Great Living Chola Temples: one serial property
**Front:** What is the Great Living Chola Temples World Heritage property?
**Definition (learn):** A serial property of three temple complexes: Brihadisvara Thanjavur (inscribed 1987), with Gangaikondacholapuram and Airavatesvara Darasuram added in 2004 (✅ owner 03 §13.9 and ✅ UNESCO WHC 250 fetched).
**Breakdown:**
- Thanjavur 1987.
- 2004 extension.
- Living places of worship.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** UNESCO criteria shown on the data panel as (ii) and (iii), but the OUV text also justifies (i) and (iv); the page is internally inconsistent (⚠️ fetched).
**Links:** ART-1114, ART-1117, ART-1118, ART-1123.
**Trap:** Not three separate properties (❌ owner 03 §9).
**Mains hook:** "Living" is the closing device: functioning temples, which is why Chola achievement is still remembered.
**Back:** *"One serial property, three temples, two inscriptions."*

### Flashcard ART-1120 — Chola wall painting: Brihadeeswara as the centre
**Front:** Which Chola temple is the most outstanding centre of Chola wall painting?
**Definition (learn):** Vijaya Cholishwaram has the first Chola wall paintings with faint lines; Brihadeeswara is "the most outstanding centre of Chola wall painting" and "the biggest centre of wall painting in India after Ajanta," with Shiva in Kailash, Sundarar, Tripurantaka, Nataraja and a portrait of Rajaraja Chola I with guru Karuvur Devar; Nayaka-period superimpositions (✅ owner 03, PDF p. 327).
**Breakdown:**
- Two temples carry surviving Chola wall painting.
- Nayaka superimpositions complicate dating.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p. 327.
**Links:** ART-1114, ART-1121, ART-1111.
**Trap:** Do not date the visible paint to Rajaraja's time without noting Nayaka overpainting.
**Mains hook:** Painting as a second register of Chola achievement.
**Back:** *"Brihadeeswara: Ajanta's second in the Indian wall."*

### Flashcard ART-1121 — Nataraja: the Chola bronze and its Aihole origin
**Front:** What is the Nataraja in tandava posture and where is its earliest known example?
**Definition (learn):** The Nataraja in tandava posture; its earliest known example is at the Ravana Phadi Cave, Aihole, under early Chalukya rule, but it "reached its peak under the Cholas" (✅ owner 03 §13.9, PDF p. 211). Many South Indian Shiva temples have a separate natana-sabha.
**Breakdown:**
- Chidambaram bronze Nataraja; Thanjavur Brihadeeswara Nataraja.
- Pallava cave reliefs and Hoysala panels are the named comparative set (PDF p. 212).
- Iconography: topic 06.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Ravana Phadi (6th c, ART-1127).
**Links:** ART-1127, ART-1114, ART-1120.
**Trap:** Do not reduce Nataraja to a Chola invention.
**Mains hook:** A bronze icon as an architectural companion of the temple.
**Back:** *"Aihole's dancer, Chola's peak."*

### Flashcard ART-1122 — From vimana to gopura: the shift of emphasis
**Front:** What does the late Chola shift to gopuram show, and where is it exemplified?
**Definition (learn):** In the 12th–13th century, the gopura became more dominant than the vimana, exemplified by the Chidambaram temple under Kulottunga I (✅ owner 03 §13.9, PDF p. 92).
**Breakdown:**
- Earlier: vimana dominance (Thanjavur).
- Later: gopura dominance (Chidambaram).
- Mechanism carried later into Vijayanagara and Nayaka (ART-1144, ART-1151).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Kulottunga I; 12th–13th c.
**Links:** ART-1074, ART-1075, ART-1114, ART-1144, ART-1151, ART-1160.
**Trap:** The Chola formula is superseded, not perpetuated (owner 03 §13.9).
**Mains hook:** The single organising mechanism of Dravida development.
**Back:** *"Vimana gives way; the gopura takes the eye."*

### Flashcard ART-1123 — 2024 Mains PYQ: the great Cholas' art and architecture
**Front:** What is the 2024 Q11 Chola demand and how should the 250-word answer run?
**Definition (learn):** "Though the great Cholas are no more yet their name is still remembered with great pride because of their highest achievements in the domain of art and architecture. Comment. (Answer in 250 words)" (✅ UPSC Mains 2024 GS Paper I, owner 03 §7).
**Breakdown:**
- Brihadishvara (early 11th c; vimana about 60 m; granite).
- Great Living Chola Temples (1987; 2004).
- Chola bronze (Nataraja).
- Airavatesvara inscription on temple administration.
- Limits: labour, extraction, incompleteness.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 15 marks, 250 words.
**Links:** ART-1114, ART-1119, ART-1121, ART-1115.
**Trap:** Praise words without named temples or techniques (owner 03 §13.1).
**Mains hook:** "Still living": monumental, portable and documented, and in use.
**Back:** *"Granite, bronze, record, and still in use."*

### Flashcard ART-1124 — Aihole: the temple laboratory
**Front:** Why is Aihole called the cradle of Hindu temple architecture, and why does it matter for Vesara?
**Definition (learn):** Aihole, on the Malaprabha river in Karnataka, is described as the "Cradle of Hindu Temple Architecture"; it was the first capital of the Early Chalukyas until 543 AD, after which Pulakeshin I shifted the capital to Vatapi (Badami); it has over 120 temples and is named after the merchant guild Ayyavole (✅ owner 03 §13.9A A, PDF pp. 95–98).
**Breakdown:**
- Single site where plan types were tried out.
- Aihole → Badami → Pattadakal: incubator, refinement, culmination (✅ book's three-stage formulation, PDF p. 103).
- Explains why a hybrid label became necessary.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 543 AD (capital shift); 120+ temples.
**Links:** ART-1125, ART-1126, ART-1127, ART-1128, ART-1129, ART-1130, ART-1087.
**Trap:** "Cradle" is the book's descriptive phrase, not a dating claim (⚠️ owner 03).
**Mains hook:** Aihole as the experiment that produced the Vesara debate.
**Back:** *"Aihole: where the temple experiments began."*

### Flashcard ART-1125 — Durga Temple, Aihole: apsidal form and a Sun dedication
**Front:** Why is the Durga Temple at Aihole important and what is its dedication?
**Definition (learn):** An apsidal temple of c. 7th–8th century under Vijayaditya, "believed to be inspired by Buddhist chaityas," named after a nearby fort (durg) and believed to be dedicated to the Sun God Aditya, not to the goddess Durga; it has high-relief Narasimha, Ardhanarishvara, Vishnu on Garuda, Varaha, Mahisamardini and Harihara (✅ owner 03, PDF pp. 96–97).
**Breakdown:**
- Apsidal plan borrowed from chaitya-form.
- Sectarian crossing of temple form.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** c. 7th–8th c AD; Vijayaditya.
**Links:** ART-1033, ART-1124, ART-1087.
**Trap:** Do not translate the name as a Durga dedication; the book corrects this (❌ owner 03).
**Mains hook:** Temple form crossed sectarian lines.
**Back:** *"Durga, named for a fort; Aditya is the god."*

### Flashcard ART-1126 — Lad Khan Temple: stone translation of timber
**Front:** What makes the Lad Khan Temple at Aihole distinctive?
**Definition (learn):** Square plan akin to a village panchayat hall; a rooftop shrine instead of a shikhara; a sloping two-tiered roof fitted with stone logs "definitely inspired by wooden structures"; an inscription refers to the Ayyavole merchant guild (✅ owner 03, PDF p. 97).
**Breakdown:**
- Timber prototype rendered in stone.
- Commercial patron class (Ayyavole) linked to form.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** c. 7th–8th c AD.
**Links:** ART-1124, ART-1074.
**Trap:** The Lad Khan name is late and anecdotal (⚠️ owner 03).
**Mains hook:** A merchant guild can be a patron of architecture.
**Back:** *"Lad Khan: wood, turned to stone, guild-funded."*

### Flashcard ART-1127 — Ravana Phadi: the earliest rock-cut phase at Aihole
**Front:** What does Ravana Phadi show?
**Definition (learn):** A 6th-century AD rock-cut site at Aihole with a Nataraja surrounded by larger-than-life saptamatrikas (✅ owner 03, PDF p. 97).
**Breakdown:**
- Rock-cut and structural work proceed side by side.
- Earliest Nataraja (ART-1121).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 6th c AD.
**Links:** ART-1121, ART-1124.
**Trap:** Do not present rock-cut → structural as a clean chronological ladder here (⚠️ owner 03).
**Mains hook:** Use Ravana Phadi to show overlapping technique.
**Back:** *"Ravana Phadi: Nataraja among the Mothers."*

### Flashcard ART-1128 — Aihole Prashasti: a monument that is also a text
**Front:** What is the Aihole Prashasti and what does it record?
**Definition (learn):** An inscription on the Jain temple wall at Meguti hill, composed by the court poet Ravikirti in honour of Pulakeshin II, in Sanskrit language and old Kannada script; it lists victories over the Gangas of Mysore, Alupas, Konkan, Kadambas of Banavasi and Kunala, and names the poets Bharavi and Kalidasa (✅ owner 03, PDF p. 98).
**Breakdown:**
- Deccan equivalent of Hathigumpha.
- Eulogy (prashasti): claims are commemorative.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Pulakeshin II; Ravikirti.
**Links:** ART-1053, ART-1124, ART-1129.
**Trap:** Do not treat the eulogy as neutral history.
**Mains hook:** Sanskrit in old Kannada script shows a bilingual inscriptional culture.
**Back:** *"Ravikirti praises Pulakeshin II: Sanskrit in Kannada script."*

### Flashcard ART-1129 — Badami cave temples: dated, patron-named, sect-plural
**Front:** What are the Badami cave temples?
**Definition (learn):** Carved from soft red sandstone in Bagalkot district near the Malaprabha and the Agastya lake; begun by Pulakeshin I, continued by Kirtivarman I, completed by Mangalesha; four caves: three Brahmanical and one Jain. Cave 3 is the largest and the earliest dated Hindu temple in the Deccan, carrying Mangalesha's four-line old-Kannada inscription of 578 AD; Cave 4 is Jain (Parsvanath, Mahavir, Bahubali) (✅ owner 03, PDF pp. 99–101).
**Breakdown:**
- Cave 1: Nataraja, Mahishasuramardini, Ardhanarishvara.
- Cave 2: Vishnu, Trivikrama, Harihara.
- Cave 3: 578 AD inscription.
- Cave 4: Jain tirthankaras.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 578 AD; Vatapi (Badami).
**Links:** ART-1124, ART-1128, ART-1131, ART-1064.
**Trap:** Badami is not a World Heritage property; it is on the Tentative List with Aihole and Pattadakal (⚠️ owner 03 table). Badami is not India's oldest surviving rock-cut group (recorded PYQ trap, owner 02).
**Mains hook:** The Deccan's answer to Ellora at smaller scale.
**Back:** *"Badami: three Hindu, one Jain, 578 AD."*

### Flashcard ART-1130 — Pattadakal: the Chalukyan culmination (UNESCO 1987)
**Front:** What is Pattadakal and why does it matter?
**Definition (learn):** A UNESCO World Heritage Site (1987) on the Malaprabha in Bagalkot district; the site of Chalukyan coronations (Pattabhisheka); largely 7th–8th century; nine Hindu and one Jain temple; "blends the north Indian Naagar style and the South Indian Dravidian style" (✅ owner 03, PDF pp. 101–102).
**Breakdown:**
- Both superstructure grammars on one site.
- Demonstrates hybridity rather than asserting it.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** UNESCO 1987 (owner-stated; UNESCO page not fetched ⚠️).
**Links:** ART-1131, ART-1132, ART-1133, ART-1134, ART-1087.
**Trap:** Cite the 1987 year, not a count of Chalukyan properties (owner 03).
**Mains hook:** Pattadakal is the decisive evidence that Vesara names a practice of selection.
**Back:** *"Pattadakal: nine Hindu, one Jain, both styles."*

### Flashcard ART-1131 — Virupaksha, Pattadakal: queen's victory temple
**Front:** Who commissioned the Virupaksha at Pattadakal and what does it commemorate?
**Definition (learn):** The largest and most profusely sculpted shrine at Pattadakal, a Shiva temple commissioned by Queen Lokmahadevi in the 8th century AD to commemorate the conquest of Kanchipuram by her husband Vikramaditya II; its shikhara is built in Dravidian style (✅ owner 03, PDF p. 102).
**Breakdown:**
- Patron: Lokmahadevi (variant Lokamahadevi).
- Pillared hall, antechamber, sanctum with circumambulation passage.
- Deep carvings of Shiva on outer-wall niches.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 8th c AD; Vikramaditya II; Kanchi conquest.
**Links:** ART-1132, ART-1135, ART-1130.
**Trap:** Do not confuse with the Hampi Virupaksha (ART-1135).
**Mains hook:** Patronage as legitimacy with a named queen, king and war.
**Back:** *"Lokamahadevi, Kanchi, Dravida spire, Shiva."*

### Flashcard ART-1132 — Papanatha, Pattadakal: the Nagara half of the pair
**Front:** How does the Papanatha temple complete the Pattadakal evidence?
**Definition (learn):** Dedicated to Shiva, similar in plan to Virupaksha, but its shikhara is built in the north Indian Nagara style; outer walls carved with Ramayana panels (✅ owner 03, PDF p. 103).
**Breakdown:**
- Same plan as Virupaksha.
- Different superstructure grammar.
- Same site.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Pattadakal.
**Links:** ART-1131, ART-1087, ART-1088.
**Trap:** Not the same superstructure as Virupaksha (❌ owner 03 §9).
**Mains hook:** The cleanest demonstration that Vesara names selection.
**Back:** *"Virupaksha south spire, Papanatha north spire, one plan."*

### Flashcard ART-1133 — Mallikarjuna, Pattadakal: a second queen's commission
**Front:** Who commissioned the Mallikarjuna temple at Pattadakal and what does it show?
**Definition (learn):** A similar but smaller temple commissioned by Queen Trilok Mahadevi, with carvings of Samudra Manthan and Mahisasuramardini (✅ owner 03, PDF pp. 102–103).
**Breakdown:**
- Two royal women commissioned temples on one site.
- Usable for gender and patronage demands.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Dates not given in the source; do not add.
**Links:** ART-1131, ART-1130, ART-1116.
**Trap:** Do not add dates or dedications the book does not give (owner 03).
**Mains hook:** Female patronage in the Chalukyan world.
**Back:** *"Trilok Mahadevi: Samudra Manthan on the wall."*

### Flashcard ART-1134 — Jain Narayana, Pattadakal: a Rashtrakuta attribution
**Front:** Which dynasty built the Jain Narayana temple at Pattadakal?
**Definition (learn):** Built by the Rashtrakuta dynasty in the 9th century AD, unlike the Hindu temples built by the Chalukyas (✅ owner 03, PDF p. 103).
**Breakdown:**
- Site continuity across a dynastic change.
- Rashtrakuta, not Chalukya.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 9th c AD.
**Links:** ART-1130, ART-1048, ART-1064.
**Trap:** Do not attribute it to the Chalukyas (❌ owner 03 §13.12).
**Mains hook:** Successive dynasties on one sacred site.
**Back:** *"Jain Narayana: Rashtrakuta, ninth century."*

### Flashcard ART-1135 — Virupaksha: Pattadakal versus Hampi
**Front:** How do the two Virupaksha temples differ?
**Definition (learn):** The Pattadakal Virupaksha is Early Chalukyan, commissioned by Queen Lokmahadevi; the Hampi Virupaksha is Vijayanagara, begun in the Sangama period and expanded by Krishnadevaraya (✅ owner 03 §9 & §13.9A).
**Breakdown:**
- Pattadakal: Chalukya, 8th c, Dravida shikhara.
- Hampi: Vijayanagara, 50-metre gopuram (book figure).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Pattadakal 8th c; Hampi Sangama period.
**Links:** ART-1131, ART-1144.
**Trap:** Naming the wrong dynasty is a recorded objective trap (❌ owner 03 §9).
**Mains hook:** Use dynasty and site to avoid conflation.
**Back:** *"Two Virupakshas: Chalukya Pattadakal, Vijayanagara Hampi."*

### Flashcard ART-1136 — Hoysala's three canonical temples and the 2023 UNESCO inscription
**Front:** Which three Hoysala temples form the inscribed property?
**Definition (learn):** Chennakeshava Temple, Belur (banks of the Yagachi); Hoysaleswara Temple, Halebidu (banks of the Dwarasamudra lake); and Keshava Temple, Somanathapura (banks of the Kaveri), all inscribed as a UNESCO World Heritage Site in 2023 (✅ owner 03 §13.9A C; owner records a UNESCO page check dated 13 August 2026, not re-fetched here).
**Breakdown:**
- Hoysala: 11th–14th c; capital at Belur, later Dwarasamudra (Halebidu).
- More than 100 temples survive (book figure).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2023; Sacred Ensembles of the Hoysalas.
**Links:** ART-1137, ART-1138, ART-1139, ART-1140.
**Trap:** Owner's table calls the Somanathapura temple "Chennakeshava"; UNESCO's component is Keshava Temple; prefer UNESCO naming (⚠️ naming variant).
**Mains hook:** Three named monuments plus a dated status line close a 15-mark Hoysala demand.
**Back:** *"Belur, Halebidu, Somanathapura: 2023."*

### Flashcard ART-1137 — Hoysala stellate plan and soapstone
**Front:** What are the plan and material of Hoysala temples?
**Definition (learn):** Built on a raised jagati following a stellate (star-shaped) design, walls in zig-zag; multiple shrines around a central pillared hall; carved from chloritic schist, "soft and easy to carve when quarried, but it hardens over time" (✅ owner 03, PDF p. 105).
**Breakdown:**
- Stellate plan; extended terrace as pradakshinapatha.
- Friezes of Ramayana, Mahabharata, animals, floral motifs.
- Lathe-turned pillars; Nandi Pavilion; kalyani.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Soapstone is the material explanation for carving density.
**Links:** ART-1077, ART-1136, ART-1139.
**Trap:** Do not extend soapstone to non-Hoysala Deccan temples (❌ owner 03 §13.9A C).
**Mains hook:** Material explains carving density.
**Back:** *"Star plan, soapstone, friezes that harden."*

### Flashcard ART-1138 — Vishnuvardhana, Ramanuja and the Belur victory
**Front:** What motives does the source attach to Vishnuvardhana's temple building?
**Definition (learn):** Vishnuvardhana (12th c), the most notable Hoysala ruler, converted from Jainism to Vaishnavism under the influence of the philosopher Ramanujacharya and commissioned the Chennakeshava temple at Belur to commemorate his victory over the Cholas, and the Hoysaleswara temple at Halebidu (✅ owner 03, PDF p. 105).
**Breakdown:**
- War, conversion and philosopher as the motives (book's account).
- Cross-link: topic 13's Vishishtadvaita.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 12th c; conversion is the book's statement about influence, not a dated event.
**Links:** ART-1136, ART-1137, ART-1139.
**Trap:** Do not build a chronology on the conversion (⚠️ owner 03).
**Mains hook:** Temple, war and doctrine in one patronage row.
**Back:** *"Vishnuvardhana: a victory, a conversion, a philosopher."*

### Flashcard ART-1139 — The safe Hoysala "Vesara" sentence
**Front:** How does the source describe Hoysala style, and why is that sentence the safest way to use "Vesara"?
**Definition (learn):** Hoysala temples are "constructed in Vesara style," having "a Dravidian morphology along with substantial influences from the Bhumija style prevalent in Central India, [and] the Nagara traditions of northern and western India" (✅ owner 03, PDF p. 106).
**Breakdown:**
- Describe morphology first, then the label.
- Do not describe Vesara as 50-50.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p. 106.
**Links:** ART-1087, ART-1137, ART-1161.
**Trap:** Vesara as a mechanical half-and-half mixture (❌ owner 03 §9).
**Mains hook:** Use the source sentence to define the label carefully.
**Back:** *"Dravidian body, Bhumija and Nagara accents."*

### Flashcard ART-1140 — Hoysala artisans who signed their work
**Front:** Who were the named Hoysala artists, and what does UNESCO say about signatures?
**Definition (learn):** Dasoja, Chavana and Mallithamma were prominent Hoysala architects and sculptors (✅ owner 03, PDF p. 106); UNESCO records that the numerous signatures left by the artists are "an unusual practice in the Indian subcontinent" (✅ owner 03, quoting UNESCO OUV).
**Breakdown:**
- Named craftsmen: rare.
- Artistic agency and standing.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Dasoja, Chavana, Mallithamma.
**Links:** ART-1136, ART-1138.
**Trap:** Do not assign a specific temple to a specific named sculptor (❌ owner 03 §13.9A C).
**Mains hook:** Named craftsmen in a "who made Indian art" demand.
**Back:** *"Dasoja, Chavana, Mallithamma: signed stone."*

### Flashcard ART-1141 — Vijayanagara: founding and four dynasties
**Front:** When and by whom was Vijayanagara founded, and which four dynasties ruled it?
**Definition (learn):** Founded by Harihara and Bukka in 1336 AD on the Tungabhadra (also Pampa Kshetra), with Hampi as capital; the four ruling dynasties were Sangama (1336–1485), Saluva (1485–1503), Tuluva (1491–1570) and Aravidu (1542–1646) (✅ owner 03 §13.9A D, PDF pp. 110–111).
**Breakdown:**
- Synthesis of Chola, Hoysala, Pandya, Chalukya and Bijapur Indo-Islamic features.
- Wide chariot streets flanked by pillared mandapas.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1336; four dynasties with dates as given.
**Links:** ART-1142, ART-1143, ART-1144, ART-1148.
**Trap:** Saluva and Tuluva overlap in the source's dates; quote the dates as the source gives them.
**Mains hook:** Name the borrowed traditions instead of saying "eclectic."
**Back:** *"Harihara and Bukka, 1336; four houses to 1646."*

### Flashcard ART-1142 — Kalyana Mandapa: the wedding hall (CSE 2019)
**Front:** Which kingdom made the Kalyana Mandapa a notable feature in the CSE 2019 item?
**Definition (learn):** A Kalyana Mandapa is a Hindu temple wedding hall, a Vijayanagara feature; the CSE 2019 question offers Chalukya, Chandela, Rashtrakuta and Vijayanagara, and the source's own key gives item 41 as (d) Vijayanagara (✅ owner 03 §13.9B, PDF pp. 168–169, 185).
**Breakdown:**
- Vitthala complex at Hampi: Kalyana Mandapa "refers to a wedding hall."
- Lepakshi Veerabhadra: incomplete Kalyana Mandapa with Shiva-Parvati wedding (PDF p. 329).
- Distractors: Pattadakal (Chalukya), Khajuraho (Chandela), Ellora Kailasa (Rashtrakuta).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** CSE 2019; key (d).
**Links:** ART-1141, ART-1145, ART-1111.
**Trap:** Kalyana Mandapa is not an Indo-Islamic or Chalukyan feature (❌ owner 03 §9; owner 04 correction recorded).
**Mains hook:** The form is a hall type, not a one-off.
**Back:** *"Wedding hall: Vijayanagara, key (d)."*

### Flashcard ART-1143 — Mahanavami Dibba: royal ceremonial platform, not a temple
**Front:** What is the Mahanavami Dibba and how should it be distinguished from temples?
**Definition (learn):** A huge, high, three-tiered stepped platform at Hampi used for ritual purposes, army reviews and the nine-day Mahanavami festival; the Portuguese traveller Domingo Paes records that Krishnadevaraya erected it to mark a victorious campaign in Odisha (✅ owner 03 §13.9A D, PDF p. 114). Attribution rests on a traveller's account (⚠️).
**Breakdown:**
- Royal ceremonial architecture.
- Carries daily-life scenes on its tiers.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Early 16th c (book's description).
**Links:** ART-1141, ART-1144, ART-1147.
**Trap:** The Virupaksha temple is not located on the Mahanavami Dibba (recorded CAPF 2024 statement pair, owner 03).
**Mains hook:** The temple/secular distinction is what UPSC tests.
**Back:** *"Dibba: royal stage for Mahanavami, not a shrine."*

### Flashcard ART-1144 — Raya Gopuram and the Hampi Virupaksha
**Front:** What is the Raya Gopuram and which temple does it belong to?
**Definition (learn):** The Virupaksha Temple at Hampi, dedicated to Shiva as the patron deity of Vijayanagara, has a 50-metre large gopuram (book figure); construction began in the Sangama period and was expanded by Krishnadevaraya; the Raya Gopuram, a monumental entrance tower, was "introduced in temple architecture for the first time by Raja Krishnadevaraya" (✅ owner 03, PDF p. 113).
**Breakdown:**
- Gopuram-dominated entrance.
- Evidence for transfer of emphasis from vimana to gopura.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 50 m (book); Sangama period; Krishnadevaraya (Tuluva).
**Links:** ART-1122, ART-1075, ART-1135.
**Trap:** Do not confuse with the Chalukyan Pattadakal Virupaksha (ART-1135).
**Mains hook:** The single clearest evidence for the vimana-to-gopura shift.
**Back:** *"Raya Gopuram: Krishnadevaraya's first gateway tower."*

### Flashcard ART-1145 — Vitthala complex and its stone chariot
**Front:** What are the key facts about the Vitthala complex at Hampi?
**Definition (learn):** A Vishnu temple in the Vitthala form, constructed by Krishnadevaraya; features an iconic stone chariot (explicitly not monolithic) that appears on the ₹50 note; musical pillars in the Rang Mandapa; and a Kalyana Mandapa (✅ owner 03, PDF pp. 113–114).
**Breakdown:**
- Stone chariot: not monolithic.
- Musical pillars: Rang Mandapa.
- Kalyana Mandapa: wedding hall.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ₹50 note reference (owner-stated).
**Links:** ART-1142, ART-1141, ART-1146.
**Trap:** Do not call the stone chariot monolithic (❌ owner 03 §13.12). Do not merge its musical pillars with Hazara Rama's (ART-1149).
**Mains hook:** Use the chariot and musical pillars as Vitthala's identifiers.
**Back:** *"Vitthala: stone chariot, singing pillars, wedding hall."*

### Flashcard ART-1146 — Yali: the guardian motif
**Front:** What is the Yali and how does it carry across Vijayanagara to Nayaka?
**Definition (learn):** A mythical creature (part horse, part lion), prominent on temple pillars of the Vijayanagara empire, a guardian protecting human beings physically and spiritually; in Tamil literature it has a cat-like body, a lion's head with elephant tusks and a serpent's tail, and is "symbolic of the Nayaka architectural identity" (✅ owner 03, PDF pp. 114, 118).
**Breakdown:**
- Vijayanagara pillars.
- Nayaka gopuram painting.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Two descriptions differ slightly.
**Links:** ART-1141, ART-1151, ART-1154.
**Trap:** Report the tradition, not one canonical anatomy (⚠️ owner 03).
**Mains hook:** A motif that demonstrably continues across the transition.
**Back:** *"Yali: guardian of pillars, identity of the Nayaka."*

### Flashcard ART-1147 — Lotus Mahal and the secular Indo-Islamic register
**Front:** Where does Indo-Islamic influence appear at Hampi?
**Definition (learn):** In the secular and royal zone: the Lotus Mahal (arched roof, Indo-Islamic influence, in the Zenana enclosure for royal women), the domed elephant stable, the Queen's Bath with stepped design and Indo-Islamic arches, and the monolithic Lakshmi Narasimha on Shesha (✅ owner 03, PDF pp. 114–115).
**Breakdown:**
- Palace forms Islamicate.
- Temple forms Dravidian.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Zenana enclosure.
**Links:** ART-1141, ART-1143.
**Trap:** Do not claim Indo-Islamic form in the Hampi temples (❌ owner 03 §13.12).
**Mains hook:** The precise defensible version of Vijayanagara "synthesis."
**Back:** *"Lotus Mahal: Islamic palace, Dravidian temple."*

### Flashcard ART-1148 — Talikota (1565), Mackenzie (1800) and UNESCO 1986 at Hampi
**Front:** What is the destruction-and-recovery arc of Hampi?
**Definition (learn):** The capital was conquered, looted and destroyed in 1565 at the Battle of Talikota by a coalition of the Adil Shahi, Nizam Shahi, Qutub Shahi and Barid Shahi, after which the capital shifted to Penukonda and then Chandragiri; ruins were brought to light in 1800 by Colonel Colin Mackenzie, first Surveyor General of India; Robert Sewell published *A Forgotten Empire* in 1900; UNESCO inscribed the Group of Monuments at Hampi in 1986 (✅ owner 03, PDF pp. 115–116).
**Breakdown:**
- 1565 Talikota.
- 1800 Mackenzie.
- 1900 Sewell.
- 1986 UNESCO.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1565; 1800; 1900; 1986.
**Links:** ART-1141, ART-1145, ART-1155.
**Trap:** Hampi was once on the Danger List and has been removed (owner 03 §13.9A D; topic 14).
**Mains hook:** Destruction, recovery and conservation status in one arc.
**Back:** *"1565 fall, 1800 found, 1986 saved."*

### Flashcard ART-1149 — Hazara Rama Temple: a second named royal builder
**Front:** Which temple at Hampi was built by Devaraya II and what is distinctive about it?
**Definition (learn):** Hazara Rama Temple, constructed by Devaraya II, dedicated to Rama, with Ramayana reliefs; its pillars produce melodious sounds when struck (✅ owner 03, PDF p. 114).
**Breakdown:**
- Patron: Devaraya II.
- Dedication: Rama; Ramayana reliefs.
- Musical pillars (not the Vitthala ones).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Devaraya II.
**Links:** ART-1141, ART-1145.
**Trap:** Do not merge its musical pillars with the Vitthala Rang Mandapa's (owner 03 §13.9A D).
**Mains hook:** A second named temple with a second named patron.
**Back:** *"Hazara Rama: Devaraya's Rama, singing pillars."*

### Flashcard ART-1150 — Nayaka style: late Dravida as repair and enlargement
**Front:** Who were the Nayakas and what kind of architecture did they produce?
**Definition (learn):** Nayakas were feudatories under Vijayanagara; after Talikota the Madurai Nayakas asserted autonomy and ruled from the 16th to 18th century; they revitalised temples previously ransacked by the Delhi Sultans and founded the Nayaka style (✅ owner 03, PDF pp. 116–117). The style flourished under Thirumalai Nayak in the 17th century at Madurai, Thanjavur, Tiruchirapalli, Gingee and Ikkeri (✅ owner 03).
**Breakdown:**
- Additive: repair and enlargement.
- Continuation of late Vijayanagara style, influenced by Chola and Pandya traditions.
- "Pallavas and Cholas were the pioneers of early Dravidian architecture whereas Nayakas were the pioneers of the late Dravidian architecture" (PDF p. 117).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 16th–18th c; Thirumalai Nayak (17th c; spelt Tirumala Nayaka in some places).
**Links:** ART-1151, ART-1152, ART-1160.
**Trap:** Do not extend "ransacked by the Delhi Sultans" into a general destruction narrative (⚠️ owner 03).
**Mains hook:** The book's own periodisation of the Dravida trajectory.
**Back:** *"Nayakas: feudatories who repaired, then enlarged."*

### Flashcard ART-1151 — Nayaka enclosure plan: concentric rectangles and graded gopuras
**Front:** How is a Nayaka temple laid out?
**Definition (learn):** The temple is enclosed within rectangular premises with high walls, entered through gopurams on the east and west; concentric rectangles lie within higher enclosing walls, leaving open space between perimeters; four entrances, each wall's gopuram larger than the previous; on the outer side, a pillared hall and a square water tank (✅ owner 03, PDF p. 117).
**Breakdown:**
- Concentric enclosures.
- Graded gopuras.
- Pillared hall and tank.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** General description (not a survey of every temple; ⚠️).
**Links:** ART-1150, ART-1075, ART-1152, ART-1122.
**Trap:** The graded-gopura rule is the book's general description, not universal (⚠️ owner 03).
**Mains hook:** Answers the question of how Dravida temples grew.
**Back:** *"Walls within walls, gates that grow outward."*

### Flashcard ART-1152 — Meenakshi Temple, Madurai: a Nayaka exemplar
**Front:** What makes the Meenakshi Temple at Madurai a model Nayaka monument?
**Definition (learn):** On the banks of the Vaigai; one of the finest examples of the Nayaka style; most of the present campus was repaired, renovated and expanded in the 17th century by Tirumala Nayaka; dedicated to Meenakshi and Sundareshwar, with brother Alagar, representing the confluence of Shaivism, Shaktism and Vaishnavism; one of the Paadal Petra Sthalam (276 Shiva temples in the Tamil Nayanar verses); temple tank Potramarai Kulam (Golden-Lotus Pond); 14 gopurams; the Tamil Nadu State Emblem is based on its gopuram (✅ owner 03, PDF pp. 118–119).
**Breakdown:**
- Form: gopuram hierarchy.
- Patron: Tirumala Nayaka.
- Doctrine: three denominations.
- Canon and state symbol.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 14 gopurams; 276 shrines in the Paadal Petra canon.
**Links:** ART-1150, ART-1151, ART-1153.
**Trap:** Do not date the whole temple to the Nayakas; the present campus is 17th-century work on older roots (❌ owner 03).
**Mains hook:** One monument supplies form, patron, date, doctrine, canon and a state symbol.
**Back:** *"Meenakshi: fourteen gopurams, one state emblem."*

### Flashcard ART-1153 — Nayaka material logic: granite core and stucco superstructure
**Front:** Why could Nayaka gopurams be huge while the sanctum stayed austere?
**Definition (learn):** Granite for the temple core; stucco (mortar over brick) for superstructures and gopurams (✅ owner 03, PDF p. 117).
**Breakdown:**
- Granite: core and sanctum.
- Stucco: gopuram imagery, repeatedly renewed.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Visible figures are not construction dates (⚠️ owner 03).
**Links:** ART-1152, ART-1151, ART-1075.
**Trap:** The date of visible stucco figures is not the date of construction.
**Mains hook:** Material explains the Nayaka skyline.
**Back:** *"Granite below, stucco above, renewed each time."*

### Flashcard ART-1154 — Rameswaram's longest prakaram and Srirangam
**Front:** What functional device does Rameswaram's prakaram show?
**Definition (learn):** Emphasis on large gopurams, large temple tanks and wide corridors called prakarams, which facilitate processions and pradakshina; Shri Ramanathaswamy Temple, Rameswaram, has the longest prakaram in the world (book's claim) (✅ owner 03, PDF pp. 117–118). Srirangam (Ranganatha, a Vishnu form, on the Kaveri) and Rameswaram had immense contribution from Nayaka rulers (PDF p. 119).
**Breakdown:**
- Prakaram = processional corridor.
- Tank = ritual water.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** "Contribution to," not "built by" (⚠️ owner 03).
**Links:** ART-1151, ART-1152, ART-1150.
**Trap:** "Built by Nayakas" is not what the source says for Srirangam and Rameswaram.
**Mains hook:** A named, functional corridor makes "big temple" concrete.
**Back:** *"Rameswaram: the longest corridor, Srirangam on the Kaveri."*

### Flashcard ART-1155 — Ramappa Temple: the engineering row and 2021 UNESCO inscription
**Front:** What engineering techniques does the Ramappa Temple show, and when was it inscribed?
**Definition (learn):** The Ramappa (Kakatiya Rudreshwara) Temple, near Ramappa Lake, Mulugu district, Telangana, is a 13th-century Shiva temple built in the interest of Recharla Rudra, a general of Ganapati Deva, taking about 40 years; UNESCO World Heritage Site 2021; "perhaps the only temple in the country which is known by the name of its architect, Ramappa"; sandbox technique (foundation pit filled with sand-lime, jaggery and black myrobalan); a floating stone in the ceiling; star-shaped platform; pinkish sandstone with black basalt for figures (✅ owner 03, PDF pp. 108–110).
**Breakdown:**
- Sandbox foundation cushion.
- Floating ceiling stone.
- Star-shaped platform.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 13th c; about 40 years; UNESCO 2021 (owner-stated).
**Links:** ART-1156, ART-1135, ART-1148.
**Trap:** Marco Polo's "brightest star in the galaxy of temples" is reported as supposed, not documented (⚠️ owner 03).
**Mains hook:** The strongest "technology of temple building" row.
**Back:** *"Ramappa: sand cushion, floating stone, architect's name."*

### Flashcard ART-1156 — Thousand Pillar Temple, Warangal: borrowed morphology and Tughlaq damage
**Front:** What do the Thousand Pillar Temple's style, damage and restoration show?
**Definition (learn):** Built between the 12th and 14th centuries by order of the Kakatiya king Rudra Deva, in the later Chalukyan and early Kakatiyan style; star-shaped platform; sand-box technique; a Trikutalayam of three shrines (Rudreshwara, Vasudeva, Surya); an inscription of Rudradeva records the construction date; damaged in the Tughlaq invasion of the Deccan and later restored with a grant from Mir Osman Ali Khan, the 7th Nizam (✅ owner 03, PDF pp. 106–108).
**Breakdown:**
- Style: later Chalukyan and early Kakatiya.
- Trikutalayam: three shrines.
- Restoration: Nizam.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 12th–14th c.
**Links:** ART-1155, ART-1137, ART-1139.
**Trap:** Warangal Fort and the Kakatiya Kala Thoranam are on the Tentative List, not inscribed (⚠️ owner 03).
**Mains hook:** Extends the borrowed-morphology argument.
**Back:** *"Thousand Pillars: three shrines, Tughlaq hit, Nizam restored."*

### Flashcard ART-1157 — Pala-Sena school: the Bengal style
**Front:** What is the Pala-Sena school of Bengal?
**Definition (learn):** The Bengal school that developed between the 8th and 12th centuries AD under the Pala and Sena dynasties: Palas were Mahayana Buddhist rulers who built viharas, chaityas and stupas; Senas were Hindu and built temples while also sustaining Buddhist architecture (✅ owner 03 §13.9D A, PDF pp. 120–121).
**Breakdown:**
- Material: burnt bricks and clay (terracotta brick).
- Tower: tall, curving shikhara with a large amalaka, similar to Odisha (ART-1083).
- Sculpture: stone and metal; lustrous finish.
- Named examples: Siddhesvara Mahadeva, Barakar; temples around Bishnupur (Tentative List).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 8th–12th c.
**Links:** ART-1083, ART-1158, ART-1085.
**Trap:** Bishnupur is a Tentative-List entry, not an inscribed WHS (⚠️ owner 14 §13.11).
**Mains hook:** Material drives form; religion layered not replaced; influence runs upward.
**Back:** *"Pala and Sena: bricks, curving spire, two faiths."*

### Flashcard ART-1158 — The Bangla roof: vernacular form entering Mughal architecture
**Front:** What is the Bangla roof and why is it significant?
**Definition (learn):** Buildings in the Sena style had a curved or sloping roof "as in bamboo huts," which became popularly known as the Bangla roof and was later adopted by Mughal architects (✅ owner 03 §13.9D A, PDF pp. 120–121).
**Breakdown:**
- Vernacular bamboo-hut form.
- Entered imperial architecture.
- Counter-example to top-down style accounts.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Later Mughal adoption.
**Links:** ART-1157, ART-1159.
**Trap:** Do not present it as an invented 'Mughal' form (⚠️).
**Mains hook:** Influence running upward from vernacular to imperial.
**Back:** *"Bamboo hut roof, Mughal adoption."*

### Flashcard ART-1159 — Regional gaps: Kerala and Ahom are not architectural evidence here
**Front:** What regional architectural evidence does this folder not hold?
**Definition (learn):** For Kerala, the sources give murals (Trivanchikulam, Triparayar, Padmanabhaswamy, Pundareekapuram, Guruvayur, Padmanabhapuram, Krishnapuram and Matancheri palaces) but no architectural style; for Ahom, the Moidams at Charaideo (inscribed 26 July 2024) are the only sourced monument, but no Ahom palaces or brick techniques (✅ owner 03 §13.9D B; owner 14 §13.8A).
**Breakdown:**
- Kerala: murals, performance, palaces — but not sloping roofs or srikovil.
- Ahom: Moidams (mound-burial system; criteria iii and iv).
- Sikh: chhatris and domes via topic 04, not Sri Harimandir Sahib.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Moidams 26 July 2024 (owner 03 cites topic 14).
**Links:** ART-1157, ART-1158.
**Trap:** Do not invent a Kerala or Ahom architectural vocabulary (❌ owner 03 §13.9D B).
**Mains hook:** Say the architectural style lies outside the evidence base and answer through murals or the named palaces.
**Back:** *"Kerala: murals not roofs. Ahom: mounds not palaces."*

### Flashcard ART-1160 — Advanced: the Pallava-to-Nayaka trajectory mechanism
**Front:** What single mechanism organises the southern temple trajectory?
**Definition (learn):** The shift of emphasis from vimana to gopura, across rock-cut → monolithic → structural → imperial vimana → gateway-dominated enclosure (⚠️ owner 03 §13.3 and §13.1).
**Breakdown:**
- Pallava: rock-cut, rathas, Shore Temple.
- Chola: Brihadishvara vimana; Gangaikondacholapuram incomplete; gopura emerging at Chidambaram.
- Chalukya/Deccan counter-current: Aihole, Badami, Pattadakal.
- Rashtrakuta: Kailasa.
- Hoysala: stellate.
- Vijayanagara: Raya Gopuram.
- Nayaka: Meenakshi.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Conservation status 1984, 1986, 1987, 2004, 2021, 2023 (owner dates).
**Links:** ART-1107, ART-1114, ART-1122, ART-1144, ART-1151.
**Trap:** A dynasty-by-dynasty list with no explained change (owner 03 §13.1).
**Mains hook:** A 20-mark route: vocabulary, Pallava, Chola, Chalukya, Rashtrakuta, Hoysala, Vijayanagara, Nayaka, status and verdict.
**Back:** *"Vimana gives way to gopura, from rock to Nayaka."*

### Flashcard ART-1161 — Advanced: Vesara as a label for a practice of selection
**Front:** How should a sophisticated answer defend or qualify the term Vesara?
**Definition (learn):** Vesara is best defended as a label for a practice rather than a third box: at Pattadakal, Dravida and Nagara shikharas stand on similar plans; the Hoysalas are described as Dravidian in morphology with Bhumija and Nagara influences; the category came afterwards to describe what Deccan patrons had already done (⚠️ owner 03 §13.9A verdict).
**Breakdown:**
- Evidence: Virupaksha (Dravida) vs Papanatha (Nagara).
- Hoysala: source's own morphology sentence.
- Label caution.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Pattadakal; Hoysala.
**Links:** ART-1087, ART-1131, ART-1132, ART-1139.
**Trap:** Do not present Vesara as a settled third category.
**Mains hook:** The honest claim is selection from both grammars.
**Back:** *"Vesara: a practice that named itself later."*

### Flashcard ART-1162 — 2022 Mains PYQ: medieval temple sculpture as social life
**Front:** How should a 10-mark answer on medieval temple sculpture as social evidence run?
**Definition (learn):** "Medieval Indian temple sculptures represent the social life of those days. How will you explain this statement?" (CSE 2022 GS-I Q1; 10 marks, 150 words; ✅ owner 03 §13.10).
**Breakdown:**
- Thesis: selected social evidence.
- Registers: performance (Khajuraho dancers and musicians), instruction (teachers and disciples), court and everyday life (Darasuram Rajagambhira), devotional movement (Nayanar legends), gender and patronage (Shembiyan Mahadevi), ritual (Kalyanasundara Murti), labour and craft (Dasoja etc.).
- Limit: erotic sculpture is not behaviour; royal temples are not village life.
- Commissioned-representation rule.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Kalyanasundara Murti, 9th c AD (PDF p. 212).
**Links:** ART-1098, ART-1116, ART-1118, ART-1140, ART-1099.
**Trap:** Repeating the claim instead of qualifying it (owner 03 §13.10).
**Mains hook:** Temple sculpture evidences what patrons chose to make visible.
**Back:** *"Seven registers, one limit: what patrons chose to show."*

### Flashcard ART-1163 — 2026 Prelims GS-I Q4: Nagara shikhara (provisional key)
**Front:** What does the 2026 Prelims GS-I Q4 demand on temple architecture?
**Definition (learn):** The 2026 Prelims GS-I question 4 demand is "Nagara-style shikhara among early Indian temple architecture examples"; the 2026 Set-A key held locally is provisional and no answer letter is recorded or inferred here (✅ owner 03 2026 PYQ block).
**Breakdown:**
- Cover the statement-level distinction between Nagara shikhara and Dravida vimana (ART-1074).
- Use Khajuraho, Odisha, Solanki as Nagara sub-schools.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Provisional key; not answered here.
**Links:** ART-1074, ART-1085, ART-1080, ART-1086.
**Trap:** Do not treat a provisional key as official; do not infer an answer (owner 03).
**Mains hook:** Objective item; the discriminator is spire type and sub-school.
**Back:** *"Nagara spire: curved, north, sub-schools."*

## INDIAN ART AND CULTURE — Part 2: Indo-Islamic and Colonial Architecture, Sculpture, Painting and Music

### GROUP — 04 Indo-Islamic and Regional Architecture (basic/04 · advanced/04)

### Flashcard ART-2001 — Trabeate to arcuate: the structural shift

**Front:** What structural change frames Indo-Islamic architecture?

**Definition (learn):** The shift from trabeate (post-and-lintel, horizontal beams) to arcuate (load-bearing arch, vault and dome) construction, which became large-scale from the 12th century AD onward. Arches and domes were known earlier; the Sultanate spread their scientific, large-scale use with stronger lime mortar.

**Breakdown:**
- Trabeate: lintel-and-beam; remained active inside Sultanate and Mughal buildings.
- Arcuate: arch, vault, dome; characterised by arch and dome entrances and bulbous or hemispherical tops.
- Coexistence: slab-and-beam work sat alongside arch and dome (hybrid method).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Arcuate introduced/expanded from the 12th century AD (✅ owner 04, visual map). Lime mortar as the technical lever (✅ owner 04, citing Satish Chandra, PDF pp. 194–196).

**Links:** ART-2002, ART-2003, ART-2021

**Trap:** "Arches and domes were introduced by Babur/the Mughals." Wrong: both were known earlier; the Sultanate normalised them.

**Mains hook:** Argue "industrialisation, not invention": the decisive contribution was scientific, large-scale execution. Qualify with the coexistence of trabeate practice.

**Back:** *"Not who invented the arch, but who made it routine."*

### Flashcard ART-2002 — "Indo-Islamic" vs colonial "Indo-Saracenic"

**Front:** Why must "Indo-Saracenic" be used with care?

**Definition (learn):** In medieval usage, Indo-Islamic and Indo-Saracenic are parallel terms for the Delhi Sultanate/Mughal synthesis. In the 19th–20th century, "Indo-Saracenic" (Indo-Gothic) names a British colonial revival style, a different historical phenomenon.

**Breakdown:**
- Medieval: structural and material synthesis of arcuate and trabeate methods, indigenous craft labour.
- Colonial: Gothic Revival frame (pointed arches, ribbed vaults, stained glass) with Indian features (bulbous domes, jaali, tall turrets).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Medieval usage PDF p. 126 (✅ owner 04); colonial usage owner 05 §2 (✅ owner 05).

**Links:** ART-2001, ART-2033

**Trap:** Treating the shared word as one phenomenon. The colonial revival is stylistic appropriation over European structure, not medieval structural hybridity.

**Mains hook:** Precise terminology is the first mark. Contrast "structural hybridity" (medieval) with "stylistic appropriation" (colonial).

**Back:** *"Same word, two centuries: medieval synthesis, colonial revival."*

### Flashcard ART-2003 — Qutb Minar: a three-reign monument

**Front:** Who built the Qutb Minar and in what sequence?

**Definition (learn):** A Delhi monument begun by Qutb-ud-Din Aibak, substantially completed by Iltutmish, and whose damaged upper portions were repaired/rebuilt by Firoz Shah Tughlaq.

**Breakdown:**
- Aibak: begins the tower.
- Iltutmish: substantial completion.
- Firoz Shah Tughlaq: repair and rebuilding of damaged upper work.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Imperial Sultanate style (Slave/Mameluke dynasty); PDF pp. 129–130 (✅ owner 04); ASI Qutb Minar complex as cited by owner (✅ owner 04).

**Links:** ART-2004, ART-2005, ART-2001

**Trap:** Crediting one sultan with the whole tower.

**Mains hook:** A layered patronage record. The same method applies to Sanchi (topic 02) and any long-construction monument.

**Back:** *"Aibak began, Iltutmish finished, Firoz Shah mended."*

### Flashcard ART-2004 — Quwwat-ul-Islam and the 27 temples

**Front:** What does the Quwwat-ul-Islam mosque inscription record?

**Definition (learn):** The Quwwat-ul-Islam mosque (Qutb complex, Delhi) carries an inscription recording reuse of material from 27 demolished Hindu and Jaina temples. It is the one specifically documented spolia case in the owner's sources.

**Breakdown:**
- Documented reuse: 27 temples, Hindu and Jaina.
- Use: evidence of continuity of building fabric across a religious and political transition.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p. 130 and ASI Qutb Minar complex (✅ owner 04, advanced §4).

**Links:** ART-2003, ART-2021

**Trap:** Describing it as the conversion of one Jain temple, or generalising into systematic destruction across the whole period (✅ owner 04 advanced §9).

**Mains hook:** Use a specific, sourced case (spolia) instead of an unsupported "temples destroyed to build mosques" claim.

**Back:** *"Twenty-seven temples, one inscription: specific, not systematic."*

### Flashcard ART-2005 — Imperial vs provincial Sultanate styles

**Front:** How is Delhi Sultanate architecture (1206–1526) divided?

**Definition (learn):** Imperial style, patronised by Delhi's rulers (Slave/Mameluke, Khilji/Seljuk, Tughlaq, Lodi), and provincial style, patronised by local rulers (Bengal, Jaunpur/Sharqi, Malwa/Pathan, Bijapur/Deccan).

**Breakdown:**
- Imperial: Slave, Khilji, Tughlaq, Lodi; Khilji red sandstone; Tughlaq grey sandstone and batter (sloping walls); Lodi double dome.
- Provincial: Bengal (Bangla roof), Jaunpur (no minars), Malwa (ventilating windows, baulis), Bijapur (bulbous dome).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Sultanate period 1206–1526 AD (✅ owner 04, §3); PDF p. 129 (✅ owner 04); material and device table (✅ owner 04 advanced §3).

**Links:** ART-2006, ART-2007, ART-2008, ART-2009, ART-2010

**Trap:** Stating that minars are a universal feature of every school.

**Mains hook:** Imperial versus provincial shows architecture as both political statement and environmental adaptation. Name a monument for each device.

**Back:** *"Delhi commissions, locals adapt: imperial for the capital, provincial for the climate."*

### Flashcard ART-2006 — The double dome (Lodi to Humayun)

**Front:** What is the double dome, and where does it mature?

**Definition (learn):** A dome built in two shells, so that the external skyline and the interior ceiling are separated. In mature form by the Lodi/Humayun transition.

**Breakdown:**
- Problem solved: interior height and exterior profile were in tension.
- Early Indian case: Sikandar Lodi's tomb.
- Monumental case: Humayun's tomb.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-stated (✅ owner 04, §4 and §13.4). Humayun's Tomb inscribed 1993 (⚠️ owner-only, see ART-2024).

**Links:** ART-2005, ART-2011

**Trap:** Assigning a single "inventor" of the double dome.

**Mains hook:** A structural-proportion answer: the double dome separated the exterior image from the interior space.

**Back:** *"Two skins: one for the sky, one for the ceiling."*

### Flashcard ART-2007 — Bangla roof (Bengal)

**Front:** What is the Bangla roof and why does it matter?

**Definition (learn):** A sloping roof in Bengal provincial architecture, translating the local hut form into masonry. It was later borrowed by Mughal architects.

**Breakdown:**
- Driver: heavy rainfall and local vernacular form.
- Evidence of adaptation: a regional roof form transferred into a monumental mosque programme.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 132–136 (✅ owner 04). Bengal school also used burnt-brick and terracotta-brick building (✅ owner 06, PDF p. 121).

**Links:** ART-2005, ART-2076

**Trap:** Attributing the Bangla roof to the Mughals. It is a Bengal provincial form, later borrowed.

**Mains hook:** Provincial architecture as proof that Indo-Islamic form was not an imported template.

**Back:** *"Bengal roofed its masonry like its huts."*

### Flashcard ART-2008 — Jaunpur (Sharqi) style

**Front:** What marks the Jaunpur (Sharqi) style?

**Definition (learn):** A provincial school of the Sharqi sultanate at Jaunpur, known for monumental screen facades and no minars. Atala Mosque is the example.

**Breakdown:**
- Device: monumental screen facade.
- Absence: no minars.
- Driver: political self-assertion of a regional sultanate.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Atala Mosque (✅ owner 04, §13.5). No minars (✅ owner 04, §9).

**Links:** ART-2005, ART-2009

**Trap:** Assuming a minar in every provincial school. Jaunpur and Malwa both avoided them.

**Mains hook:** Use the device plus monument as the evidence unit.

**Back:** *"Jaunpur: screens, not minars."*

### Flashcard ART-2009 — Malwa (Pathan) school

**Front:** What features distinguish Malwa's Pathan school?

**Definition (learn):** A provincial school with large ventilating windows, lightly arched airy pavilions and baulis (step-wells). It has no minars.

**Breakdown:**
- Climate response: heat and ventilation.
- Water: baulis for storage.
- Form: no minars.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 133–136 (✅ owner 04 advanced §2 and basic §4).

**Links:** ART-2005, ART-2008, ART-2010

**Trap:** Confusing a baoli (step-well) with a tomb or a minar.
