**Mains hook:** Environmental adaptation argument against a single monolithic Indo-Islamic style.

**Back:** *"Malwa breathes and stores water: big windows, baulis, no minars."*

### Flashcard ART-2010 — Bijapur (Deccan) and the Gol Gumbaj

**Front:** What is the Bijapur school's signature?

**Definition (learn):** A Deccan provincial school with a three-arched facade and a near-spherical bulbous dome, exemplified by the Gol Gumbaj.

**Breakdown:**
- Facade: three-arched.
- Dome: near-spherical, bulbous.
- Driver: Deccani court culture and engineering ambition.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gol Gumbaj (✅ owner 04, §13.5).

**Links:** ART-2005, ART-2009, ART-2023

**Trap:** Placing Bijapur in the Mughal or Delhi tradition. It is a separate Deccan school.

**Mains hook:** Adaptation thesis: the dome pushed toward a sphere.

**Back:** *"Bijapur bends the dome into a sphere."*

### Flashcard ART-2011 — Akbar's architecture and Fatehpur Sikri

**Front:** What were Akbar's architectural materials and key works?

**Definition (learn):** Red sandstone and the four-centred "Tudor" arch dominated under Akbar. Fatehpur Sikri was the capital (1571–1585), with the Buland Darwaza and the white-marble tomb of Sheikh Salim Chishti.

**Breakdown:**
- Material: red sandstone (imperial scale).
- Arch: four-centred Tudor arch.
- Sites: Agra Fort (begun under Akbar; most interior structures are Shah Jahan's); Fatehpur Sikri.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Capital 1571–1585; Ibadat Khana 1575 (✅ owner 04, PDF pp. 138–140).

**Links:** ART-2012, ART-2013, ART-2014

**Trap:** Attributing Agra Fort's interiors to Akbar.

**Mains hook:** Scale and sandstone as an imperial signature; marble reserved for the sacred.

**Back:** *"Akbar: red sandstone, Tudor arch, Fatehpur Sikri 1571–85."*

### Flashcard ART-2012 — Ibadat Khana and Din-i-Ilahi

**Front:** What happened at the Ibadat Khana and what was Din-i-Ilahi's reach?

**Definition (learn):** The Ibadat Khana at Fatehpur Sikri (1575) hosted interfaith debates under Sulh-i-Kul ("universal tolerance"). This led to Din-i-Ilahi (1582), a syncretic order that "never had mass acceptance."

**Breakdown:**
- Setting: Ibadat Khana (architecture).
- Policy: Sulh-i-Kul.
- Outcome: Din-i-Ilahi, with limited following.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1575; 1582; PDF p. 140 (✅ owner 04).

**Links:** ART-2011, ART-2021

**Trap:** Describing Din-i-Ilahi as widely adopted. Keep the physical setting and the doctrinal outcome separate.

**Mains hook:** The 2025 GS-I Q2 (Akbar's religious syncretism) is primarily owned by Medieval History. Architecture supplies only the setting. ⚠️ Owner routing.

**Back:** *"Ibadat Khana debated; Din-i-Ilahi never spread."*

### Flashcard ART-2013 — Itmad-ud-Daulah's tomb

**Front:** What is Itmad-ud-Daulah's tomb, and why is it a turning point?

**Definition (learn):** A tomb commissioned by Empress Nur Jahan for her father Mirza Ghiyas Beg (titled I'timad-ud-Daulah). It is described as the first Mughal structure built entirely of white marble.

**Breakdown:**
- Patron: Nur Jahan.
- Material: white marble.
- Ornament: pietra dura inlay, described as transitional toward the Taj.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Construction 1622–1628 (✅ Wikipedia). Patron and "first all-white-marble" (✅ owner 04). Architect not named (✅ Wikipedia).

**Links:** ART-2011, ART-2014

**Trap:** Calling it the first Mughal pietra dura work. Wikipedia calls it transitional in its inlay and does not call it the first to use pietra dura. ⚠️ Owner phrase "with fine pietra dura" is a feature, not a "first".

**Mains hook:** A datable material shift that stands independent of inscriptions.

**Back:** *"Nur Jahan's father's tomb: the first all-marble Mughal building."*

### Flashcard ART-2014 — Mughal material chronology

**Front:** Why does material date Mughal buildings?

**Definition (learn):** Akbar's red sandstone gives way to white marble, which begins with Itmad-ud-Daulah's tomb under Jahangir/Nur Jahan and culminates under Shah Jahan.

**Breakdown:**
- Akbar: red sandstone, scale.
- Jahangir/Nur Jahan: white marble becomes the chief material.
- Shah Jahan: marble, garden and inlay culmination.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p. 141 (✅ owner 04 advanced §2).

**Links:** ART-2011, ART-2013, ART-2015

**Trap:** Using red sandstone and white marble interchangeably. The shift is a datable marker.

**Mains hook:** Material dating works for undocumented Mughal-period structures, as a qualified technique rather than a universal rule.

**Back:** *"Sandstone for scale, marble for surface."*

### Flashcard ART-2015 — Shah Jahan and the Taj authorship

**Front:** Who designed the Taj Mahal?

**Definition (learn):** Shah Jahan's reign is the Mughal culmination: Taj Mahal, Red Fort and Jama Masjid. Nitin associates Ustad Ahmad Lahori with several projects. Satish Chandra treats the Taj as a collective workshop achievement with no single settled designer.

**Breakdown:**
- Culmination works: Taj Mahal, Red Fort, Jama Masjid.
- Named specialist: Ustad Ahmad Lahori (associated, not proved sole designer).
- Collective workshop model: Satish Chandra, Part 2.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Satish Chandra, *Part 2*, PDF p. 388 (✅ owner 04).

**Links:** ART-2014, ART-2016

**Trap:** Naming Ustad Ahmad Lahori as the uncontested sole architect of the Taj.

**Mains hook:** Reject single-genius narratives; workshop production is the better model.

**Back:** *"Many hands built the Taj; one name is not the whole story."*

### Flashcard ART-2016 — Aurangzeb and Bibi Ka Maqbara

**Front:** How should Aurangzeb's building record and Bibi Ka Maqbara be assessed?

**Definition (learn):** Aurangzeb commissioned fewer major imperial buildings and was economy-minded (Satish Chandra). Nitin links the reduced building to puritanism. Mughal architectural forms continued in regional 18th-century practice. Bibi Ka Maqbara should be compared formally with the Taj.

**Breakdown:**
- Patronage: fewer imperial commissions.
- Motive: debated (religious personality, war, fiscal priority, economy).
- Continuity: Mughal idiom in regional practice.
- Comparison: patronage, scale, material, garden.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 04 advanced §2 and §9.

**Links:** ART-2015, ART-2019

**Trap:** Dismissing Bibi Ka Maqbara as a "poor imitation," and explaining reduced building through a single religious motive.

**Mains hook:** Separate fewer imperial commissions from the survival of the idiom.

**Back:** *"Fewer tombs, same idiom: compare, don't insult."*

### Flashcard ART-2017 — Rajput architecture: jharokha and Hawa Mahal

**Front:** What are the signature devices of Rajput architecture?

**Definition (learn):** A regional style that developed under Mughal influence and retained distinct features: the jharokha (hanging balcony) and bow-shaped arched cornices.

**Breakdown:**
- Device 1: jharokha.
- Device 2: bow-shaped arched cornice.
- Example: Hawa Mahal, Jaipur.
- Driver: court display and climate-screened viewing.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 144–147 (✅ owner 04).

**Links:** ART-2018, ART-2019, ART-2020

**Trap:** Treating Rajput architecture as decayed Mughal work.

**Mains hook:** Regional styles as continuing traditions, not derivatives.

**Back:** *"Jharokha for display, Hawa Mahal for the view."*

### Flashcard ART-2018 — Sikh architecture: Harmandir Sahib

**Front:** What are the features of Sikh architecture?

**Definition (learn):** Multiple chhatris, shallow cornices, onion domes and foliated arches. Harmandir Sahib (Golden Temple) is the example.

**Breakdown:**
- Chhatris (multiple).
- Shallow cornices.
- Onion dome.
- Foliated arch.
- Driver: congregational and devotional programme.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 144–147 (✅ owner 04).

**Links:** ART-2017, ART-2019

**Trap:** Assuming the onion dome is a Mughal import without regional context. Describe it as a regional Sikh grammar.

**Mains hook:** Regional divergence as a counter-example to homogenised Mughal influence.

**Back:** *"Chhatri, onion dome, foliated arch: Harmandir's grammar."*

### Flashcard ART-2019 — Awadh architecture: mortar

**Front:** What distinguishes Awadh (Lucknow) architecture?

**Definition (learn):** 18th–19th century architecture in Lucknow that used mortar rather than marble or sandstone. Examples are the Bara Imambara, Chota Imambara and Rumi Darwaza.

**Breakdown:**
- Material: mortar.
- Examples: Bara Imambara, Chota Imambara, Rumi Darwaza.
- Context: cost and speed. Famine-relief employment as context is ⚠️ owner-only.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 144–147 (✅ owner 04).

**Links:** ART-2016, ART-2017, ART-2018

**Trap:** Treating mortar as a "lesser" material or as a sign of Mughal decline.

**Mains hook:** Materials and cost as design drivers in regional courts.

**Back:** *"Awadh builds in mortar: Imambaras and Rumi Darwaza."*

### Flashcard ART-2020 — Ornamental vocabulary of Indo-Islamic architecture

**Front:** Name the decorative terms and their precise meanings.

**Definition (learn):**
- Jaali: perforated stone or lattice screen; the importance of light in Islamic religious space.
- Arabesque: continuous, regularly splitting geometric-vegetal ornament.
- Calligraphy with optical scaling: letters enlarged high on a facade to look even from below.
- Pietra dura / parchin kari: inlay of cut coloured (often semi-precious) stones into marble.
- Charbagh: square garden divided into four identical quadrants.

**Breakdown:** Light (jaali), pattern (arabesque), inscription (calligraphy), inlay (pietra dura), garden (charbagh).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 126–127 (✅ owner 04).

**Links:** ART-2001, ART-2014, ART-2021

**Trap:** Calligraphy's optical scaling is not pictorial foreshortening. Figural avoidance is general in mosque decoration, not universal.

**Mains hook:** Precise vocabulary beats "intricate designs."

**Back:** *"Jaali admits light, arabesque never stops, charbagh splits four ways."*

### Flashcard ART-2021 — The synthesis mechanism

**Front:** How should the Indo-Islamic synthesis be argued?

**Definition (learn):** Hybrid building method arises from imported arcuate knowledge, indigenous craft labour and trabeate practice, local materials and borrowed motifs (lotus, bell, swastika). Outcome: regionally differentiated buildings.

**Breakdown:**
- Imported: arch, vault, dome, lime mortar.
- Indigenous: craftsmen and trabeate practice.
- Local: stone, brick, mortar.
- Borrowed motifs: lotus, bell, swastika.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Satish Chandra's Sultanate and Mughal chapters (✅ owner 04, §13.7).

**Links:** ART-2001, ART-2004, ART-2020

**Trap:** "Two styles meeting" or "fixed Hindu and Muslim essences." Both are wrong.

**Mains hook:** A synthesis of practice (shared workshops, materials and structural methods), not a meeting of two fixed styles.

**Back:** *"One industry, borrowed technology, local hands."*

### Flashcard ART-2022 — The Kalyana Mandapa routing correction

**Front:** Is a kalyana mandapa an Indo-Islamic form, as the CSE 2019 Prelims route suggests?

**Definition (learn):** No. A kalyana mandapa is a wedding hall within a Hindu temple complex. The owner's answer is Vijayanagara, verified from the source's own answer key (item 41 = (d)).

**Breakdown:**
- Substantive owner: basic/03 §13.9B (Vitthala Temple, Hampi; Lepakshi).
- This file's contribution: Indo-Islamic formal vocabulary explains Vijayanagara's secular buildings (Lotus Mahal, Queen's Bath, domed elephant stable), not the temples.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-stated answer key (⚠️ owner-only; key not independently checked here).

**Links:** ART-2001

**Trap:** Answering a temple-hall question with arcuate vocabulary.

**Mains hook:** Know the boundary of each owner. Temple hall type belongs to basic/03.

**Back:** *"Kalyana mandapa: wedding hall, Hindu temple, Vijayanagara."*

### Flashcard ART-2023 — Maratha Military Landscapes (UNESCO 2025)

**Front:** What current UNESCO fortification anchor relates to regional architecture?

**Definition (learn):** The Maratha Military Landscapes of India, inscribed by UNESCO in 2025. A living comparative fortification case, Maratha in origin rather than Mughal.

**Breakdown:**
- Year: 2025 (owner-stated).
- Relevance: comparison of fortification traditions in regional architecture.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Inscription year 2025 (⚠️ owner-stated; components and boundaries not verified here).

**Links:** ART-2010, ART-2024

**Trap:** Stating component forts, criteria or dimensions beyond the year.

**Mains hook:** A current anchor for a fortification comparison. Verify the UNESCO property page first.

**Back:** *"2025: Maratha forts on the UNESCO list. Year only; check details."*

### Flashcard ART-2024 — UNESCO years: Fatehpur Sikri and Humayun's Tomb

**Front:** What are the UNESCO inscription years for Fatehpur Sikri and Humayun's Tomb?

**Definition (learn):** Owner-stated inscription years: Fatehpur Sikri 1986; Humayun's Tomb 1993. Counts and other UNESCO detail route through topic 14.

**Breakdown:**
- Fatehpur Sikri: 1986.
- Humayun's Tomb: 1993.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ Owner-only (✅ owner 04, §13.9); not re-fetched here.

**Links:** ART-2011, ART-2023

**Trap:** Adding counts, dates or criteria not in the owner.

**Mains hook:** Cite the inscription year as the current-heritage anchor in a Mughal architecture answer.

**Back:** *"Fatehpur Sikri 1986, Humayun's Tomb 1993: cite years only."*

### GROUP — 05 Colonial and Post-Independence Architecture (basic/05 · advanced/05)

### Flashcard ART-2025 — Colonial architecture by coloniser, and the "colonial hangover"

**Front:** How is colonial architecture in India sequenced, and what did the book call the post-1947 problem?

**Definition (learn):** Colonial architecture is classified by coloniser: Portuguese (Iberian style, patio houses, Baroque), French (Cartesian grid, "anonymous architecture"), and British (Indo-Gothic, then post-1911 Neo-Roman). After 1947, the Revivalist and Modernist schools "could not break away from the colonial hangover" (the book's own verdict).

**Breakdown:**
- Portuguese: Church-authority display.
- French: rational urban plan.
- British: Indo-Gothic (19th to early 20th century), then Neo-Roman/Neo-Classical after 1911.
- Post-1947: Revivalist and Modernist schools.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 149–154 (✅ owner 05). The "colonial hangover" phrase is the book's own (PDF p. 154).

**Links:** ART-2026, ART-2029, ART-2033, ART-2034, ART-2045

**Trap:** Treating "colonial hangover" as meaning post-1947 architecture was identical to colonial architecture. The critique is that neither school achieved a fully autonomous break.

**Mains hook:** Use the coloniser sequence as the spine, then the hangover critique as the transition into independence.

**Back:** *"Coloniser by coloniser, then 1947 without a clean break."*

### Flashcard ART-2026 — Sé Cathedral, Goa

**Front:** What is the Sé Cathedral of Goa?

**Definition (learn):** A Portuguese-era church in Goa, built in late-Gothic style, known for its "Golden Bell."

**Breakdown:** Portuguese; late-Gothic; Golden Bell.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Built 1619 (✅ owner 05, PDF pp. 149–150).

**Links:** ART-2025, ART-2027, ART-2028

**Trap:** Confusing the late-Gothic Sé with the Baroque Basilica of Bom Jesus (ART-2027).

**Mains hook:** Church architecture as institutional display of religious authority.

**Back:** *"Sé 1619: late-Gothic, Golden Bell."*

### Flashcard ART-2027 — Basilica of Bom Jesus and the Baroque

**Front:** What is the Basilica of Bom Jesus, and what is the Baroque?

**Definition (learn):** Baroque is an elaborate, theatrical style, developed in late-16th-century Europe to express Church strength, introduced to India by the Portuguese. The Basilica of Bom Jesus (Goa) is a Baroque example, a World Heritage property that houses St Francis Xavier's remains.

**Breakdown:**
- Style: Baroque, contrasting colours, theatrical detail.
- Institution: Church strength.
- Building: Basilica of Bom Jesus, Goa.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Built 1605 (✅ owner 05). WHS status ⚠️ routed via topic 14 (owner-stated).

**Links:** ART-2025, ART-2026, ART-2028

**Trap:** Describing Baroque as an Indian style. It is a European style applied to Indian sites.

**Mains hook:** Style as messaging. Baroque communicates Church authority.

**Back:** *"Bom Jesus 1605: Baroque for Church strength."*

### Flashcard ART-2028 — Diu Fort

**Front:** What is the Portuguese-era Diu Fort?

**Definition (learn):** A Portuguese fort at Diu, dated 1535 in the owner's list of Portuguese landmarks.

**Breakdown:** Portuguese; fort (military-institutional architecture); dated 1535.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1535 (✅ owner 05, PDF pp. 149–150).

**Links:** ART-2025, ART-2026, ART-2027

**Trap:** Mixing up the Diu fort with the Goa churches. It is a defensive structure.

**Mains hook:** Portuguese presence was both religious and military.

**Back:** *"Diu 1535: the fort, not the church."*

### Flashcard ART-2029 — French Cartesian grid and "anonymous architecture"

**Front:** What was the French urban signature in India?

**Definition (learn):** A rational Cartesian grid plan in urban planning, used at Puducherry and Chandernagore, alongside "anonymous architecture": simple, unornamented facades.

**Breakdown:**
- Plan: Cartesian grid (urban plan, not monument).
- Facade: anonymous, unornamented.
- Places: Puducherry; Chandernagore.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p. 150 (✅ owner 05). "Anonymous" is the book's descriptive label.

**Links:** ART-2025, ART-2030

**Trap:** Treating "anonymous" as the book's critique. It is a descriptive label.

**Mains hook:** The French signature is the plan rather than the monument.

**Back:** *"French India is a grid, not a monument."*

### Flashcard ART-2030 — French churches and coastal enclaves

**Front:** Which French churches and enclaves are named?

**Definition (learn):** The Church of the Sacred Heart of Jesus (Puducherry) and the Sacred Heart Church (Chandannagar). French coastal towns extended to Mahe (Kerala), Karaikal (Tamil Nadu) and Yanam (Andhra Pradesh).

**Breakdown:**
- Churches: Puducherry, Chandannagar.
- Enclaves: Mahe (Kerala), Karaikal (Tamil Nadu), Yanam (Andhra Pradesh).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p. 150 (✅ owner 05).

**Links:** ART-2029, ART-2025

**Trap:** Putting Chandannagar in Puducherry. They are separate French centres.

**Mains hook:** Use the enclaves to show the spread of French settlement across three states.

**Back:** *"Puducherry and Chandannagar churches; Mahe, Karaikal, Yanam enclaves."*

### Flashcard ART-2031 — Victoria Memorial, Kolkata

**Front:** What is the Victoria Memorial and who designed it?

**Definition (learn):** A British Indo-Gothic monument in Kolkata, built in white marble, designed by William Emerson.

**Breakdown:** Architect: William Emerson. Material: white marble. Style: British Indo-Gothic.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Built 1906–1921 (✅ owner 05, PDF pp. 151–152).

**Links:** ART-2032, ART-2033

**Trap:** Assigning it to the post-1911 Neo-Roman style.

**Mains hook:** A date-bound monument on the Indo-Gothic side of the colonial sequence.

**Back:** *"Victoria Memorial 1906–21: Emerson, white marble, Indo-Gothic."*

### Flashcard ART-2032 — St Paul's Cathedral and other Indo-Gothic buildings

**Front:** Which other Indo-Gothic buildings are named?

**Definition (learn):** St Paul's Cathedral, Kolkata (1847, cruciform plan). Also the Madras High Court and Mysore Palace.

**Breakdown:**
- St Paul's Cathedral: 1847, cruciform plan.
- Madras High Court.
- Mysore Palace.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 151–152 (✅ owner 05).

**Links:** ART-2031, ART-2033

**Trap:** Dating St Paul's to the post-1911 Neo-Roman phase.

**Mains hook:** Give one building and one date for each colonial phase.

**Back:** *"St Paul's 1847: cruciform, Kolkata."*

### Flashcard ART-2033 — Indo-Gothic/Indo-Saracenic: the colonial concept

**Front:** What is the colonial Indo-Gothic (Indo-Saracenic) style?

**Definition (learn):** A 19th–20th century British style that combines Gothic Revival elements (pointed arches, ribbed vaults, stained glass) with Indian features (bulbous domes, jaali, tall turrets).

**Breakdown:**
- European frame: pointed arches, ribbed vaults, stained glass.
- Indian features: bulbous domes, jaali, tall turrets.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p. 151 (✅ owner 05).

**Links:** ART-2002, ART-2031, ART-2032, ART-2034

**Trap:** Conflating it with the medieval Indo-Islamic usage (ART-2002).

**Mains hook:** Stylistic appropriation over European structure, not structural hybridity.

**Back:** *"Gothic frame, Indian dress: the colonial revival."*

### Flashcard ART-2034 — Neo-Roman (Neo-Classical) New Delhi after 1911

**Front:** What is the post-1911 Neo-Roman style?

**Definition (learn):** The British Raj style used for New Delhi after 1911, associated with Lutyens and Baker, described as the "Rome of Hindustan." It introduced the "upturned dome" atop the Supreme Court and the old Parliament/Rashtrapati Bhavan complex.

**Breakdown:**
- Architects: Lutyens and Baker.
- Imperial aim: permanence.
- Signature: upturned dome.
- Book's critique: "anonymous... without any interesting features," "congested and cramped."

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** After 1911 capital transfer (✅ owner 05); PDF p. 153.

**Links:** ART-2025, ART-2033, ART-2045

**Trap:** Treating Indo-Gothic and Neo-Roman as one style. The book makes them sequential.

**Mains hook:** Imperial permanence staged in a new capital, with internal stylistic incoherence per the book's critique.

**Back:** *"1911: Rome of Hindustan, upturned dome."*

### Flashcard ART-2035 — Le Corbusier and Chandigarh

**Front:** Who designed Chandigarh, and for whom?

**Definition (learn):** Chandigarh was designed by Le Corbusier, a French architect, commissioned by the Punjab government. It is the first great post-colonial planned statement.

**Breakdown:** Designer: Le Corbusier (French). Client: Punjab government. Problem: a new capital for a partitioned state.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p. 154 (✅ owner 05).

**Links:** ART-2036, ART-2039, ART-2045

**Trap:** Calling Chandigarh an Indian architect's design.

**Mains hook:** The paradox of the first post-colonial statement designed by a foreign architect.

**Back:** *"Chandigarh: Corbusier's city for Punjab."*

### Flashcard ART-2036 — Laurie Baker: low-cost and climate-responsive housing

**Front:** What did Laurie Baker contribute, and what was his Pritzker status?

**Definition (learn):** Laurie Baker revolutionised low-cost mass housing in Kerala using local materials, filler slab construction, and ventilation and thermal-comfort design. He was nominated for the Pritzker Prize in 2006 and did not win it.

**Breakdown:**
- Method: local material, filler slab (reduces steel and cement), ventilation.
- Client: the poor.
- Nicknames (owner): "Architect of the Poor," "Conscience Keeper of Indian Architecture," "Gandhi of Indian Architecture."

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Pritzker nomination 2006 (✅ Wikipedia; does not state that he won). Filler slab, Kerala housing and nicknames (✅ owner 05; ⚠️ the nicknames and filler slab are not on the Wikipedia page fetched).

**Links:** ART-2041, ART-2037, ART-2045

**Trap:** Claiming Baker won the Pritzker. He was nominated.

**Mains hook:** Baker changed the client and the cost structure, not merely the ornament.

**Back:** *"Baker: nominated 2006, never awarded: cost, not chrome."*

### Flashcard ART-2037 — Charles Correa

**Front:** What are Charles Correa's main works and honours?

**Definition (learn):** Institutional and civic architect. Works: Madhya Pradesh Assembly Building; Mahatma Gandhi Memorial Museum (Ahmedabad); LIC Building (Connaught Place, New Delhi).

**Breakdown:**
- Madhya Pradesh Assembly Building.
- Mahatma Gandhi Memorial Museum, Ahmedabad.
- LIC Building, Connaught Place.
- Courtyard, open-to-sky planning.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Padma Vibhushan 2006 (✅ owner 05, PDF p. 155; not verified here).

**Links:** ART-2038, ART-2045

**Trap:** Mixing up Correa's Padma Vibhushan (2006, owner-stated, ⚠️ owner-only) with Doshi's posthumous Padma Vibhushan (2023) and his Padma Bhushan (2020).

**Mains hook:** Correa's institutional modernism is larger-scale and state-sponsored, contrasted with Baker's community housing.

**Back:** *"Correa: assembly, museum, insurance, courtyards."*

### Flashcard ART-2038 — Achyut Kanvinde

**Front:** What are Achyut Kanvinde's works and honour?

**Definition (learn):** Modern architect who combined modernism with Brutalist elements. Works: IIT Kanpur; National Science Centre (Delhi); Dudhsagar Dairy plant; ISKCON Temple (New Delhi).

**Breakdown:**
- IIT Kanpur.
- National Science Centre, Delhi.
- Dudhsagar Dairy plant.
- ISKCON Temple, New Delhi.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Padma Shri 1974 (✅ owner 05, PDF p. 155; ⚠️ owner-only).

**Links:** ART-2037, ART-2045

**Trap:** Presenting Kanvinde as a Baker-type low-cost architect. He is institutional.

**Mains hook:** Institutional modernism adapts modern forms for civic and educational buildings.

**Back:** *"Kanvinde: IIT Kanpur, Dudhsagar, ISKCON, Brutalism."*

### Flashcard ART-2039 — G.B. Deolalikar and the Supreme Court (1958)

**Front:** Who designed the Supreme Court of India, and when?

**Definition (learn):** G.B. Deolalikar designed the Supreme Court of India (1958), the first major post-Independence state commission by an Indian architect.

**Breakdown:** Architect: G.B. Deolalikar. Year: 1958. Significance: handover of state architecture to Indian authorship.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1958 (✅ owner 05, §13.4 and §13.5A).

**Links:** ART-2035, ART-2043

**Trap:** Naming Chandigarh or the 2023 Parliament as the first Indian state commission. Chandigarh was by Le Corbusier.

**Mains hook:** Step two of a 20-mark post-1947 architects answer.

**Back:** *"1958: the Supreme Court, Deolalikar, the first Indian state commission."*

### Flashcard ART-2040 — B.V. Doshi and the 2018 Pritzker Prize

**Front:** What did B.V. Doshi achieve and which works are named?

**Definition (learn):** Balkrishna Vithaldas Doshi, the first Indian to win the Pritzker Architecture Prize (2018). Named works: Aranya Low Cost Housing (Indore); Sangath (his own studio); IIM Bangalore.

**Breakdown:**
- Pritzker 2018 (first Indian laureate).
- Aranya Low Cost Housing, Indore: "over 6,500 residences," "over 80,000 individuals."
- IIM Bangalore: "inspired by traditional maze-like Indian cities and temples."
- Influences: Le Corbusier and Louis Kahn.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Pritzker 2018, first Indian laureate (✅ Wikipedia; ✅ owner 05 for the Pritzker citation, "verified 13 Aug 2026" per owner, not re-fetched here). IIM Bangalore 1977 start (✅ Wikipedia). Aranya: the owner gives 1989; Wikipedia gives 1982. ⚠️ Unresolved conflict.

**Links:** ART-2036, ART-2041

**Trap:** Aranya's date (⚠️ owner says 1989, Wikipedia says 1982). Check the Pritzker citation before using a date.

**Mains hook:** Aranya (incremental housing at scale) and IIM Bangalore (institutional architecture organised as a city) are the strongest modern climate and cost answers.

**Back:** *"Doshi 2018: Aranya for the poor, IIM as a city."*

### Flashcard ART-2041 — Pritzker precision: Doshi won, Baker was nominated

**Front:** Which Pritzker fact is the likeliest discriminator in a post-1947 architects answer?

**Definition (learn):** B.V. Doshi won the 2018 Pritzker. Laurie Baker was nominated in 2006 and did not win.

**Breakdown:**
- Doshi: won, 2018, first Indian laureate.
- Baker: nominated, 2006, did not win.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ Wikipedia (Baker nominated 2006; Doshi 2018 first Indian). Owner 05 §13.8.

**Links:** ART-2036, ART-2040

**Trap:** Reversing or merging the two facts. Never claim Baker won.

**Mains hook:** Getting this pair right is the single most likely discriminator.

**Back:** *"Doshi won 2018; Baker was only nominated 2006."*

### Flashcard ART-2042 — Raj Rewal, Hall of Nations and the demolition case

**Front:** What are Raj Rewal's works and why is the Hall of Nations significant?

**Definition (learn):** Raj Rewal designed the Hall of Nations at Pragati Maidan, New Delhi, inaugurated 1972, torn down overnight in April 2017. Also the Asian Games Village (Asiad Village), New Delhi, 1982 (⚠️ owner-only).

**Breakdown:**
- Hall of Nations: 1972, designed by Rewal, demolished 2017.
- Significance: a modern-heritage demolition case.
- Owner's reason: 20th-century architecture falls outside the AMASR Act's 100-year threshold (⚠️ owner analysis).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1972; 2017 (✅ Wikipedia). Asian Games Village 1982 (⚠️ owner only).

**Links:** ART-2039, ART-2045

**Trap:** Giving Rewal any Padma award, Pritzker or other honour. The owner says none is in the evidence base, and the owner corrected an earlier claimed "Padma Shri 1989". Do not state the structural system, span or dimensions.

**Mains hook:** Converts an architects question into a conservation-policy argument.

**Back:** *"Rewal's Hall of Nations: 1972 built, 2017 gone."*

### Flashcard ART-2043 — New Parliament House, Samvidhan Sadan and the Mitaoli debate

**Front:** What happened to the 1927 building when the new Parliament was inaugurated?

**Definition (learn):** The new Parliament House (designed by Bimal Patel, inaugurated 2023) became Parliament's main working building. The 1927 building was retained and renamed Samvidhan Sadan.

**Breakdown:**
- New building: Bimal Patel, 2023, main working building.
- Old building: 1927, renamed Samvidhan Sadan.
- Debated claim: Chausath Yogini Temple (Mitaoli) influence on the old building.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 05 (PDF p. 156 for Parliament). Mitaoli influence ⚠️ debated.

**Links:** ART-2039, ART-2045

**Trap:** Attaching the Mitaoli reference to the 2023 building. The debated resemblance concerns the 1927 building, and direct design borrowing is not documented.

**Mains hook:** A datable case of state architecture revisiting the colonial-era seat of power.

**Back:** *"2023 new House, 1927 becomes Samvidhan Sadan."*

### Flashcard ART-2044 — Mumbai's Victorian Gothic and Art Deco ensembles (UNESCO 2018)

**Front:** Which Mumbai ensemble is a current colonial-era UNESCO anchor?

**Definition (learn):** The Victorian Gothic and Art Deco Ensembles of Mumbai, a UNESCO World Heritage property inscribed in 2018 (owner-stated).

**Breakdown:**
- Victorian Gothic: colonial Indo-Gothic phase.
- Art Deco: a later modern phase.
- Current status: check UNESCO for boundary and component detail.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2018 inscription (⚠️ owner-only; not fetched here).

**Links:** ART-2033, ART-2045

**Trap:** Giving boundary or component details without a dated UNESCO source.

**Mains hook:** Close a colonial-heritage answer with a current UNESCO status line.

**Back:** *"Mumbai 2018: Victorian Gothic and Art Deco, UNESCO."*

### Flashcard ART-2045 — Continuity and change across 1947

**Front:** What did independence change in architecture, and what did it keep?

**Definition (learn):** The break came less from changing the style of state buildings than from changing whom architecture was built for. Continuities: monumental civic planning and imported vocabularies. Changes: programme, material and symbolism. Baker and Correa and Doshi show the clearest breaks (cost and climate).

**Breakdown:**
- Power and axis: continuity of planning grammar; change of client.
- Climate: Correa and Baker: courtyards, shade, ventilation.
- Material and economy: Baker's filler slab and rat-trap bond.
- Symbolism: nation, still in inherited monumental language.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 05 §13.6; "could not break away from the colonial hangover" (PDF p. 154).

**Links:** ART-2025, ART-2034, ART-2036, ART-2043

**Trap:** A clean break narrative after 1947. Or the reverse: treating every post-1947 building as colonial.

**Mains hook:** Three successive answers to "whom is a modern Indian building for?" (owner 05 §13.5A thesis).

**Back:** *"The break was a change of client, not of ornament."*

### GROUP — 06 Sculpture, Pottery and Iconography (basic/06 · advanced/06)

### Flashcard ART-2046 — The six-factor method for reading sculpture

**Front:** How should an Indian sculpture be read as historical evidence?

**Definition (learn):** Read every image through six variables, in order: material, posture and gesture, iconographic attribute, patron, site context, and cross-site comparison. Applied to Khajuraho it turns description ("erotic sculptures exist") into argument.

**Breakdown:**
- Material: what the medium permits.
- Posture/gesture: static or dynamic.
- Iconographic attribute: which motif recurs.
- Patron: who commissioned it and why.
- Site: where it is placed and who sees it.
- Comparison: what it resembles and differs from.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 06 §13.4 (✅ owner 06, method is the owner's analytical framework).

**Links:** ART-2064, ART-2065, ART-2072

**Trap:** Letting attribute identification override an available inscription or documented provenance. Attribute reading is supporting evidence.

**Mains hook:** Method thesis: an answer that applies the six variables in order outperforms one that describes appearance.

**Back:** *"Material, pose, attribute, patron, site, comparison: in that order."*

### Flashcard ART-2047 — Cire-perdue (lost-wax) casting

**Front:** What is cire-perdue, and how far back does it go?

**Definition (learn):** Lost-wax bronze casting: a wax figure is coated in clay, heated to melt out the wax, and the mould is filled with molten metal. Known since the Indus period, and the source says it is still practised in many parts of the country.

**Breakdown:**
- Step 1: wax figure.
- Step 2: clay coating.
- Step 3: heating removes the wax.
- Step 4: molten metal fills the void.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Indus period; PDF pp. 190, 193, 195 (✅ owner 06).

**Links:** ART-2049, ART-2068, ART-2064

**Trap:** Continuity of technique is not continuity of style or meaning.

**Mains hook:** Material and technique make the aesthetic possible: free limbs and open silhouette of the Nataraja.

**Back:** *"Wax melts out, metal fills in."*

### Flashcard ART-2048 — Harappan seals: Pashupati and Unicorn

**Front:** Which Harappan seals are named, and how should the Pashupati figure be described?

**Definition (learn):** The Pashupati seal (steatite, Mohenjo-daro) shows a horned seated figure often read as proto-Shiva. R.S. Sharma calls that identification doubtful. The Unicorn Seal is the other named seal.

**Breakdown:**
- Pashupati seal: steatite, Mohenjo-daro, horned seated figure.
- Unicorn seal: named.
- Common seal animals: unicorn, humped bull, rhinoceros, tiger, elephant, buffalo, bison, goat, markhor, ibex, crocodile.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 191–193 (✅ owner 06).

**Links:** ART-2050, ART-2068

**Trap:** Calling "Pashupati" a deciphered identity. The script is undeciphered, and the name is a modern label.

**Mains hook:** Harappan visual culture is recoverable through material and motif; its meaning is not.

**Back:** *"Horned figure, disputed Shiva; unicorn named, script silent."*

### Flashcard ART-2049 — The Dancing Girl and Harappan bronzes

**Front:** What is the Dancing Girl, and why does it matter?

**Definition (learn):** One of the earliest celebrated South Asian lost-wax bronzes. Often described through the later term tribhanga (a three-bend posture).

**Breakdown:**
- Technique: cire-perdue bronze.
- Posture: tribhanga (described in the owner's vocabulary).
- Significance: sophisticated technique in the Harappan corpus.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 191–192 (✅ owner 06). Site not stated in owner; ⚠️ Mohenjo-daro is commonly given (standard knowledge, not verified here).

**Links:** ART-2047, ART-2048, ART-2050

**Trap:** Calling all Harappan sculpture crude. The Dancing Girl and Pashupati seal show refined technique.

**Mains hook:** Sophistication alongside crudity in the same corpus.

**Back:** *"Dancing Girl: earliest famous lost-wax dancer."*

### Flashcard ART-2050 — Harappan terracotta figures and interpretive labels

**Front:** Which Harappan figures are named, and which labels are modern?

**Definition (learn):** Terracotta female figures, the Bearded Priest (steatite, Mohenjo-daro), the Dancing Girl and the Pashupati seal form the Harappan figural corpus. "Mother Goddess," "Priest-King" and "Pashupati" are modern labels, not deciphered identities.

**Breakdown:**
- Terracotta female figures: automatic "Mother Goddess" label is interpretive.
- Bearded Priest: steatite, Mohenjo-daro.
- Labels: "Mother Goddess," "Priest-King," "Pashupati."

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 191–193 (✅ owner 06).

**Links:** ART-2048, ART-2049, ART-2075

**Trap:** Treating modern labels as Harappan self-identities. The script is undeciphered.

**Mains hook:** Caution in interpreting prehistoric art: label is hypothesis.

**Back:** *"Labels are guesses; the script is still silent."*

### Flashcard ART-2051 — Harappan pottery: red ware, not Black-and-Red Ware

**Front:** What is the typical Harappan pottery?

**Definition (learn):** Harappan pottery is predominantly red ware, often painted in black, with plain and painted varieties. Black-and-Red Ware is a separate, wider archaeological category found in several cultures.

**Breakdown:**
- Harappan: red ware, black-painted designs.
- Black-and-Red Ware: separate technical category.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 194, 199 (✅ owner 06).

**Links:** ART-2052, ART-2053

**Trap:** Calling Harappan painted pottery "Black-and-Red Ware." Wrong category.

**Mains hook:** Pottery is a datable material-culture spine for any sources question.

**Back:** *"Harappan: red pots, black paint, not Black-and-Red Ware."*

### Flashcard ART-2052 — The pottery sequence

**Front:** What is the pottery chronology from Neolithic to Mauryan?

**Definition (learn):** Neolithic hand-made coarse ware, then Chalcolithic painted ware (Ahar, Malwa, Jorwe), Harappan Red Ware, Painted Grey Ware (Vedic age), and Mauryan Northern Black Polished Ware (NBPW).

**Breakdown:**
1. Neolithic: hand-made coarse ware.
2. Chalcolithic: painted ware (Ahar, Malwa, Jorwe).
3. Harappan: Red Ware.
4. Vedic: Painted Grey Ware.
5. Mauryan: NBPW.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 194, 199 (✅ owner 06).

**Links:** ART-2051, ART-2053

**Trap:** Mixing Painted Grey Ware (Vedic) with NBPW (Mauryan).

**Mains hook:** The chronology gives a sources question a datable spine.

**Back:** *"Coarse, painted, red, grey, then black polish."*

### Flashcard ART-2053 — Northern Black Polished Ware (NBPW)

**Front:** What is NBPW?

**Definition (learn):** Fine, highly lustrous tableware, often black or steel-grey but also other shades. Associated with early historic urbanisation, not exclusively the Mauryan court.

**Breakdown:**
- Surface: lustrous.
- Shades: black, steel-grey, others.
- Association: early historic urban sites.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 06 (vocabulary and §3). Chandraketugarh NBPW relics (✅ owner 06, PDF p. 38).

**Links:** ART-2052, ART-2075

**Trap:** Assuming NBPW equals the Mauryan court's exclusive ware.

**Mains hook:** Ware and urbanisation together.

**Back:** *"NBPW: lustrous, urban, not just Mauryan court."*

### Flashcard ART-2054 — Yaksha and Yakshi: Didarganj and Parkham

**Front:** What are Yaksha and Yakshi, and which Mauryan examples are named?

**Definition (learn):** Folk deities or nature spirits linked with water, fertility, trees and wilderness, absorbed into Vedic, Buddhist, Jain and Hindu worship. Yakshi is the female counterpart, associated with fertility.

**Breakdown:**
- Yaksha: male nature spirit.
- Yakshi: female, fertility.
- Examples: Didarganj Chauri-bearer/Yakshi; Parkham Yaksha.
- Parkham: "massive strength and gravity."

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 197–198 (✅ owner 06). Didarganj Mauryan date ⚠️ debated.

**Links:** ART-2055, ART-2075

**Trap:** Dating Didarganj firmly to the Mauryas. Its polish supports the attribution, but the debate remains.

**Mains hook:** Popular cult entering monumental sculpture: evidence against a purely court-driven art history.

**Back:** *"Yakshas: folk spirits, Mauryan polish, shared worship."*

### Flashcard ART-2055 — Shalabhanjika

**Front:** What is the Shalabhanjika motif?

**Definition (learn):** A yakshi holding a tree, common as a bracket figure on stupa gateways (Sanchi, Bharhut) and later in Hoysala temples. It symbolises fertility and auspiciousness.

**Breakdown:**
- Figure: yakshi with tree.
- Locations: Sanchi, Bharhut, Hoysala.
- Meaning: fertility, auspiciousness.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 198–199 (✅ owner 06). Carried across Hindu, Buddhist and Jain art for a millennium (✅ owner 06 §13.8).

**Links:** ART-2054, ART-2072

**Trap:** Describing the motif as Buddhist-only. It spans three traditions.

**Mains hook:** A motif traceable across a millennium and three religions.

**Back:** *"Shalabhanjika: a yakshi and a tree, for luck."*

### Flashcard ART-2056 — Kirtimukha

**Front:** What is the Kirtimukha motif?

**Definition (learn):** A monster-faced decorative motif common in South Indian temple iconography, also found in Buddhist architecture.

**Breakdown:**
- Form: monster face.
- Locations: South Indian temples; Buddhist architecture.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p. 208 (✅ owner 06).

**Links:** ART-2072, ART-2058

**Trap:** Confining Kirtimukha to Hindu shrines.

**Mains hook:** Shared motifs across religious architecture.

**Back:** *"Kirtimukha: the monster face in South Indian temples."*

### Flashcard ART-2057 — Mathura school

**Front:** What defines the Mathura school?

**Definition (learn):** An indigenous school of red sandstone sculpture, influenced by "all three religions of the time" (Buddhism, Hinduism and Jainism). Uses avayudhas (attribute-symbols) for Hindu gods, Buddha largely in Abhaya Mudra, and a larger, geometrically decorated halo than Gandhara.

**Breakdown:**
- Material: spotted red sandstone.
- Scope: Buddhist, Hindu, Jain.
- Treatment: avayudha for gods (Shiva through linga); Buddha in Abhaya Mudra; halo.
- Patronage: Kushana and successors.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 200–204 (✅ owner 06).

**Links:** ART-2058, ART-2059, ART-2060

**Trap:** Treating Mathura as exclusively Buddhist or exclusively Hindu.

**Mains hook:** Mathura's shared visual grammar for Indian deities.

**Back:** *"Mathura: red stone, three faiths, attribute symbols."*

### Flashcard ART-2058 — Gandhara school

**Front:** What defines Gandhara sculpture?

**Definition (learn):** A Buddhist iconographic programme executed in a Hellenised idiom, mainly in bluish-grey sandstone (later stucco) under Kushana patronage. "Heavy Greek/Hellenistic, layered with Parthian and Central Asian/Bactrian elements."

**Breakdown:**
- Subject: Buddhist.
- Surface: wavy hair, slender musculature, pleated garments, elongated earlobes, composed stillness.
- Material: bluish-grey sandstone, stucco.
- Diffusion: into Central Asia and East Asia.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 200–205 (✅ owner 06).

**Links:** ART-2057, ART-2059, ART-2073

**Trap:** Calling Gandhara "Greek art" in India. The subject is Indian; the surface treatment is borrowed.

**Mains hook:** Thesis: Buddhist programme in Hellenised idiom under Kushana patronage.

**Back:** *"Gandhara: Buddhist subject, Hellenised surface, Kushana patron."*

### Flashcard ART-2059 — First Buddha image: Gandhara vs Mathura

**Front:** Who made the first anthropomorphic Buddha image: Gandhara or Mathura?

**Definition (learn):** Both schools developed anthropomorphic Buddha imagery in broadly overlapping early-CE contexts, both within the Kushana age. Priority remains debated.

**Breakdown:**
- Aniconic phase: footprint, throne, wheel, tree.
- Anthropomorphic: Gandhara and Mathura, overlapping.
- Exam-safe position: parallel development.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 201–203 (✅ owner 06; R.S. Sharma records both in the Kushana age).

**Links:** ART-2062, ART-2057, ART-2058

**Trap:** "Gandhara made the first human Buddha image." Not settled.

**Mains hook:** Parallel development thesis; don't claim a single origin.

**Back:** *"Two schools, one century: no settled first."*

### Flashcard ART-2060 — Amaravati (Vengi) school

**Front:** What defines the Amaravati school?

**Definition (learn):** The Krishna river basin school, also called the Vengi school. Narrative relief medallions telling full episodes, Tribhanga posture used extensively, and developed on indigenous lines.

**Breakdown:**
- Material: greenish-white limestone.
- Treatment: narrative medallions (e.g. Buddha taming an elephant); tribhanga.
- Patronage: Satavahana; later Ikshvaku (3rd–4th century AD).
- Diffusion: the Buddha image with robe on the left shoulder and right hand in Abhayamudra, replicated across South and Southeast Asia.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 200–204, 299–301 (✅ owner 06).

**Links:** ART-2057, ART-2058, ART-2046

**Trap:** Crediting Amaravati's features to foreign influence. The source says it "developed on purely indigenous lines with no foreign influence."

**Mains hook:** Amaravati solved the problem of telling a story on a curved stone surface.

**Back:** *"Amaravati: limestone stories, tribhanga, Satavahana."*

### Flashcard ART-2061 — Gupta and Sarnath sculpture; Sultanganj Buddha

**Front:** What is the Gupta-period Sarnath idiom, and where does the Sultanganj Buddha fit?

**Definition (learn):** Gupta and post-Gupta sculpture developed around Sarnath, using cream-coloured sandstone and metal, with subtly modelled, thinly draped figures. The c. 7th-century Sultanganj copper Buddha is safer described as late/post-Gupta.

**Breakdown:**
- Material: cream-coloured sandstone and metal.
- Style: restrained, thinly draped figures.
- Sultanganj: copper Buddha, late/post-Gupta.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 203–204 (✅ owner 06). The c. 7th-century dating is owner-stated.

**Links:** ART-2060, ART-2057, ART-2058

**Trap:** Dating Sultanganj firmly to the Gupta period.

**Mains hook:** Gupta restraint against Gandhara drama and Chandella density.

**Back:** *"Sarnath: cream stone, thin drapery. Sultanganj: post-Gupta."*

### Flashcard ART-2062 — Aniconic symbols of the Buddha's life

**Front:** Which symbols indicated the Buddha's life events?

**Definition (learn):** The early Buddhist sculptors indicated the Buddha's presence through symbols rather than the human form.

**Breakdown:**
- Birth: bull, elephant, lotus, footprint.
- Great renunciation: horse, empty throne, begging bowl.
- Enlightenment: vajrasana, Bodhi tree.
- Sermons: wheel, lion, deer.
- Death/parinirvana: stupa.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p. 776 (✅ owner 06); PDF p. 201 (footprint, throne).

**Links:** ART-2063, ART-2067, ART-2059

**Trap:** Saying the early phase "prohibited" animal imagery. What it avoided was the human figure of the Buddha.

**Mains hook:** Aniconism-to-iconism is a change in devotional practice, not only artistic skill.

**Back:** *"Bull birth, horse leaves, Bodhi wakes, wheel preaches, stupa ends."*

### Flashcard ART-2063 — The empty seat: presence marker (2026 PYQ)

**Front:** What does the empty seat symbolise in early Buddhist art?

**Definition (learn):** The empty throne is a presence-marker: it indicates the Buddha without showing him, recording a doctrinal reluctance to embody the Buddha. Later human images mark a change in devotional practice.

**Breakdown:**
- Empty throne: renunciation event.
- Presence, not absence.
- Later human image: devotional shift.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2026 Prelims GS-I Q12 (routed; provisional 2026 Set-A key held locally, answer not recorded here) (✅ owner 06).

**Links:** ART-2062, ART-2059

**Trap:** Treating the empty seat as an absence. It marks the Buddha's presence.

**Mains hook:** Symbol as theology: the empty seat is a presence-marker.

**Back:** *"Empty seat: the Buddha is present by absence."*

### Flashcard ART-2064 — Nataraja iconography (Chola bronze)

**Front:** Read a Nataraja attribute by attribute.

**Definition (learn):** Shiva as Lord of Dance in Tandava posture. The attributes carry assigned meanings.

**Breakdown:**
- Rear right hand: damaru (creation sound).
- Rear left hand: fire (destruction).
- Front right: Abhaya Mudra (reassurance).
- Front left: points to the upraised foot, Gajahasta ("elephant hand"), the path to salvation.
- Left leg in bhujangatrasita stance: tirobhava, kicking away the veil of maya.
- Supporting foot: subdues the dwarf Apasmara/Muyalaka (ignorance).
- Matted locks: Ganga. One male and one female earring: Ardhanarishvara. Snake: kundalini. Nimbus: cycles of time.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 211–212 (✅ owner 06). Chidambaram and Thanjavur Brihadeeswara bronzes named (PDF p. 212).

**Links:** ART-2046, ART-2065, ART-2066

**Trap:** Describing the Nataraja as generic admiration. Attribute reading is the method.

**Mains hook:** The attribute-by-attribute method is the reusable iconography answer.

**Back:** *"Damaru, fire, abhaya, foot up, dwarf down."*

### Flashcard ART-2065 — Nataraja: origin at Aihole, peak under the Cholas

**Front:** Did the Cholas originate the Nataraja?

**Definition (learn):** No. The owner's sources place the earliest known Nataraja sculpture at the Ravana Phadi Cave, Aihole, under early Chalukya rule. The Cholas brought it to its canonical, portable peak.

**Breakdown:**
- Origin: early Chalukya Aihole (owner-stated).
- Peak: Chola bronzes (canonical, processional form).
- Wikipedia's Aihole page places Ravana Phadi in the 6th century and mentions a 6th-century dancing Shiva in a niche, but does not call it the earliest (✅ Wikipedia).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p. 211 (✅ owner 06). Earliest claim ⚠️ not confirmed by the Wikipedia fetch.

**Links:** ART-2064, ART-2046

**Trap:** Origin vs peak: the dynasty most famous for a form did not originate it.

**Mains hook:** Peak-not-origin thesis; use the Aihole–Chola chain as a correction.

**Back:** *"Aihole began it, the Cholas perfected it."*

### Flashcard ART-2066 — Rampurva: the lion and the bull

**Front:** What is the Rampurva pillar pair, and what does it show?

**Definition (learn):** Rampurva (Bihar) has two Ashokan pillars: one with a lion capital inscribed with Major Pillar Edicts I–VI, and one with a bull capital that has no inscription. Rampurva is one of the seven locations of the Major Pillar Edicts.

**Breakdown:**
- Lion pillar: inscribed (MPE I–VI); imperial message.
- Bull pillar: uninscribed.
- Interpretation: the lion was recruited to speak; the bull was not.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 233–234 (✅ owner 06).

**Links:** ART-2067, ART-2068, ART-2074

**Trap:** Saying the bull pillar is uninscribed "because it is earlier," or assigning it to a non-Ashokan dynasty. The source says only that it lacks inscriptions.

**Mains hook:** One site, one patron, two animals, two functions: the best single unit for a lion-and-bull answer.

**Back:** *"Rampurva: lion speaks with edicts, bull stays silent."*

### Flashcard ART-2067 — The Sarnath abacus and the national emblem

**Front:** What does the Sarnath capital's animal programme mean, and what is its modern status?

**Definition (learn):** The Sarnath capital has four lions back-to-back on a circular abacus, with bull, horse, lion and elephant carvings and the Dharmachakra. The four abacus animals mark the four directions: galloping horse (west), elephant (east), bull (south), lion (north).

**Breakdown:**
- Elephant: Queen Maya's dream (white elephant entering the womb).
- Bull: Taurus, the zodiac sign of Buddha's birth month.
- Horse: Kanthaka, which left princely life.
- Lion: attainment of enlightenment.
- Four lions: Buddha spreading Dharma in all directions; first sermon (Dharmachakrapravartana).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner PDF pp. 42–44 (✅ owner 06). Sarnath capital c. 280 BCE and only three lions visible on the emblem (✅ Wikipedia, State Emblem of India). Adopted 26 January 1950 (✅ Wikipedia). Satyameva Jayate from the Mundaka Upanishad (✅ Wikipedia). Earlier emblem in use from 30 December 1947 (✅ Wikipedia).

**Links:** ART-2066, ART-2062, ART-2068

**Trap:** Giving the abacus animals any meaning beyond the four the source assigns. Do not confuse the 1947 pre-motto emblem with the 1950 adopted one.

**Mains hook:** A mythology question becomes a living-symbol conclusion.

**Back:** *"Four lions face four ways; the emblem since 26 January 1950."*

### Flashcard ART-2068 — Harappan lion absence, bull prominence, and Kalibangan

**Front:** What does the Harappan animal record show about lion and bull?

**Definition (learn):** The humped bull is frequent on Harappan seals, while the lion is absent from the seal corpus. "No evidence of cow and lion has been found on any seal." The bronze bull of Kalibangan was cast by the cire-perdue method.

**Breakdown:**
- Bull: privileged image in Harappan culture; meaning unrecoverable.
- Lion: absent; its Indian career begins later and is tied to imperial and royal use.
- Cow: absent.
- Kalibangan bronze bull: second Harappan bull object.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 191, 193 (✅ owner 06). CSE 2001 reproduced item: "Cow" as the animal not represented (owner-stated answer; ⚠️ not key-checked here).

**Links:** ART-2047, ART-2048, ART-2067

**Trap:** Placing a lion on a Harappan seal. Also avoid a Nandi or bull myth from memory.

**Mains hook:** The honest caution: the bull was prominent; what it meant cannot be recovered from an undeciphered script.

**Back:** *"Bull on seals, no lion, no cow, bronze bull at Kalibangan."*

### Flashcard ART-2069 — Nandi mandapa: the bull as architecture

**Front:** Which named temples place Nandi in a dedicated building or pavilion, and what size claims are sourced?

**Definition (learn):** Nandi, Shiva's vahana (mount), is placed before the sanctum in a dedicated mandapa or pavilion. The vahana is defined as the mount of the main deity placed just before the sanctum.

**Breakdown:**
- Brihadishvara, Thanjavur: Nandi mandap with a monolithic Nandi, second largest in the country.
- Lepakshi Veerabhadra (Andhra Pradesh): largest monolithic Nandi in the country.
- Kailasa, Ellora (Cave 16): Nandi mandapa and two flagstaff pillars.
- Ramappa, Kakatiya: monolithic Nandi and Nandi pavilion.
- Hoysala (Somanathapura): Nandi pavilion and kalyani.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 61, 72, 91, 105, 107, 109, 329 (✅ owner 06).

**Links:** ART-2064, ART-2070, ART-2068

**Trap:** Narrating a Nandi origin myth, a Vedic bull identification or a "bull of dharma" reading from memory. The owner's sources do not support these.

**Mains hook:** The bull's position encodes its relationship to Shiva.

**Back:** *"Thanjavur second, Lepakshi largest, Nandi before Shiva."*

### Flashcard ART-2070 — Yali and Vyala: composite lions in temple architecture

**Front:** What are Yali and Vyala, and where do they appear?

**Definition (learn):** Yali is a composite lion figure that becomes symbolic of the Nayaka architectural identity, carved on gopurams and Vijayanagara pillars. Vyala is a hybrid lion-bodied animal at Khajuraho on Vishnu temples.

**Breakdown:**
- Yali (Tamil literature): cat-like body, lion's head, elephant's tusks, serpent's tail; more powerful than lion, tiger or elephant; a guardian creature.
- Yali (South Indian chapter): head and body of a lion, trunk and tusks of an elephant.
- Vyala: hybrid imaginary animals with lion bodies (Khajuraho).
- Hoysala friezes: elephants, lions, scrolls, horses, makaras and swans.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 81, 105, 114, 118, 209 (✅ owner 06).

**Links:** ART-2069, ART-2074

**Trap:** Presenting either Yali anatomy as an error. The owner says both come from the same source. Also do not state Yali counts.

**Mains hook:** In temple architecture the lion is hybridised and multiplied; it guards and bears load, unlike the Mauryan lion.

**Back:** *"Yali: lion-headed guardian with elephant tusks."*

### Flashcard ART-2071 — Kalyanasundara Murti (panigrahana)

**Front:** What is the Kalyanasundara Murti?

**Definition (learn):** A 9th-century AD sculpture group that renders panigrahana, the marriage ceremony, through two statuettes: Shiva accepting Parvati's right hand as she steps forward with a bashful expression.

**Breakdown:**
- Ritual: panigrahana (marriage).
- Figures: Shiva and Parvati.
- Gesture: right hand accepted.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 9th century AD; PDF p. 212 (✅ owner 06).

**Links:** ART-2072, ART-2064

**Trap:** Treating carvings as photographs of ordinary marriage ceremonies. It is a commissioned, ritual representation.

**Mains hook:** The best case for "sculpture depicts a social ritual."

**Back:** *"Shiva takes Parvati's hand: marriage in stone, ninth century."*

### Flashcard ART-2072 — Temple sculpture as evidence of social life (2022 Q1)

**Front:** How should sculpture be used to explain social life in medieval temples?

**Definition (learn):** Sculpture is commissioned representation. It is strong evidence of what patrons chose to make visible and of available technique and materials. It is weak evidence of belief, behaviour or the condition of ordinary people.

**Breakdown:**
- Registers: performance (dancers, musicians), instruction, court life, devotion, gender and patronage.
- Named Khajuraho evidence: dancers, musicians, teachers and disciples; Vyalas (PDF p. 81).
- Named South Indian evidence: Saptamatrikas and Kirtimukha (PDF pp. 208–209).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** CSE 2022 GS-I Q1 (10 marks; 150 words). Owner 06 §13.6C; basic/03 §13.10.

**Links:** ART-2046, ART-2071, ART-2056

**Trap:** Generalising from royal temples to village life, or using Khajuraho's erotic sculpture as a demographic claim.

**Mains hook:** The answer needs the sources' limits stated inside it.

**Back:** *"Sculpture shows what patrons chose, not how people lived."*

### Flashcard ART-2073 — PYQ route: Gandhara's Central Asian and Greco-Bactrian elements (CSE 2019)

**Front:** How should the CSE 2019 Gandhara question be answered?

**Definition (learn):** "Highlight the Central Asian and Greco-Bactrian elements in Gandhara art" (10 marks; 150 words). Name the two strands separately: Greco-Bactrian (Hellenistic surface and body) and Central Asian (Kushana, Parthian, Saka layering).

**Breakdown:**
- Greco-Bactrian: wavy hair, slender musculature, pleated drapery, composed stillness, elongated earlobes, bluish-grey sandstone and stucco.
- Central Asian: Kushana patronage; "layered" influence.
- Indian core not borrowed: the Buddhist subject, abhaya mudra, halo, monastic robe.
- Diffusion outward into Central and East Asia.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 200–205, 299–301 (✅ owner 06).

**Links:** ART-2058, ART-2057, ART-2060

**Trap:** Answering "Greek influence" six times. Do not name individual rulers or artists from memory.

**Mains hook:** Use Mathura as a contrast to show these features are not "what Indian sculpture looked like then."

**Back:** *"Bactrian surface, Kushana patron, Indian subject."*

### Flashcard ART-2074 — PYQ route: lion and bull significance (CSE 2022, 15 marks)

**Front:** How do you structure "Discuss the significance of the lion and bull figures in Indian mythology, art and architecture"?

**Definition (learn):** A 15-mark, 250-word question with three separate legs: mythology, art and architecture. The strongest unit is Rampurva (the inscribed lion and uninscribed bull). Then Sarnath's abacus, aniconic birth/sermon meaning, Harappan seals, Nandi mandapas and Yali/Vyala.

**Breakdown:**
- Thesis: two functions, not decorative animals (the bull for generative power, the lion for authority).
- Mythology leg: bull as vahana; Taurus birth month; lion as enlightenment; Yali in Tamil literature.
- Art leg: Harappan humped bull, lion absent; Kalibangan bronze bull; Buddhist aniconic bull/lion.
- Architecture leg: Nandi mandapas; Yali and Vyala.
- Verdict: survival of the Sarnath lions on the national emblem since 26 January 1950.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 06 §13.6A (✅ owner 06). Emblem date (✅ Wikipedia).

**Links:** ART-2066, ART-2067, ART-2068, ART-2069, ART-2070

**Trap:** Inventing a Nandi or Rudra-bull myth, or a Vedic bull identification. Do not date the Rampurva bull pillar apart from Ashoka.

**Mains hook:** Symbol meanings are reconstructed from context and later texts; depiction records patron choice.

**Back:** *"Bull for generation, lion for authority, one emblem still."*

### Flashcard ART-2075 — Terracotta from Harappa to early historic sites

**Front:** What is the Harappan and early historic terracotta record?

**Definition (learn):** Terracotta is fired clay: cheap, widely distributed, least durable in the open and continuously practised. In the Harappan corpus, terracotta female figures, plough models (Bahawalpur, Banawali) and seals. Terracotta sculptures were less numerous and cruder than bronzes (PDF p. 193). Chandraketugarh (North 24 Parganas, West Bengal) has terracotta art, NBPW and Maurya/Kushana/Gupta sculpture.

**Breakdown:**
- Harappan: terracotta female figures; plough models; terracotta seals; pinching method.
- Early historic: Chandraketugarh terracotta, moulded and modelled.
- CSE 2021 pairing (Chandraketugarh : terracotta art) is routed in the source (PDF p. 167).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 37–38, 167, 190, 193 (✅ owner 06).

**Links:** ART-2050, ART-2053, ART-2076

**Trap:** Dating Chandraketugarh or assigning it to a dynasty beyond the listed periods. Do not state figurine dimensions, firing temperatures or counts.

**Mains hook:** Terracotta is the only medium that is cheapest, most widely distributed, least durable in the open and most continuously practised.

**Back:** *"Clay: cheap, common, fragile, unbroken practice."*

### Flashcard ART-2076 — Gupta terracotta, Bengal terracotta bricks, and living craft

**Front:** How does terracotta move from object to building, and what can be said about living centres?

**Definition (learn):** In the Gupta period terracotta carries a full religious programme: gods and goddesses, male and female figures, Buddha figures at Devnimori (Gujarat), plaques at Bikaner and Ahichchhatra, heads from Akhnur (Kashmir), objects from Bhita. The Bengal school used burnt bricks and clay, "known as terracotta bricks," as its main building material.

**Breakdown:**
- Gupta: gods, goddesses, plaques, heads.
- Bengal: terracotta bricks as the principal building material.
- Living craft: Gundiyali and Lodai (Kutch, Gujarat) are "famous pottery making villages" (pottery, not figurative terracotta).
- Cire-perdue metal technique still practised (PDF p. 193).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 121, 196, 208 (✅ owner 06).

**Links:** ART-2007, ART-2075, ART-2050

**Trap:** Naming living terracotta centres (Bankura, Molela or any other) as verified. The owner's sources do not carry them. Do not confuse pottery villages with figurative terracotta.

**Mains hook:** State the mechanism (hereditary artisans, local clay, shrine demand, GI-type protection) and admit that named living centres are outside the verified evidence.

**Back:** *"Gupta plaques, Bengal bricks, Kutch pots: no unverified names."*

### GROUP — 07 Painting Traditions (basic/07 · advanced/07)

### Flashcard ART-2077 — Format, patronage and audience: the mural-to-miniature shift

**Front:** Why did Indian painting move from walls to manuscripts, albums and prints?

**Definition (learn):** Painting's format tracks its payer. Monumental murals for collective devotional viewing (Ajanta, Pallava and Chola temples) gave way to portable manuscript and album miniatures for private connoisseurship (Mughal, Rajasthani, Pahari), then to print and canvas for a mass and market public.

**Breakdown:**
- Murals: institutional or royal patronage; public, collective viewing.
- Manuscript and album: individual or elite patronage; private, portable.
- Print and canvas: market and nationalist public.
- Mechanism: as the payer changes, size, portability and subject change.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 07 §1 and §13.5 (✅ owner 07).

**Links:** ART-2079, ART-2080, ART-2090, ART-2091, ART-2099

**Trap:** A chronology of schools without the patronage link is only a list.

**Mains hook:** Format-patronage thesis (the most reusable argument in this topic).

**Back:** *"Whoever pays changes the size, surface and audience."*

### Flashcard ART-2078 — Shadanga: the six limbs of painting

**Front:** What are the six limbs (Shadanga) of Indian painting?

**Definition (learn):** The classical treatise list of six principles: Roopbheda (appearance), Pramana (proportion and structure), Bhava (expression), Lavanya Yojana (aesthetic composition), Sadrishya (resemblance), and Varnikabhanga (use of brush and colours).

**Breakdown:**
1. Roopbheda: looks or appearance.
2. Pramana: proportion.
3. Bhava: expression.
4. Lavanya Yojana: aesthetic composition.
5. Sadrishya: resemblance.
6. Varnikabhanga: brush and colours.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 07 vocabulary (✅ owner 07; PDF p. 309 outline).

**Links:** ART-2077, ART-2079

**Trap:** Treating the Shadanga as a historical school. It is a classical theory of painting.

**Mains hook:** Use the six limbs as a vocabulary for judging a painting's formal qualities.

**Back:** *"Looks, proportion, feeling, composition, likeness, brushwork."*

### Flashcard ART-2079 — Fresco secco: the Ajanta technique

**Front:** What is the technique used at Ajanta?

**Definition (learn):** Fresco secco (also called tempera): pigment applied to dried plaster, not wet plaster. Ajanta's technique is described this way, not as true (wet) fresco.

**Breakdown:**
- Surface: prepared plaster.
- Timing: pigment applied after the surface had dried.
- Label: fresco secco or tempera.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 58, 325 (✅ owner 07).

**Links:** ART-2080, ART-2088, ART-2077

**Trap:** "All Indian mural technique is true fresco." Ajanta is fresco secco. Sittanavasal is described as fresco with mineral colours, which is a different claim.

**Mains hook:** Technique terminology is precision. Keep "true fresco" for wet plaster only.

**Back:** *"Dry plaster, dry paint: fresco secco, not true fresco."*

### Flashcard ART-2080 — Ajanta: Buddhist cave murals

**Front:** What is Ajanta's painting programme and PYQ link?

**Definition (learn):** Buddhist cave murals with Jataka tales, Bodhisattvas and divine figures, painted over prepared plaster. Ajanta's focus is described as spiritual. Padmapani is located at Ajanta.

**Breakdown:**
- Subject: Jataka tales, Bodhisattvas, divine figures.
- Technique: fresco secco.
- Date: Ajanta paintings dated 4th–6th century AD (owner, in the Sittanavasal context).
- Icon: Padmapani.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 58, 323, 325 (✅ owner 07). CSE 2017 locates Padmapani at Ajanta (owner-stated PYQ route).

**Links:** ART-2079, ART-2081, ART-2083, ART-2084

**Trap:** Locating Padmapani at Bagh, or describing Ajanta's technique as true fresco.

**Mains hook:** Ajanta as the reference point for the Bagh comparison.

**Back:** *"Ajanta: Jataka walls, dry plaster, Padmapani."*

### Flashcard ART-2081 — Bagh Caves, Cave 4 (Rang Mahal)

**Front:** Where are the Bagh Caves, and what are the key facts?

**Definition (learn):** A group of nine Buddhist caves on the bank of the Baghni river in Dhar district, Madhya Pradesh. Most are viharas with a few chaitya halls. Cave 4, known as Rang Mahal, has murals of Jataka tales. Called the "Ajanta of Central India."

**Breakdown:**
- Location: Baghni river, Dhar district, Madhya Pradesh.
- Form: nine caves; mainly viharas; few chaitya halls.
- Key cave: Cave 4, Rang Mahal.
- Subject: Jataka tales, Bodhisattvas, divine figures; Buddhist religious themes.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Dating: 5th–6th century AD in one passage and 5th–7th century CE in another (PDF pp. 63, 323). The book gives two different ranges; report the range as the book's own (✅ owner 07).

**Links:** ART-2080, ART-2082, ART-2083, ART-2084

**Trap:** Not a gorge site, not in Maharashtra. Do not extend "Rang Mahal" to Jaisalmer Fort or Shah Jahan's Rang Mahal.

**Mains hook:** Identify by site, river/district, period, cave number, cave name and subject, in that order, then stop.

**Back:** *"Bagh: Baghni, Dhar, nine caves, Cave 4 Rang Mahal."*

### Flashcard ART-2082 — Hallisalasya dance in the Bagh paintings (2026 Prelims)

**Front:** What does the source say about the Hallisalasya dance in the Bagh caves?

**Definition (learn):** "Bagh Cave paintings also depict Hallisalasya Dance, which is a classical Indian dance form which used to be performed in royal courts, temples, and ceremonial gatherings. In the Gupta period, it was often depicted as a devotional or courtly dance in art and sculpture."

**Breakdown:**
- Dance: Hallisalasya.
- Settings: royal courts, temples, ceremonial gatherings.
- Gupta period: devotional or courtly in art and sculpture.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p. 63 (✅ owner 07). 2026 Prelims GS-I Q5 routed; provisional 2026 Set-A key held locally, answer not recorded here.

**Links:** ART-2081, ART-2083

**Trap:** Assigning the dance to a specific Bagh cave or to a modern classical dance form. The owner attributes it to the Bagh paintings generally, and no iconographic reading beyond "devotional or courtly."

**Mains hook:** The whole local evidence for the routed demand is this sentence.

**Back:** *"Hallisalasya: a Bagh dance, devotional or courtly."*

### Flashcard ART-2083 — The Gupta-period cave painting pair: Ajanta and Bagh

**Front:** Which two cave painting sites are the Gupta-period examples?

**Definition (learn):** "The two examples of Gupta period mural paintings are Ajanta Caves and Bagh Caves" (owner source). The source reproduces CSE 2009's question with option (a) Bagh Caves, and its answer key gives item 77 as (a).

**Breakdown:**
- Pair: Ajanta and Bagh.
- Corroboration: CSE 2009 reproduced item, option (a) Bagh.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 181, 186, 324 (✅ owner 07).

**Links:** ART-2080, ART-2081

**Trap:** Naming Sittanavasal, Pallava or Chola murals as Gupta-period cave painting.

**Mains hook:** The most likely objective pairing for Gupta painting.

**Back:** *"Gupta cave painting: Ajanta and Bagh."*

### Flashcard ART-2084 — Bagh versus Ajanta: a bounded contrast

**Front:** How far can the "Bagh is more materialistic" contrast be pushed?

**Definition (learn):** The source describes Bagh's murals as "more materialistic than spiritualistic", "more tightly modelled, stronger outlines, more earthly and human." It is a relative, descriptive contrast of emphasis. Bagh's subjects remain Buddhist religious themes.

**Breakdown:**
- Formal: tighter modelling, stronger outlines, more earthly figures (safe to write).
- Emphasis: "more materialistic than spiritualistic" (qualify it).
- Technique: no distinct Bagh term given; do not transfer Ajanta's fresco secco label to Bagh.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 63, 323 (✅ owner 07).

**Links:** ART-2080, ART-2081

**Trap:** Presenting "materialistic" as a doctrinal classification, or as a contradiction of Bagh's Buddhist subject.

**Mains hook:** Attribute the contrast to the source and immediately qualify it with religious subject matter.

**Back:** *"Bagh: earthlier figures, same Buddhist walls."*

### Flashcard ART-2085 — Pallava murals: Mahendravarman, Narasimhavarman II and the survivors

**Front:** Which Pallava mural evidence and patrons are named?

**Definition (learn):** Mahendravarman was "the first great patron of art and architecture" with the appellations Vichitra Chitta, Chettakari and Chitrakarppulli ("a tiger among painters"). Wall paintings survive at Mamandur rock-cut caves. Narasimhavarman II Rajasimha (700–720 CE) patronised the best Pallava wall paintings: the ambulatory of Kailasanatha Temple, Kanchipuram, with Shiva-Parvati legends. Panamalai (Talagirishvara) preserves Parvati watching Shiva's dance.

**Breakdown:**
- Patron 1: Mahendravarman (Mamandur).
- Patron 2: Narasimhavarman II (Kailasanatha, Kanchi; 700–720 CE).
- Panamalai: Parvati watching Shiva's dance.
- Jain caves at Annamalai Hill: royal figures and a Jain muni.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 88, 324 (✅ owner 07).

**Links:** ART-2086, ART-2087, ART-2089

**Trap:** Treating the epithets as biography. They are honorifics.

**Mains hook:** Named rulers and named locations give a 10-mark art answer without advanced reading.

**Back:** *"Mahendra painted Mamandur; Narasimha painted Kanchi."*

### Flashcard ART-2086 — Survival bias: "only two" Pallava and Chola mural sites

**Front:** Does the survival of few murals show the tradition was minor?

**Definition (learn):** No. The owner's sources say only two Pallava mural locations (Kailasanatha, Kanchipuram; Talagirishvara, Panamalai) and two Chola mural temples survive. This constrains claims about scale, not about quality. Survival bias likely explains the gap.

**Breakdown:**
- Pallava: two surviving principal locations.
- Chola: two surviving mural temples.
- Literature: wall painting in Tamil Nadu from the 7th century AD; Silappadikaram and Manimekalai (5th century) mention it.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 88, 324 (✅ owner 07).

**Links:** ART-2085, ART-2087, ART-2077

**Trap:** Reading "two survivors" as a thin tradition. That confuses preservation with production.

**Mains hook:** Provenance-gap thesis: textual evidence says the practice existed; material evidence is thin.

**Back:** *"Few survivors, not few painters."*

### Flashcard ART-2087 — Chola murals: Brihadeeswara and Rajaraja's guru portrait

**Front:** Which Chola mural sites and figures are named?

**Definition (learn):** Two surviving Chola temples: Vijaya Cholishwaram (the first Chola temple with murals, only faint lines visible) and Brihadeeswara (Thanjavur), "the most outstanding centre of Chola wall painting" and "the biggest centre of wall painting in India after Ajanta." Subjects include Shiva in Kailash, Sundarar, Tripurantaka, Nataraja, Dakshinamurti, Lingodbhava and a portrait of Rajaraja Chola I with his guru Karuvur Devar.

**Breakdown:**
- Vijaya Cholishwaram: faint lines.
- Brihadeeswara: Rajaraja-period paintings with Nayaka-period superimposition.
- Royal portrait: Rajaraja I with Karuvur Devar.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p. 327 (✅ owner 07).

**Links:** ART-2086, ART-2085, ART-2088

**Trap:** Ignoring Nayaka superimposition. A single wall can hold two periods; conservation must choose which layer to show.

**Mains hook:** A named royal portrait inside a temple is unusually strong patronage evidence.

**Back:** *"Brihadeeswara: Rajaraja with his guru, under Nayaka paint."*

### Flashcard ART-2088 — Sittanavasal: a link from Ajanta to Thanjavur

**Front:** What is Sittanavasal's role in the painting chain?

**Definition (learn):** Pandya-period Jain rock-cut cave paintings at Sittanavasal (floral creepers, dancing celestial nymphs, birds; poorly preserved). Its murals used fresco with mineral colours, and are said to link Ajanta (4th–6th century AD) to the 11th-century Chola paintings at Thanjavur.

**Breakdown:**
- Patron: Pandya.
- Technique: fresco, mineral colours.
- Chain: Ajanta, then Sittanavasal, then Thanjavur.
- Tirumalaipuram: nothing significant remains.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 324–325 (✅ owner 07).

**Links:** ART-2079, ART-2087, ART-2089

**Trap:** Stating the link as proven. The book's own hedge is "said to."

**Mains hook:** Converts a list of sites into a chain of transmission.

**Back:** *"Sittanavasal: the bridge that is said to link."*

### Flashcard ART-2089 — Armamalai Cave: a Jain cave turned Shaiva

**Front:** What is the Armamalai Cave?

**Definition (learn):** A rock-cut Shiva cave in Tamil Nadu with 8th–9th century murals of the Pallava period, later patronised by the Cholas. Originally a Jain monastery, it preserves Jain Tirthankaras, monks and devotees, and the tales of the Astathik Palakas on walls and roof.

**Breakdown:**
- Original: Jain monastery.
- Later: Shiva cave.
- Murals: Pallava-period, 8th–9th century.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p. 327 (✅ owner 07). Panel attribution ⚠️ not settled.

**Links:** ART-2085, ART-2088

**Trap:** Assigning individual panels to a fixed patron.

**Mains hook:** Sectarian reuse as a Pallava-to-Chola bridge.

**Back:** *"Armamalai: Jain walls, Shaiva roof, Pallava to Chola."*

### Flashcard ART-2090 — Pala and Western Indian/Jain manuscripts: the miniature's origin

**Front:** Where does the miniature tradition begin?

**Definition (learn):** Miniature painting traces a lineage from Pala Buddhist manuscript illustration in eastern India and Western Indian/Jain manuscript painting of Gujarat-Rajasthan. The source calls the Western Indian group Jain school. "Apabhramsa school" is not the only or preferred label.

**Breakdown:**
- Pala: Buddhist manuscripts, eastern India.
- Western Indian/Jain: Gujarat-Rajasthan.
- Then: Delhi Sultanate-period transition, then Mughal.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 309–377 (✅ owner 07).

**Links:** ART-2091, ART-2077

**Trap:** Treating every manuscript as Mughal. Miniatures have distinct pre-Mughal origins.

**Mains hook:** Use the Pala and Jain lineage before the Mughal shift.

**Back:** *"Pala Buddhist, Jain Gujarat: the miniature begins before Mughals."*

### Flashcard ART-2091 — The Mughal atelier: Akbar's karkhana and the Tutinama

**Front:** How did Akbar's atelier produce painting?

**Definition (learn):** The Mughal miniature was produced by a collaborative karkhana (imperial workshop) with a mixed Hindu-Muslim workforce. Akbar's karkhana assembled artists from Gwalior, Gujarat, Lahore, Kashmir and Malwa. Illustrated manuscripts such as the Tutinama show narrative action.

**Breakdown:**
- Organisation: collaborative karkhana.
- Workforce: Hindu and Muslim artists from several regions.
- Product: large illustrated manuscripts, narrative action (Tutinama).
- Dispersal: later artists moved to Rajasthan and the Pahari centres.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Satish Chandra, *Medieval History 1526–1748* (✅ owner 07 advanced §4; PDF pp. 389–392).

**Links:** ART-2092, ART-2090, ART-2094, ART-2096

**Trap:** Describing the Mughal style as the work of isolated geniuses, or as "sealed dynastic boxes."

**Mains hook:** Dispersal after imperial patronage contracted seeded the regional schools.

**Back:** *"Akbar's karkhana: many hands, many regions, one Tutinama."*

### Flashcard ART-2092 — Jahangir and the album: portraiture and natural history

**Front:** What did Jahangir's reign change in Mughal painting?

**Definition (learn):** The shift from manuscript series toward individual portraiture and album pages, with natural-history subjects (flora and fauna). Mansur is the named painter.

**Breakdown:**
- Format: album page, individual portrait.
- Subjects: flora, fauna, portraiture.
- Painter: Mansur.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** CSE 2019 Prelims Q17 routed: "Mughal painting tradition shift to individual portraiture," key not held locally (✅ owner 07).

**Links:** ART-2091, ART-2077

**Trap:** Attributing the album shift to Akbar. The Jahangir marker is the shift from manuscript series to individual portraiture.

**Mains hook:** The format change (manuscript to album) is also a patronage change.
