**Back:** *"Jahangir: albums, portraits, Mansur's animals."*

### Flashcard ART-2093 — Deccani painting

**Front:** What distinguishes Deccani painting?

**Definition (learn):** Saturated colour, elongated figures and fantastic landscape, with Persianate-Deccan court culture.

**Breakdown:**
- Palette: saturated colour.
- Figures: elongated.
- Landscape: fantastic.
- Court: Persianate-Deccan.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 07 (§13.6).

**Links:** ART-2091, ART-2094

**Trap:** Calling Deccani painting Mughal because the roots are shared Persianate.

**Mains hook:** Shared Persianate resources do not make every miniature Mughal.

**Back:** *"Deccan: saturated colour, long figures, fantasy landscape."*

### Flashcard ART-2094 — Mewar, Bundi and Kota: court, hunt and Ragamala

**Front:** What marks the Mewar, Bundi and Kota schools?

**Definition (learn):** Bold, flat colour; court, hunt, Krishna and Ragamala themes with regional variation. Bundi and Kota hunt scenes differ from Mewar court sets.

**Breakdown:**
- Palette: bold, flat colour.
- Themes: court, hunt, Krishna, Ragamala.
- Distinction: Bundi/Kota hunt vs Mewar court.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 07 (§13.6).

**Links:** ART-2091, ART-2095, ART-2096

**Trap:** Treating Mewar, Bundi and Kota as one school.

**Mains hook:** Sub-school-level distinctions by theme and palette.

**Back:** *"Mewar court, Bundi hunt, Kota hunt: bold colours."*

### Flashcard ART-2095 — Kishangarh and Bani Thani (CSE 2018)

**Front:** Which school produced Bani Thani?

**Definition (learn):** Kishangarh, with lyrical Radha-Krishna imagery and elongated features. Bani Thani is associated with the painter Nihal Chand.

**Breakdown:**
- School: Kishangarh.
- Painter: Nihal Chand (association).
- Theme: Radha-Krishna.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** CSE 2018 Prelims Q42 routed: Bani Thani and its Rajasthani attribution; key not held locally (✅ owner 07).

**Links:** ART-2094, ART-2096

**Trap:** Attributing Bani Thani to Kangra or to any school other than Kishangarh. Do not invent an artist for an unattributed work.

**Mains hook:** Kishangarh, not Kangra, for Bani Thani.

**Back:** *"Bani Thani: Kishangarh, Nihal Chand."*

### Flashcard ART-2096 — Pahari schools: Basohli to Guler and Kangra

**Front:** How do the Pahari schools develop?

**Definition (learn):** From the intense colour and bold line of Basohli (early devotional) to the softer naturalism and lyrical line of Guler and Kangra (late Pahari), with Krishna poetry and Gita Govinda themes. Other Pahari centres: Basholi, Jammu/Dogra, Kullu-Mandi.

**Breakdown:**
- Basohli: intense colour, bold line, early devotional.
- Guler/Kangra: softer naturalism, lyrical line.
- Themes: Krishna poetry; Gita Govinda.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 309–377 (✅ owner 07).

**Links:** ART-2094, ART-2095

**Trap:** Calling Kangra early Pahari. It is late.

**Mains hook:** Pahari sub-schools as a sequence within one tradition.

**Back:** *"Basohli bold, Kangra soft: Pahari moves toward naturalism."*

### Flashcard ART-2097 — Tanjore, Mysore painting and Ganjifa

**Front:** What are the South Indian miniature traditions?

**Definition (learn):** Tanjore painting, known for gold-coating and gesso relief, devotional icons. Mysore painting is distinct. Ganjifa (Ganjapa) cards are associated with Mysore.

**Breakdown:**
- Tanjore: gold foil, gesso relief, devotional icons.
- Mysore: distinct from Tanjore.
- Ganjifa: Mysore Ganjifa cards.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 07 (§13.6). Mysore Ganjifa GI tag: ⚠️ owner-stated, verify against the GI Registry.

**Links:** ART-2096, ART-2112

**Trap:** Merging Tanjore and Mysore painting. Also do not assume a GI tag is permanent.

**Mains hook:** South Indian devotional miniatures as a regional, material-based school.

**Back:** *"Tanjore: gold on devotional wood; Mysore: a different school."*

### Flashcard ART-2098 — Company painting and Bazaar/Kalighat

**Front:** What are Company painting and bazaar painting?

**Definition (learn):** Company painting served European patrons and documentation (Patna, Varanasi, Thanjavur). Bazaar and Kalighat painting served pilgrimage and commercial publics. Both are internally diverse.

**Breakdown:**
- Company: colonial, documentation, private collection.
- Bazaar: commercial, pilgrimage.
- Kalighat (Bengal): bazaar tradition.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 07 (§3, §13.5).

**Links:** ART-2099, ART-2100

**Trap:** Treating Company and Bazaar painting as one school.

**Mains hook:** Company painting is the colonial hybrid that the Bengal School later rejected.

**Back:** *"Company for colonists, Kalighat for pilgrims and markets."*

### Flashcard ART-2099 — Raja Ravi Varma: academic realism for a print public

**Front:** What did Raja Ravi Varma paint, and what was his style?

**Definition (learn):** European academic realism applied to Indian mythological subjects, reproduced for a mass print public. Works named: Ladies in the Moonlight, Shakuntala, Damayanti and Swan, and Ravana kidnapping Sita and killing Jatayu.

**Breakdown:**
- Style: academic realism, oil on canvas.
- Subjects: Indian mythology.
- Audience: print circulation.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p. 359 (✅ owner 07).

**Links:** ART-2100, ART-2077

**Trap:** Calling Ravi Varma a folk or Bengal School artist.

**Mains hook:** Academic realism is the first of the two rejections in the Bengal School's double reaction.

**Back:** *"Ravi Varma: European realism, Indian myths, printed."*

### Flashcard ART-2100 — The Bengal School: a two-front nationalist painting

**Front:** What were the Bengal School's features, and what did it reject?

**Definition (learn):** A nationalist, anti-academic movement led by Abanindranath Tagore: Indian themes (Mahakali, Shiva-Parvati, Krishna and Gopis); Ajanta-inspired rhythm and grace; linear delicacy; wash technique with subdued colour; visible Mughal and Rajasthani influence. Its rejection was twofold: against Company hybridity and Ravi Varma's academic realism.

**Breakdown:**
- Abanindranath Tagore: Bharat Mata (1905); Arabian Nights series; Journey's End.
- Features: six stylistic criteria in the owner's list.
- Double rejection: Company and Ravi Varma.
- Japanese wash technique and transnational networks also shaped it (owner advanced).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 359–361 (✅ owner 07).

**Links:** ART-2099, ART-2101, ART-2098

**Trap:** Treating the Bengal School as merely traditional-style painting. It was a political argument about technique.

**Mains hook:** The technique itself was a political statement.

**Back:** *"Bengal School: Ajanta wash against Company and Ravi Varma."*

### Flashcard ART-2101 — Nandalal Bose: art in the state's own iconography

**Front:** What did Nandalal Bose contribute?

**Definition (learn):** Associated with Santiniketan. Known for the white-on-black Gandhi sketch of the Dandi March (1930s). He was entrusted with illustrating the original document of the Constitution of India and with sketching the emblems of the Bharat Ratna and Padma awards. Works named: Shiva and Sati; Tiller of the Soil (Indian Farmer).

**Breakdown:**
- Santiniketan.
- Dandi March sketch.
- Constitution original document illustration.
- Bharat Ratna and Padma emblems.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 361–362 (✅ owner 07).

**Links:** ART-2100, ART-2102

**Trap:** Dating the Dandi sketch beyond the 1930s, or attributing other works not named.

**Mains hook:** Art entering the iconography of the state.

**Back:** *"Bose: the Dandi March in white-on-black; state honour emblems."* Hmm: "illustrating the original document" — "illustrating the original Constitution" — let me use: *"Bose: the Dandi March in white-on-black, the Constitution's illustrations."* Keep accurate.

### Flashcard ART-2102 — Progressive Artists Group (1947)

**Front:** Who formed the Progressive Artists Group?

**Definition (learn):** Formed after 1947 by six founding members: F.N. Souza, S.H. Raza, M.F. Husain, K.H. Ara, H.A. Gade and S.K. Bakre. It blended progressive themes with abstraction, drawing on European Modernism without a uniform style.

**Breakdown:**
- Founders: Souza, Raza, Husain, Ara, Gade, Bakre.
- Approach: progressive themes, abstraction.
- Influence: European Modernism.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 363–364 (✅ owner 07).

**Links:** ART-2100, ART-2110

**Trap:** Describing the group as a single style. It had no uniform style.

**Mains hook:** The post-independence break from academic realism and Bengal School revivalism.

**Back:** *"Six founders, 1947, no single style."*

### Flashcard ART-2103 — Madhubani (Mithila), Bihar

**Front:** What is Madhubani painting?

**Definition (learn):** Painting by women of villages around Madhubani town in Bihar (Mithila), with natural pigments in line-and-fill technique on paper or cloth. Depicts Krishna, Rama, Durga, Lakshmi and Shiva with symbolic motifs; the fish is a motif of luck and fertility.

**Breakdown:**
- Region: Bihar (Mithila).
- Technique: natural pigments, line-and-fill.
- Subjects: Krishna, Rama, Durga, Lakshmi, Shiva.
- Motif: fish for luck and fertility.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p. 364 (✅ owner 07).

**Links:** ART-2104, ART-2107, ART-2112

**Trap:** Confusing Madhubani with Warli's geometric white-on-earth idiom.

**Mains hook:** Ritual origin, temple use and market export form one craft chain (topic 12).

**Back:** *"Mithila: fish for luck, line-and-fill, natural pigments."*

### Flashcard ART-2104 — Warli (Maharashtra)

**Front:** What is Warli painting?

**Definition (learn):** White pigment on a mud or cow-dung base, geometric figures, communal scenes. Maharashtra.

**Breakdown:**
- Region: Maharashtra.
- Pigment: white on mud or cow-dung ground.
- Form: geometric figures.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 07 (§13.6).

**Links:** ART-2103, ART-2105

**Trap:** Mixing Warli with Gond's dotted or linear infill.

**Mains hook:** Community scenes in geometric grammar.

**Back:** *"Warli: white on mud, geometric people."*

### Flashcard ART-2105 — Pattachitra (Odisha)

**Front:** What is Pattachitra?

**Definition (learn):** Cloth or palm-leaf scroll painting from Odisha, with Jagannath and Puranic themes.

**Breakdown:**
- Region: Odisha.
- Support: cloth or palm-leaf.
- Themes: Jagannath, Puranic.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 07 (§13.6).

**Links:** ART-2106, ART-2107

**Trap:** Mixing Pattachitra with Phad's Rajasthani narrative scroll.

**Mains hook:** Religious scroll painting from Odisha.

**Back:** *"Pattachitra: Odisha's Jagannath on cloth."*

### Flashcard ART-2106 — Kalamkari (Andhra Pradesh)

**Front:** What is Kalamkari?

**Definition (learn):** Hand-drawn or block-printed textile art from Andhra Pradesh, using natural dyes and mordants, for temple hangings and narrative cloth. A textile process, which overlaps with topic 12.

**Breakdown:**
- Region: Andhra Pradesh.
- Process: hand-drawn or block-printed.
- Materials: natural dyes and mordants.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 07 (§13.6); PDF p. 364 context (✅ owner 07).

**Links:** ART-2105, ART-2103

**Trap:** Treating Kalamkari as a painting of the same type as Pattachitra. It is textile-based.

**Mains hook:** Craft and painting meet in textile.

**Back:** *"Kalamkari: dyes and blocks on textile."*

### Flashcard ART-2107 — Pichhwai (Nathdwara)

**Front:** What is Pichhwai, and what is its origin?

**Definition (learn):** Rajasthan (mainly Nathdwara) painting depicting Krishna as Sreenathji within Vaishnavism. Originally temple hangings for the Pushtimarg Sampradaya; now a major Nathdwara export. Vallabhacharya, founder of Pushtimarg, is credited with introducing the form in the 16th century.

**Breakdown:**
- Region: Rajasthan (Nathdwara).
- Subject: Krishna as Sreenathji.
- Origin: temple hangings.
- Chain: ritual origin → temple use → export market.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p. 364 (✅ owner 07). Vallabhacharya's 16th-century introduction is owner-stated (⚠️ not verified here).

**Links:** ART-2103, ART-2106

**Trap:** Calling Pichhwai a secular folk painting. It is devotional.

**Mains hook:** A craft-ecology chain applied to painting.

**Back:** *"Pichhwai: Krishna as Sreenathji, from temple hanging to export."*

### Flashcard ART-2108 — Thangka (Himalayan Buddhist)

**Front:** What is a Thangka?

**Definition (learn):** A scroll painting on cotton or silk (applique) from the Himalayan Buddhist regions, with religious iconography.

**Breakdown:**
- Region: Himalayan Buddhist regions.
- Support: cotton or silk, applique.
- Subject: religious iconography.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 07 (§2, §13.6).

**Links:** ART-2105, ART-2109

**Trap:** Calling Thangka a folk secular form. It is religious.

**Mains hook:** Religious scroll painting as a living Himalayan tradition.

**Back:** *"Thangka: Himalayan scroll, Buddhist icon."*

### Flashcard ART-2109 — Phad (Rajasthan) and Gond (Madhya Pradesh)

**Front:** What are Phad and Gond painting?

**Definition (learn):** Phad is a Rajasthani narrative scroll painting, distinct from Pattachitra. Gond painting is from Madhya Pradesh, with dotted and linear infill, distinct from Warli's geometric white idiom.

**Breakdown:**
- Phad: Rajasthan, narrative scroll.
- Gond: Madhya Pradesh, dotted and linear infill.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p. 364 and owner 07 §13.6 (✅ owner 07).

**Links:** ART-2108, ART-2104

**Trap:** Mixing Phad with Pattachitra, or Gond with Warli.

**Mains hook:** Each named folk tradition has its own region, community and material base.

**Back:** *"Phad: Rajasthan's narrative scroll; Gond: dots and lines."*

### Flashcard ART-2110 — Indian Cubism and M.F. Husain's horse

**Front:** What is the Cubist current in Indian painting?

**Definition (learn):** Indian Cubist painting, inspired by European Cubism, broke down and reassembled objects using abstract forms. M.F. Husain frequently used the horse motif to depict the fluidity of motion.

**Breakdown:**
- Influence: European Cubism.
- Method: broke down and reassembled objects.
- Motif: horse (M.F. Husain).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p. 363 (✅ owner 07).

**Links:** ART-2102, ART-2100

**Trap:** Treating Indian Cubism as imitation. The owner's point is that modernism was absorbed, not merely copied.

**Mains hook:** Modernism absorbed rather than imitated.

**Back:** *"Husain's horse: Cubism, fluid motion, Indian."*

### Flashcard ART-2111 — Rabindranath Tagore, Sister Nivedita and the Bengal School network

**Front:** What are the Bengal School's other figures and the Sister Nivedita link?

**Definition (learn):** Rabindranath Tagore made small-format paintings using dominant black lines. Some art historians link his paintings to his writings (a scholarly reading, not fact). Other Bengal School painters: Asit Kumar Haldar, Manishi Dey, Mukul Dey, Sunayani Devi. Sister Nivedita (Margaret Elizabeth Noble, 1867–1911) promoted science and art, mentored young Bengal School artists, and called Abanindranath's Bharat Mata "the first great masterpiece in a new style."

**Breakdown:**
- Rabindranath: black lines, small format.
- Other painters: Haldar, Dey, Dey, Sunayani Devi.
- Nivedita: institutional and intellectual support.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 361–363 (✅ owner 07).

**Links:** ART-2100, ART-2101

**Trap:** Stating Rabindranath's links to his writings as fact.

**Mains hook:** A school needed networks as well as artists.

**Back:** *"Nivedita praised Bharat Mata; Tagore drew in black."*

### Flashcard ART-2112 — Other folk and tribal painting traditions

**Front:** Name the other folk and tribal painting traditions in the owner's list.

**Definition (learn):** Kalighat (Bengal), Cheriyal scroll (Telangana), Pithora (Gujarat), Saura and Paitkar (associated with tribal Odisha and Jharkhand traditions), and Santhal painting.

**Breakdown:**
- Kalighat: Bengal.
- Cheriyal scroll: Telangana.
- Pithora: Gujarat.
- Saura and Paitkar: tribal Odisha and Jharkhand.
- Santhal painting.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p. 309–377 outline (✅ owner 07).

**Links:** ART-2098, ART-2103, ART-2109

**Trap:** Treating folk and tribal painting as one homogeneous category.

**Mains hook:** Use a named tradition, its region and its material when a question asks about living heritage.

**Back:** *"Kalighat, Cheriyal, Pithora, Saura, Santhal: each its own region."*

### GROUP — 08 Indian Music (basic/08 · advanced/08)

### Flashcard ART-2113 — Hindustani and Carnatic: shared root, different grammar

**Front:** How are Hindustani and Carnatic music related?

**Definition (learn):** Two classical traditions that developed from a shared older theory. They differ in classification system (thaat vs melakarta), improvisational grammar, repertoire, ornament and performance sequence. Neither difference is "foreign versus pure."

**Breakdown:**
- Shared: saptaswara, raga, tala, older treatises.
- Hindustani: North Indian court, devotional and Persianate networks; gharana transmission; alap, bol-bant, taan, layakari.
- Carnatic: South Indian temple, court and sabha networks; guru-shishya transmission; manodharma (alapana, niraval, kalpana swara, tanam, RTP).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 08 §3 and §13.6 (✅ owner 08).

**Links:** ART-2114, ART-2115, ART-2118, ART-2144

**Trap:** Calling them merely geographic labels for one practice, or saying Carnatic music lacks improvisation.

**Mains hook:** Shared-root thesis: differentiated by classification and improvisational grammar, not by improvisation or foreign origin.

**Back:** *"Same root, different grammar, different patron."*

### Flashcard ART-2114 — Saptaswara: the seven notes

**Front:** What are the seven notes of Indian music?

**Definition (learn):** The base scale shared by both traditions: Shadja (Sa), Rishabha (Re), Gandhara (Ga), Madhyama (Ma), Panchama (Pa), Dhaivata (Dha), Nishada (Ni).

**Breakdown:**
1. Sa: Shadja.
2. Re: Rishabha.
3. Ga: Gandhara.
4. Ma: Madhyama.
5. Pa: Panchama.
6. Dha: Dhaivata.
7. Ni: Nishada.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 08, PDF pp. 411 ff.

**Links:** ART-2113, ART-2115, ART-2116

**Trap:** Confusing the seven notes with the twelve-note set from which a thaat is drawn.

**Mains hook:** The shared theory base of both systems.

**Back:** *"Sa Re Ga Ma Pa Dha Ni: the seven shared notes."*

### Flashcard ART-2115 — Thaat vs raga, and the ten Hindustani thaats

**Front:** How does a thaat differ from a raga?

**Definition (learn):** A thaat is a parent scale: seven of the twelve notes, stated in ascending order only, no vaadi or samvadi note, no emotional quality, and not sung. A raga is a melodic entity with aaroha-avaroha (ascent and descent), vaadi and samvadi notes, five, six or seven notes, and a time-season-rasa association.

**Breakdown:**
- Thaat: parent scale; pedagogic classification.
- Raga: specific melody; can be sung.
- Ten thaats in current Hindustani pedagogy: Bilawal, Khamaj, Kafi, Asavari, Bhairavi, Bhairav, Kalyan, Marwa, Poorvi, Todi.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 419–421 (✅ owner 08, §13.4).

**Links:** ART-2114, ART-2116, ART-2118, ART-2120

**Trap:** Treating thaat and raga as interchangeable. Also, Carnatic melakartas are not Bhatkhande's thaats.

**Mains hook:** The thaat-versus-raga distinction is the single most testable point in classification.

**Back:** *"Thaat is the scale; raga is the song."*

### Flashcard ART-2116 — Raga, time, season and mood

**Front:** How does a raga link to time and mood?

**Definition (learn):** A raga is associated with a time of day or season and an evoked rasa. Examples: Bhairav (dawn, peace), Malkauns (midnight, bravery), Kafi/Desh (Shringara, love), Bhairavi (Karuna, pathos). These associations are tradition-specific, not universal laws.

**Breakdown:**
- Time: Bhairav at dawn; Malkauns at midnight.
- Mood: peace, bravery.
- Rasa pairs: Kafi/Desh with Shringara; Bhairavi with Karuna.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 411 ff (✅ owner 08, §4 and §13.4).

**Links:** ART-2115, ART-2117

**Trap:** "Ragas create emotions" as a universal claim. The association is conventional.

**Mains hook:** The aesthetic system that links melody to hour and mood.

**Back:** *"Bhairav at dawn for peace; Malkauns at midnight for valour."*

### Flashcard ART-2117 — The nine rasas shared with dance

**Front:** What are the nine rasas, and how do they link to music?

**Definition (learn):** The Natya Shastra framework: Shringara (love), Hasya (humour), Karuna (pathos), Raudra (anger), Bhayanaka (horror), Veer (bravery), Adbhuta (wonder), Bibhatsya (disgust), Shant (peace). Music applies it to ragas.

**Breakdown:**
- Nine rasas: Shringara, Hasya, Karuna, Raudra, Bhayanaka, Veer, Adbhuta, Bibhatsya, Shant.
- Music link: raga-rasa correspondences.
- Dance link: same framework (topic 09).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 411 ff (✅ owner 08, §4).

**Links:** ART-2116, ART-2118

**Trap:** Treating the rasa framework as a music-only invention. It is shared aesthetic vocabulary.

**Mains hook:** A shared aesthetic vocabulary across performing arts.

**Back:** *"Nine rasas: love, humour, pathos, anger, horror, valour, wonder, disgust, peace."*

### Flashcard ART-2118 — Melakarta: the 72 parent ragas of Carnatic music

**Front:** What is the melakarta system?

**Definition (learn):** Venkatamakhin's 17th-century Carnatic system of 72 parent (Melakarta or Janaka) ragas, each sampurna (all seven notes in ascent and descent). Janya (derived) ragas come from them. Mayamalavagowla yields Malahari.

**Breakdown:**
- 72 parent ragas: sampurna.
- Janya: derived ragas.
- Example: Mayamalavagowla gives Malahari.
- Treatise: Chaturdandiprakashika.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 412, 416 (✅ owner 08).

**Links:** ART-2115, ART-2119, ART-2140

**Trap:** Equating 72 melakartas with 10 thaats.

**Mains hook:** Carnatic parent-scale classification is different in kind from Hindustani thaats.

**Back:** *"72 parent scales, Venkatamakhin, not Bhatkhande's ten."*

### Flashcard ART-2119 — Treatises and their contributions

**Front:** Which treatise fixed which concept?

**Definition (learn):** Bharata's Natyashastra discusses music with drama and the rasa framework. Matanga's Brihaddeshi (6th–8th century) focuses on defining the term raga. Sharngadeva's Sangita Ratnakara (13th century) is a major pre-bifurcation authority. Venkatamakhin's Chaturdandiprakashika (17th century) underlies the melakarta approach.

**Breakdown:**
- Natyashastra: music with drama; rasa.
- Brihaddeshi: definition of raga.
- Sangita Ratnakara: pre-bifurcation.
- Chaturdandiprakashika: melakarta.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 412, 416 (✅ owner 08, §13.5).

**Links:** ART-2117, ART-2118, ART-2120, ART-2121

**Trap:** Answering a treatise question with title-plus-author recall. Answer by contribution.

**Mains hook:** A treatise question is won by saying which concept each text fixed.

**Back:** *"Natya gave rasa, Brihaddeshi named raga, Ratnakara preceded the split."*

### Flashcard ART-2120 — V.N. Bhatkhande

**Front:** Who was V.N. Bhatkhande, and what did he systematise?

**Definition (learn):** V.N. Bhatkhande (1860–1936) systematised modern Hindustani pedagogy, notably the thaat-based classification. The Bhatkhande institution was founded in 1926 and later renamed the Bhatkhande Music Institute Deemed University.

**Breakdown:**
- Dates: 1860–1936.
- Contribution: thaat classification; pedagogy.
- Institution: 1926.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 08, §13.5 (dates and institution per owner).

**Links:** ART-2115, ART-2119, ART-2121

**Trap:** Crediting Bhatkhande with the melakarta system. That is Venkatamakhin's.

**Mains hook:** Institutionalisation made classical music transmissible outside lineages.

**Back:** *"Bhatkhande: ten thaats and a 1926 institute."*

### Flashcard ART-2121 — V.D. Paluskar and the Gandharva Mahavidyalaya (1901)

**Front:** Which institution did V.D. Paluskar found, and when?

**Definition (learn):** V.D. Paluskar (1872–1931) set up the Gandharva Mahavidyalaya in 1901 expressly to teach music. Routed to the 2025 Prelims GS-I Q18 ("First Gandharva Mahavidyalaya (Vishnu Digambar Paluskar, 1901)").

**Breakdown:**
- Person: V.D. Paluskar (1872–1931).
- Institution: Gandharva Mahavidyalaya.
- Year: 1901.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 451–452 (✅ owner 08). The 2025 Q18 official key is held locally but not recorded in the owner.

**Links:** ART-2120, ART-2119

**Trap:** Confusing Paluskar's 1901 institution with Bhatkhande's 1926 institution.

**Mains hook:** Institutionalisation of music teaching in the early twentieth century.

**Back:** *"Paluskar 1901: Gandharva Mahavidyalaya, to teach music."*

### Flashcard ART-2122 — Dhrupad

**Front:** What is Dhrupad?

**Definition (learn):** The oldest Hindustani vocal style, associated with Mian Tansen in the owner's account, and carried by the Dagar gharana (Dagar Vani).

**Breakdown:**
- Status: oldest Hindustani vocal style (owner).
- Gharana: Dagar (Dagar Vani).
- Related: Agra gharana blends khayal with dhrupad-dhamar.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 411 ff (✅ owner 08).

**Links:** ART-2123, ART-2126, ART-2137

**Trap:** Treating Tansen as the inventor of dhrupad or of ragas. The owner's sources do not support invention claims.

**Mains hook:** Dhrupad and khayal as two structures and emotional registers.

**Back:** *"Dhrupad: oldest vocal form, Dagar vani."*

### Flashcard ART-2123 — Khayal, Sadarang and Muhammad Shah

**Front:** What is Khayal, and what role did Sadarang play?

**Definition (learn):** The dominant later Hindustani vocal style. Sadarang (Nyamat Khan, 1670–1748) and Adarang later strengthened khayal as court musicians under Muhammad Shah. The Gwalior gharana is the oldest khayal gharana.

**Breakdown:**
- Sadarang: Nyamat Khan, 1670–1748.
- Ruler: Muhammad Shah.
- Gharana: Gwalior (oldest khayal gharana).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Satish Chandra, *Part 2*, PDF pp. 394–395 (✅ owner 08); Sadarang dates (✅ owner 08 advanced table).

**Links:** ART-2122, ART-2128, ART-2135

**Trap:** Dating the invention of khayal to Amir Khusrau. The owner flags this as contested.

**Mains hook:** Khayal's growth under a court is an example of patronage shaping a form.

**Back:** *"Khayal: Sadarang and Adarang, under Muhammad Shah."*

### Flashcard ART-2124 — Thumri, Tappa, Ghazal and Tarana

**Front:** What are the semi-classical Hindustani styles?

**Definition (learn):** Thumri, Tappa, Ghazal and Tarana are named Hindustani styles, each with its own origin and performance context. Patiala gharana specialises in thumri and ghazal.

**Breakdown:**
- Thumri: semi-classical, lyrical.
- Tappa: distinct structure.
- Ghazal: Persian-derived.
- Tarana: named vocal style.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 411 ff (✅ owner 08).

**Links:** ART-2122, ART-2131

**Trap:** Treating semi-classical styles as classical forms. They are distinct.

**Mains hook:** Distinct structure, tempo and emotional register per style.

**Back:** *"Thumri, Tappa, Ghazal, Tarana: semi-classical, each its own mood."*

### Flashcard ART-2125 — The gharana as a patronage ecology

**Front:** What is a gharana?

**Definition (learn):** A hereditary or regional lineage of musical transmission with a distinct style (vani) and named exponents. Functionally, a family labour structure that concentrated a scarce skill for court or elite patrons, reproducing it by descent or apprenticeship. When princely courts declined, the lineages moved to concert, radio, teaching and recording.

**Breakdown:**
- Structure: household, lineage.
- Mechanism: concentrated skill, patron tie, transmission.
- Afterlife: concert, radio, recording.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 08 §13.7 (✅ owner 08).

**Links:** ART-2126, ART-2127, ART-2128, ART-2129, ART-2130, ART-2131, ART-2132, ART-2133, ART-2151

**Trap:** Calling a gharana merely a "school of thought." It is a labour and patronage structure.

**Mains hook:** The gharana's durability shows music was reproduced by social organisation as much as by theory.

**Back:** *"A gharana is a family that kept a skill alive when courts fell."*

### Flashcard ART-2126 — Dagar gharana

**Front:** What is the Dagar gharana's specialism?

**Definition (learn):** The Dagar gharana is associated with the Dagar vani in dhrupad.

**Breakdown:** Specialism: Dagar vani; genre: dhrupad.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 08, §4 and §13.7.

**Links:** ART-2122, ART-2125

**Trap:** Assigning Dagar to khayal. It is a dhrupad lineage.

**Mains hook:** Gharana identity by genre and vani.

**Back:** *"Dagar: dhrupad's vani."*

### Flashcard ART-2127 — Darbhanga gharana

**Front:** What is the Darbhanga gharana's emphasis?

**Definition (learn):** Khandar vani, with equal focus on alaap and layakari.

**Breakdown:** Vani: Khandar; emphasis: alaap and layakari equally.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 08.

**Links:** ART-2125, ART-2128

**Trap:** Describing every gharana as having the same balance.

**Mains hook:** Gharana specialisation as a distinguishing feature.

**Back:** *"Darbhanga: Khandar vani, alaap and layakari equal."*

### Flashcard ART-2128 — Gwalior gharana

**Front:** What is the Gwalior gharana?

**Definition (learn):** The oldest khayal gharana, with balanced emphasis across the elements of performance.

**Breakdown:** Genre: khayal; status: oldest khayal gharana; emphasis: balanced.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 08, §4 and §13.7.

**Links:** ART-2123, ART-2125

**Trap:** Assigning Gwalior to dhrupad only. Its distinction is khayal.

**Mains hook:** The Gwalior line as the early khayal anchor.

**Back:** *"Gwalior: the oldest khayal house."*

### Flashcard ART-2129 — Kirana gharana

**Front:** What is the Kirana gharana's emphasis, and who are its exponents?

**Definition (learn):** Kirana emphasises precise swara focus. The owner's table on Bhimsen Joshi lists "precise tuning of swaras, slow tempo, melody over rhythm and clear pronunciation." Exponents named: Abdul Karim Khan (populariser), Abdul Wahid Khan, Gangubai Hangal, Bhimsen Joshi.

**Breakdown:**
- Emphasis: swara precision.
- Exponents: Abdul Karim Khan, Abdul Wahid Khan, Gangubai Hangal, Bhimsen Joshi.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 425, 433, 435 (✅ owner 08). Gangubai Hangal date not in source (⚠️).

**Links:** ART-2125, ART-2152, ART-2157

**Trap:** Giving Kirana to Bismillah Khan. He is associated with Benaras.

**Mains hook:** Kirana as a precise, melody-led school.

**Back:** *"Kirana: swara precision, Bhimsen, Gangubai, Abdul Karim."*

### Flashcard ART-2130 — Agra gharana

**Front:** What is the Agra (Rangila) gharana?

**Definition (learn):** A blend of khayal with dhrupad-dhamar, with stress on bandish. Exponents named: Faiyyaz Khan and Vilayat Hussain Khan.

**Breakdown:** Blend: khayal and dhrupad-dhamar; stress: bandish; exponents: Faiyyaz Khan, Vilayat Hussain Khan.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 08. Exponents' dates not in the source (⚠️).

**Links:** ART-2122, ART-2123, ART-2157

**Trap:** Presenting Agra as a pure khayal school.

**Mains hook:** Gharana identity as a blend of genres.

**Back:** *"Agra: khayal meets dhamar, bandish first."*

### Flashcard ART-2131 — Patiala gharana

**Front:** What does the Patiala gharana specialise in?

**Definition (learn):** Thumri and ghazal specialisation. Bade Ghulam Ali Khan is named.

**Breakdown:** Specialism: thumri and ghazal; exponent: Bade Ghulam Ali Khan.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 08, §4 and §13.7.

**Links:** ART-2124, ART-2125

**Trap:** Treating Patiala as a khayal-first gharana.

**Mains hook:** Gharana specialisation in semi-classical genres.

**Back:** *"Patiala: thumri and ghazal, Bade Ghulam Ali."*

### Flashcard ART-2132 — Bhendibazaar gharana

**Front:** What is the Bhendibazaar gharana's emphasis?

**Definition (learn):** Breath-control emphasis.

**Breakdown:** Emphasis: breath control.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 08, §4 and §13.7.

**Links:** ART-2125

**Trap:** Assuming the breath-control emphasis is shared by all gharanas.

**Mains hook:** A gharana distinguished by a technical discipline.

**Back:** *"Bhendibazaar: breath is the discipline."*

### Flashcard ART-2133 — Benaras gharana

**Front:** Which exponents does the Benaras gharana produce?

**Definition (learn):** The Benaras gharana of Hindustani classical music, which the source says produced "gems like Bismillah Khan, Sitara Devi, Girija Devi."

**Breakdown:** Gharana: Benaras; exponents: Bismillah Khan, Sitara Devi, Girija Devi.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p. 291 (✅ owner 08).

**Links:** ART-2153, ART-2134

**Trap:** Attributing Benaras gharana to Bhimsen Joshi. He is Kirana.

**Mains hook:** Benaras as a sitar and shehnai centre.

**Back:** *"Benaras: Bismillah, Sitara, Girija."*

### Flashcard ART-2134 — Sitar gharanas: Jaipur, Varanasi, Etawah

**Front:** Which sitar gharanas are named?

**Definition (learn):** Jaipur, Varanasi and Etawah. The source names no others for sitar.

**Breakdown:** Sitar gharanas: Jaipur; Varanasi; Etawah.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 449–450 (✅ owner 08).

**Links:** ART-2147, ART-2133, ART-2154

**Trap:** Giving Ravi Shankar a gharana. The source gives none.

**Mains hook:** Instrument gharanas as lineages of technique.

**Back:** *"Sitar: Jaipur, Varanasi, Etawah."*

### Flashcard ART-2135 — The Aurangzeb correction

**Front:** What did Aurangzeb do to music?

**Definition (learn):** Singing was removed from the imperial court, but instrumental music continued. Royal women and nobles patronised music. Aurangzeb himself played the veena, and substantial music literature was produced.

**Breakdown:**
- Court singing: curtailed.
- Instrumental: continued.
- Patrons: royal women, nobles.
- Writing: substantial music literature.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Satish Chandra, *Part 2*, PDF pp. 394–395 (✅ owner 08).

**Links:** ART-2123, ART-2137

**Trap:** "Aurangzeb banned music" or "buried music." The owner corrects this.

**Mains hook:** Loss of one patronage node redirects a tradition rather than ending it.

**Back:** *"Court singing curbed; veena and music writing continued."*

### Flashcard ART-2136 — Pandit Mallikarjun Mansur: the open gap

**Front:** Which gharana is Pandit Mallikarjun Mansur attributed to?

**Definition (learn):** The folder's sources do not name Pandit Mallikarjun Mansur or attribute him to a gharana. The routed 2026 Prelims Q19 asks about him and Hindustani gharanas. The owner says to leave that item blank.

**Breakdown:**
- Source status: not in owner sources.
- Route: gharana logic only.
- Prohibited: inventing a gharana attribution.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2026 Prelims GS-I Q19; provisional 2026 Set-A key held locally, answer not recorded here (✅ owner 08).

**Links:** ART-2125, ART-2129, ART-2130, ART-2163

**Trap:** Guessing from nearby gharana names (Kirana, Agra or Benaras).

**Mains hook:** The correct behaviour is to leave the item blank rather than guess.

**Back:** *"Mansur: not in the sources; do not guess a gharana."*

### Flashcard ART-2137 — Tansen and the court patrons

**Front:** What do the court musicians and patrons teach about patronage?

**Definition (learn):** Tansen was under Akbar (earlier under Raja Ramchandra of Rewa). Raja Man Singh Tomar compiled the Mankautuhal at Gwalior. Ibrahim Adil Shah II wrote the Kitab-i-Nauras. Maharana Kumbha of Mewar wrote Sangeet Raja and Sangeet Mimansa and played the veena. Rulers funded and wrote.

**Breakdown:**
- Tansen: Akbar; Rewa.
- Man Singh Tomar: Mankautuhal, Gwalior.
- Ibrahim Adil Shah II: Kitab-i-Nauras.
- Maharana Kumbha: Sangeet Raja, Sangeet Mimansa.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 431–433 (✅ owner 08); CSE 2019 Q16 routed: Mian Tansen and Mughal court music (key not held locally).

**Links:** ART-2122, ART-2135, ART-2143

**Trap:** Saying Tansen "invented many ragas." The owner treats this claim cautiously.

**Mains hook:** Patronage was not only money; rulers also authored.

**Back:** *"Tansen at Akbar's court; Kumbha wrote and played."*

### Flashcard ART-2138 — Annamacharya and Purandara Dasa

**Front:** What are the dates and key facts for Annamacharya and Purandara Dasa?

**Definition (learn):** Annamacharya (1408–1503): the first known Carnatic composer, patronised by Vijayanagara rulers, composed sankirtanas in praise of Lord Venkateswara, remembered as "Grandfather of Telugu songwriting." Purandara Dasa (1484–1564): called the founding father or Pitamaha of Carnatic music; composed Dasa Sahitya; a Krishna devotee; patronised by Vijayanagara.

**Breakdown:**
- Annamacharya: 1408–1503; Venkateswara; Vijayanagara.
- Purandara Dasa: 1484–1564; Dasa Sahitya; Krishna; Vijayanagara.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 429, 433–434 (✅ owner 08).

**Links:** ART-2139, ART-2143

**Trap:** Naming Annamacharya's songs as Krishna-centred. His compositions praise Venkateswara. Also avoid naming individual sankirtanas.

**Mains hook:** Temple and empire as the Carnatic patron type.

**Back:** *"Annamacharya: Venkateswara, 1408–1503. Purandara: Pitamaha, 1484–1564."*

### Flashcard ART-2139 — The Trinity and the 2018 Tyagaraja trap

**Front:** Who are the Carnatic Trinity, and what is the 2018 trap?

**Definition (learn):** Tyagaraja, Muthuswami Dikshitar and Syama Sastri comprise the Trinity of Carnatic music. Tyagaraja's kritis are mainly Rama-centred (owner basic §9). The Tyagaraja Aradhana at Thiruvaiyaru commemorates his samadhi.

**Breakdown:**
- Trinity: Tyagaraja, Muthuswami Dikshitar, Syama Sastri.
- Tyagaraja: no birth or death years in the local source.
- Aradhana: Thiruvaiyaru, Tamil Nadu.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p. 878 (✅ owner 08). CSE 2018 Prelims Q44 routed.

**Links:** ART-2138, ART-2141, ART-2143

**Trap:** Making Tyagaraja and Annamacharya contemporaries (Annamacharya is 1408–1503), or saying Tyagaraja's kritis are mainly in praise of Krishna. Do not supply dates for Tyagaraja, Dikshitar or Sastri.

**Mains hook:** The Carnatic canon organised around a saint's samadhi.

**Back:** *"Trinity: Tyagaraja, Dikshitar, Sastri. Rama, not Krishna, mostly."*

### Flashcard ART-2140 — Kshetrayya and Bhadrachala Ramadasu

**Front:** What do Kshetrayya and Bhadrachala Ramadasu contribute?

**Definition (learn):** Kshetrayya (1600–1680): composer of padams and keertanas, a travelling poet; his padams are still sung in Bharatanatyam and Kuchipudi. Bhadrachala Ramadasu (1620–1680): a vaggeyakara (composer of words and music) of Telugu devotional compositions for Rama, imprisoned by the Golconda Sultan (the source's statement).

**Breakdown:**
- Kshetrayya: padams and keertanas; dance transmission.
- Ramadasu: Rama compositions; vaggeyakara.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p. 434 (✅ owner 08).

**Links:** ART-2141, ART-2151, ART-2143

**Trap:** Building a political narrative on Ramadasu's imprisonment beyond the source's statement.

**Mains hook:** Kshetrayya as the clearest music-to-dance transmission row.

**Back:** *"Kshetrayya: padams for dancers. Ramadasu: Rama in his own words and tune."*

### Flashcard ART-2141 — Swathi Thirunal and the Thanjavur Quartet

**Front:** What is the link between Swathi Thirunal and the Thanjavur Quartet?

**Definition (learn):** Swathi Thirunal Rama Varma (1813–1846), Maharaja of Travancore, composed in both Hindustani and Carnatic styles and was a patron of the Thanjavur Quartet (Chinnaiah, Ponniah, Sivanandam, Vadivelu). The Quartet, court musicians in the 19th century, codified the Bharatanatyam repertoire. The Royal Asiatic Society mourned his death.

**Breakdown:**
- Swathi Thirunal: 1813–1846; Travancore; composer in two traditions.
- Thanjavur Quartet: four brothers; codified Bharatanatyam repertoire.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p. 434 (✅ owner 08).

**Links:** ART-2140, ART-2138, ART-2151

**Trap:** Dating the Quartet to the Mughal or Chola period. They are 19th century.

**Mains hook:** The best ruler-as-composer case: patron and author in one person.

**Back:** *"Swathi Thirunal, 1813–46: composer-patron of the Thanjavur Quartet."*

### Flashcard ART-2142 — Kriti and the composition anatomy; concert order

**Front:** What is a kriti, and what is the concert sequence?

**Definition (learn):** The kriti is the central, highly evolved Carnatic composed form, set to a fixed raga and fixed tala, with primacy of the sahitya (lyrics). Its three-part anatomy: Pallavi (repeated thematic lines), Anu Pallavi (two lines after the pallavi), and Charana (the final and longest verse). A typical concert: Varnam, Alapana, Neraval, Kalpana swaras, ending with a lighter piece such as a Tillana.

**Breakdown:**
- Kriti: fixed raga and tala; sahitya primacy.
- Anatomy: Pallavi, Anu Pallavi, Charana.
- Other forms: Varnam, Padam, Javali, Tillana.
- Improvisation: Swara-kalpana; Tanam (free rhythm, with mridangam); Ragam (no mridangam).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 428–429 (✅ owner 08).

**Links:** ART-2143, ART-2144, ART-2139

**Trap:** Defining a kriti by a fixed number of sections beyond the three-part anatomy the source gives.

**Mains hook:** The safest anatomy for any Carnatic composition, plus the concert order.

**Back:** *"Pallavi, Anu Pallavi, Charana; Varnam opens, Tillana closes."*

### Flashcard ART-2143 — Kirtana and sankirtana vs kriti

**Front:** How does the source use kirtana and sankirtana?

**Definition (learn):** The source uses kirtana for Annamacharya's devotional songs in praise of Venkateswara, and sankirtana for the same corpus. Sankirtana is also Manipur's ritual singing, drumming and dancing by the Vaishnava community. Kriti is the evolved concert form.

**Breakdown:**
- Kirtana: devotional song (Annamacharya).
- Sankirtana: same corpus; Manipur ritual form.
- Kriti: evolved concert form.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 433, 443, 456 (✅ owner 08).

**Links:** ART-2138, ART-2142

**Trap:** Asserting a formal definition of kirtana or a dated evolution from kirtana to kriti. The source gives neither.

**Mains hook:** Say what the source says and stop.

**Back:** *"Kirtana: the earlier devotional song; kriti: the concert form."*

### Flashcard ART-2144 — Manodharma: the "less improvisation" correction

**Front:** Does Carnatic music have little improvisation?

**Definition (learn):** No. Carnatic music has extensive manodharma (improvisation) through alapana, niraval, kalpana swara, tanam and ragam-tanam-pallavi. The source's line "less scope for improvisation" refers to codified style, not absence of improvisation.

**Breakdown:**
- Manodharma: alapana, niraval, kalpana swara, tanam, RTP.
- Hindustani equivalent: alap, bol-bant, taan, layakari.
- The source's claim: "only one particular prescribed style of singing."

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 428–429 and the source's own improvisation sections (✅ owner 08).

**Links:** ART-2113, ART-2142

**Trap:** Repeating "less scope for improvisation" without this correction.

**Mains hook:** Codified style is not absent improvisation.

**Back:** *"Carnatic improvises, in its own forms."*

### Flashcard ART-2145 — Amir Khusrau: contested attributions

**Front:** Which musical inventions are attributed to Amir Khusrau?

**Definition (learn):** The sitar, tabla, khayal and qawwali are popularly attributed to Amir Khusrau. These are contested attributions, not certain. Flag them as "popularly attributed to" unless a contemporaneous source is cited.

**Breakdown:**
- Sitar: popular attribution, contested.
- Tabla: popular attribution, contested.
- Khayal, qawwali: popular attribution, contested.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 08 advanced §1, §2; §13.10 (✅ owner 08). No invention date is given for tabla or sitar.

**Links:** ART-2122, ART-2123, ART-2146

**Trap:** Presenting Amir Khusrau as the certain, sole inventor of any of these.

**Mains hook:** Source-critical attribution. The owner applies the same discipline to Nataraja origins.

**Back:** *"Khusrau: popularly credited, never certainly proved."*

### Flashcard ART-2146 — The four-fold instrument classification (A-S-G-T)

**Front:** What are the four families of musical instruments?

**Definition (learn):** Tata (strings), Sushira (wind), Avanaddha (membrane percussion) and Ghana (non-drum percussion). Western equivalents: chordophones, aerophones, membranophones, idiophones.

**Breakdown:**
- Tata: "string instruments"; plucked, bowed or struck.
- Sushira: "all the wind instruments."
- Avanaddha: struck membrane over a resonator.
- Ghana: struck solid body; "do not require tuning."

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 447–450 (✅ owner 08).

**Links:** ART-2147, ART-2148, ART-2149, ART-2150, ART-2151

**Trap:** Filing ghatam or jaltarang as drums (membranophones). Both are Ghana.

**Mains hook:** Family is set by sound mechanism, not by striking.

**Back:** *"A-S-G-T: skin struck, air blown, solid struck, string plucked."*

### Flashcard ART-2147 — Tata Vadya: string instruments

**Front:** Which instruments belong to the Tata family?

**Definition (learn):** Stretched strings. Classical bowed: sarangi, esraj/dilruba, violin. Plectral: sitar, veena, tanpura, guitar. Struck: gotuvadyam, swaramandal. Folk: chikara, sarinda, tingteila, kamaicha, tumbi, ektara, dotara, onavillu.

**Breakdown:**
- Bowed: sarangi (Bhangash family), kamaicha (Manganiyars), chikara, sarinda.
- Plectral: sitar, veena (Saraswati's instrument), tanpura, dotara (Bauls), tumbi (Punjab, Bhangra), ektara (wandering monks).
- Struck: gotuvadyam, swaramandal; santoor (100 strings, Jammu and Kashmir, Sufiana kalam).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 447–450 (✅ owner 08).

**Links:** ART-2146, ART-2134, ART-2151

**Trap:** Calling a struck string instrument a percussion instrument. The string, not the striking, fixes the family.

**Mains hook:** The string fixes the family.

**Back:** *"Tata: strings, bowed, plucked or struck, the string decides."*

### Flashcard ART-2148 — Sushira Vadya: wind instruments

**Front:** Which instruments belong to the Sushira family?

**Definition (learn):** Wind instruments whose sound comes from an enclosed air column. Classical: bansuri (flute), shehnai, pungi, ninkirns. Folk: pungi or been (snake charmers; Kalbelia dance), algoza (Punjab), tangmuri (Meghalaya), titti (South India), mashak, gogona (Assam, Bihu), ejuk tapung.

**Breakdown:**
- Shehnai: Bismillah Khan.
- Bansuri: Hariprasad Chaurasia; Krishna as mythic association.
- Gogona: Assam, Bihu.
- Pungi/been: Rajasthan, Kalbelia.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p. 448–450 (✅ owner 08).

**Links:** ART-2146, ART-2153, ART-2151

**Trap:** Calling shehnai a genre. It is an instrument; dhrupad and khayal are genres.

**Mains hook:** Family from mechanism, genre from performance.

**Back:** *"Sushira: air through a hollow: shehnai, bansuri, pungi, gogona."*

### Flashcard ART-2149 — Avanaddha Vadya: membrane percussion

**Front:** Which instruments are Avanaddha, and what is the discriminator?

**Definition (learn):** Percussion instruments with a stretched membrane over a resonator, struck with the hand or a stick. Classical: tabla (Hindustani), mridangam (Carnatic), dhol, drum, congo, bhumi dundubhi ("ancient earth drum"). Folk: ghumot (Goa, Ganesh festival), idakka (Kerala), udukai (Tamil Nadu), sambal (Maharashtra, Konkan), tamak (Santhals), diggi (Uttar Pradesh).

**Breakdown:**
- Membrane: the discriminator from Ghana.
- Tabla: Hindustani.
- Mridangam: Carnatic.
- Ghumot: Goa.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 447–450 (✅ owner 08).

**Links:** ART-2146, ART-2150

**Trap:** Mixing up tabla (Hindustani) and mridangam (Carnatic). This is the most repeated instrument pairing in objective papers.

**Mains hook:** The skin separates Avanaddha from Ghana.

**Back:** *"Avanaddha: skin on a body. Tabla north, mridangam south."*

### Flashcard ART-2150 — Ghana Vadya: non-drum percussion

**Front:** What is the Ghana family?

**Definition (learn):** Non-drum percussion instruments whose body vibrates. They do not require tuning. Classical: manjira/jhanj, jaltarang, kanch-tarang, ghungroo, ghatam, khartal. Folk: chimta (Punjab, fire tongs), gharha (Punjab, earthen pots), andelu (Burra-Katha).

**Breakdown:**
- Ghatam: a clay pot (Carnatic percussion).
- Jaltarang: bowls, no membrane.
- Chimta: fire tongs used as an instrument.
- Owner's gloss: Ghana classical set as "rhythm-keepers, ancient (Harappan)."

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 447–450 (✅ owner 08). "Ancient (Harappan)" gloss is the source's; do not convert it into an excavated-object date.

**Links:** ART-2146, ART-2149

**Trap:** Filing the ghatam or jaltarang under membranophones, or stating a date from the "ancient" gloss.

**Mains hook:** Non-drum percussion as a separate family.

**Back:** *"Ghana: a pot, bowls, tongs, no skin, no tuning."*

### Flashcard ART-2151 — Folk instruments and their communities

**Front:** Which folk instruments are tied to which communities or regions?

**Definition (learn):** Kamaicha: Rajasthan, Manganiyars. Dotara: Bengal, Bauls. Ektara: wandering monks. Tumbi: Punjab, Bhangra. Gogona: Assam, Bihu. Idakka: Kerala. Udukai: Tamil Nadu. Sambal: Maharashtra. Tangmuri: Meghalaya. Chimta: Punjab. Ghumot: Goa.

**Breakdown:**
- Kamaicha: Rajasthan.
- Dotara: Bengal.
- Gogona: Assam.
- Tumbi: Punjab.
- Santoor: Jammu and Kashmir.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 447–450 (✅ owner 08).

**Links:** ART-2146, ART-2147, ART-2148, ART-2149, ART-2150

**Trap:** Placing Santoor in Rajasthan or Kamaicha in Kashmir.

**Mains hook:** Instruments are community tools, not interchangeable sounds.

**Back:** *"Kamaicha: Manganiyars. Dotara: Bauls. Gogona: Bihu."*

### Flashcard ART-2152 — Pandit Bhimsen Joshi

**Front:** Who was Bhimsen Joshi, and what is his recognition?

**Definition (learn):** A Hindustani khayal vocalist (1922–2011) of the Kirana gharana, famous for "Mile Sur Mera Tumhara." Bharat Ratna: announced 2008; conferred 2009 (Wikipedia).

**Breakdown:**
- Dates: 1922–2011 (owner); death 24 January 2011, Pune (✅ Wikipedia).
- Gharana: Kirana.
- Form: khayal, vocal.
- Honour: Bharat Ratna.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner: Bharat Ratna 2008 (✅ owner 08). Wikipedia: "The awards list says 2009, and a photo caption says conferred at his residence in 2009" (✅ Wikipedia). ⚠️ Conflict: 2008 vs 2009.

**Links:** ART-2129, ART-2155, ART-2156

**Trap:** Giving him the Benaras gharana (he is Kirana), or treating the 2008/2009 year as settled.

**Mains hook:** A recognition sequence (1998, 1999, 2001, 2008/9) across traditions.

**Back:** *"Bhimsen: Kirana, khayal, Mile Sur; Bharat Ratna 2008, conferred 2009."*

### Flashcard ART-2153 — Ustad Bismillah Khan

**Front:** Who was Ustad Bismillah Khan?

**Definition (learn):** A shehnai maestro (1916–2006) of the Benaras gharana who popularised the shehnai in classical and national events. Bharat Ratna 2001. The Ustad Bismillah Khan Yuva Puraskar, for artists under 40 (owner), is named after him.

**Breakdown:**
- Instrument: shehnai (Sushira).
- Gharana: Benaras.
- Honour: Bharat Ratna 2001.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p. 448, p. 291 (✅ owner 08). Bharat Ratna 2001 (✅ Wikipedia Bharat Ratna list).

**Links:** ART-2133, ART-2148, ART-2156

**Trap:** Attributing the Kirana gharana to him. Do not add a birthplace, guru or disciple.

**Mains hook:** A living national-event shehnai.

**Back:** *"Bismillah: shehnai, Benaras, Bharat Ratna 2001."*

### Flashcard ART-2154 — Pandit Ravi Shankar

**Front:** Who was Ravi Shankar, and what is his instrument family?

**Definition (learn):** A sitar maestro (1920–2012) who globalised Indian classical music and collaborated internationally. Bharat Ratna 1999. The owner gives no gharana for him.

**Breakdown:**
- Instrument: sitar (Tata Vadya, plectral, chordophone).
- Honour: Bharat Ratna 1999.
- Gharana: none in the owner's sources.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 433, 435 (✅ owner 08); 1920–2012 (✅ owner 08).

**Links:** ART-2134, ART-2147, ART-2156

**Trap:** Giving Ravi Shankar a gharana. The source gives none.

**Mains hook:** A sitar maestro as the globaliser of the instrument.

**Back:** *"Ravi Shankar: sitar, 1999 Bharat Ratna, no gharana given."*

### Flashcard ART-2155 — M.S. Subbulakshmi

**Front:** Who was M.S. Subbulakshmi?

**Definition (learn):** A Carnatic vocalist (1916–2004), global ambassador of Carnatic music. Bharat Ratna 1998.

**Breakdown:**
- Tradition: Carnatic vocal.
- Honour: Bharat Ratna 1998.
- Role: global ambassador.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 433, 435 (✅ owner 08). Bharat Ratna 1998 (✅ Wikipedia Bharat Ratna list).

**Links:** ART-2152, ART-2156, ART-2118

**Trap:** Placing her in the Hindustani tradition.

**Mains hook:** A Carnatic recognition in the same sequence as Hindustani ones.

**Back:** *"Subbulakshmi: Carnatic vocal, Bharat Ratna 1998."*

### Flashcard ART-2156 — Bharat Ratna and the state as patron

**Front:** What dated recognition sequence can an answer use?

**Definition (learn):** Four Bharat Ratna years for musicians across two traditions and two instruments: M.S. Subbulakshmi 1998, Ravi Shankar 1999, Bismillah Khan 2001, Bhimsen Joshi 2008 (announced; conferred 2009 per Wikipedia). The argument: after courts ended, the state replaced the patron with recognition.

**Breakdown:**
- 1998: M.S. Subbulakshmi (Carnatic vocal).
- 1999: Ravi Shankar (sitar).
- 2001: Bismillah Khan (shehnai).
- 2008/2009: Bhimsen Joshi (khayal).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 08 §13.7C; ✅ Wikipedia (1998, 1999, 2001; Bhimsen 2009 conferral).

**Links:** ART-2152, ART-2153, ART-2154, ART-2155, ART-2162

**Trap:** Describing recognition as a pure gift. Keep the state-recognition argument qualified.

**Mains hook:** Recognition replaced employment as the reward structure.

**Back:** *"1998, 1999, 2001, 2008: recognition replaces the court."*

### Flashcard ART-2157 — Other Kirana and Agra exponents

**Front:** Who are the other named Kirana and Agra exponents?

**Definition (learn):** Kirana: Gangubai Hangal (undated in the source; named as a Kirana exponent only), Abdul Karim Khan and Abdul Wahid Khan (popularisers; 20th century). Agra (Rangila): Faiyyaz Khan and Vilayat Hussain Khan (undated).

**Breakdown:**
- Kirana: Gangubai Hangal, Abdul Karim Khan, Abdul Wahid Khan.
- Agra: Faiyyaz Khan, Vilayat Hussain Khan.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 08 (names). Dates ⚠️ not in the source.

**Links:** ART-2129, ART-2130

**Trap:** Adding awards or dates for Gangubai Hangal or the Agra exponents.

**Mains hook:** Name exponents as exponents only.

**Back:** *"Kirana: Gangubai, Abdul Karim, Abdul Wahid. Agra: Faiyyaz, Vilayat."*

### Flashcard ART-2158 — Folk music bank I: Baul, Pandavani, Panihari, Lavani, Alha, Ovi

**Front:** What are the regions, themes and markers of six folk forms?

**Definition (learn):**
- Baul: West Bengal, Assam, Tripura; a Bengali religious sect and a song form, with Bhakti and Sufi influence; exponents include Lalon Fakir.
- Pandavani: Chhattisgarh; Mahabharata with Bhima as hero; gayan and vadan with tambura; Teejanbai.
- Panihari: Rajasthan; water-themed songs.
- Lavani: Maharashtra; song-dance to the dholki.
- Alha: Madhya Pradesh (also Braj, Awadhi, Bhojpuri); heroic ballads.
- Ovi: Maharashtra and Goa; women's songs, marriage, lullabies.

**Breakdown:** Occasion and theme: pandavani (epic), panihari (water), lavani (dance-song), ovi (women's life).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 438–440 (✅ owner 08). Teejanbai: Padma Shri 1988 (✅ Wikipedia and official record per owner; owner's PDF 1987 conflict noted), Padma Bhushan 2003, Padma Vibhushan 2019 (✅ Wikipedia).

**Links:** ART-2159, ART-2160, ART-2161

**Trap:** Dating Teejanbai's Padma Shri to 1987 (the local PDF). The official record and Wikipedia say 1988.

**Mains hook:** Folk music answers occasions (wedding, childbirth, water, war), not just states.

**Back:** *"Baul: Bengal sect-song. Pandavani: Bhima's epic. Lavani: dholki dance."*

### Flashcard ART-2159 — Folk music bank II: Maand, Powada, Khongjom Parba, Zikir, Sohar, Wanawan

**Front:** What are the regions, themes and markers of six more folk forms?

**Definition (learn):**
- Maand: Rajasthan; courtly praise of Rajput rulers.
- Powada: Maharashtra; heroic deeds, Shivaji.
- Khongjom Parba: Manipur; the Battle of Khongjom (1891); folk music as historical memory.
- Zikir: Assam; embodies the teaching of Islam.
- Sohar: Bihar; sung at childbirth.
- Wanawan: Jammu and Kashmir; auspicious wedding songs.

**Breakdown:** Ruler praise (maand), hero (powada), war memory (Khongjom), religion (zikir), birth (sohar), wedding (wanawan).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 438–441 (✅ owner 08).

**Links:** ART-2158, ART-2160, ART-2161

**Trap:** Presenting folk forms as "less developed" classical forms. The difference is function and transmission.

**Mains hook:** Folk music as historical memory (Khongjom).

**Back:** *"Maand praises rulers, Powada heroes, Sohar births, Khongjom battles."*

### Flashcard ART-2160 — Folk music bank III: Pai, Bhavageete, Mando, Tamang Selo, Ja-jin-ja, Nagaland songs, Dollu Kunitha

**Front:** What are the other folk forms in the owner's bank?

**Definition (learn):** Pai songs (Madhya Pradesh; rain, harvest, festivals); Bhavageete (Karnataka and Maharashtra; love, nature, philosophy); Mando (Goa; Indo-Western blend, social themes); Tamang Selo (Sikkim; love, sorrow, humour); Ja-jin-ja / Nyioga (Arunachal Pradesh; during and at the end of marriage ceremonies); Heliamleu, Neuleu, Hereileu, Hekaileu (Nagaland; dancing songs, legends, war songs and songs about oneself); Dollu Kunitha (Karnataka; a drum dance named after the dollu, performed by the Kuruba community).

**Breakdown:**
- Harvest and festival: pai.
- Philosophy: bhavageete.
- Marriage: ja-jin-ja/nyioga.
- Nagaland: four types.
- Drum dance: dollu kunitha.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 438–441 (✅ owner 08).

**Links:** ART-2158, ART-2159, ART-2161

**Trap:** Giving a Nagaland song type a region outside the list.

**Mains hook:** Occasion-based answers (marriage, harvest, war) rather than only state-matching.

**Back:** *"Pai for rain, Bhavageete for philosophy, Dollu for drums."*

### Flashcard ART-2161 — Classical and folk: function, not hierarchy

**Front:** How does the book distinguish classical from folk music?

**Definition (learn):** Classical traditions: codified theory, long apprenticeship, concert or temple settings. Folk traditions: community transmission, occasion-bound performance, regional language. The book's own phrase: "classical bound by tradition and folk as the free voice of the people." The line is a working distinction; classical and folk intermingle over time.

**Breakdown:**
- Classical: codified, apprenticed, concert or temple.
- Folk: communal, occasion-bound, regional language.
- Fusion: the book notes intermingling (PDF p. 444).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 438, 444 (✅ owner 08).

**Links:** ART-2158, ART-2159, ART-2160

**Trap:** Ranking folk below classical. The owner rejects a hierarchy.

**Mains hook:** The difference is of function and transmission, not of value.

**Back:** *"Classical bound by tradition; folk the free voice of the people."*

### Flashcard ART-2162 — Safeguarding: Sangeet Natak Akademi vs UNESCO ICH

**Front:** Which institutions recognise and safeguard music, and how do they differ?

**Definition (learn):** Sangeet Natak Akademi (SNA) recognises and awards classical musicians nationally (the national instrument). The Ustad Bismillah Khan Yuva Puraskar is for artists under 40 (owner). UNESCO ICH inscription is a separate international recognition. Never treat the two as one status.

**Breakdown:**
- SNA: national awards, awardee lists updated periodically.
- Yuva Puraskar: for artists under 40 (owner).
- UNESCO ICH: international.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ Owner-stated; cite an SNA awardee only with a dated SNA release (owner 08 §7, §13.10).

**Links:** ART-2156, ART-2151

**Trap:** Presenting an SNA award as a UNESCO status, or citing an awardee from memory.

**Mains hook:** Patronage becoming bureaucratic and generational.

**Back:** *"SNA recognises; UNESCO inscribes. Two different statuses."*

### Flashcard ART-2163 — 2026 Prelims: Carnatic–Hindustani raga equivalence (provisional)

**Front:** Is there a one-to-one equivalence between Carnatic and Hindustani ragas?

**Definition (learn):** No simple one-to-one equivalence is claimed in the owner's sources. The two systems classify differently: Carnatic by 72 melakartas with janya derivation; Hindustani by ten thaats (pedagogic) and ragas. A raga-by-raga equivalence claim must be checked against a source.

**Breakdown:**
- Carnatic: melakarta and janya.
- Hindustani: thaat and raga.
- Equivalence: not claimed.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2026 Prelims GS-I Q1 routed; provisional 2026 Set-A key held locally, answer not recorded here (✅ owner 08).

**Links:** ART-2113, ART-2115, ART-2118

**Trap:** Treating a melakarta and a thaat as equivalent. They are different classification systems.

**Mains hook:** Shared roots but different parent-scale systems.

**Back:** *"Melakarta is not thaat: no easy equivalence."*

## INDIAN ART AND CULTURE — Part 3: Dance, Theatre, Languages and Literature, Crafts and Religion

> Section note: Prefix `ART`, block `ART-3001` onward. Grounded in `upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Indian-Art-and-Culture/basic/` and `advanced/` owners 09–13 (filenames `09_` to `13_`). Tags: `(✅ owner NN)` = taken from the owner file; `(✅ Wikipedia: <page>)` = confirmed on a page fetched while building (Wikipedia is a secondary source); `⚠️` = owner-only, memory-only, unconfirmed, or an owner-vs-source conflict (flagged inside the card). Today is 1 November 2026; owner as-of dates are quoted as the owners give them.

### GROUP — Indian Dance

### Flashcard ART-3001 — Indian dance as Natya Shastra performance grammar (concept card)
**Front:** What is the core theoretical frame behind every Indian classical dance answer?
**Definition (learn):** Indian dance is analysed through the Natya Shastra's performance vocabulary: nritta (pure movement), nritya (expression), natya (drama), abhinaya, hasta/mudra, karana, rasa and bhava, with Lasya and Tandava as qualities. (✅ owner 09 §2, §13.5)
**Breakdown:**
- Movement layer: karana, nritta.
- Expression layer: hasta/mudra, abhinaya, bhava, rasa.
- Drama layer: natya.
- Institutional layer: SNA's eight classical forms (ART-3008), recognition status (ART-3020).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** No single date for the text. Owner 09 treats the Natya Shastra as a layered text. (✅ owner 09 §2)
**Links:** ART-3002, ART-3003, ART-3004, ART-3005, ART-3006, ART-3023.
**Trap:** Treating dance as a list of styles with no grammar. The precision test is always which component is being named.
**Mains hook:** Start any dance answer by naming the grammar, then the form. Qualification: the grammar is shared across forms, but each form applies it differently.
**Back:** *"Grammar first, style second: movement, expression, drama."*

### Flashcard ART-3002 — Natya Veda (the Natya Shastra as composite art)
**Front:** Why is the Natya Shastra described as the "fifth Veda" of performance?
**Definition (learn):** A text conventionally attributed to Bharata that presents drama as a composite art combining text (paathya), enactment (abhinaya), music (geet) and rasa. (✅ owner 09 §2)
**Breakdown:**
- Four elements: paathya, abhinaya, geet, rasa.
- The owner notes the text explicitly unites music, sculpture, poetry and drama as one art (✅ owner 09 advanced §2).
- Dating: owner 10 gives c. 200 BC–200 AD as the common range for the first treatise on dramaturgy (✅ owner 10 §4); owner 09 warns against a falsely exact composition date. ⚠️ The range is a conventional scholarly span, not a single date.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 10 attributes the Natya Veda's creation by Brahma from the four Vedas. (✅ owner 10 §4)
**Links:** ART-3001, ART-3026 (Lokadharmi/Natyadharmi), ART-3031 (staging prescriptions).
**Trap:** Quoting the Natya Shastra as dated to a single year. Use a span and say it is layered.
**Mains hook:** Use it to argue that dance, music and drama were analysed together, which is why sculpture-reading draws on dance vocabulary (ART-3023).
**Back:** *"Four limbs of one art: words, acting, song, rasa."*

### Flashcard ART-3003 — Lasya and Tandava
**Front:** What is the difference between Lasya and Tandava?
**Definition (learn):** Lasya is the graceful, expressive aspect of dance (bhava, rasa and abhinaya emphasis). Tandava is the rhythmic, forceful aspect, emphasising movement. (✅ owner 09 §2, PDF p. 459)
**Breakdown:**
- Lasya: graceful, expressive, emphasis on bhava.
- Tandava: rhythmic, vigorous, emphasis on movement.
- Both are qualities present in dance generally, not two separate dance systems.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Source page reference: PDF p. 459 (owner 09). (✅ owner 09)
**Links:** ART-3021 (gender trap), ART-3001, ART-3024 (tribhanga).
**Trap:** Mapping Lasya to women and Tandava to men. The owner calls this a qualitative pair, not a gender rule.
**Mains hook:** A reading of a temple dancer figure can be argued through Lasya or Tandava quality; name the quality, not the gender.
**Back:** *"Lasya flows, Tandava drives; both live in every dance."*

### Flashcard ART-3004 — Nritta, Natya and Nritya
**Front:** Distinguish nritta, natya and nritya as defined by Nandikeshvara's Abhinaya Darpana.
**Definition (learn):** Nritta is rhythmic movement without expression or story. Natya is dramatic or story representation. Nritya is expression and emotion conveyed through mudras. (✅ owner 09 §2, PDF p. 460)
**Breakdown:**
- Nritta: pure rhythm, steps and body patterns.
- Natya: drama, narration of a story.
- Nritya: expressive interpretation through hand gestures and face.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Source: Abhinaya Darpana by Nandikeshvara, as cited by owner 09 (PDF p. 460). (✅ owner 09)
**Links:** ART-3001, ART-3006, ART-3005.
**Trap:** Using the three terms as synonyms for "dance." Each names a different component.
**Mains hook:** The precision test in almost every dance question is whether the answer names the right component.
**Back:** *"Nritta moves, Nritya speaks, Natya tells."*

### Flashcard ART-3005 — The nine rasas
**Front:** List the nine rasas and their emotions.
**Definition (learn):** The nine emotional flavours used in Indian performance: Shringara (love), Roudra (anger), Bibhatsa (disgust), Veera (heroism), Shanta (peace), Haasya (laughter), Karuna (sorrow), Bhayanaka (horror), Adbhuta (wonder). (✅ owner 09 §2, PDF p. 460)
**Breakdown:**
- Shared framework with music (owner 08 cross-link).
- Used in dance as performance vocabulary; philosophical theory of rasa is owned by the Philosophy folder (owner 09 §11).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Nine rasas as listed; PDF p. 460. (✅ owner 09)
**Links:** ART-3001, ART-3002, ART-3006.
**Trap:** Presenting rasa as a doctrine essay in a dance answer. Use it as a vocabulary of expression.
**Mains hook:** Link rasa to the expressive face or gesture a sculpture shows, and say what the evidence cannot prove.
**Back:** *"Love, anger, disgust, heroism, peace, laughter, sorrow, horror, wonder."*

### Flashcard ART-3006 — Hasta/mudra and karana (the 108 trap)
**Front:** What is the difference between a mudra (hasta) and a karana, and what does the number 108 refer to?
**Definition (learn):** Hasta/mudra is a codified hand gesture. Karana is a coordinated unit of whole-body movement. The number 108 belongs to karanas in the Natya Shastra tradition, not to a universal count of mudras. (✅ owner 09 §2, §13.5)
**Breakdown:**
- Mudra: hand gesture, expression layer.
- Karana: whole-body movement unit, movement layer.
- Hasta-list counts vary by treatise. ⚠️ Owner gives no other count; do not supply one.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 108 karanas. (✅ owner 09)
**Links:** ART-3004, ART-3001, ART-3023 (shared gesture vocabulary with sculpture).
**Trap:** "108 mudras" is the examiner's wrong option. The correct link is karana.
**Mains hook:** Use mudra and karana when reading a figure in stone; name which one the figure shows.
**Back:** *"Hand speaks, body moves: mudra for the hand, karana for the body, 108 for karanas."*

### Flashcard ART-3007 — Guru-shishya parampara and sampradaya
**Front:** Why does Indian classical dance depend on guru-shishya transmission?
**Definition (learn):** Guru-shishya parampara is the master-disciple chain through which dance knowledge is passed on. Each lineage carries a distinct tradition called a sampradaya, which explains stylistic plurality within one named form. (✅ owner 09 §2, PDF p. 461)
**Breakdown:**
- Transmission: guru to shishya.
- Output: sampradayas (distinct lineages).
- Consequence: variation inside one form.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p. 461. (✅ owner 09)
**Links:** ART-3010 (Bharatanatyam revival), ART-3014 (Mohiniyattam), ART-3016 (Odissi gotipua/mahari).
**Trap:** Assuming one form has one fixed repertoire. Lineage is the reason it does not.
**Mains hook:** Explain why revival needed hereditary knowledge holders; the guru chain is also why revival displaced people (ART-3010).
**Back:** *"Knowledge flows guru to disciple; each lineage is a school."*

### Flashcard ART-3008 — SNA's eight classical dance forms
**Front:** Name the eight classical dance forms recognised by the Sangeet Natak Akademi.
**Definition (learn):** The standard SNA list of eight classical dance forms: Bharatanatyam, Kuchipudi, Kathakali, Mohiniyattam, Kathak, Odissi, Manipuri and Sattriya. (✅ owner 09 §3, PDF pp. 461-462)
**Breakdown:**
- South: Bharatanatyam (TN), Kuchipudi (AP), Kathakali and Mohiniyattam (Kerala).
- North and East: Kathak (North India), Odissi (Odisha), Manipuri (Manipur), Sattriya (Assam).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Eight forms; SNA list (a twentieth-century institutional act, owner 09 advanced §2). (✅ owner 09)
**Links:** ART-3009 (Ministry list), ART-3020 (recognition trap), ART-3010 to ART-3018 (form cards).
**Trap:** Treating the SNA list as an ancient classification. It is a modern recognition act.
**Mains hook:** Name the eight accurately and then state that the list is institutional, not timeless.
**Back:** *"Bharata, Kuchi, Kathak, Odi, Manipur, Sattri, Mohini, Kathakali."*

### Flashcard ART-3009 — The Ministry of Culture list of nine (Chhau)
**Front:** Which form makes the Ministry of Culture's list of nine, and why must it not be merged with the SNA list?
**Definition (learn):** Owner 09 notes a separate Ministry of Culture list of nine classical dance forms that includes Chhau. The two institutional lists differ and must be kept separate. (✅ owner 09 §3, §13.6)
**Breakdown:**
- SNA list: eight forms (ART-3008).
- Ministry list: nine forms including Chhau.
- Chhau is a regional form and is not absorbed into the eight-form SNA list (owner 09 advanced §Semantic control).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Count nine (Ministry list, per owner 09). ⚠️ The owner does not give the year of the Ministry list.
**Links:** ART-3008, ART-3020.
**Trap:** Answering "eight" when the question asks about the Ministry list, or "nine" when it asks about SNA.
**Mains hook:** Name which institution's list is meant; two lists can coexist without conflict.
**Back:** *"SNA says eight, Ministry says nine, Chhau is the extra."*

### Flashcard ART-3010 — Bharatanatyam: Sadir, devadasi tradition and revival
**Front:** How did Bharatanatyam move from the devadasi temple tradition to the modern stage?
**Definition (learn):** Bharatanatyam (Tamil Nadu) traces to Sadir, the solo temple-dancer performance also called Dashiattam. It was revived by E. Krishna Iyer after the devadasi system's decline and given global recognition by Rukmini Devi Arundale. (✅ owner 09 §4, PDF p. 462)
**Breakdown:**
- Origin: Sadir / Dashiattam, performed by devadasis.
- Decline: anti-nautch disruption (owner 09 §9, advanced §9).
- Revival: E. Krishna Iyer; Rukmini Devi Arundale.
- Technique: adavu (movement units), hasta (gestures), Bhavabhinaya (facial expression); performance opens with worship of Ganapati and Nataraja (PDF p. 463).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Revival and recognition are twentieth-century; no single year given in owner for Krishna Iyer's revival. ⚠️
**Links:** ART-3007, ART-3011, ART-3021, ART-3023 (sculpture-dance vocabulary).
**Trap:** Presenting Bharatanatyam as an unbroken ancient tradition. Owner says continuity and rupture must be held together.
**Mains hook:** Name the displaced practitioners (devadasis) alongside the revivers; a revival that omits them is incomplete.
**Back:** *"Sadir was temple solo; revival gave it a stage and removed its keepers."*

### Flashcard ART-3011 — Tanjore Quartet and the Bharatanatyam recital order
**Front:** Who defined the Bharatanatyam recital structure, and what is its item order?
**Definition (learn):** The Tanjore Quartet (Chinnaiah, Ponniah, Vadivelu, Sivanandam) defined the recital structure: Alarippu, Jatiswaram, Shabdam, Varnam, Padam, Jawali, Thillana. (✅ owner 09 §4, PDF pp. 462-463)
**Breakdown:**
- Opening: Alarippu.
- Middle: Jatiswaram, Shabdam, Varnam (the central item).
- Lyrical: Padam, Jawali.
- Close: Thillana.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Four named musicians, seven-item order. (✅ owner 09)
**Links:** ART-3010, ART-3008.
**Trap:** Reordering the items in a question about recital structure.
**Mains hook:** The recital order is a codified structure; the owner gives no date for the codification. ⚠️
**Back:** *"Alarippu opens, Varnam centres, Thillana closes."*

### Flashcard ART-3012 — Kuchipudi
**Front:** What is the origin and identity of Kuchipudi?
**Definition (learn):** Kuchipudi (Andhra Pradesh) is named after Kuchelapuram village, originally performed by travelling actors called Kusselavas and associated with Siddhendra. (✅ owner 09 §4, PDF p. 464)
**Breakdown:**
- Place: Kuchelapuram village, Andhra Pradesh.
- Performers: travelling actors (Kusselavas).
- Association: Siddhendra.
- Character: dance-drama, speech and enactment, not a temple solo.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Named village and performer group. (✅ owner 09)
**Links:** ART-3008, ART-3010 (contrast with Bharatanatyam).
**Trap:** Describing Kuchipudi as temple-solo dance like Bharatanatyam. Its origin is itinerant dance-drama.
**Mains hook:** Use Kuchipudi to show that classical forms had different social origins: travelling troupe versus temple solo.
**Back:** *"Kuchelapuram, travelling Kusselavas, Siddhendra: dance-drama on the road."*

### Flashcard ART-3013 — Kathakali
**Front:** What are the defining features of Kathakali?
**Definition (learn):** A Kerala dance-drama derived from Ramanattam, marked by gesture-led acting, elaborate make-up and costume, and an all-male troupe. (✅ owner 09 §5, §13.4, PDF pp. 466-468)
**Breakdown:**
- Song text: Attakkatha. Language: Manipravalam (Sanskrit plus Malayalam).
- Drums: chenda and maddala.
- Elemental symbol: sky or ether (owner).
- Patronage shift: with the breakdown of the feudal set-up, support changed (PDF p. 467).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Source PDF pp. 466-468. (✅ owner 09)
**Links:** ART-3014 (Mohiniyattam, same state), ART-3008, ART-3007.
**Trap:** Confusing Kathakali (male troupe dance-drama) with Mohiniyattam (female solo lasya), both from Kerala.
**Mains hook:** Use Kathakali for the feudal-patronage shift example in any performing-arts-and-patronage answer.
**Back:** *"Kathakali: Attakkatha song, all-male troupe, gesture speaks, sky element."*

### Flashcard ART-3014 — Mohiniyattam
**Front:** What identifies Mohiniyattam, and how does it differ from Kathakali?
**Definition (learn):** Kerala's solo lasya-oriented dance, the "dance of the enchantress," developed under Travancore rulers, notably Swathi Thirunal, and revived by V.N. Menon with Kalyani Amma. (✅ owner 09 §13.4, PDF pp. 469-470)
**Breakdown:**
- Movement: gentle footwork with no thumping; dominant Lasya; 40 basic movements (Atavakul/Atavus).
- Costume: white or off-white Kasavu saree with gold brocade; no elaborate facial make-up; ghungroo.
- Music and narration: Manipravalam; cymbals, veena, drums, flute.
- Element: air (owner).
- Nagabandha is a Manipuri mudra, not a Mohiniyattam feature (owner 09 §13.4).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 19th-century development under Vadivelu (owner). ⚠️ No further date given in the owner.
**Links:** ART-3013, ART-3003 (Lasya), ART-3017 (Nagabandha).
**Trap:** Attributing Nagabandha to Mohiniyattam. It belongs to Manipuri.
**Mains hook:** Use the Kasavu and the unthumped footwork as the discriminators; they separate it from Bharatanatyam and Kathakali.
**Back:** *"Mohini: Kasavu white, no thump, Lasya, air."*

### Flashcard ART-3015 — Kathak
**Front:** What defines Kathak and its footwork?
**Definition (learn):** The North Indian classical dance associated with storytelling, tatkar footwork and chakkars (spins). (✅ owner 09 §5, §13.4)
**Breakdown:**
- Movement: tatkar (footwork), chakkars (spins).
- Narrative: storytelling; temple and court histories (owner 09 §13.4).
- Contrast: spins and flat-footed tatkar against Bharatanatyam's bent-knee araimandi.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Region: North India. Owner gives no founding date. ⚠️ (owner 09 notes these six forms rely on chapter outline; verify specifics).
**Links:** ART-3008, ART-3010, ART-3001.
**Trap:** Giving Kathak Bharatanatyam's bent-knee stance. The footwork is the discriminator.
**Mains hook:** Name tatkar and chakkar when a question asks how Kathak differs from southern forms.
**Back:** *"Kathak: North, storytelling, tatkar feet, spinning chakkars."*

### Flashcard ART-3016 — Odissi
**Front:** What are the identifying features and history of Odissi?
**Definition (learn):** The Odisha classical dance whose earliest examples are linked to the Udayagiri-Khandagiri caves. Its name derives from Odra nritya in the Natya Shastra. (✅ owner 09 §13.4, PDF pp. 470-472)
**Breakdown:**
- Postures: tribhanga (three-bend) and chowk (masculine, hands spread); static lower body with torso movement; called "mobile sculpture."
- Sequence: Mangalacharan, Batu nritya, Pallavi, Tharijham, conclusion (Moksha or Trikhanda majura).
- Music: Hindustani accompaniment with manjira, pakhawaj, sitar, flute; Gita Govinda lyrics.
- Costume: Bomkai or Sambalpuri sarees; element: water.
- History: maharis (women practitioners) patronised by the Jain king Kharavela; after Vaishnavism, gotipuas (boys dressed as women) continued it; Nartala at royal courts; revived mid-20th century through Charles Fabri and Indrani Rahman.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Kharavela named as patron (owner). Mid-20th-century revival (owner, no exact year). ⚠️ Owner gives no year for revival.
**Links:** ART-3007, ART-3010, ART-3017, ART-3023, ART-3024.
**Trap:** Confusing tribhanga (Odissi) with the general sculptural three-bend; or equating gotipua with maharis (different periods).
**Mains hook:** Odissi shows how a dance survived by changing practitioners (maharis to gotipuas) and by revival; name both.
**Back:** *"Odissi: tribhanga, chowk, water, maharis then gotipuas."*

### Flashcard ART-3017 — Manipuri
**Front:** What identifies Manipuri dance?
**Definition (learn):** Manipur's classical dance centred on devotion over sensuality, with a veiled face and Nagabandha mudra. (✅ owner 09 §13.4, PDF pp. 473-474)
**Breakdown:**
- Origin: Shiva-Parvati dance with local Gandharvas (mythological).
- Devotional base: Vaishnava raslila; 15th-century Vaishnavism.
- Revival: Raja Bhag Chandra (18th century); Rabindranath Tagore introduced it at Santiniketan.
- Movement: lasya emphasised; facial expression less important; face veiled.
