**Definition (learn):** A brocade weave from Varanasi, Uttar Pradesh, using supplementary-weft zari. (✅ owner 12 §4, §13.5)
**Breakdown:**
- Region: Varanasi.
- Technique: supplementary weft, zari.
- Category: weave, not embroidery.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None. (✅ owner 12)
**Links:** ART-3103, ART-3101.
**Trap:** Calling Banarasi brocade embroidery.
**Mains hook:** Weave versus embroidery is a key discriminator.
**Back:** *"Banarasi: weave with zari, not stitch."*

### Flashcard ART-3103 — Chikankari: white-on-white embroidery
**Front:** What is Chikankari?
**Definition (learn):** White-on-white embroidery from Lucknow, Uttar Pradesh. (✅ owner 12 §4, §13.5)
**Breakdown:**
- Region: Lucknow.
- Technique: embroidery.
- Repertoire: white-on-white.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None. (✅ owner 12)
**Links:** ART-3102, ART-3104.
**Trap:** Confusing Chikankari with weave.
**Mains hook:** Embroidery, not weave.
**Back:** *"Chikankari: Lucknow, white on white, stitched."*

### Flashcard ART-3104 — Kantha: running-stitch quilting from reused cloth
**Front:** What is Kantha?
**Definition (learn):** Running-stitch quilting and embroidery from reused cloth, from Bengal. (✅ owner 12 §4, §13.5)
**Breakdown:**
- Region: Bengal.
- Technique: running stitch.
- Material: reused cloth; reuse is intrinsic.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None. (✅ owner 12)
**Links:** ART-3103, ART-3105.
**Trap:** Treating Kantha as a woven cloth.
**Mains hook:** Reuse is part of the form.
**Back:** *"Kantha: Bengal, reused cloth, running stitch."*

### Flashcard ART-3105 — Kalamkari: hand-drawn or block-printed cotton
**Front:** What is Kalamkari, and what PYQ trap does it carry?
**Definition (learn):** Hand-drawn or block-printed cotton textile from Andhra Pradesh and Telangana centres, using mordant and resist dyeing. CSE 2015 defines it as hand-painted cotton textile in South India. (✅ owner 12 §4, §9, §13.5)
**Breakdown:**
- Process: mordant and resist.
- Surface process; overlaps topic 07 painting (owner).
- PYQ: CSE 2015 definition route.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** CSE 2015. (✅ owner 12)
**Links:** ART-3106, ART-3123.
**Trap:** Calling it a woven fabric.
**Mains hook:** Process defines Kalamkari, not only the state.
**Back:** *"Kalamkari: cotton, hand-drawn or printed, mordant and resist."*

### Flashcard ART-3106 — Dhokra: lost-wax casting, not a single-state monopoly
**Front:** What is Dhokra and where is it made?
**Definition (learn):** Lost-wax non-ferrous metal casting by artisan communities in central and eastern India. It is not a single-state monopoly. (✅ owner 12 §4, §13.5)
**Breakdown:**
- Technique: lost-wax casting.
- Metal: non-ferrous.
- Geography: central and eastern artisan communities.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None. (✅ owner 12)
**Links:** ART-3091, ART-3090.
**Trap:** Tying Dhokra to one state.
**Mains hook:** Name the process, then the communities.
**Back:** *"Dhokra: lost-wax, many states, not one."*

### Flashcard ART-3107 — Glassware: from the Mahabharata to Brahmapuri
**Front:** What is the antiquity chain for Indian glassmaking?
**Definition (learn):** Glassmaking's earliest Indian reference is in the Mahabharata. Painted Grey Ware culture (Ganges Valley, 1000 BC) has glass evidence. A glass industry at Brahmapuri/Kolhapur (Maharashtra, 2nd century BC to 2nd century AD) produced lenticular beads. The Shatapatha Brahmana uses kanch/kaca for glass. (✅ owner 12 §4, §13.4, PDF p. 379)
**Breakdown:**
- Textual: Mahabharata; Shatapatha Brahmana (kanch/kaca).
- Archaeological: Painted Grey Ware, Ganges valley, 1000 BC; Brahmapuri/Kolhapur.
- Product: lenticular beads.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1000 BC; 2nd century BC to 2nd century AD. (✅ owner 12)
**Links:** ART-3091, ART-3108.
**Trap:** Treating the Mahabharata reference as archaeological proof of a 1000 BC glass industry. Owner keeps them separate.
**Mains hook:** Glass has the fullest antiquity chain in this owner.
**Back:** *"Glass: Mahabharata, Painted Grey Ware 1000 BC, Brahmapuri beads."*

### Flashcard ART-3108 — Ivory carving and wildlife regulation
**Front:** How should ivory carving be treated in a contemporary answer?
**Definition (learn):** Ivory carving belongs to historical craft study. Contemporary answers must acknowledge wildlife-protection and trade restrictions. New ivory production must not be presented as a safeguarding objective. (✅ owner 12 §4, §13.6)
**Breakdown:**
- Historical: a craft class.
- Contemporary: wildlife law.
- Rule: no safeguarding claim for new production.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None. (✅ owner 12)
**Links:** ART-3091, ART-3123.
**Trap:** Proposing ivory production as heritage revival.
**Mains hook:** Separate historical documentation from current legal compliance.
**Back:** *"Ivory: history yes, new production no."*

### Flashcard ART-3109 — Eri silk: production in Meghalaya (answerable fact)
**Front:** Where is Eri silk commonly produced, and by whom?
**Definition (learn):** Eri silk is commonly produced in Meghalaya, where the Garo, Khasi and Jaintia tribes are skilled weavers. They weave shawls, sarongs and mekhela chadors using both silk and cotton. (✅ owner 12 §13.5A, §13.9, PDF p. 391)
**Breakdown:**
- Fibre: Eri silk.
- State: Meghalaya.
- Communities: Garo, Khasi, Jaintia.
- Products: shawls, sarongs, mekhela chadors.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p. 391. (✅ owner 12)
**Links:** ART-3110, ART-3116.
**Trap:** Attributing Eri silk exclusively to Assam. Confusing it with Muga.
**Mains hook:** Answer the production half; do not answer certification without a source.
**Back:** *"Eri: Meghalaya, Garo-Khasi-Jaintia, silk and cotton shawls."*

### Flashcard ART-3110 — Muga silk (Assam) and the mekhela chador trap
**Front:** What is Muga, and why can the mekhela chador not identify a state alone?
**Definition (learn):** Muga is golden silk from Assam, used for sarees, mekhela chadors and other garments. The mekhela chador is woven in both Assam and Meghalaya. (✅ owner 12 §13.5A, PDF p. 390-391)
**Breakdown:**
- Muga: golden, Assam.
- Mekhela chador: Assam and Meghalaya.
- Eri: Meghalaya (ART-3109).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 390-391. (✅ owner 12)
**Links:** ART-3109, ART-3111.
**Trap:** "Mekhela chador means Assam." Not alone.
**Mains hook:** Use Muga for Assam and Eri for Meghalaya.
**Back:** *"Muga is golden Assam; mekhela chador is shared."*

### Flashcard ART-3111 — Manipur: Moirang Phee and Phanek
**Front:** Name the Manipuri silk fabric and the wraparound skirt.
**Definition (learn):** Moirang Phee is a silk fabric combining silk and cotton. The Phanek is a wraparound skirt worn by Manipuri women, woven in vibrant colours with traditional motifs. (✅ owner 12 §13.5A, PDF p. 390)
**Breakdown:**
- Fabric: Moirang Phee.
- Garment: Phanek.
- Motifs: traditional.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p. 390. (✅ owner 12)
**Links:** ART-3110, ART-3117.
**Trap:** Confusing Phanek with mekhela chador.
**Mains hook:** Named fabric plus named garment.
**Back:** *"Manipur: Moirang Phee fabric, Phanek skirt."*

### Flashcard ART-3112 — Arunachal Pradesh: Adi, Apatani, Nyishi and Monpa
**Front:** Which Arunachal communities are named for weaves and colours?
**Definition (learn):** Adi, Apatani and Nyishi for intricate weaves; Monpa for vibrant colours. Products: handwoven jackets, skirts, shawls, traditional dresses. (✅ owner 12 §13.5A, PDF p. 391)
**Breakdown:**
- Adi, Apatani, Nyishi: weaves.
- Monpa: colour.
- Products: jackets, skirts, shawls, dresses.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p. 391. (✅ owner 12)
**Links:** ART-3113, ART-3114.
**Trap:** Giving all four communities the same weave.
**Mains hook:** Four communities, two different design logics.
**Back:** *"Arunachal: Adi, Apatani, Nyishi weave; Monpa colour."*

### Flashcard ART-3113 — Mizoram: women weavers and the Puan
**Front:** Who weaves in Mizoram, and what is the Puan?
**Definition (learn):** Women are primarily responsible for weaving. The Puan is cloth with exclusive needlework in a stripe or arrow shape, worn by both men and women. (✅ owner 12 §13.5A, PDF p. 391)
**Breakdown:**
- Weavers: women.
- Puan: needlework stripe or arrow.
- Wearers: men and women.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p. 391. (✅ owner 12)
**Links:** ART-3112, ART-3122.
**Trap:** Saying the Puan is women-only.
**Mains hook:** The gendered labour is stated by the source and usable in livelihood answers.
**Back:** *"Mizoram: women weave the Puan, worn by all."*

### Flashcard ART-3114 — Nagaland: tribal motifs and natural dyes
**Front:** What identifies Nagaland's textiles?
**Definition (learn):** Each tribe has unique patterns and motifs reflecting cultural identity. Vibrant colours, intricate designs, natural dyes. (✅ owner 12 §13.5A, PDF pp. 390-391)
**Breakdown:**
- Identity: motif per tribe.
- Dyes: natural.
- Discriminator: motif as identity, not one state style.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 390-391. (✅ owner 12)
**Links:** ART-3112, ART-3123.
**Trap:** A single Nagaland style.
**Mains hook:** Motifs carry tribe identity.
**Back:** *"Nagaland: each tribe its motif, natural dyes."*

### Flashcard ART-3115 — Tripura: stripe and embroidery grammar
**Front:** What is the pattern grammar of Tripura textiles?
**Definition (learn):** Vertical and horizontal stripes with dispersed embroidery in various colours. (✅ owner 12 §13.5A, PDF p. 391)
**Breakdown:**
- Pattern: stripes, horizontal and vertical.
- Embroidery: dispersed.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p. 391. (✅ owner 12)
**Links:** ART-3114, ART-3111.
**Trap:** Calling it a named fabric; it is a pattern grammar.
**Mains hook:** Pattern grammar, not a fabric name.
**Back:** *"Tripura: stripes plus scattered embroidery."*

### Flashcard ART-3116 — Oeko-Tex and Eri silk: the bounded certification gap
**Front:** Can the owner answer the Oeko-Tex half of the 2026 Eri silk demand?
**Definition (learn):** Oeko-Tex is a private, voluntary textile-testing standard, a different instrument from a statutory GI. The folder holds no dated source on its criteria, scope, issuing standards or any producer's status. Answer only the production half and the certification-versus-GI distinction. (✅ owner 12 §13.5A, §13.9) ⚠️
**Breakdown:**
- Answerable: production fact (ART-3109).
- Bounded: Oeko-Tex details.
- Distinction: voluntary standard versus statutory GI (ART-3095).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None for Oeko-Tex in this folder. ⚠️ 2026 Q29 key is provisional.
**Links:** ART-3109, ART-3095.
**Trap:** Conflating a voluntary standard with a GI.
**Mains hook:** Say what the source supports and stop there.
**Back:** *"Oeko-Tex is a private standard; GI is statute. Do not mix them."*

### Flashcard ART-3117 — 2018 Prelims: Chapchar Kut and Thang-ta
**Front:** What does the 2018 Prelims Q22 test?
**Definition (learn):** The routed 2018 GS-I Q22 demand names Chapchar Kut, Khongjom Parba and Thang-ta as folk cultural traditions. The key was unavailable locally. (✅ owner 12 PYQ block) ⚠️
**Breakdown:**
- Items: Chapchar Kut, Khongjom Parba, Thang-ta.
- Thang-ta: sword-and-spear (ART-3051).
- Key: unavailable locally.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2018 Q22. (✅ owner 12)
**Links:** ART-3051, ART-3111.
**Trap:** Matching by state alone without the form.
**Mains hook:** Name the form and state.
**Back:** *"2018 Q22: Chapchar Kut, Khongjom Parba, Thang-ta, matched by form."*

### Flashcard ART-3118 — 2018 Prelims: Puthukuli, Sujni and Uppada textile pairs
**Front:** What does the 2018 Prelims Q54 test?
**Definition (learn):** Regional textile craft heritage pairs (Puthukuli, Sujni, Uppada) matched to their regions. The owner gives only the names; the key was unavailable locally. (✅ owner 12 PYQ block) ⚠️
**Breakdown:**
- Demand: pair each textile with its region.
- Owner gives no region detail here.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2018 Q54. (✅ owner 12)
**Links:** ART-3099, ART-3117.
**Trap:** Guessing a region without the source. ⚠️ Verify each pairing from a craft source before answering.
**Mains hook:** Name region and process, not only a label.
**Back:** *"2018 Q54: pair each textile with its place, verified."*

### Flashcard ART-3119 — GI as a narrow instrument: what it does not guarantee
**Front:** What does a GI tag not secure?
**Definition (learn):** A GI protects the exclusive use of a place-linked product name by authorised users, for goods only, for a renewable ten-year term. It does not secure income, working conditions, raw-material access or skill transmission. (✅ owner 12 §13.2, advanced §2)
**Breakdown:**
- Secures: name against misuse.
- Does not secure: income, labour, raw material, skill, design copying.
- Result: GI-tagged crafts can still decline.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None. (✅ owner 12)
**Links:** ART-3090, ART-3092, ART-3122.
**Trap:** Presenting GI as comprehensive safeguarding.
**Mains hook:** "Necessary but strikingly narrow" is the owner's thesis.
**Back:** *"GI guards the name; livelihood needs other institutions."*

### Flashcard ART-3120 — 2024 Q13: Industrial Revolution and handicrafts (adjacent boundary)
**Front:** How should a craft answer handle the 2024 Q13 deindustrialisation question?
**Definition (learn):** 2024 Q13 asks how far the Industrial Revolution in England was responsible for the decline of Indian handicrafts and cottage industries (250 words). Primary owner is Modern History and Economy. This topic supplies the craft objects and classes only. (✅ owner 12 §7, §13.7, advanced §8)
**Breakdown:**
- Supplied: glassware, cloth, metal crafts, medieval production organisation.
- Not supplied: tariff policy, revenue, deindustrialisation verdict.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2024 Q13 (verbatim in owner). (✅ owner 12)
**Links:** ART-3091, ART-3121, ART-3122.
**Trap:** Answering the causal economic debate from craft description.
**Mains hook:** State the boundary explicitly.
**Back:** *"Q13: craft objects from here, causal verdict from Modern History."*

### Flashcard ART-3121 — 2024 Q20: cultural diversity and marginality (adjacent boundary)
**Front:** How should a craft answer handle the 2024 Q20 diversity-marginality question?
**Definition (learn):** 2024 Q20 asks for a critical analysis of high correlation between cultural diversity and socio-economic marginality (250 words). Primary owner is Indian Society. This topic supplies one instance: tribal and rural craft communities. (✅ owner 12 §7, §13.7)
**Breakdown:**
- Supplied: one craft-community instance.
- Not supplied: the correlation argument.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2024 Q20 (verbatim in owner). (✅ owner 12)
**Links:** ART-3123, ART-3120.
**Trap:** Offering the sociological verdict from craft evidence.
**Mains hook:** Use the craft case as illustration only.
**Back:** *"Q20: craft as one example, the verdict belongs to Indian Society."*

### Flashcard ART-3122 — Production organisation before the market: karkhanas and dadni
**Front:** How was medieval craft production organised?
**Definition (learn):** Satish Chandra records artisans, master-craftsmen, karkhanas, putting-out or dadni relations and export textiles. R.S. Sharma records Harappan bead, shell, pottery, metal and textile skills and Sangam-era cotton and silk trade. (✅ owner 12 §4, §13.6, PDF pp. 317-327, 97-103, 250-260)
**Breakdown:**
- Harappan: bead, shell, pottery, metal, textile.
- Sangam: cotton and silk trade.
- Medieval: karkhanas, dadni, export textiles.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner page references. (✅ owner 12)
**Links:** ART-3119, ART-3120, ART-3113.
**Trap:** Imagining craft as household-only production.
**Mains hook:** Craft was organised through intermediaries and markets for centuries.
**Back:** *"Craft was never only household: karkhanas and dadni."*

### Flashcard ART-3123 — Tribal craft: community-held technique and appropriation
**Front:** How should tribal art and craft be argued without freezing communities?
**Definition (learn):** Tribal textiles and crafts are community-held techniques: fibre, weaver group and garment named together. Name producing communities, recognise individual makers and design change, and note appropriation: a GI protects a name, so motifs can be copied outside it. (✅ owner 12 §13.6, §9, advanced §9)
**Breakdown:**
- Named communities: Garo, Khasi, Jaintia, Adi, Apatani, Nyishi, Monpa, Mizo.
- Design change: continuous adaptation.
- Appropriation: motif copying outside the protected name.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-only analysis. (✅ owner 12)
**Links:** ART-3109, ART-3113, ART-3119, ART-3121.
**Trap:** "Tribal craft is anonymous timeless expression."
**Mains hook:** Name the community and the limit of legal protection.
**Back:** *"Name the people, track the change, mind the copying."*

### GROUP — Religion, Philosophy and Cultural Synthesis

### Flashcard ART-3124 — Religion and philosophy as cultural frameworks (concept card)
**Front:** Why does this owner treat religion and philosophy as culture, not theology?
**Definition (learn):** Religion and philosophy are studied as frameworks that shape expressive form, patronage, language, institutions and practice. Doctrinal theology belongs to the Philosophy folder. (✅ owner 13 §1, §11)
**Breakdown:**
- Bhakti and Sufi traditions: ART-3138 to ART-3174.
- Philosophy and monuments: ART-3177 to ART-3181.
- Smaller communities: ART-3182 to ART-3186.
- Festivals and ICH: ART-3187, ART-3189.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-only synthesis. (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3125, ART-3138, ART-3177, ART-3189.
**Trap:** Writing a doctrine essay in a culture answer.
**Mains hook:** Frame the answer as cultural formation and qualify with limits.
**Back:** *"Culture first: form, patron, language, place, limit."*

### Flashcard ART-3125 — Purushartha: the four goals
**Front:** What are the four purusharthas?
**Definition (learn):** Artha (wealth), Dharma (social regulation), Kama (physical pleasure and love), Moksha (salvation). (✅ owner 13 §2, PDF pp. 860-861)
**Breakdown:**
- Artha: Arthashastra.
- Dharma: Dharmasastra.
- Kama: Kamasutra.
- Moksha: darshana texts.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 860-861. (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3126, ART-3177.
**Trap:** Reducing purusharthas to "four stages of life."
**Mains hook:** Use the frame to explain how a temple shows wealth, duty, desire and release together.
**Back:** *"Artha, Dharma, Kama, Moksha: four goals, four texts."*

### Flashcard ART-3126 — The Shada Darshanas (six orthodox schools)
**Front:** Name the six orthodox schools and the criterion for "orthodox."
**Definition (learn):** Samkhya, Yoga, Nyaya, Vaisheshika, Mimamsa and Vedanta are the six orthodox schools that accept Vedic authority. They are schools of inquiry within one tradition, not competing religions. (✅ owner 13 §2, §9, PDF p. 861)
**Breakdown:**
- Orthodox: Vedic authority accepted.
- Heterodox: Charvaka and Buddhist/Jain schools (owner 13 advanced; ART-3130).
- Common frame: purushartha.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p. 861. (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3127, ART-3128, ART-3129, ART-3131, ART-3130.
**Trap:** "Six competing religions."
**Mains hook:** Six schools share a Vedic-accepting frame.
**Back:** *"Six who accept the Veda: Samkhya, Yoga, Nyaya, Vaisheshika, Mimamsa, Vedanta."*

### Flashcard ART-3127 — Samkhya: purusha, prakriti and three pramanas
**Front:** What are Samkhya's core categories and its means of knowledge?
**Definition (learn):** Classical Samkhya distinguishes purusha and prakriti. Three means of knowledge: Pratyaksha (perception), Anumana (inference), Shabda (testimony). (✅ owner 13 §4, §13.6, PDF pp. 862-863)
**Breakdown:**
- Ontology: purusha, prakriti.
- Pramanas: perception, inference, testimony.
- Attribution: Kapila (traditional).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 862-863. (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3126, ART-3128.
**Trap:** Assigning the extant Samkhya Sutra to Kapila as settled fact. Owner says the text is late.
**Mains hook:** Separate tradition from textual date.
**Back:** *"Purusha and prakriti; perception, inference, testimony; Kapila only by tradition."*

### Flashcard ART-3128 — Yoga: Patanjali's Yogasutra
**Front:** What does Patanjali's Yogasutra systematise?
**Definition (learn):** A layered text of debated date, commonly placed in the early centuries BCE/CE. It systematises control of citta-vritti (mental fluctuations) and an eight-limbed practice. (✅ owner 13 §4, §13.6)
**Breakdown:**
- Aim: control of citta-vritti.
- Practice: eight limbs.
- Date: debated, early centuries BCE/CE.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Early centuries BCE/CE (owner). ⚠️ Do not fix a precise date (owner 13 §9).
**Links:** ART-3127, ART-3126.
**Trap:** Reducing all Indian yoga to "union of individual and universal spirit."
**Mains hook:** Yoga as a system of practice, not one metaphysical formula.
**Back:** *"Yogasutra: stop the mind's waves, eight limbs, date debated."*

### Flashcard ART-3129 — Mimamsa: salvation through Vedic ritual
**Front:** What does Mimamsa hold about salvation, and who are its exponents?
**Definition (learn):** Salvation is attained through performance of Vedic ritual with proper understanding. Sources: Sutras of Jaimini, Sabar Swami, Kumarila Bhatta. The owner notes it legitimised social divisions and reinforced Brahman dominance. (✅ owner 13 §13.6A, PDF p. 866)
**Breakdown:**
- Means: Vedic ritual.
- Exponents: Jaimini, Sabar Swami, Kumarila Bhatta.
- Social effect: legitimised divisions (owner's claim).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p. 866. (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3126, ART-3130.
**Trap:** Calling Mimamsa devotional.
**Mains hook:** Ritual authority, not devotion.
**Back:** *"Mimamsa: ritual done right, Jaimini to Kumarila."*

### Flashcard ART-3130 — Charvaka or Lokayata: the materialist school
**Front:** What does Charvaka teach, and what does the 2007 Mains note ask?
**Definition (learn):** The heterodox materialist school rejects the supernatural, God, Brahman and salvation, and recognises four elements: fire, earth, water, air. The 2007 Mains demand "Write a short note on Charvaka" is reproduced in the source (PDF p. 873). (✅ owner 13 §13.6A, PDF pp. 870-873)
**Breakdown:**
- Ontology: four elements.
- Rejects: God, Brahman, salvation, supernatural.
- PYQ: 2007 short note.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 870-873. (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3126, ART-3129.
**Trap:** Listing Charvaka among the six orthodox schools.
**Mains hook:** Charvaka is the materialist counterpoint.
**Back:** *"Charvaka: four elements, no soul, no god."*

### Flashcard ART-3131 — Vedanta and the Prasthanatrayi
**Front:** What are Vedanta's core claims and its three primary sources?
**Definition (learn):** Vedanta means the end of the Vedas and upholds the Upanishadic philosophy. Brahman is reality; the world is unreal or maya; atman is similar to Brahman; liberation comes through realising Brahman. Oldest foundational text: Brahmasutra of Badarayana. Prasthanatrayi: Upanishads, Bhagavad Gita, Brahma Sutras. (✅ owner 13 §13.6A, PDF pp. 867-868)
**Breakdown:**
- Brahman: reality.
- Maya: the world's status in Advaita reading.
- Karma and rebirth supported.
- Liberation: realisation of Brahman.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 867-868. (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3132, ART-3133, ART-3134.
**Trap:** Saying all Vedanta is Advaita.
**Mains hook:** Name the sub-school, then its Brahman and means.
**Back:** *"Vedanta: Brahman is real, Prasthanatrayi is the base."*

### Flashcard ART-3132 — Advaita: Shankaracharya, jnana and the four mathas
**Front:** What does Advaita hold about Brahman and salvation, and who founded its monastic order?
**Definition (learn):** Advaita holds Brahman is without attributes (nirguna). Knowledge (jnana) is the main means to salvation, through knowing the identity of Atman and Brahman. Adi Shankaracharya (8th century AD, probably from Kerala) established the Dashanami Sampradaya and four mathas: Dwaraka (west), Puri (east), Sringeri (south), Badrikashrama (north). Jivanmukti: liberation in this life. (✅ owner 13 §13.6A, PDF pp. 836, 867-868)
**Breakdown:**
- Brahman: attributeless.
- Means: jnana.
- Liberation: Jivanmukti, in this life.
- Order: four mathas; Upadesasahasri.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 8th century AD; four mathas as listed. (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3131, ART-3133, ART-3134.
**Trap:** Giving Shankara a devotional means of salvation.
**Mains hook:** Shankara's Brahman is reached by knowledge, which produced a monastic order.
**Back:** *"Shankara: Brahman without attributes, knowledge saves, four mathas."*

### Flashcard ART-3133 — Vishishtadvaita: Ramanuja, Brahman with attributes, devotion
**Front:** How does Ramanuja's Vishishtadvaita differ from Shankara on Brahman and salvation?
**Definition (learn):** Ramanuja (11th-12th century) holds Brahman with attributes, against Shankaracharya. Love and devotion, not knowledge alone, are the means of salvation. Ramanujacharya influenced Hoysala king Vishnuvardhana's conversion from Jainism to Vaishnavism (owner cites PDF p. 105; topic 03). (✅ owner 13 §13.4A, §13.6A, PDF pp. 867-868)
**Breakdown:**
- Brahman: with attributes.
- Means: devotion.
- Saguna school (ART-3139).
- Cultural result: Chennakeshava at Belur (owner 13 §13.6A).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 11th-12th century (owner). (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3132, ART-3134, ART-3139, ART-3180.
**Trap:** "Part Advaita, part Dvaita." Reject.
**Mains hook:** Philosophy and devotional practice line up in Ramanuja.
**Back:** *"Ramanuja: Brahman with attributes, devotion saves, Belur follows."*

### Flashcard ART-3134 — Dvaita: Madhvacharya, Brahma Sampradaya, and the Pushtimarg trap
**Front:** What does Madhvacharya's school hold, and why must Pushtimarg not be attributed to him?
**Definition (learn):** Madhvacharya founded the Dvaita Vedanta school and the Brahma Sampradaya, which is associated with Vishnu as Para-Brahma. Gaudiya Vaishnavism and ISKCON belong to this sampradaya. Pushtimarg was founded by Vallabhacharya (c. 1500 AD). (✅ owner 13 §13.6A, PDF pp. 744, 856)
**Breakdown:**
- Dvaita: dualism.
- Lineage: Brahma Sampradaya, Gaudiya, ISKCON.
- Pushtimarg: Vallabhacharya, c. 1500 AD.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** c. 1500 AD (owner). (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3132, ART-3133, ART-3146, ART-3145.
**Trap:** Pairing Madhva with Pushtimarg, a recorded local PYQ trap.
**Mains hook:** Give the sampradaya chain, not an invented soteriology.
**Back:** *"Madhva: Brahma line, ISKCON. Vallabha: Pushtimarg, c. 1500."*

### Flashcard ART-3135 — Nayanars (63) and Alvars (12): canons and compilations
**Front:** What are the canonical numbers and compilations for the Tamil Shaiva and Vaishnava saints?
**Definition (learn):** Nayanars are the canonical 63 Tamil Shaiva saints. Alvars are the canonical 12 Tamil Vaishnava saints, whose hymns were compiled as the Nalayira Divya Prabandham (4,000 verses). Tevaram, compiled by Appar, Sambandar and Sundarar in the 10th century AD, is the Nayanar hymn corpus. (✅ owner 13 §4, §13.4, §13.4C, PDF pp. 829-831)
**Breakdown:**
- Nayanars: 63; Shaiva; Tevaram.
- Alvars: 12; Vaishnava; Nalayira Divya Prabandham.
- Composition language: Tamil, not Telugu.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Alvars c. 6th-9th centuries (owner, regional frame). (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3136, ART-3137, ART-3138, ART-3180.
**Trap:** Saying the Alvar or Nayanar hymns were in Telugu.
**Mains hook:** Devotion in the spoken tongue was itself a cultural contribution.
**Back:** *"63 Nayanars, 12 Alvars, 4,000 verses: all in Tamil."*

### Flashcard ART-3136 — Nayanar versus Alvar: a comparison pair
**Front:** Compare the Nayanars and Alvars.
**Definition (learn):** Nayanars (Shaiva, 63) and Alvars (Vaishnava, 12) both preached devotion as the path to salvation in Tamil and opposed Buddhist and Jain asceticism. Only Andal is a woman among the Alvars; three women are among the Nayanars. (✅ owner 13 §4, §13.4, §13.4B)
**Breakdown:**
- Deity: Shiva vs Vishnu.
- Number: 63 vs 12.
- Canon: Tevaram vs Nalayira Divya Prabandham.
- Women: Andal (Alvar); three Nayanar women (ART-3157).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As listed. (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3135, ART-3137, ART-3157.
**Trap:** Mixing the two canons or their deities.
**Mains hook:** Use the pair to show Shaiva and Vaishnava devotion as parallel.
**Back:** *"Nayanar: Shiva, 63. Alvar: Vishnu, 12."*

### Flashcard ART-3137 — Andal: the only woman among the Alvars
**Front:** Who is Andal, and what is the anachronistic shortcut to avoid?
**Definition (learn):** Andal is the only woman among the twelve Alvars. She composed Thiruppavai and Nachiyar Thirumozhi in pure Tamil, 8th century. Her hymns are in the Nalayira Divya Prabandham. (✅ owner 13 §4, §13.4B)
**Breakdown:**
- Role: sole woman Alvar.
- Works: Thiruppavai, Nachiyar Thirumozhi.
- Date: 8th century (owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 8th century (owner). (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3135, ART-3136, ART-3160.
**Trap:** Calling her "Meera of the South." The owner rejects it as anachronistic.
**Mains hook:** Her presence is real and rare; the roster cannot measure all Bhakti gender participation.
**Back:** *"Andal: the one woman in twelve, Tamil verses, not Meera."*

### Flashcard ART-3138 — Bhakti's four causes: a causal model
**Front:** What four conditions does the source give for the rise of Bhakti?
**Definition (learn):** Reaction against Brahmanical orthodoxy and caste rigidity; promotion of equality for marginalised communities; stagnation of Buddhism and Jainism creating space for devotional religion; royal patronage (for example, Chola support for Shaivism in Tamil Nadu). (✅ owner 13 §4, §13.4, PDF p. 829)
**Breakdown:**
- Social critique: caste, orthodoxy.
- Inclusion: marginalised communities.
- Space: Buddhist and Jain decline.
- Patronage: Chola-Shaivism.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p. 829. (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3135, ART-3139, ART-3160, ART-3180.
**Trap:** Dropping royal patronage as incidental. Owner names it as causal.
**Mains hook:** Argue a four-factor model, then the limit.
**Back:** *"Four causes: critique, inclusion, space left, patron."*

### Flashcard ART-3139 — Saguna versus Nirguna Bhakti
**Front:** What is the difference between saguna and nirguna Bhakti?
**Definition (learn):** Saguna worships God with form and attributes. Nirguna worships God as formless and without attributes, emphasising knowledge over rituals, rejecting idol worship and opposing Brahmin supremacy. (✅ owner 13 §2, §13.4A, PDF pp. 828, 835)
**Breakdown:**
- Saguna: Rama-Bhakti, Krishna-Bhakti; Ramanuja, Ramananda, Chaitanya, Tulsidas, Surdas named.
- Nirguna: Kabir, Guru Nanak, Dadu Dayal, Ravidas named.
- Nirguna blended Vaishnava Bhakti, Nathpanthi and Sufi traditions (PDF p. 835).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 828-835. ⚠️ A later classification applied to earlier material (owner).
**Links:** ART-3140, ART-3133, ART-3144, ART-3145.
**Trap:** A monolithic Bhakti description.
**Mains hook:** Use the two schools to show internal variety.
**Back:** *"Saguna: form and image. Nirguna: formless and anti-ritual."*

### Flashcard ART-3140 — Kabir: Varanasi, dohas and the Ramananda link
**Front:** What do the sources give on Kabir?
**Definition (learn):** Born in Varanasi (PDF p. 291). Wrote in Hindi and Sant Bhasha. One of three key Nirguna figures (PDF p. 835). Famous for dohas used by common people (PDF p. 687). Disciple of Ramananda and part of the Ramanandi Sampradaya, later founding the Kabirpanthi sect (PDF pp. 743-744). Included in the Adi Granth as one of two Muslim Bhagats with Baba Farid (PDF p. 657). Songs exemplify the Suphi strand in Baul Sangeet (PDF p. 438). (✅ owner 13 §13.4A)
**Breakdown:**
- Place and language: Varanasi; Hindi, Sant Bhasha.
- Form: doha.
- Lineage: Ramananda, then Kabirpanthi.
- Text: Adi Granth.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 291, 657, 687, 743-744. (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3139, ART-3141, ART-3142, ART-3081, ART-3143, ART-3190.
**Trap:** Attributing the Bijak to Dadu Dayal. A recorded local PYQ trap. Also do not present the Ramananda link without the Kabirpanthi break.
**Mains hook:** Use his dohas as the clearest case of devotional verse entering speech.
**Back:** *"Kabir: Varanasi doha, Ramananda's disciple, Kabirpanthi after."*

### Flashcard ART-3141 — Guru Nanak, Sant Bhasha and the Adi Granth
**Front:** What is Guru Nanak's place in the Nirguna literary record?
**Definition (learn):** Named as a key Nirguna figure (PDF p. 835). His dohas, with Kabir's and Dadu's, are in the Adi Granth, a very composite text written in Gurmukhi (PDF p. 695). Sant Bhasha draws on Punjabi, Apabhramsa, Hindi, Braj, Sanskrit, Khariboli and Persian (PDF p. 657). (✅ owner 13 §13.4A, §13.4)
**Breakdown:**
- Role: Nirguna figure; literary contribution.
- Text: Adi Granth (Gurmukhi).
- Language: Sant Bhasha as synthesis.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 657, 695, 835. (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3140, ART-3142, ART-3161.
**Trap:** Writing Sikh doctrine. The owner owns only the literary contribution.
**Mains hook:** The Adi Granth is cross-regional and cross-confessional circulation without a merged religion.
**Back:** *"Nanak: dohas in the Adi Granth, Gurmukhi, Sant Bhasha."*

### Flashcard ART-3142 — Dadu Dayal
**Front:** What is the owner's evidence on Dadu Dayal?
**Definition (learn):** The third named Nirguna figure (PDF p. 835). His dohas are in the Adi Granth (PDF p. 695). The source gives little beyond naming. (✅ owner 13 §13.4A)
**Breakdown:**
- Role: Nirguna, third figure.
- Text: dohas in the Adi Granth.
- Limit: no biography in the owner.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 695, 835. (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3140, ART-3141, ART-3139.
**Trap:** Attributing the Bijak to Dadu. The Bijak is Kabir's.
**Mains hook:** Name only what the source states.
**Back:** *"Dadu: Nirguna third, dohas in the Adi Granth, Bijak is not his."*

### Flashcard ART-3143 — Ravidas (Raidas) and Mirabai's preceptor
**Front:** What do the sources say about Ravidas and Mirabai?
**Definition (learn):** Born in Varanasi (PDF p. 291). Named among the Bhagats in the Adi Granth (PDF p. 657). Guru Raidas was the preceptor of Mirabai (PDF p. 837). (✅ owner 13 §13.4A)
**Breakdown:**
- Birth: Varanasi.
- Text: Adi Granth Bhagat.
- Link: preceptor of Mirabai (source statement).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 291, 657, 837. (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3154, ART-3141, ART-3140.
**Trap:** Presenting the preceptor link as a dated relationship. The owner says it is the source's statement.
**Mains hook:** Use it as a caste-crossing link with a qualifier.
**Back:** *"Ravidas: Varanasi Bhagat, Mirabai's guru, a source's claim."*

### Flashcard ART-3144 — Tulsidas and the Ramcharitmanas (Awadhi)
**Front:** What is Tulsidas's contribution and its UNESCO status?
**Definition (learn):** Tulsidas, a Vaishnava devotee of Rama of Awadh, wrote Ramcharitmanas in Awadhi. Other works: Vinaya Patrika, Krishna Gitavali, Kavitavali, Gitavali, Sahitya Ratna, Dohavali, Vairagya Sandipani. He belonged to the Ramanandi sect. Ramcharitmanas was inscribed on UNESCO's Memory of the World register in 2024 (owner). (✅ owner 13 §13.4A, PDF pp. 620, 688, 743)
**Breakdown:**
- Language: Awadhi.
- Text: Ramcharitmanas, epic retelling.
- Sect: Ramanandi.
- MoW: 2024 (owner, manuscript register year).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2024 MoW (owner, PDF p. 620). ⚠️ MoW 2024 owner-only.
**Links:** ART-3139, ART-3085, ART-3083.
**Trap:** Reading 2024 as the composition date.
**Mains hook:** A Sanskrit epic made available in a spoken dialect.
**Back:** *"Tulsidas: Awadhi Ramayana, MoW 2024, not composed in 2024."*

### Flashcard ART-3145 — Surdas, Braj Bhasha and the Ashtachhap (1565)
**Front:** What is Surdas's contribution and the Ashtachhap's significance?
**Definition (learn):** Surdas wrote Sur Sagar in Braj Bhasha, on Krishna's infancy and adolescent affairs with the gopis (PDF p. 688). He is the most well-known of the Ashtachhap, eight poet-disciples of Vallabhacharya who sang Haveli Sangeet, established 1565 with Pushtimarg (PDF p. 837). (✅ owner 13 §13.4A, §13.4C)
**Breakdown:**
- Language: Braj Bhasha.
- Work: Sur Sagar.
- Institution: Ashtachhap, Pushtimarg, 1565.
- Each member assigned part of daily worship.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1565 AD (owner). (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3134, ART-3144, ART-3161.
**Trap:** Merging the eight poets into one name.
**Mains hook:** A liturgical division of labour, institutional rather than spontaneous.
**Back:** *"Surdas: Sur Sagar, Braj, one of eight, 1565."*

### Flashcard ART-3146 — Chaitanya Mahaprabhu: Gaudiya Vaishnavism, Jatra and Gaudiya Nritya
**Front:** What performance forms does the owner link to Chaitanya?
**Definition (learn):** Chaitanya promoted Gaudiya Vaishnavism, associated with the Brahma Sampradaya (ISKCON's lineage). He is said to have revived Gaudiya Nritya (PDF p. 479) and initiated Jatra, open-air folk theatre of eastern India, to propagate his teachings in rural Bengal (PDF p. 531). (✅ owner 13 §13.4A, PDF pp. 479, 531, 744)
**Breakdown:**
- Sect: Gaudiya, Brahma Sampradaya.
- Dance: Gaudiya Nritya (said).
- Theatre: Jatra (said).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 479, 531, 744. ⚠️ "It is said" is the source's hedge.
**Links:** ART-3134, ART-3034, ART-3161.
**Trap:** Stating the dance and theatre claims as fact. Keep the hedge.
**Mains hook:** Bhakti created performance forms as well as texts.
**Back:** *"Chaitanya: Gaudiya line, Jatra and Gaudiya Nritya, said to be his."*

### Flashcard ART-3147 — Basavanna, the Lingayat tradition and the Anubhava Mantapa
**Front:** What did Basavanna found, and where is the Anubhava Mantapa?
**Definition (learn):** Basavanna (12th century CE), founder of the Lingayat tradition, emphasised worship of the Ishtalinga, an egalitarian society, rejection of caste and elaborate ritual. Scripture: Vachanas. He encouraged post-puberty marriage and remarriage and women's participation. He established the Anubhava Mantapa at Basavakalyan (Bidar district), led by Allama Prabhu, recognised as the birthplace of Vachana literature. (✅ owner 13 §13.4A, PDF pp. 832-834)
**Breakdown:**
- Tradition: Lingayat, Karnataka, Kannada.
- Ishtalinga: symbol of formless divine.
- Vachanas.
- Anubhava Mantapa, Basavakalyan.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 12th century CE; Basavakalyan, Bidar district (owner). (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3148, ART-3139, ART-3161.
**Trap:** Forcing the Ishtalinga into a Saguna/Nirguna binary.
**Mains hook:** Egalitarian rhetoric, institutionally embedded in one mantapa.
**Back:** *"Basavanna: Ishtalinga, Vachanas, Anubhava Mantapa, 12th century."*

### Flashcard ART-3148 — Trap: Lingayats are not Veerashaivas
**Front:** Are Lingayats and Veerashaivas the same community?
**Definition (learn):** No. Traditional Veerashaiva is said to pre-date Basavanna, traces its origin to the Panchacharyas (Renuka, Daruka, Ghantakarna, Dhenukarna, Vishvakarna), accepts some Vedic tradition and may acknowledge varna. (✅ owner 13 §13.4A, §13.9)
**Breakdown:**
- Lingayat: Basavanna's tradition.
- Veerashaiva: older, Panchacharya origin.
- Do not project Basavanna's anti-caste stance onto Veerashaivism.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Panchacharyas named. (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3147, ART-3161.
**Trap:** Using the two as synonyms.
**Mains hook:** Keep the two traditions apart when discussing caste.
**Back:** *"Lingayat is Basavanna; Veerashaiva is older, Panchacharya-rooted."*

### Flashcard ART-3149 — Jnaneshwar: 1275-96, the oldest Marathi work
**Front:** What is Jnaneshwar's contribution?
**Definition (learn):** Dates 1275-96. The oldest known work in Marathi is his, from the 13th century. Credited with beginning kirtan in Maharashtra. Wrote a detailed commentary on the Bhagavad Gita in Marathi (PDF p. 696). (✅ owner 13 §13.4A, PDF pp. 696, 743)
**Breakdown:**
- Dates: 1275-96.
- Work: Marathi Gita commentary.
- Performance: kirtan credited.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1275-96. (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3150, ART-3152, ART-3161.
**Trap:** Treating "credited with" as proof.
**Mains hook:** The oldest Marathi work is a survival statement.
**Back:** *"Jnaneshwar: 1275-96, Gita in Marathi, kirtan credited."*

### Flashcard ART-3150 — Namdev: abhangas and the Adi Granth
**Front:** What is Namdev's place in the Marathi and Punjabi record?
**Definition (learn):** Dates 1270-1350 (owner). Wrote many abhangas on devotion (PDF p. 696). Named among the Bhagats in the Adi Granth (PDF p. 657), a Marathi saint in the Punjabi canon. Abhanga is a Maharashtra song form in praise of Vithobha (PDF p. 446). (✅ owner 13 §13.4A)
**Breakdown:**
- Dates: 1270-1350.
- Form: abhanga.
- Text: Adi Granth.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1270-1350. (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3149, ART-3152, ART-3190.
**Trap:** Treating the PYQ statement on Namdev's monotheism as the book's assertion (PDF p. 851).
**Mains hook:** A Marathi saint in the Punjabi canon shows cross-regional circulation.
**Back:** *"Namdev: 1270-1350, abhangas, in the Adi Granth."*

### Flashcard ART-3151 — Eknath: a conflicting date and the sixteenth-century answer
**Front:** What are Eknath's dates, and what should an answer say?
**Definition (learn):** The book gives two conflicting dates: Eknath (1533-99) among the Varkari saints (PDF p. 743), and born in 1518 in the Marathi literature chapter (PDF p. 696). The conflict is unresolved in this folder. Write "sixteenth-century" and his contribution. (✅ owner 13 §9, §13.9)
**Breakdown:**
- Source A: 1533-99.
- Source B: born 1518.
- Safe phrase: sixteenth-century Varkari saint.
- Report both only for a chronology question.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As listed. ⚠️ Unresolved conflict in the owner.
**Links:** ART-3152, ART-3149.
**Trap:** Stating either date as the date, or building a chronology on it.
**Mains hook:** Source conflicts are reported, not silently resolved.
**Back:** *"Eknath: sixteenth century, two dates, neither the date."*

### Flashcard ART-3152 — Tukaram: 1598-1650, Tukaramgatha and the 2022 Dehu shrine
**Front:** What is Tukaram's contribution and the owner's dated anchor?
**Definition (learn):** The greatest poet in Marathi literature, 1598-1650. Preached asceticism, toleration and devotion; abhangas compiled as the Tukaramgatha; discussed Pravritti and Nivritti (passion for life against renunciation). The Sant Tukaram Shila Mandir at Dehu, Pune district, was inaugurated in 2022 (PDF p. 836). (✅ owner 13 §13.4A, PDF pp. 696, 836)
**Breakdown:**
- Dates: 1598-1650.
- Work: Tukaramgatha.
- Theme: Pravritti vs Nivritti.
- Dated anchor: Dehu shrine, 2022.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1598-1650; 2022. (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3149, ART-3151, ART-3153.
**Trap:** Presenting Tukaram as only ascetic. The owner stresses the Pravritti tension.
**Mains hook:** Use the Pravritti-Nivritti tension for a tradition that includes family and business.
**Back:** *"Tukaram: 1598-1650, Tukaramgatha, life versus renunciation."*

### Flashcard ART-3153 — Ramdas and the Dasbodh
**Front:** What is Ramdas's principal work?
**Definition (learn):** Seventeenth-century Marathi saint who wrote Dasbodh, containing sermons on abstract and practical topics (PDF p. 696). (✅ owner 13 §13.4A)
**Breakdown:**
- Period: 17th century.
- Work: Dasbodh.
- Limit: political readings belong to Medieval History.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 17th century. (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3152, ART-3149.
**Trap:** Reading Ramdas as political without the historian's sources.
**Mains hook:** Name the work, leave politics to History.
**Back:** *"Ramdas: Dasbodh sermons, 17th century."*

### Flashcard ART-3154 — Mirabai: royal Rajput Krishna-Bhakta
**Front:** Who was Mirabai, and what qualification does the owner require?
**Definition (learn):** From a ruling Rajput family, married to the son of Rana Sanga of Mewar. Treated Krishna as her husband; her poems present Krishna as yogi and lover. "Paayoji Maine Ram Ratan Dhan Paayo" is a known composition. Guru Raidas was her preceptor (PDF p. 837). She renounced the world for Krishna and wrote bhajans (PDF p. 688). (✅ owner 13 §13.4B, PDF pp. 445, 688, 837)
**Breakdown:**
- Region: Rajasthan, Mewar.
- Tradition: Krishna-Bhakti.
- Class fact: elite marriage, so renunciation is class-specific.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 688, 837. (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3143, ART-3144, ART-3160.
**Trap:** A generic "she defied society" framing. Use the class fact.
**Mains hook:** Her elite status is part of the story.
**Back:** *"Mirabai: Mewar queen, Krishna as husband, Raidas her guru."*

### Flashcard ART-3155 — Janabai: the strongest caste-and-gender case
**Front:** Who was Janabai?
**Definition (learn):** Maharashtra, Shudra caste, 13th-14th century. Worked in the household of Namdeva. Despite no formal education she composed over 300 poems about her life, chores and the restrictions of a low-caste woman (PDF p. 838). Recorded as the oldest known female writer in Marathi (PDF p. 696). (✅ owner 13 §13.4B)
**Breakdown:**
- Caste: Shudra.
- Setting: Namdeva's household.
- Output: 300+ poems.
- Milestone: oldest female writer in Marathi.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 13th-14th century; 300+ poems (owner). (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3150, ART-3156, ART-3160.
**Trap:** Listing her name only. Owner says do not lose her case.
**Mains hook:** Her poetry survives from inside a male saint's household; say so.
**Back:** *"Janabai: Shudra woman, 300 poems, Namdeva's household."*

### Flashcard ART-3156 — Bahinabai: labour in women's abhangas
**Front:** What is Bahina's distinctive content?
**Definition (learn):** Bahina (Bahinabai), 17th century Maharashtra. Wrote abhangas portraying women's working life, especially in the fields (PDF p. 838). (✅ owner 13 §13.4B)
**Breakdown:**
- Period: 17th century.
- Content: women's labour.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 17th century. (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3155, ART-3160.
**Trap:** Describing her as devotion-only. The owner says labour is the content.
**Mains hook:** Use labour as the evidence for women's voices.
**Back:** *"Bahina: fields and labour in abhangas."*

### Flashcard ART-3157 — Karaikkal Ammaiyar and the three Nayanar women
**Front:** Who are the three women among the 63 Nayanars?
**Definition (learn):** Karaikkal Ammaiyar, a poet devoted to Shiva from childhood who adopted asceticism; Mangayarkkarasiyar, a Pandya queen; Isaignaniyar, mother of Nayanar Sundarar. Isaignaniyar's inclusion is attributed to her familial connection (PDF pp. 831, 838). (✅ owner 13 §13.4B)
**Breakdown:**
- Karaikkal Ammaiyar: poet, asceticism.
- Mangayarkkarasiyar: queen.
- Isaignaniyar: included through a male relative.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 831, 838. (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3136, ART-3158, ART-3160.
**Trap:** Presenting all three as inclusion on merit.
**Mains hook:** The source itself flags unequal inclusion.
**Back:** *"Three Nayanar women: poet, queen, and mother by family link."*

### Flashcard ART-3158 — Akka Mahadevi: "elder sister" in Virashaiva Karnataka
**Front:** Who is Akka Mahadevi and what honorific did men give her?
**Definition (learn):** A devotee of Shiva in southern Karnataka, Virashaiva or Lingayat, 12th century. Called "Akka" (elder sister) by Basavanna, Prabhu Deva, Madivalayya and Chenna Basavanna (PDF pp. 837-838). (✅ owner 13 §13.4B)
**Breakdown:**
- Region: southern Karnataka.
- Honorific: "Akka," given by men.
- Date: 12th century.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 837-838. (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3147, ART-3160.
**Trap:** Reading the honorific as unqualified equality. Owner says it is standing conferred by men.
**Mains hook:** Recognition given by men is evidence of standing, with a limit.
**Back:** *"Akka Mahadevi: called elder sister by men, 12th century."*

### Flashcard ART-3159 — Lal Ded: a bounded gap
**Front:** What can be written about Lal Ded from the folder's sources?
**Definition (learn):** Lal Ded is attributed to Kashmir. No local source in the folder carries her dates, verses, language or biography. Cite her only as a Kashmiri example of regional breadth. (✅ owner 13 §13.4B, §13.9) ⚠️
**Breakdown:**
- Attribution: Kashmir.
- Gap: dates, verses, doctrine, language.
- Use: regional breadth only.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None in the folder. ⚠️
**Links:** ART-3160, ART-3136.
**Trap:** Supplying a date or verse from memory.
**Mains hook:** State the gap rather than fill it.
**Back:** *"Lal Ded: Kashmir, nothing more from this folder."*

### Flashcard ART-3160 — Women in Bhakti: two unequal routes and the qualification
**Front:** How should a women-in-Bhakti answer be qualified?
**Definition (learn):** Women participated "by defying social norms by leaving home for devotion or practising it within their households" (PDF p. 837), two different and unequal routes. Only one of twelve Alvars was a woman; one Nayanar woman's inclusion rested on a male relative; Janabai survives from inside a male saint's household. Do not claim Bhakti emancipated women. (✅ owner 13 §13.4B, §13.9)
**Breakdown:**
- Route 1: leaving home (Mirabai, Karaikkal Ammaiyar, Akka Mahadevi).
- Route 2: practice within household (Janabai, Bahina).
- Limit: appropriation by later movements (owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p. 837. (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3137, ART-3154, ART-3155, ART-3157, ART-3190.
**Trap:** "Bhakti gave women equality."
**Mains hook:** Two routes, one limit, one caution about modern appropriation.
**Back:** *"Two routes, not one liberation: leave home or stay and sing."*

### Flashcard ART-3161 — Bhakti literature: the three transfers (15-mark engine)
**Front:** What is the thesis for "contribution of Bhakti literature to regional languages"?
**Definition (learn):** Bhakti's literary contribution rests on three concrete transfers: composition moved from Sanskrit to the spoken tongue; authorship moved outward from the Brahman scholar to weavers, tailors, maidservants and cultivators; and it fixed the first canonical texts of several modern literary languages. Its institutional afterlife was less egalitarian than its verses. (✅ owner 13 §13.4C)
**Breakdown:**
- Transfer 1: language.
- Transfer 2: authorship.
- Transfer 3: canonical texts.
- Eight named units (ART-3135, 3140, 3141, 3149, 3150, 3144, 3145, 3147).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-only synthesis. (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3135, ART-3140, ART-3141, ART-3144, ART-3145, ART-3147, ART-3149, ART-3150, ART-3162.
**Trap:** Giving each saint the same three sentences.
**Mains hook:** Structural, not sentimental; close with the institutional limit.
**Back:** *"Language, authors, canons: and the institutions reattached them."*

### Flashcard ART-3162 — Hindi's boost from Bhakti
**Front:** What does the source say about Hindi's growth and the Bhakti movement?
**Definition (learn):** Hindi received its biggest boost with the Bhakti movement, which shunned Sanskrit as the language of Brahmins. From the 12th century a sharp rise in Bengali, Hindi, Marathi and Gujarati followed (PDF p. 687). (✅ owner 13 §13.4C, PDF p. 687)
**Breakdown:**
- Cause: Bhakti's rejection of Sanskrit gatekeeping.
- Effect: regional language rise from the 12th century.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** From the 12th century (owner). (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3081, ART-3161.
**Trap:** Saying Bhakti alone made Hindi; the owner gives Raso and Kabir's dohas separately.
**Mains hook:** The strongest single sentence for a language-growth demand.
**Back:** *"Bhakti shunned Sanskrit; Hindi, Bengali, Marathi, Gujarati rose."*

### Flashcard ART-3163 — Sufism: tasawwuf, silsilahs and the Ba-shara and Be-shara split
**Front:** What is Sufism and how are its orders classed?
**Definition (learn):** Sufism is the mystical arm of Islam, tasawwuf, derived from Arabic suf (wool) or safa (purity). In India it began around 1300 AD and reached South India by the 15th century (PDF p. 840). Orders are silsilahs; the Ain-i-Akbari mentions a dozen. Ba-shara (in favour of Sharia) and Be-shara (against it: Qalandars, Madaris, Malangs, Haidaris, who left few written accounts). (✅ owner 13 §13.5A, PDF pp. 840, 843, 848)
**Breakdown:**
- Name: tasawwuf.
- Timeline: c. 1300 AD India; 15th century South.
- Orders: silsilahs.
- Split: Ba-shara, Be-shara.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As listed. (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3164, ART-3167, ART-3173.
**Trap:** Treating Sufism as one movement.
**Mains hook:** Name the order and its legal stance.
**Back:** *"Tasawwuf: silsilahs, Ba-shara inside Sharia, Be-shara outside."*

### Flashcard ART-3164 — Chishti order: Moinuddin Chishti at Ajmer
**Front:** What are the key facts about Khwaja Moinuddin Chishti?
**Definition (learn):** Dates 1143-1236 AD. From Chisht near Herat, where the order began in 930 AD. Established his khanqah at Ajmer. Worked for the poorest; known as Gharib Nawaz. An annual Urs is held at Ajmer. Jahanara Begum, daughter of Shah Jahan, wrote his biography Munis al-Arwah. The order's philosophy rests on Wahdat-al-wujud, "very similar to the Vedantic philosophy" (PDF p. 843). (✅ owner 13 §13.5A, PDF pp. 843)
**Breakdown:**
- Dates: 1143-1236.
- Seat: Ajmer khanqah.
- Name: Gharib Nawaz.
- Biography: Munis al-Arwah (Jahanara).
- Doctrine: Wahdat-al-wujud.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1143-1236; 930 AD (order's origin, owner). (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3163, ART-3165, ART-3166, ART-3174.
**Trap:** Reading the Vedanta resemblance as merger. The owner calls it resemblance of doctrine-language.
**Mains hook:** Ajmer's Urs is a living syncretic practice (ART-3172).
**Back:** *"Moinuddin: Ajmer khanqah, Gharib Nawaz, Urs, Wahdat-al-wujud."*

### Flashcard ART-3165 — Nizamuddin Auliya and Amir Khusrau
**Front:** What is the Nizamuddin Auliya and Amir Khusrau link?
**Definition (learn):** Sheikh Nizamuddin Auliya of Delhi, known as Mahbub-e-Ilahi, sought God through renunciation and service. Addressed as sultan-ul-mashaikh. Amir Khusrau was his most famous disciple, credited as father of Qawwali and introducing Ghazal, Tarana and Trivat. He bestowed the title Ain-i-Hind on Sheikh Sirajuddin Usmani (PDF pp. 844, 431). (✅ owner 13 §13.5A, PDF pp. 431, 844)
**Breakdown:**
- Saint: Nizamuddin Auliya, Delhi.
- Disciple: Amir Khusrau.
- Music: Qawwali, Ghazal, Tarana, Trivat.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 431, 844. (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3164, ART-3171, ART-3166.
**Trap:** Crediting Khusrau with certainly inventing sitar, tabla, khayal or qawwali. The owner prohibits it.
**Mains hook:** The most productive Sufi-to-culture link in the owner.
**Back:** *"Nizamuddin's disciple Khusrau: Qawwali, Ghazal, Tarana, Trivat (not every instrument)."*

### Flashcard ART-3166 — Other Chishti saints, including the Salim Chishti counter-case
**Front:** Name the other Chishti saints and the counter-case.
**Definition (learn):** Qutbuddin Bakhtiyar Kaki, disciple of Moinuddin, in whose memory Qutb-ud-din Aibak began the Qutub Minar. Fariduddin Ganj-i-shakar (Baba Farid), in Punjab; verses in the Adi Granth. Sheikh Nasiruddin Mahmud, Chiraag-e-Dilli. Sheikh Hamiduddin of Nagaur, Sultan-e-Tarkeen, who translated Persian books into Hindi. Sheikh Burhanuddin Gharib, founder of the Chishti community in South India. Sheikh Salim Chishti of Fatehpur Sikri, favoured by Akbar, who named his first son Salim after him (PDF pp. 843-844). (✅ owner 13 §13.5A)
**Breakdown:**
- Delhi: Kaki, Nasiruddin.
- Punjab: Baba Farid.
- Nagaur: Hamiduddin.
- South: Burhanuddin Gharib.
- Counter-case: Salim Chishti at the Mughal court.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 843-844. (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3165, ART-3164, ART-3140.
**Trap:** Saying the Chishti order always stayed away from power. Salim Chishti shows otherwise.
**Mains hook:** The same order began distant from power and later stood near it.
**Back:** *"Kaki, Farid, Hamiduddin, Burhanuddin; Salim Chishti stood at court."*

### Flashcard ART-3167 — Suhrawardi order: property, ilm and contrast with the Chishtis
**Front:** What distinguishes the Suhrawardi order from the Chishtis?
**Definition (learn):** Founded by Shihabuddin Suhrawardi in Baghdad; established in India by Bahauddin Zakariya (1182-1262), titled Sheikh-ul-Islam by Iltutmish, first khanqah at Multan. A Sufi may own property, knowledge and hal. Fusion of mysticism and ilm. Popular in Punjab, Sindh, Kashmir, parts of Bengal. Zenith under Sheikh Ruknuddin (1251-1335). They rejected Chishti practices such as bowing at initiation (PDF p. 845). (✅ owner 13 §13.5A)
**Breakdown:**
- India: Bahauddin Zakariya, Multan.
- Property: allowed.
- Contrast: Chishti bowing rejected.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1182-1262; 1251-1335. (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3164, ART-3166, ART-3169.
**Trap:** Describing "the Sufis" as one position.
**Mains hook:** Property and state proximity distinguish Suhrawardis from Chishtis.
**Back:** *"Suhrawardi: Multan, property allowed, ilm with mysticism."*

### Flashcard ART-3168 — Qadiriyya: Shah Nimatullah, Miyan Mir and Dara Shikoh
**Front:** What do the sources give on the Qadiriyya?
**Definition (learn):** Introduced by Shah Nimatullah, popular in Punjab, especially Sindh. Its pirs supported Wahdat al-wujud. Miyan Mir enrolled Prince Dara Shikoh. Urdu poets Hasrat Mohani and Muhammad Iqbal belonged to this order (PDF p. 845). (✅ owner 13 §13.5A)
**Breakdown:**
- Founder in India: Shah Nimatullah.
- Region: Punjab, Sindh.
- Doctrine: Wahdat al-wujud.
- Link: Dara Shikoh.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p. 845. (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3163, ART-3167, ART-3080.
**Trap:** Dara Shikoh's translation project belongs to Medieval History.
**Mains hook:** Use the Qadiri link for Urdu and Persian cultural continuity.
**Back:** *"Qadiri: Shah Nimatullah, Miyan Mir, Dara Shikoh, Iqbal."*

### Flashcard ART-3169 — Naqshbandi: the radical counter-current
**Front:** Why is the Naqshbandi order the standing refutation of a uniform syncretic Sufism?
**Definition (learn):** Established by Khwaja Baha-ud-Din Naqshband, popular under Akbar. Its most popular saint, Ahmad Sirhindi, styled himself Mujaddid. The silsilah was radical and had Aurangzeb among its followers (PDF pp. 845-846). (✅ owner 13 §13.5A)
**Breakdown:**
- Founder: Baha-ud-Din Naqshband.
- Key figure: Ahmad Sirhindi, Mujaddid.
- Follower: Aurangzeb.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 845-846. (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3167, ART-3173, ART-3192.
**Trap:** "All Sufis were syncretic." Naqshbandi refutes it.
**Mains hook:** Cite this whenever a harmony narrative is invited.
**Back:** *"Naqshbandi: radical, Sirhindi Mujaddid, Aurangzeb followed."*

### Flashcard ART-3170 — Shattari and Kubrawiya/Firdausia
**Front:** What do the sources give on the Shattari and Kubrawiya orders?
**Definition (learn):** Shattari, established by Sheikh Sirajuddin Abdullah Shattar, popular in Malwa, Jaunpur and Bengal. Tansen, Akbar's court musician, was a follower (PDF p. 846). Kubrawiya/Firdausia, founded by Najm-ad-Din Kubra, popular in eastern India, Bangladesh and Mauritius; the source says it did not undergo major expansion (PDF p. 846). (✅ owner 13 §13.5A)
**Breakdown:**
- Shattari: Malwa, Jaunpur, Bengal; Tansen.
- Kubrawiya: eastern India, Bangladesh, Mauritius.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p. 846. (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3163, ART-3167.
**Trap:** Describing Kubrawiya as expansionist.
**Mains hook:** Order-to-music link: Shattari and Tansen.
**Back:** *"Shattari: Tansen's order. Kubrawiya: stable, eastern India."*

### Flashcard ART-3171 — Sufi practice vocabulary
**Front:** Which Sufi terms must be used precisely?
**Definition (learn):** Fana (spiritual merger with Allah); insan-e-kamil (perfect human); zikr/dhikr (remembrance; reciter a dakir); wahdat al-wujud (unity of God and being); sama (spiritual dance and music, which the source says is not Islamic); muraqabah (meditation); ziyarat (pilgrimage to tombs); pir/shaikh and murid; khalifa and muqaddam; wilaya; tasfiyat al-qulub (purification). (✅ owner 13 §13.5A, PDF pp. 840-842)
**Breakdown:**
- Experience: fana, muraqabah, zikr.
- Doctrine: wahdat al-wujud, insan-e-kamil.
- Music: sama.
- Institution: pir, murid, khalifa, muqaddam.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 840-842. Stages: Khanqah (10th c.), Tariqa (11th-14th c.), Tarifa (15th c. onward) (PDF p. 841). (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3163, ART-3169, ART-3172.
**Trap:** Translating fana as "merging into a different religion." It is a spiritual state.
**Mains hook:** Use the term that names the practice.
**Back:** *"Fana, zikr, sama, wahdat al-wujud: precise words."*

### Flashcard ART-3172 — Phool Walon Ki Sair and Ajmer's Urs: living shared practice
**Front:** What dated shared practices does the owner give?
**Definition (learn):** The Phool Walon Ki Sair of Delhi carries floral fans from Khwaja Bakhtiyar Kaki's tomb to the Yogmaya Temple. Patronised by Akbar II in the 19th century, banned by the British, reinstated in 1962 by Jawaharlal Nehru (PDF p. 878). Ajmer's Urs is an annual congregation after Moinuddin's death. (✅ owner 13 §13.4, PDF p. 878)
**Breakdown:**
- Sair: Kaki tomb to Yogmaya Temple.
- Dates: Akbar II (19th c.); banned by British; reinstated 1962.
- Urs: Ajmer.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1962 (owner). (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3164, ART-3174.
**Trap:** Calling the Sair an ancient unbroken practice.
**Mains hook:** Dated practice with a named patron and a banning.
**Back:** *"Phool Walon Ki Sair: Kaki to Yogmaya, banned, reinstated 1962."*

### Flashcard ART-3173 — Sufi source genres: malfuzat, maktubat, tazkira
**Front:** Distinguish the three Sufi source genres and name the examples.
**Definition (learn):** Malfuzat are recorded conversations and discourses. Maktubat are letters. Tazkiras are biographies. Munis al-Arwah (Jahanara) is a biography. Early Chishti teachings are compiled in Suhrawardi's Awarif al-Ma'arif and Ali Hujwiri's Kashf al-Mahjub (PDF p. 845). (✅ owner 13 §2, §13.5A)
**Breakdown:**
- Malfuzat: conversations.
- Maktubat: letters.
- Tazkira: biographies.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p. 845. (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3163, ART-3164.
**Trap:** Treating the genres as interchangeable evidence.
**Mains hook:** Source genre sets the evidentiary value.
**Back:** *"Malfuzat talk, maktubat letters, tazkira lives."*

### Flashcard ART-3174 — Synthesis: practice, not doctrinal merger; Din-i-Ilahi's limit
**Front:** Where is Sufi-Bhakti synthesis demonstrable, and where does it overreach?
**Definition (learn):** Synthesis is strongest in shared shrines, pilgrimage, music, vernacular literature, architecture and translation. It overreaches with merged theology or a single composite religion. Din-i-Ilahi "never had mass acceptance" (PDF p. 140). Satish Chandra: Sufi ideological structures remained Islamic, interaction without identity-erasure. (✅ owner 13 §13.5, §13.5A, PDF p. 140)
**Breakdown:**
- Demonstrable: practice, space, form.
- Overreach: doctrine, identity.
- Elite syncretism: Din-i-Ilahi did not become a social fact.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p. 140. (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3164, ART-3172, ART-3188.
**Trap:** "Composite religion" or a general harmony without a named practice.
**Mains hook:** Keep practice and doctrine at separate levels.
**Back:** *"Synthesis in practice, not in doctrine; Din-i-Ilahi never caught on."*

### Flashcard ART-3175 — Somnath, Al-Biruni and Pran Pratishtha: a bounded gap
**Front:** What can be said about the 2022 Somnath and Pran Pratishtha demand?
**Definition (learn):** Supported: Somnath heads the twelve Jyotirlingas list (PDF p. 123). Al-Biruni is in the source as 11th-century author of Kitab-ul-Hind (PDF pp. 1016, 1031-1033); the owner notes he called Khajuraho the capital of Jajahuti (PDF p. 82). Not supported locally: any Al-Biruni account of the Somnath raid, and the term Pran Pratishtha or any dated consecration. (✅ owner 13 §13.5B) ⚠️
**Breakdown:**
- Supported: Jyotirlinga list, Al-Biruni's identity.
- Unsupported: Al-Biruni's Somnath account; Pran Pratishtha definition.
- Hand off: raid narrative to Medieval History.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 123, 1016, 1031-1033. (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3176, ART-3177.
**Trap:** Writing an Al-Biruni Somnath account or defining Pran Pratishtha from memory.
**Mains hook:** Disclose the gap rather than fabricate.
**Back:** *"Somnath: Jyotirlinga yes; Al-Biruni's raid account and Pran Pratishtha no."*

### Flashcard ART-3176 — The twelve Jyotirlingas (the owner's list)
**Front:** Name the twelve Jyotirlingas listed in the source, including Somnath.
**Definition (learn):** Somnath (Saurashtra), Nageshvara (Devbhumi Dwarka district), Mallikarjuna Swami (Srisailam), Mahakaleshwar (Ujjain), Omkareshwar (Khandwa district), Kashi Vishwanath (Varanasi), Kedarnath (PDF p. 123). (✅ owner 13 §13.5B, PDF p. 123)
**Breakdown:**
- Named in owner: seven of twelve.
- Frame: Shiva's twelve shrines.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p. 123. ⚠️ The owner names seven; the other five are not listed.
**Links:** ART-3175.
**Trap:** Listing seven as the full twelve.
**Mains hook:** Use the list, not a count from memory.
**Back:** *"Somnath, Nageshvara, Srisailam, Ujjain, Omkareshwar, Varanasi, Kedarnath: seven of twelve."*

### Flashcard ART-3177 — Philosophy shaping monuments: the 2020 Mains route (concept)
**Front:** How does a philosophy-and-monuments answer work without a bolted-on monument?
**Definition (learn):** CSE 2020 GS-I Q11 asks how Indian philosophy and tradition conceived and shaped monuments and art (15 marks, 250 words). Each unit must show a doctrine converting into a design decision, then be qualified. (✅ owner 13 §13.6B, PDF p. 72, 860-861)
**Breakdown:**
- Thesis: doctrine made buildable.
- Conception: aniconism to symbol; relic cult to stupa.
- Shaping: shramana withdrawal; vahana; bhakti.
- Art programme: purushartha at Khajuraho.
- Counter-weight: patronage, material, dynasty.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p. 72. (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3124, ART-3178, ART-3179, ART-3180, ART-3181, ART-3125.
**Trap:** A philosophy essay with a monument added.
**Mains hook:** Philosophy supplied the programme; patronage decided the scale.
**Back:** *"Doctrine made buildable; patron decides scale."*

### Flashcard ART-3178 — Aniconism to symbol; relic cult to stupa
**Front:** How did aniconism and the relic cult shape early Buddhist art?
**Definition (learn):** A doctrinal reluctance to embody the Buddha led to symbols (empty throne, Bodhi tree, wheel, stupa, footprint). The relic cult produced the stupa, built for circumambulation (anda, harmika, chattra, vedika, pradakshinapatha). Stupa-making is pre-Buddhist and not exclusively Buddhist (owner). (✅ owner 13 §13.6B, owner 06 cross-link via owner 13)
**Breakdown:**
- Aniconism: symbols at Bharhut and early Sanchi.
- Relic cult: stupa parts.
- Pre-Buddhist note: strongest counter to "Buddhism invented the stupa."
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Sanchi Great Stupa cited as example. (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3177, ART-3179.
**Trap:** Saying Buddhism invented the stupa.
**Mains hook:** Doctrine shaped building type; pre-Buddhist origins qualify it.
**Back:** *"Aniconism gave symbols; relic cult gave the circumambulated stupa."*

### Flashcard ART-3179 — Shramana withdrawal and excavated architecture
**Front:** How did ascetic withdrawal shape building type?
**Definition (learn):** Ascetic practice produced excavated architecture: viharas for residence, chaityas for congregation, cut into hillsides away from settlements. Barabar and Nagarjuni for Ajivikas under Ashoka and Dasharatha; Ellora viharas outnumber chaityas; Udayagiri-Khandagiri for Jains. Royal endowment also served political display (owner). (✅ owner 13 §13.6B, unit 4)
**Breakdown:**
- Viharas: residence.
- Chaityas: congregation.
- Sites: Barabar, Nagarjuni, Ellora, Udayagiri-Khandagiri.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As listed. (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3177, ART-3186.
**Trap:** Treating royal endowment as pure piety.
**Mains hook:** Best case of doctrine dictating building type.
**Back:** *"Withdrawal cut caves: viharas to live, chaityas to gather."*

### Flashcard ART-3180 — Vahana to Nandi mandapa; bhakti to enclosure and gopuram
**Front:** How do the vahana and bhakti shape temple plans?
**Definition (learn):** The vahana (mount) gets a separate structure on the temple axis, just before the sanctum (PDF p. 72). Nandi mandapas at Brihadishvara, Lepakshi, Ellora Kailasa, Ramappa and the Hoysala group. Bhakti produced enclosures, corridors, tanks and gopurams visible from outside. Patronage and politics operate alongside devotion (owner). (✅ owner 13 §13.6B, PDF p. 72)
**Breakdown:**
- Vahana: Nandi mandapa.
- Bhakti: enclosure, corridor, gopuram.
- Nayaka prakaram and gopura system (owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p. 72. (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3177, ART-3133, ART-3135.
**Trap:** Treating a Hoysala temple as purely devotional. It is also a dynastic statement.
**Mains hook:** Five named cases make the vahana chain sourced.
**Back:** *"Vahana: Nandi hall on the axis; bhakti: gopuram and corridor."*

### Flashcard ART-3181 — Garbhagriha and Vedanta: a bounded correspondence
**Front:** Can a temple plan be derived from Vedanta?
**Definition (learn):** No. The garbhagriha as a small, dark core under an elaborate exterior can be argued as a correspondence with Brahman and maya, flagged as interpretation. The folder's sources do not state that the plan was derived from Vedanta. Do not supply Vastu or Shilpa-shastra rules. (✅ owner 13 §13.6B unit 7, §13.9) ⚠️
**Breakdown:**
- Correspondence: unmanifest centre, manifest exterior.
- Bounded: no derivation claim.
- Prohibited: Vastu, Shilpa-shastra rules.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None. ⚠️
**Links:** ART-3131, ART-3177.
**Trap:** Saying Vedanta derived temple plans.
**Mains hook:** Label the correspondence as an argument, not a documented fact.
**Back:** *"Garbhagriha: a correspondence to argue, not a derivation to claim."*

### Flashcard ART-3182 — Parsi fire temples: three grades and plain exterior
**Front:** What are the three grades of Parsi fire temple and why is the exterior plain?
**Definition (learn):** Atash Behram ("Fire of Victory"), Atash Adaran and Atash Dadgah. There are eight Atash Behrams in India and more than 100 Dadgahs, mostly in Maharashtra and Gujarat. The exterior is kept simple because the point is to hold a sacred fire and Yasna ceremony, not to glorify the faith. There is an inner sanctum with vents for smoke (PDF p. 147). (✅ owner 13 §13.6C)
**Breakdown:**
- Grades: Atash Behram, Adaran, Dadgah.
- Count: eight Atash Behrams; 100+ Dadgahs (owner).
- Architecture: plain exterior, inner sanctum, vents.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p. 147. (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3183, ART-3177, ART-3036.
**Trap:** Decorating the exterior as for a public temple. The owner says the plainness is doctrinal.
**Mains hook:** A doctrine producing a building decision, in the source's words.
**Back:** *"Parsi fire temple: plain outside, sacred fire inside, three grades."*

### Flashcard ART-3183 — Parsi texts, migration and the Sanjan fire temple
**Front:** What are the Parsi texts and the migration account?
**Definition (learn):** The Avesta (Yashts, Khordeh Avesta); Denkard (10th century AD, "Encyclopaedia of Zoroastrianism", no divine status); Bundahishn ("Primal Creations"); Qissa-i Sanjan, whose first chapter ends with the fire temple at Sanjan, Gujarat. Founded by Zarathustra in Persia around the 7th century BC; first contact with India between the 8th and 10th centuries AD (PDF pp. 655-656, 760, 762). (✅ owner 13 §13.6C)
**Breakdown:**
- Texts: Avesta, Denkard, Bundahishn, Qissa-i Sanjan.
- Founder: Zarathustra.
- Migration: 8th-10th century AD.
- Monotheism: Ahura Mazda vs Angra Mainyu.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 7th century BC; 8th-10th century AD (owner). (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3182, ART-3184.
**Trap:** Giving Parsi population figures. The owner gives none.
**Mains hook:** Migration, trade and refuge as formation.
**Back:** *"Avesta, Denkard, Bundahishn, Qissa-i Sanjan: from Persia to Sanjan."*

### Flashcard ART-3184 — The five Jewish communities, distinguished by language
**Front:** Name the five major Jewish communities of India and their language markers.
**Definition (learn):** Malayalam-speaking Cochin Jews; Marathi-speaking Bene Israel (largest); Baghdadi Jews, who came as traders and settled around Surat, now mainly Mumbai and Kolkata; Bnei Menashe on the India-Myanmar border in Manipur and Mizoram (Mizo, Kuki and Chin tribes); Bene Ephraim, Telugu-speaking, converted in the 1990s (PDF pp. 763-764). (✅ owner 13 §13.6C)
**Breakdown:**
- Cochin: Malayalam.
- Bene Israel: Marathi; largest.
- Baghdadi: traders.
- Bnei Menashe: Manipur and Mizoram.
- Bene Ephraim: Telugu; 1990s.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp. 763-764. (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3182, ART-3185.
**Trap:** Aggregating them as one "Indian Jewish" community.
**Mains hook:** Language marks the community; the differences are the evidence.
**Back:** *"Cochin Malayalam, Bene Israel Marathi, Baghdadi traders, Bnei Menashe NE, Bene Ephraim Telugu."*

### Flashcard ART-3185 — St Thomas Christians: the 52 AD tradition as the community's record
**Front:** What is the status of the 52 AD Malabar arrival tradition?
**Definition (learn):** As per the written records of the Saint Thomas Christians (Syrian Christians), Thomas the Apostle sailed to Malabar in Kerala in 52 AD (PDF p. 755). Present it as the tradition's account, not settled history. The folder gives no church building or liturgy. Kerala murals' economic base is attributed to contact with Greeks, Romans and Persians (PDF p. 331). (✅ owner 13 §13.6C) ⚠️
**Breakdown:**
- Tradition: Thomas, 52 AD, Malabar.
- Framing: "as per the written records of."
- Gap: no church or liturgy described.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 52 AD (community record). ⚠️
**Links:** ART-3184, ART-3182, ART-3186.
**Trap:** Presenting 52 AD as settled history or describing a Syrian church building.
**Mains hook:** Name the record that carries the claim.
**Back:** *"52 AD is the community's record, not a certified date."*

### Flashcard ART-3186 — Ellora's shared sacred space
**Front:** What does Ellora show about shared sacred space?
**Definition (learn):** Ellora's 34 caves: 12 Buddhist, 17 Hindu, 5 Jain. Khajuraho has Hindu and Jain temples in one idiom. Evidence of continuing sanctity and competitive patronage, not of a designed interfaith project (owner 13 §13.6B unit 9). (✅ owner 13 §13.6B, basic/02, basic/03)
**Breakdown:**
- Ellora: 12 Buddhist, 17 Hindu, 5 Jain.
- Khajuraho: Hindu and Jain.
- Limit: no designed tolerance.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 34 caves. (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3179, ART-3177.
**Trap:** Treating Ellora as planned religious tolerance.
**Mains hook:** Shared sites show sanctity and rivalry together.
**Back:** *"Ellora: 12 Buddhist, 17 Hindu, 5 Jain; shared site, not a plan."*

### Flashcard ART-3187 — Deepavali (Diwali) on the UNESCO ICH list, 2025
**Front:** When did UNESCO inscribe Deepavali, and why does it matter for this owner?
**Definition (learn):** UNESCO inscribed Deepavali on the Representative List of the Intangible Cultural Heritage of Humanity in 2025 (20th session). It is a living, pan-India religious-cultural practice. (✅ Wikipedia: Diwali infobox; ✅ owner 13 §8)
**Breakdown:**
- List: Representative, ICH.
- Year: 2025 (20th session).
- Reference number: 02312 (Wikipedia infobox).
- Not an SNA classical form.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2025 (20th session). (✅ Wikipedia)
**Links:** ART-3019, ART-3020, ART-3022, ART-3189.
**Trap:** Calling Deepavali an SNA classical form or a Memory of the World entry.
**Mains hook:** Close a festival answer with this dated ICH anchor.
**Back:** *"Deepavali: UNESCO living heritage, 2025."*

### Flashcard ART-3188 — Akbar's syncretism (2025 Q2): the adjacent boundary
**Front:** How should a Bhakti-Sufi answer handle the 2025 Q2 Akbar question?
**Definition (learn):** 2025 Q2 asks the main aspects of Akbar's religious syncretism (150 words). Primary owner is Medieval History. This topic supplies the cultural-synthesis context: Sulh-i-Kul and the Ibadat Khana as mechanism (topic 04), and Din-i-Ilahi's lack of mass acceptance as the limit. (✅ owner 13 §7, advanced §8)
**Breakdown:**
- Owner: Medieval History.
- Supplied: Bhakti-Sufi context.
- Limit: Din-i-Ilahi "never had mass acceptance."
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2025 Q2 (verbatim in owner). (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3174, ART-3165.
**Trap:** Writing Akbar's doctrine as the owner's answer.
**Mains hook:** Name the mechanism and the limit together.
**Back:** *"Akbar: Sulh-i-Kul mechanism, Din-i-Ilahi limit."*

### Flashcard ART-3189 — Fairs and festivals as cultural systems
**Front:** What are the four festival types, and what does the system framing add?
**Definition (learn):** Pilgrimage-congregation (Kumbh Mela at Prayagraj, Haridwar, Ujjain, Nashik); agricultural and seasonal (Pongal, Bihu, Onam, Baisakhi, Hornbill-linked calendars); ritual-performance (Ramlila, Ramman, Mudiyettu, Durga Puja, Garba); multi-community (Deepavali; saint-shrine fairs). (✅ owner 13 §4, §13.7)
**Breakdown:**
- Systems: sacred geography, temporary urbanism, artisan economy, transmission, safeguarding.
- Limit: participation varies; no uniform meaning.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As listed. (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3187, ART-3124.
**Trap:** A festival calendar by state.
**Mains hook:** Describe the system, not the list.
**Back:** *"Festivals: pilgrimage, harvest, performance, shared. Name the system."*

### Flashcard ART-3190 — Trap: Bhakti and Sufism were different movements; Bhakti did not abolish caste
**Front:** Are Bhakti and Sufism the same movement, and did Bhakti abolish caste or gender hierarchy?
**Definition (learn):** No to both. Bhakti is a Hindu devotional movement; Sufism is the Islamic mystical movement. They developed in parallel and influenced each other. Individual saints varied; inclusion, institutionalisation and contestation must be studied together. (✅ owner 13 §9, §13.9)
**Breakdown:**
- Distinct movements.
- Inclusion varied; contestation real.
- Gender: two unequal routes (ART-3160).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None. (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3160, ART-3163, ART-3138.
**Trap:** The exam "identical movements" option.
**Mains hook:** Hold inclusion, institutionalisation and contestation together.
**Back:** *"Bhakti and Sufi: parallel, not identical; inclusive in verse, mixed in practice."*

### Flashcard ART-3191 — 2022 Prelims Q95: Ramanuja on salvation and devotion
**Front:** How should the 2022 Prelims Q95 on Ramanuja's salvation and devotion be answered?
**Definition (learn):** Vishishtadvaita holds Brahman with attributes. Love and devotion, not knowledge alone, are the means. The same position makes Ramanuja a Saguna Bhakti figure. (✅ owner 13 §13.6A, PDF pp. 867-868) (owner's key not held locally)
**Breakdown:**
- Brahman: with attributes.
- Means: devotion.
- Contrast: Shankara's jnana.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2022 Q95 (owner). Key unavailable locally.
**Links:** ART-3133, ART-3132.
**Trap:** Choosing the knowledge-based option.
**Mains hook:** Philosophy and devotional practice line up.
**Back:** *"Ramanuja: attributes and devotion, not knowledge alone."*

### Flashcard ART-3192 — Trap: "Sufism was uniformly syncretic" and the Naqshbandi refutation
**Front:** Was Sufism uniformly syncretic, and what should an answer avoid?
**Definition (learn):** No. Sufi orders differed: Suhrawardis accepted property and state proximity; Chishtis rejected bowing at initiation; Naqshbandis were radical and counted Aurangzeb among followers. Do not describe "the Sufis" as one position. (✅ owner 13 §9, §13.9, §13.5A)
**Breakdown:**
- Suhrawardi: property and state.
- Chishti: no bowing, court-distance.
- Naqshbandi: radical.
