**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None. (✅ owner 13)
**Links:** ART-3167, ART-3169, ART-3164.
**Trap:** A single Sufi position in an exam option.
**Mains hook:** Name the order before the claim.
**Back:** *"Sufis differed: Suhrawardi, Chishti, Naqshbandi, not one line."*

## INDIAN ART AND CULTURE — Part 4: Heritage Conservation, UNESCO and Cinema

> Section note: Owners: `upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Indian-Art-and-Culture/basic` and `advanced`, files 14–15. Fetch status as of **2026-11-01** (today). Time-varying counts carry their as-of date. ⚠️ = unverified or owner-internal conflict; ✅ = source-grounded.

### GROUP — Heritage Conservation, Institutions and UNESCO - MUST-DO

### Flashcard ART-4001 — Four heritage categories: the instrument firewall
**Front:** Why are World Heritage, Intangible Cultural Heritage, Memory of the World and Geographical Indication not interchangeable "heritage tags"?
**Definition (learn):** Four recognition systems, each with its own legal instrument, criteria and granting body. Only the first three are UNESCO-run; GI is a domestic intellectual-property category.
**Breakdown:**
- Tangible/mixed/natural → World Heritage Site (1972 Convention; World Heritage Committee).
- Living practice → Intangible Cultural Heritage (2003 Convention; ICH Intergovernmental Committee).
- Documentary heritage → Memory of the World International Register (UNESCO documentary programme).
- Origin-linked goods → Geographical Indication under the GI (Registration and Protection) Act, 1999 (domestic; GI Registry, Chennai, DPIIT).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 14, §13.4) India's status as of 2026-11-01: WHS 45; ICH Representative List 16; MoW International Register 14 (owner, ⚠️ not re-fetched); GI 658 goods as of July 2025 (owner).
**Links:** ART-4002 (WHS), ART-4004 (ICH), ART-4006 (MoW), ART-4007 (GI), ART-4033 (National Culture Fund).
**Trap:** A UNESCO "tag" does not include GI; a Tentative List entry is not an inscription; inscription does not transfer ownership.
**Mains hook:** Name the precise category and its instrument before claiming "UNESCO recognition"; protection failures often come from using the wrong instrument (instrument-fit thesis).
**Back:** *"Four tags, four laws: 1972 site, 2003 living, documentary register, domestic GI."*

### Flashcard ART-4002 — World Heritage Site under the 1972 Convention
**Front:** What is a World Heritage Site and what governing treaty creates it?
**Definition (learn):** A property inscribed for outstanding universal value (cultural, natural or mixed) under the 1972 Convention concerning the Protection of the World Cultural and Natural Heritage.
**Breakdown:**
- Cultural, natural or mixed property classification.
- Inscription is by the World Heritage Committee; India's record splits 37 cultural, 7 natural, 1 mixed.
- Serial properties (several components under one name) are a distinct form (Hoysalas 2023, Maratha forts 2025, Sarnath 2026).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 14 §2, PDF p. 723) Committee has 21 members. ✅ UNESCO India state page fetched 2026-11-01: 45 properties (37 cultural, 7 natural, 1 mixed). India ratified the 1972 Convention on 14 November 1977 (✅ UNESCO state page).
**Links:** ART-4003 (Committee), ART-4001, ART-4035 (count), ART-4038–4040 (recent inscriptions).
**Trap:** Owner Basic §5 table prints "36 cultural + 7 natural + 1 mixed = 44" — conflicts with the 37-cultural total in its own §4 and with the live UNESCO page; use 37 and 45 with the date. ⚠️ Owner-internal inconsistency recorded.
**Mains hook:** Cite the WHS count with its access date; WHS is a recognition status, not a funding or ownership transfer.
**Back:** *"Forty-five badges on the live list: thirty-seven, seven, one."*

### Flashcard ART-4003 — World Heritage Committee and its advisory bodies
**Front:** Who decides WHS inscriptions and which bodies advise the Committee?
**Definition (learn):** The World Heritage Committee, a 21-member body of States Parties, decides on inscriptions and Danger-List placement, advised by IUCN (natural), ICOMOS (cultural) and ICCROM (conservation training and research).
**Breakdown:**
- Committee (21 States Parties) → decides.
- IUCN → natural heritage evaluation.
- ICOMOS → cultural heritage evaluation.
- ICCROM → conservation expertise and training.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 14 §2, PDF p. 723); advisory-body roles are standard UNESCO practice (⚠️ not re-fetched on this card).
**Links:** ART-4002, ART-4001, ART-4004 (the ICH Intergovernmental Committee is a separate body).
**Trap:** The Committee is not the General Assembly of the Convention; the ICH Intergovernmental Committee is a different body under the 2003 Convention.
**Mains hook:** Recognition is granted by a political committee advised by technical bodies, so listings reflect nomination quality and state reporting, not automatic merit.
**Back:** *"Twenty-one decide, three advise: IUCN nature, ICOMOS culture, ICCROM care."*

### Flashcard ART-4004 — Intangible Cultural Heritage under the 2003 Convention
**Front:** What is Intangible Cultural Heritage and which UNESCO lists operate under the 2003 Convention?
**Definition (learn):** Living "practices, representations, expressions, knowledge, skills" with associated instruments and cultural spaces, transmitted across generations and recreated by communities (✅ owner 14 §2, PDF pp. 732–733).
**Breakdown:**
- Representative List of the Intangible Cultural Heritage of Humanity.
- List of ICH in Need of Urgent Safeguarding.
- Register of Good Safeguarding Practices.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Convention ratified by India on 9 September 2005 (✅ UNESCO ICH India page, fetched 2026-11-01).
**Links:** ART-4005 (three lists), ART-4042–4056 (element ledger), ART-4001.
**Trap:** Inscription on the Representative List is not safeguarding success; India has no element on the Urgent Safeguarding List and no entry on the Register (see ART-4036).
**Mains hook:** Recognition is running ahead of demonstrated transmission crisis — a strong "recognition is not safeguarding" argument.
**Back:** *"Living, not lodged: practices people keep alive, listed in three ways."*

### Flashcard ART-4005 — The three ICH lists: what each means
**Front:** Distinguish the Representative List, the Urgent Safeguarding List and the Register of Good Safeguarding Practices.
**Definition (learn):** Three distinct instruments under the 2003 Convention with different purposes: visibility, urgent rescue, and best-practice sharing.
**Breakdown:**
- Representative List → visibility and awareness of diversity.
- Urgent Safeguarding List → elements whose viability is at risk despite existing efforts.
- Register of Good Safeguarding Practices → programmes that have worked, shared as models.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** India: 16 on the Representative List; 0 on the Urgent Safeguarding List; 0 on the Register (✅ UNESCO ICH India page, fetched 2026-11-01; ✅ owner 14 §13.8B(iii) PDF pp. 733, 736).
**Links:** ART-4004, ART-4036.
**Trap:** A candidate who says "India has ICH elements in danger" is confusing the Representative List with the Urgent list; a "latest inclusion" question concerns the Representative List only.
**Mains hook:** The absence of Indian elements on the Urgent list is a negative fact to be cited with its date, not a claim that all Indian traditions are safe.
**Back:** *"Show, rescue, share: Representative, Urgent, Register."*

### Flashcard ART-4006 — Memory of the World International Register
**Front:** What is the Memory of the World Register and how many Indian inscriptions does the owner record?
**Definition (learn):** UNESCO's documentary-heritage register for manuscripts, archives and records of world significance.
**Breakdown:**
- Documentary, not monumental, heritage.
- Indian inscriptions include the Bhagavadgita and Natyashastra manuscripts (2025) per owner.
- Custody and digitisation sit with national bodies (National Archives, National Mission for Manuscripts).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** (✅ owner 14 §4, §13.4; ⚠️ count 14 is owner-stated, not re-fetched) As of the owner's date: 14 International Register inscriptions. ⚠️ Owner gives "verified 21 Jul 2026" in one table and "4 Sep 2026" elsewhere.
**Links:** ART-4001, ART-4022 (National Archives), ART-4025 (NMM).
**Trap:** Manuscript inscription year and the text's composition date are different. Do not conflate them.
**Mains hook:** Documentation (digitisation, cataloguing) is the least-contested form of protection and the precondition for any provenance claim.
**Back:** *"Documents are heritage too: the register that remembers manuscripts."*

### Flashcard ART-4007 — Geographical Indication: domestic, not UNESCO
**Front:** Under which Act is a GI protected in India and why is it not a UNESCO category?
**Definition (learn):** Origin-linked intellectual property, registered domestically under the Geographical Indications of Goods (Registration and Protection) Act, 1999, administered from the GI Registry at Chennai under DPIIT.
**Breakdown:**
- Act year: 1999.
- Registry: Chennai, DPIIT.
- Count: 658 goods as of July 2025 (owner; time-bound).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §13.4; the Registry location and count are owner-stated (⚠️ not re-fetched); the 658 figure is dated July 2025 and not a current total.
**Links:** ART-4001, ART-4004, ART-4051 (Thatheras craft).
**Trap:** Calling GI a "UNESCO heritage tag" is a substantive error; the owner itself warns that the 658 figure cannot be treated as a current total.
**Mains hook:** Use GI for craft names and origin; use ICH or WHS for practice or site recognition.
**Back:** *"Origin law, not UNESCO law: GI 1999, Chennai registry."*

### Flashcard ART-4008 — AMASR Act, 1958
**Front:** What statute governs centrally protected monuments and under which Act does ASI function?
**Definition (learn):** The Ancient Monuments and Archaeological Sites and Remains Act, 1958, the central statute for protected monuments; ASI functions under it.
**Breakdown:**
- Central protection of monuments of national importance.
- ASI administers protected sites under this Act.
- Later amended (2010 Amendment and Validation Act, per Wikipedia; see ART-4013, ART-4014).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §2, PDF p. 956; ✅ Wikipedia AMASR page fetched 2026-11-01 (Act passed 1958; amended 2010).
**Links:** ART-4009, ART-4011, ART-4013, ART-4014.
**Trap:** AMASR is a statute, not a scheme; Adarsh Smarak is a scheme.
**Mains hook:** The AMASR regime protects fabric but does not deliver funding or community transmission — name it for fabric only.
**Back:** *"Fifty-eight protects the stone: AMASR, ASI's statute."*

### Flashcard ART-4009 — Antiquities and Art Treasures Act, 1972
**Front:** What does the Antiquities and Art Treasures Act, 1972 empower the State to do?
**Definition (learn):** A central statute on antiquities and art treasures under which the government and Director General may acquire antiquities for preservation, regulate their movement and penalise damage.
**Breakdown:**
- Acquisition for preservation.
- Control of movement (illicit trade).
- Compensation demands and penalties for damage.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §13.8C (PDF p. 988); Wikipedia ASI page names it as "recommended central protection" (⚠️ wording differs). Year 1972 ✅ owner.
**Links:** ART-4008, ART-4029 (Article 49 export prohibition), ART-4032.
**Trap:** The owner calls it the "Antiquities and Art Treasure Act" in one place and "Art Treasures" elsewhere — use the official "Antiquities and Art Treasures Act, 1972".
**Mains hook:** Illicit export is both a statutory and constitutional concern (Article 49).
**Back:** *"Seventy-two controls the trade: acquire, regulate movement, penalise damage."*

### Flashcard ART-4010 — Adarsh Smarak scheme
**Front:** What is the Adarsh Smarak scheme and what does it not do?
**Definition (learn):** An ASI scheme identifying 100 monuments for enhanced visitor amenities.
**Breakdown:**
- Type: scheme (executive programme), not a statute.
- Named examples: Bhimbetka, Hampi, Konark, Elephanta, Mahabalipuram Shore Temple, Taj Mahal, Humayun's Tomb.
- Purpose: visitor experience; protection still comes from AMASR.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §2, §13.5, PDF pp. 956–957. Scheme status is dynamic; ⚠️ no launch year in owner.
**Links:** ART-4008, ART-4011, ART-4032 (Adopt a Heritage).
**Trap:** An amenities scheme is not a conservation-authority transfer.
**Mains hook:** Match amenities to visitor-load risk, and protection to AMASR, rather than listing all instruments.
**Back:** *"A hundred smiles, not a handover: Adarsh Smarak for amenities."*

### Flashcard ART-4011 — Archaeological Survey of India: founding and first DG
**Front:** When was ASI founded, by whom, and who was its first Director-General?
**Definition (learn):** The central archaeology and monument body of India, founded 1861 under Alexander Cunningham, widely called the father of Indian archaeology.
**Breakdown:**
- 1861: Cunningham as Archaeological Surveyor.
- 1871: Survey revived as a separate department; Cunningham appointed Director-General.
- Under the Ministry of Culture; AMASR Act 1958 is its administrative base.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §4, §13.5 (PDF pp. 956–957); ✅ Wikipedia ASI page fetched 2026-11-01 (founded 1861; Cunningham; DG on 1871 revival). ⚠️ Owner says "first Director-General" in 1861 wording; Wikipedia gives 1871 for the DG post.
**Links:** ART-4008, ART-4010, ART-4012, ART-4071 (2023 PYQ on colonial archaeologists).
**Trap:** Founding (1861) and the DG appointment (1871) are different dates in the sources; do not state "first DG in 1861" without qualification.
**Mains hook:** ASI's monument mandate is the legal and institutional layer for every protected-site claim.
**Back:** *"Sixty-one founded, seventy-one made DG: Cunningham's survey."*

### Flashcard ART-4012 — ASI Underwater Archaeology Wing
**Front:** What specialised wing does ASI maintain for submerged sites?
**Definition (learn):** The Underwater Archaeology Wing of ASI, the ASI unit for submerged and maritime archaeology.
**Breakdown:**
- Part of ASI's archaeological functions.
- Relevant to submerged and maritime sites (the owner names the wing only).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §4, §13.5 (PDF pp. 956–957). ⚠️ Wing's founding year, location and projects not in owner; not verified.
**Links:** ART-4011, ART-4008.
**Trap:** Do not state the wing's founding year, its headquarters or any named underwater site without a dated source.
**Mains hook:** Underwater heritage shows ASI's mandate beyond surface monuments, but the legal basis for submerged remains needs its own verification.
**Back:** *"Under the water too: ASI's wing for submerged heritage."*

### Flashcard ART-4013 — AMASR spatial rule: prohibited and regulated areas
**Front:** What are the prohibited and regulated areas around a centrally protected monument?
**Definition (learn):** Domestic legal zones around a centrally protected monument in which construction and repair are restricted or require permission under the AMASR framework.
**Breakdown:**
- Prohibited area: first 100 metres from the monument.
- Regulated area: the next zone, which the owner states as 200 metres.
- Permissions come from the competent authority under the statute.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §5, §13.6 (100 m / 200 m). ⚠️ ✅ Wikipedia AMASR page fetched 2026-11-01 gives the prohibited area as 100 m in both sections, but gives the regulated area as **200 m** in its Provisions section and **300 m** in its National Monuments Authority section.
**Links:** ART-4008, ART-4014, ART-4068 (buffer-zone trap).
**Trap:** Do not state the regulated distance as 300 m or 200 m without citing the source; the two statements in the Wikipedia page conflict and must be flagged, not silently resolved. Never equate these zones with UNESCO buffer zones.
**Mains hook:** Construction pressure is controlled by a domestic spatial regime; name it precisely with its distances and competent authority.
**Back:** *"Hundred no-go, then the regulated ring: Indian law, not UNESCO's buffer."*

### Flashcard ART-4014 — National Monuments Authority (NMA)
**Front:** What is the National Monuments Authority and what does it regulate?
**Definition (learn):** A statutory body under the Ministry of Culture set up to regulate construction, repair and development in prohibited and regulated areas around centrally protected monuments.
**Breakdown:**
- Legal basis: AMASR (Amendment and Validation) Act, 2010 (✅ Wikipedia fetched 2026-11-01).
- Year set up: 2011 (✅ Wikipedia fetched 2026-11-01).
- Functions: process applications through an online portal, frame monument-specific bye-laws, categorise monuments, recommend to the central government; has a chairperson and members appointed by the Government.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ Not in the owner files; the owner's §3 table names "NMMA" without expanding it, so the NMA/NMMA relation is unresolved in this repository.
**Links:** ART-4008, ART-4013, ART-4024 (owner abbreviation NMMA).
**Trap:** NMA regulates construction near monuments; ASI protects and maintains monuments. Do not give NMA's membership or chairperson by name.
**Mains hook:** The NMA is the regulatory layer that makes the AMASR zones enforceable against urban expansion.
**Back:** *"Eleven set up under the 2010 Act: the NMA polices the monument ring."*

### Flashcard ART-4015 — INTACH (Indian National Trust for Art and Cultural Heritage)
**Front:** What is INTACH, when was it founded and what is its mandate?
**Definition (learn):** A charitable non-profit trust, registered as a society, working for awareness, documentation, conservation and policy on India's living, built and natural heritage.
**Breakdown:**
- Founded: 1984, New Delhi (27 January 1984 per infobox).
- Registered under the Societies Registration Act, 1860.
- Mandate: awareness, protection of living/built/natural heritage, documenting unprotected buildings, policy and legal interventions, conservation training, emergency response, sponsorship.
- Headquarters: 71 Lodhi Estate, New Delhi.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ Wikipedia INTACH page fetched 2026-11-01. ⚠️ INTACH is **not** covered in the owner files; the brief-level card rests on the fetched page only.
**Links:** ART-4008 (statutory ASI), ART-4011, ART-4032 (scheme-type partnerships).
**Trap:** INTACH is a non-statutory civil-society body; it does not carry AMASR or ASI legal powers.
**Mains hook:** Civil-society documentation fills the gap where statutes protect only centrally listed monuments.
**Back:** *"Eighty-four, civil-society trust: INTACH documents what the statute misses."*

### Flashcard ART-4016 — Sahitya Akademi
**Front:** When was Sahitya Akademi founded, under which ministry, and what is its remit?
**Definition (learn):** India's national academy of letters, promoting Indian literature and its translation across recognised languages.
**Breakdown:**
- Government resolution: 15 December 1952; inaugurated 12 March 1954.
- Parent: Ministry of Culture.
- Remit: literature in recognised languages; awards and fellowships.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §4 (PDF p. 964); ✅ Wikipedia fetched 2026-11-01 (1954; government resolution 1952; Ministry of Culture; autonomous).
**Links:** ART-4017 (24 languages), ART-4018 (SNA), ART-4020 (LKA).
**Trap:** The owner gives "est. 1954" which matches the inauguration; the 1952 resolution is the constituting act. Quote both if asked.
**Mains hook:** Literary transmission is an institutional function; Sahitya Akademi is the literary counterpart to SNA and LKA.
**Back:** *"Fifty-two decided, fifty-four opened: Sahitya Akademi for letters."*

### Flashcard ART-4017 — Sahitya Akademi: 24 languages and its awards
**Front:** How many languages does Sahitya Akademi recognise and which awards and fellowships does it give?
**Definition (learn):** Recognition covers 24 languages: 22 Eighth Schedule languages plus English and Rajasthani.
**Breakdown:**
- Sahitya Akademi Award (Rs 1 lakh per language per Wikipedia; ⚠️ amount date-bound).
- Bhasha Samman; Sahitya Akademi Fellowship (lifetime).
- Dr Ananda Coomaraswamy Fellowship and Premchand Fellowship (owner).
- Yuva Puraskar, founded 2011 per Wikipedia.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §4 (PDF p. 964); ✅ Wikipedia fetched 2026-11-01 (24 languages; 22 Eighth Schedule).
**Links:** ART-4016, ART-4018.
**Trap:** Rajasthani and English are recognised by the Akademi but Rajasthani is not an Eighth Schedule language; do not say "22 languages" for the Akademi's recognition.
**Mains hook:** Language recognition by an academy is a policy choice, not a Constitution-level grant.
**Back:** *"Twenty-two plus two: the Akademi's twenty-four."*

### Flashcard ART-4018 — Sangeet Natak Akademi (SNA)
**Front:** When was SNA set up, under which ministry, and what awards does it give?
**Definition (learn):** India's national academy of music, dance and drama, an autonomous body under the Ministry of Culture.
**Breakdown:**
- Ministry resolution: 31 May 1952 (per Wikipedia).
- Functional and inaugurated: 1953, inaugurated 28 January 1953 by Rajendra Prasad; first chairman P.V. Rajamannar.
- Awards: Sangeet Natak Akademi Award (Puraskar); Fellowship (Ratna Sadasya; first Fellow 1954); Ustad Bismillah Khan Yuva Puraskar (2006); Tagore Ratna and Tagore Puraskar (2012).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §4 (est. 1952; PDF pp. 965–967); ✅ Wikipedia fetched 2026-11-01 (1953 functional; 1952 ministry act).
**Links:** ART-4019 (NSD), ART-4016, ART-4020 (LKA).
**Trap:** Owner says "est. 1952"; the Wikipedia page dates the start of functions to 1953. Both are sourced; cite which year the question uses.
**Mains hook:** SNA is the first call for performing-arts safeguarding; name it rather than "cultural bodies".
**Back:** *"Fifty-two set, fifty-three seated: SNA for song, dance and drama."*

### Flashcard ART-4019 — National School of Drama and SNA
**Front:** What is the status of the National School of Drama in relation to SNA?
**Definition (learn):** The owner states that the National School of Drama was parented by SNA and became independent in 1975.
**Breakdown:**
- Owner claim: parented by SNA, independent since 1975 (PDF pp. 965–967).
- Wikipedia's SNA page does not mention NSD, so the claim is not confirmed by the fetched page.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §4, §13.5. ⚠️ 1975 date not confirmed by a second source in this build.
**Links:** ART-4018.
**Trap:** Do not state the NSD's founding year or its location without verification.
**Mains hook:** Use NSD only as an example of an institution separating from a parent body, with the date flagged.
**Back:** *"Born under SNA, own school since seventy-five (verify first)."*

### Flashcard ART-4020 — Lalit Kala Akademi (LKA)
**Front:** When was LKA founded and what are its flagship events?
**Definition (learn):** National academy of fine arts (visual arts), autonomous under the Ministry of Culture.
**Breakdown:**
- Founded 1954 (5 August 1954, New Delhi).
- National Exhibition of Art (NEA): its most prestigious yearly event.
- Triennale-India, begun 1968.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §4 (PDF pp. 967–968); ✅ Wikipedia fetched 2026-11-01 (1954; NEA; Triennale-India 1968).
**Links:** ART-4018, ART-4016, ART-4006.
**Trap:** Do not confuse LKA (fine arts) with SNA (performing arts) or with NGMA (not in owner files).
**Mains hook:** The visual-arts academy is the institutional counterpart to SNA in the arts ecology.
**Back:** *"Fifty-four founded, sixty-eight triennale: LKA for the visual arts."*

### Flashcard ART-4021 — Anthropological Survey of India
**Front:** What is the Anthropological Survey of India's role in cultural institutions?
**Definition (learn):** The national body for anthropological research on India's peoples and communities.
**Breakdown:**
- Role: anthropological research; documents communities relevant to living heritage.
- Owner names it only in a list of institutions.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §3, §13.5 (PDF pp. 955–971). ⚠️ No founding year, ministry or project list is in the owner or fetched pages; none is stated here.
**Links:** ART-4004 (ICH community practices), ART-4018.
**Trap:** Do not place ASI's archaeology mandate under the Anthropological Survey; they are separate.
**Mains hook:** Community documentation links anthropology to living-heritage safeguarding.
**Back:** *"Anthropology of peoples, not stones: ASI's sister body."*

### Flashcard ART-4022 — National Archives of India
**Front:** When was the National Archives of India established and what does it hold?
**Definition (learn):** The repository of non-current records of the Government of India, held in trust for administrators and scholars.
**Breakdown:**
- Established 1891 at Calcutta as the Imperial Record Department (Wikipedia).
- Parent: Ministry of Culture.
- Location: New Delhi.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ Wikipedia National Archives page fetched 2026-11-01; ✅ owner 14 §13.5 (documentary records). Owner gives no date.
**Links:** ART-4006 (MoW), ART-4025 (NMM).
**Trap:** Do not confuse the Archives (government records) with the National Mission for Manuscripts (private and community manuscripts).
**Mains hook:** Documentary custody is the least-contested heritage protection and the base for any provenance claim.
**Back:** *"Ninety-one records: the Archives keeps the government's memory."*

### Flashcard ART-4023 — Asiatic Society
**Front:** What does the Asiatic Society do according to the owner?
**Definition (learn):** A research and library institution for oriental studies.
**Breakdown:**
- Oriental studies and research.
- Library and documentation.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §13.5 (role only). ⚠️ Founding year and location are not in the owner and are not stated here.
**Links:** ART-4022.
**Trap:** Do not assume its founding year without verification.
**Mains hook:** Oriental scholarship institutions preserve manuscripts; name the mandate precisely.
**Back:** *"Oriental studies, scholarly library: research, not monuments."*

### Flashcard ART-4024 — NMMA (owner abbreviation, unresolved)
**Front:** What does the owner's abbreviation NMMA stand for and what does it do?
**Definition (learn):** The owner lists "NMMA" among India's cultural institutions with the role "monument/antiquity documentation".
**Breakdown:**
- Owner role: monument and antiquity documentation.
- Expansion: not given in the owner files.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §3, §13.5 (role). ⚠️ The expansion is not stated in the repository and is not guessed here.
**Links:** ART-4014 (NMA, a different statutory body), ART-4011.
**Trap:** Do not conflate NMMA with the National Monuments Authority (NMA) or with the National Mission for Manuscripts; the repository uses NMMA in one place only.
**Mains hook:** Use the body name only after confirming its expansion from an official source.
**Back:** *"Initials without a name: check before you cite."*

### Flashcard ART-4025 — National Mission for Manuscripts (NMM)
**Front:** What is the National Mission for Manuscripts' role?
**Definition (learn):** A national mission for the survey, documentation and digitisation of manuscripts.
**Breakdown:**
- Role: manuscript documentation and digitisation (owner).
- Pairs with the National Archives for documentary custody.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §13.5 (role). ⚠️ Founding year not in owner files or fetched pages.
**Links:** ART-4006, ART-4022.
**Trap:** Do not assume NMM holds manuscripts itself; its role is documentation and digitisation.
**Mains hook:** Digitisation is the first defence for fragile manuscripts; name NMM when the demand is about manuscripts.
**Back:** *"Mission for manuscripts: find them, copy them, catalogue them."*

### Flashcard ART-4026 — IGNCA (Indira Gandhi National Centre for the Arts)
**Front:** When was IGNCA launched and how is it organised?
**Definition (learn):** An autonomous institute under the Ministry of Culture as a resource centre and research body for arts and humanities.
**Breakdown:**
- Launched 19 November 1985; Trust constituted and registered 24 March 1987 (Wikipedia).
- Functions: resource centre for written, oral and visual sources; arts research and reference publishing; tribal and folk arts division; performances and seminars; arts-science dialogue.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ Wikipedia IGNCA page fetched 2026-11-01; ✅ owner 14 §13.5 (arts research, documentation). No statute named (Wikipedia).
**Links:** ART-4025, ART-4022.
**Trap:** Launch year (1985) and Trust registration (1987) differ; IGNCA is not a statutory body per the fetched page.
**Mains hook:** IGNCA is the holistic-arts research body; its statute-free status makes its funding and mandate policy-dependent.
**Back:** *"Eighty-five launched, eighty-seven trusted: IGNCA holds the arts together."*

### Flashcard ART-4027 — ICCR (Indian Council for Cultural Relations)
**Front:** What is ICCR's role?
**Definition (learn):** The body for cultural diplomacy and international cultural relations (owner role).
**Breakdown:**
- Role: cultural relations with other nations (owner).
- Founding year and parent ministry not in the owner files.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §13.5 (role only). ⚠️ Founding and ministry not verified here.
**Links:** ART-4133 (soft power), ART-4032.
**Trap:** Do not present ICCR as a heritage conservation body; its mandate is relations.
**Mains hook:** Cultural diplomacy is a soft-power instrument distinct from domestic heritage protection.
**Back:** *"Relations, not repair: ICCR carries culture abroad."*

### Flashcard ART-4028 — CCRT (Centre for Cultural Resources and Training)
**Front:** What does CCRT do?
**Definition (learn):** The body for cultural-resource training (owner role).
**Breakdown:**
- Role: cultural-resource training (owner).
- Founding year and parent not in the owner files.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §13.5 (role). ⚠️ Expansion, founding year and location not verified in this build.
**Links:** ART-4018, ART-4026.
**Trap:** Do not state CCRT's founding year, programmes or courses without a dated source.
**Mains hook:** Training institutions complement academies in transmitting skills.
**Back:** *"Resources and training: the cultural-skill school."*

### Flashcard ART-4029 — Article 29: minority culture right
**Front:** What does Article 29 protect?
**Definition (learn):** A fundamental right of any section of citizens with a distinct language, script or culture to conserve it.
**Breakdown:**
- Protects the right to conserve language, script and culture.
- Owner frames it as the minority-culture protection.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §4, §13.6 (PDF pp. 984–985). Constitutional text not fetched in this build.
**Links:** ART-4030, ART-4031.
**Trap:** Article 29 is a conservation right of a section of citizens, not a State duty to monuments (that is Article 49).
**Mains hook:** Cultural rights of minorities tie heritage to constitutional rights; Polity owns the doctrine.
**Back:** *"Twenty-nine guards the tongue: a group's right to keep its culture."*

### Flashcard ART-4030 — Article 49: State duty to protect monuments
**Front:** What does Article 49 require of the State?
**Definition (learn):** The State must protect monuments and objects of national importance from spoliation, disfigurement, destruction, removal, disposal or export.
**Breakdown:**
- Verbs: spoliation, disfigurement, destruction, removal, disposal, export.
- Covers both damage and illicit export.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §4, §13.6 (PDF p. 984). Quoted from the owner's text.
**Links:** ART-4008, ART-4009, ART-4029.
**Trap:** Article 49 is a Directive-level obligation of the State (Part IV); do not present it as an enforceable Fundamental Right. ⚠️ Polity-level check outside this file.
**Mains hook:** Use Article 49's exact verbs to show that illicit export is constitutionally as well as statutorily wrong.
**Back:** *"Forty-nine's five sins: spoil, deface, destroy, remove, export."*

### Flashcard ART-4031 — Article 51A(f): citizen's duty to preserve heritage
**Front:** What fundamental duty concerns heritage?
**Definition (learn):** Every citizen must value and preserve the rich heritage of the composite culture.
**Breakdown:**
- Part IVA, Article 51A, clause (f).
- Duty of the citizen, not the State.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §4, §13.6 (PDF pp. 984–985). ⚠️ Clause-letter and insertion history not re-verified in this build.
**Links:** ART-4029, ART-4030.
**Trap:** Do not state that 51A(f) is enforceable in court; it is a fundamental duty for citizens. Polity owns the doctrine.
**Mains hook:** Duty-based heritage argument needs both State and citizen roles; cite 51A(f) for the citizen dimension.
**Back:** *"Fifty-one-A-F: each citizen values the composite culture."*

### Flashcard ART-4032 — Adopt a Heritage (participation scheme)
**Front:** What is Adopt a Heritage and what does it not transfer?
**Definition (learn):** A participation scheme that lets a public or private partner support visitor amenities at a protected site; the owner records the earlier term "Monument Mitra" and a later "Adopt a Heritage 2.0" framing.
**Breakdown:**
- Type: scheme (executive programme).
- Scope: visitor amenities, not structural conservation.
- Monument stays under ASI and AMASR.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §13.8C (PDF p. 988, "Monument Mitra"). ⚠️ The "2.0" framing and ministry locus are owner-cited to PIB; the PIB page could not be fetched (403) in this build.
**Links:** ART-4010, ART-4033, ART-4008.
**Trap:** Adoption does not hand over a monument; any answer saying partners were "given" monuments to maintain is a legal error.
**Mains hook:** Partnerships fix amenities and funding gaps; protection remains statutory.
**Back:** *"Adopt the amenities, keep the monument: partners serve visitors, not stone."*

### Flashcard ART-4033 — National Culture Fund (NCF)
**Front:** What is the National Culture Fund and when was it established?
**Definition (learn):** A funding mechanism to promote art and culture, letting institutions and individuals support it directly as partners.
**Breakdown:**
- Established 1996 under the Charitable Endowments Act, 1890 (owner).
- Type: fund, not a conservation authority.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §13.8C (PDF p. 990); fully local-sourced.
**Links:** ART-4032, ART-4001.
**Trap:** NCF channels money; it does not carry out or regulate conservation.
**Mains hook:** A funding channel is the right instrument for "money" problems; AMASR is the instrument for fabric.
**Back:** *"Ninety-six funds the culture: money, not monuments' law."*

### Flashcard ART-4034 — PRASHAD and HRIDAY: bounded gap
**Front:** Can PRASHAD and HRIDAY be cited with their full forms, ministry and launch years?
**Definition (learn):** The owner records that neither scheme is carried by any local source and neither was verified officially.
**Breakdown:**
- Status: bounded gap; not verified in this repository.
- Safe answer: describe the mechanism (pilgrimage and heritage-city infrastructure through the tourism ministry) and state that the named schemes are outside verified evidence.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §13.8C (explicit bounded gap). Not fetched in this build.
**Links:** ART-4032, ART-4010.
**Trap:** Do not write PRASHAD or HRIDAY's full forms, ministry, launch year or components.
**Mains hook:** Admit a verification gap rather than inventing a scheme name.
**Back:** *"Two schemes, no proof: name the mechanism, not the acronym."*

### Flashcard ART-4035 — India's WHS count: as-of 2026-11-01
**Front:** How many World Heritage Sites does India have and what is the breakdown?
**Definition (learn):** The count of inscribed World Heritage Sites for India, time-varying.
**Breakdown:**
- 45 in all: 37 cultural, 7 natural, 1 mixed (Khangchendzonga National Park, 2016).
- Natural: Great Himalayan National Park, Kaziranga, Keoladeo, Manas, Nanda Devi and Valley of Flowers, Sundarbans, Western Ghats.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ UNESCO WHC India state page fetched **2026-11-01** (45: 37 + 7 + 1). Owner pages: "verified 4 September 2026" and "21 July 2026, 44 properties" — the owner's own dates conflict; the live page is the reference.
**Links:** ART-4002, ART-4038, ART-4039, ART-4040, ART-4061 (Santiniketan), ART-4062 (Hoysalas).
**Trap:** The owner's "36 cultural + 7 + 1 = 44" table is out of date against the live page; do not cite 44 as today's total.
**Mains hook:** State the count with the access date and the classification (cultural/natural/mixed).
**Back:** *"Forty-five on the day: thirty-seven, seven, one."*

### Flashcard ART-4036 — India's ICH count and safeguarding status: as-of 2026-11-01
**Front:** How many Indian elements are on the UNESCO ICH Representative List and are any on the Urgent or Good-Practice lists?
**Definition (learn):** The live UNESCO state page for India, time-varying.
**Breakdown:**
- Representative List: 16 elements.
- Urgent Safeguarding List: none (all listed as RL).
- Register of Good Safeguarding Practices: none.
- Pending: "Henna: rituals, aesthetic and social practices" nomination, on-going for 2026, not inscribed.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ UNESCO ICH India page fetched **2026-11-01**. ✅ Convention ratified 9 September 2005. The owner's "15 items as on August 2025" is pre-Deepavali and must not be carried forward.
**Links:** ART-4004, ART-4005, ART-4041 (Deepavali), ART-4056 (Garba).
**Trap:** Do not use 15 (pre-2025 book count); do not claim an Indian element is on the Urgent list.
**Mains hook:** Recognition ahead of safeguarding; the absence of Urgent-list elements is a current, dated fact.
**Back:** *"Sixteen recognised, none rescued: the ICH check-up."*

### Flashcard ART-4037 — Memory of the World: India's count (owner-stated)
**Front:** How many Indian inscriptions does the owner give for the Memory of the World International Register?
**Definition (learn):** The owner's documentary-register count.
**Breakdown:**
- Owner: 14 International Register inscriptions after the Bhagavadgita and Natyashastra manuscript additions (2025).
- Owner gives "verified 21 Jul 2026" and "4 Sep 2026" in different places.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §4, §5, §13.4. ⚠️ Not re-fetched in this build; the count is dated only by the owner.
**Links:** ART-4006, ART-4022.
**Trap:** Do not treat the 14 as current without a live UNESCO MoW check.
**Mains hook:** Documentary inscriptions are dated and need re-verification.
**Back:** *"Fourteen by the owner's day: check the live register."*

### Flashcard ART-4038 — Moidams — Mound-Burial System of the Ahom Dynasty (2024)
**Front:** What is the Moidams WHS, when was it inscribed and why is it significant?
**Definition (learn):** A Tai-Ahom royal necropolis of mound-covered vaults at Charaideo, Assam, inscribed as India's 43rd WHS.
**Breakdown:**
- Inscribed 26 July 2024 (owner; year 2024 ✅ UNESCO list fetched).
- Criteria (iii) and (iv) (owner).
- Form: mound over a hollow vault, shrine and octagonal wall, continuing ancestor-worship landscape.
- Pressures: rainfall, erosion, vegetation growth (owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §8, §13.8; ✅ UNESCO list shows 2024.
**Links:** ART-4035, ART-4069 (ownership trap), ART-4073 (2026 PYQ).
**Trap:** Moidams are not ordinary earthen tumuli; the Ahom political history belongs to medieval history.
**Mains hook:** A funerary landscape is a living conservation case: access and ritual use are management variables.
**Back:** *"Charaideo mounds, twenty-four: Ahom kings in vaulted hills."*

### Flashcard ART-4039 — Maratha Military Landscapes of India (2025)
**Front:** What is the Maratha Military Landscapes WHS and what is its status?
**Definition (learn):** A serial World Heritage property of forts across Maharashtra and Tamil Nadu, inscribed in 2025 as India's 44th property.
**Breakdown:**
- Serial property; owner says twelve forts.
- States: Maharashtra and Tamil Nadu (owner).
- Position: 44th (owner) — superseded in the count by Sarnath (45th).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §13.8A; ✅ UNESCO list shows 2025. ⚠️ The count of twelve forts is owner-stated, not re-fetched.
**Links:** ART-4035, ART-4040.
**Trap:** Do not describe Maratha Landscapes as India's latest WHS after Sarnath (2026).
**Mains hook:** Serial property shows that one WHS can span multiple states and sites.
**Back:** *"Forts in a chain, forty-four: Maratha landscape, 2025."*

### Flashcard ART-4040 — Ancient Buddhist Site of Sarnath (2026)
**Front:** What is the Sarnath WHS, when was it inscribed and what is its position?
**Definition (learn):** A serial Buddhist archaeological property in Uttar Pradesh, inscribed 25 July 2026 as India's 45th WHS.
**Breakdown:**
- Serial property with two archaeological zones (owner).
- Former Tentative-List-only status is obsolete.
- Inscription 2026 (✅ UNESCO list fetched 2026-11-01).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §8, §13.8A; ✅ UNESCO list shows 2026 and total 45. Exact day 25 July from owner.
**Links:** ART-4035, ART-4039, ART-4064 (Tentative-list trap).
**Trap:** Tentative-list status is not inscription; Sarnath's status changed from list to inscription.
**Mains hook:** The newest WHS is the most time-sensitive fact; always state the date.
**Back:** *"Sarnath, twenty-five July twenty-six: forty-five."*

### Flashcard ART-4041 — Deepavali ICH inscription (2025)
**Front:** When and where was Deepavali inscribed and what did it do to India's total?
**Definition (learn):** Deepavali inscribed on the Representative List of ICH in 2025, bringing India to 16 elements.
**Breakdown:**
- Inscribed 10 December 2025 at the 20th session of the Intergovernmental Committee, Red Fort, Delhi (owner).
- First time India hosted the session (owner).
- Nomination prepared by Sangeet Natak Akademi under the Ministry of Culture (owner).
- Among 67 global nominations assessed (owner; ⚠️ count not re-fetched).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ UNESCO ICH India page (2025 entry, fetched 2026-11-01); ✅ owner 14 §13.8B.
**Links:** ART-4036, ART-4074 (latest-inclusion trap), ART-4018 (SNA nominated).
**Trap:** Deepavali is the current latest Indian Representative List element; older PYQ answers (Garba 2023) are date-bound.
**Mains hook:** A festival inscription shows ICH recognition of everyday practice; still not Urgent-list safeguarding.
**Back:** *"Deepavali, ten December: Red Fort, sixteenth on the list."*

### Flashcard ART-4042 — Kutiyattam, Sanskrit theatre (2008)
**Front:** Which Indian element was inscribed first in the ICH ledger and in which domain?
**Definition (learn):** Kutiyattam, the Sanskrit theatre tradition of Kerala, inscribed in 2008 in the performing-arts domain.
**Breakdown:**
- Domain: performing arts.
- Region: Kerala.
- Owner: "the folder's oldest Indian inscription" among the ledger rows.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ UNESCO ICH India page (2008); ✅ owner 14 §13.8B.
**Links:** ART-4036, ART-4047 (Kalbelia), ART-4048 (Mudiyettu).
**Trap:** Kutiyattam is a Sanskrit theatre tradition, not a folk performance; 2008 is shared with Vedic chanting and Ramlila.
**Mains hook:** Classical performing traditions show that ICH covers living theatre, not only folk culture.
**Back:** *"Oh-eight, Sanskrit stage: Kutiyattam leads the Indian list."*

### Flashcard ART-4043 — Tradition of Vedic chanting (2008)
**Front:** When was Vedic chanting inscribed and what domain?
**Definition (learn):** Pan-Indian oral tradition of Vedic chanting, inscribed in 2008.
**Breakdown:**
- Domain: oral traditions and expressions.
- Pan-Indian scope.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ UNESCO ICH India page (2008); ✅ owner 14 §13.8B.
**Links:** ART-4042, ART-4049 (Ladakh Buddhist chanting).
**Trap:** Vedic chanting is pan-Indian, not a single-state element; do not give a state.
**Mains hook:** Oral transmission is ICH's central idea: knowledge carried by the voice.
**Back:** *"Oh-eight, voice on the Vedas: oral knowledge across India."*

### Flashcard ART-4044 — Ramlila (2008)
**Front:** What is the Ramlila entry and when was it inscribed?
**Definition (learn):** The traditional performance of the Ramayana in North India, inscribed in 2008 under performing arts and social practice.
**Breakdown:**
- Domain: performing arts / social practice.
- Region: North India.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ UNESCO ICH India page (2008); ✅ owner 14 §13.8B.
**Links:** ART-4042, ART-4045 (Ramman).
**Trap:** Ramlila is a performance tradition; do not confuse it with Ramman (Garhwal, 2009).
**Mains hook:** Community performance renewed each year is a classic ICH example.
**Back:** *"Oh-eight, Ram on stage: the Ramayana performed by communities."*

### Flashcard ART-4045 — Ramman, Garhwal Himalayas (2009)
**Front:** What is Ramman and when was it inscribed?
**Definition (learn):** The religious festival and ritual theatre of the Garhwal Himalayas, Uttarakhand, inscribed 2009.
**Breakdown:**
- Domain: social practices, rituals and festive events.
- Region: Uttarakhand.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ UNESCO ICH India page (2009); ✅ owner 14 §13.8B (topics 10, 13).
**Links:** ART-4044, ART-4048 (Mudiyettu, ritual theatre).
**Trap:** Ramman is a Garhwal ritual festival; do not place it in Kerala or Rajasthan.
**Mains hook:** A festival is ICH only when the community's continuing practice is the element.
**Back:** *"Oh-nine, Garhwal's festival theatre: Ramman."*

### Flashcard ART-4046 — Chhau dance (2010)
**Front:** Which states does Chhau dance span and when was it inscribed?
**Definition (learn):** A performing-arts tradition of Odisha, Jharkhand and West Bengal, inscribed 2010.
**Breakdown:**
- Domain: performing arts.
- States: Odisha, Jharkhand, West Bengal (owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ UNESCO ICH India page (2010); ✅ owner 14 §13.8B (topic 09).
**Links:** ART-4047, ART-4048.
**Trap:** Chhau spans three states; do not attribute it to one.
**Mains hook:** Shared regional traditions complicate state-level claims; the inscription names the multi-state practice.
**Back:** *"Ten, three states, one mask dance: Chhau."*

### Flashcard ART-4047 — Kalbelia folk songs and dances of Rajasthan (2010)
**Front:** What is Kalbelia and what instrument is associated with it?
**Definition (learn):** Folk songs and dances of the Kalbelia community of Rajasthan, inscribed 2010.
**Breakdown:**
- Domain: performing arts.
- Instrument: the Been/Pungi (owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ UNESCO ICH India page (2010); ✅ owner 14 §13.8B (PDF p. 490).
**Links:** ART-4046, ART-4042.
**Trap:** Kalbelia is community-specific; do not generalise it to all Rajasthani folk music.
**Mains hook:** Community-linked folk performance is exactly the practice that needs transmission support.
**Back:** *"Ten, Rajasthan's Been dance: Kalbelia."*

### Flashcard ART-4048 — Mudiyettu, Kerala (2010)
**Front:** What is Mudiyettu and when was it inscribed?
**Definition (learn):** A ritual theatre and dance drama of Kerala, inscribed 2010.
**Breakdown:**
- Domain: performing arts / ritual.
- Region: Kerala.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ UNESCO ICH India page (2010); ✅ owner 14 §13.8B (topic 10).
**Links:** ART-4042 (Kutiyattam, also Kerala), ART-4045.
**Trap:** Mudiyettu (2010) is not Kutiyattam (2008); both are Kerala ritual theatre but separate entries.
**Mains hook:** Two Kerala performance forms inscribed separately show the list's element-by-element approach.
**Back:** *"Ten, Kerala's ritual drama: Mudiyettu, not Kutiyattam."*

### Flashcard ART-4049 — Buddhist chanting of Ladakh (2012)
**Front:** What is the Ladakh Buddhist chanting element and when was it inscribed?
**Definition (learn):** Recitation of sacred Buddhist texts in the trans-Himalayan Ladakh region, inscribed 2012.
**Breakdown:**
- Domain: oral traditions.
- Region: Ladakh, Jammu and Kashmir (owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ UNESCO ICH India page (2012); ✅ owner 14 §13.8B (topics 08, 13).
**Links:** ART-4043, ART-4048.
**Trap:** Ladakh's element is recitation of Buddhist texts, not a monastic architecture element.
**Mains hook:** Recitation traditions link oral transmission to religious identity.
**Back:** *"Twelve, Ladakh's chant: Buddhist texts by voice."*

### Flashcard ART-4050 — Sankirtana of Manipur (2013)
**Front:** What is Sankirtana and what does the owner say about it?
**Definition (learn):** Ritual singing, drumming and dancing mainly by the Vaishnava community of Manipur, inscribed 2013.
**Breakdown:**
- Domain: performing arts / ritual.
- Community: Vaishnava (owner).
- Links to Manipuri dance (owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ UNESCO ICH India page (2013); ✅ owner 14 §13.8B (PDF pp. 443, 474).
**Links:** ART-4044, ART-4046.
**Trap:** Sankirtana is ritual singing, not Manipuri dance; the dance is a related but separate art.
**Mains hook:** A devotional performance tradition shows how ICH covers worship and art together.
**Back:** *"Thirteen, Manipur's Vaishnava song: Sankirtana."*

### Flashcard ART-4051 — Thatheras brass and copper craft (2014)
**Front:** Which craft is the folder's only craft element on the ICH list and where is it practised?
**Definition (learn):** Traditional brass and copper utensil making among the Thatheras of Jandiala Guru, Punjab, inscribed 2014.
**Breakdown:**
- Domain: traditional craftsmanship.
- Place: Jandiala Guru, Punjab.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ UNESCO ICH India page (2014); ✅ owner 14 §13.8B (topic 12).
**Links:** ART-4007 (GI is a different category), ART-4036.
**Trap:** Thatheras craft is an ICH element, not a GI registration; the GI and ICH categories are distinct.
**Mains hook:** Craft transmission is an economic and skill question; ICH recognition does not guarantee livelihood.
**Back:** *"Fourteen, Jandiala's brass and copper: the Thatheras craft."*

### Flashcard ART-4052 — Yoga (2016)
**Front:** When was Yoga inscribed and under which domain?
**Definition (learn):** Yoga inscribed in 2016 in the domain of knowledge and practices concerning nature and the universe; pan-Indian.
**Breakdown:**
- Domain: knowledge and practices concerning nature and the universe.
- Scope: pan-Indian (owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ UNESCO ICH India page (2016); ✅ owner 14 §13.8B (topic 13).
**Links:** ART-4036, ART-4054 (Kumbh Mela 2017).
**Trap:** Yoga's ICH inscription is a practice-and-knowledge entry, not an Indian monument or a sport.
**Mains hook:** Knowledge practices extend ICH into health and philosophy; the inscription is about transmission.
**Back:** *"Sixteen, knowledge of nature: Yoga, pan-Indian."*

### Flashcard ART-4053 — Nowruz / Novruz: multinational, with a date conflict
**Front:** Is Nowruz an Indian-only inscription and in which year is it recorded?
**Definition (learn):** A multinational social practice and festive event shared by several States Parties; India is listed among them.
**Breakdown:**
- Multinational: not an Indian-only element.
- Owner year: 2016.
- UNESCO India page year: 2024 for the multinational entry (fetched 2026-11-01).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §13.8B (multinational flag); ✅ UNESCO ICH India page lists "Nawrouz/Novruz/Nowrouz" with year 2024. ⚠️ The owner's 2016 and the live page's 2024 conflict; flagged, not resolved.
**Links:** ART-4036, ART-4041.
**Trap:** "India-only" questions must exclude Nowruz; the year must be checked on the live page.
**Mains hook:** Shared inscriptions show ICH as a transnational category, not a national prize.
**Back:** *"Shared spring, not Indian alone: Nowruz is multinational."*

### Flashcard ART-4054 — Kumbh Mela (2017)
**Front:** When was Kumbh Mela inscribed and what description does the UNESCO graphic give?
**Definition (learn):** A social practice, ritual and festive event inscribed in 2017.
**Breakdown:**
- Domain: social practices, rituals and festive events.
- Owner quotes the ICH graphic: "the world's largest peaceful gathering".
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ UNESCO ICH India page (2017); ✅ owner 14 §13.8B (PDF p. 735; OCR-garbled source).
**Links:** ART-4052, ART-4056 (Garba), ART-4055 (Durga Puja).
**Trap:** The "world's largest gathering" wording is UNESCO's description; do not present it as a measured count.
**Mains hook:** Mass religious gatherings are ICH as social practice; safeguarding means crowd and environmental management.
**Back:** *"Seventeen, the great gathering: Kumbh Mela."*

### Flashcard ART-4055 — Durga Puja in Kolkata (2021)
**Front:** When was Durga Puja inscribed and what does the owner describe as its elements?
**Definition (learn):** Durga Puja in Kolkata, West Bengal, inscribed 2021 under social practices and festive events.
**Breakdown:**
- Domain: social practices and festive events.
- Elements (owner): performances, rituals, artistry, the making of idols and pandals.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ UNESCO ICH India page (2021); ✅ owner 14 §13.8B (PDF p. 735; topics 12, 13).
**Links:** ART-4054, ART-4041.
**Trap:** The inscription is for Durga Puja in Kolkata, not every Durga Puja in India.
**Mains hook:** The inscription names a city's festival, showing that ICH is locally scoped.
**Back:** *"Twenty-one, Kolkata's Puja: festival in the city, not the nation."*

### Flashcard ART-4056 — Garba of Gujarat (2023) and the PYQ "latest inclusion"
**Front:** What is Garba of Gujarat and why does the CSE 2024 "latest inclusion" item point to Garba?
**Definition (learn):** A Navratri circle dance of Gujarat around a lamp-lit earthen pot (Garbha deep), inscribed 2023.
**Breakdown:**
- Domain: social practices / performing arts.
- Region: Gujarat.
- Owner: 2023 nomination described as a Navratri circle dance.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ UNESCO ICH India page (2023); ✅ owner 14 §13.8B (PDF p. 482). At the CSE 2024 paper's date Garba was the latest of the four options.
**Links:** ART-4074 (date-bound trap), ART-4041 (Deepavali today), ART-4072 (2024 PYQ).
**Trap:** Garba was the latest in 2024; Deepavali (2025) is the latest today.
**Mains hook:** Date-bound facts need an explicit as-of date in every answer.
**Back:** *"Twenty-three, Navratri circle: Garba then, Deepavali now."*

### Flashcard ART-4057 — Nalanda Mahavihara (2016)
**Front:** What is the Nalanda WHS, when was it inscribed and what are its key features?
**Definition (learn):** The Archaeological Site of Nalanda Mahavihara (Nalanda University) at Nalanda, Bihar, inscribed 2016.
**Breakdown:**
- Owner (UNESCO page read 13 Aug 2026): 23-hectare site; remains from c. 3rd century BCE; monastic-scholastic functions from the 5th to 13th centuries CE.
- Components: 11 viharas and 14 temples plus smaller shrines (owner).
- Criteria (iv) and (vi) (owner).
- Pala patronage context (owner, PDF p. 120).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ UNESCO WHC India page fetched 2026-11-01 (2016); ✅ owner 14 §13.8A.
**Links:** ART-4035, ART-4069 (ownership trap).
**Trap:** Nalanda's WHS inscription is 2016, not the medieval founding date.
**Mains hook:** Nalanda shows a site whose value is scholarly and monastic, not just architectural.
**Back:** *"Sixteen, Bihar's ancient university: Nalanda, twenty-three hectares."*

### Flashcard ART-4058 — Victorian Gothic and Art Deco Ensembles of Mumbai (2018)
**Front:** What kind of property is the Mumbai ensemble and when was it inscribed?
**Definition (learn):** An urban ensemble of Victorian Gothic and Art Deco buildings in Mumbai, Maharashtra, inscribed 2018.
**Breakdown:**
- Type: ensemble, not a single monument.
- State: Maharashtra.
- Owner: "the distinction a 'which is a WHS' question tests".
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ UNESCO WHC India page fetched 2026-11-01 (2018); ✅ owner 14 §13.8A.
**Links:** ART-4035, ART-4059.
**Trap:** Ensemble status is not a single building; do not describe it as one structure.
**Mains hook:** Urban heritage raises the question of living cities and development pressure.
**Back:** *"Eighteen, Mumbai's ensemble: Gothic and Deco together."*

### Flashcard ART-4059 — Dholavira: a Harappan City (2021)
**Front:** What is distinctive about Dholavira as a WHS?
**Definition (learn):** A Harappan city in Gujarat, inscribed 2021, known for water harvesting and management.
**Breakdown:**
- Water: elaborate system of dams and channels into connected reservoirs (owner citing book).
- Inscription: ten large Harappan signs (owner citing book).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ UNESCO WHC India page fetched 2026-11-01 (2021); ✅ owner 14 §13.8A (PDF pp. 166, 183).
**Links:** ART-4035, ART-4058, ART-4060.
**Trap:** Dholavira is a Harappan city (Indus Valley), not a medieval fort; do not conflate with Lothal (not in owner).
**Mains hook:** Water management is the core of Dholavira's conservation and interpretation.
**Back:** *"Twenty-one, Harappan water city: Dholavira's dams."*

### Flashcard ART-4060 — Kakatiya Rudreshwara (Ramappa) Temple (2021)
**Front:** What is distinctive about Ramappa temple and when was it inscribed?
**Definition (learn):** A 13th-century Kakatiya temple dedicated to Shiva in Telangana, inscribed 2021.
**Breakdown:**
- Built 13th century AD by the Kakatiya dynasty.
- Patron: general Recharla Rudra under Ganapati Deva (owner).
- Techniques: sandbox foundation and floating stone (owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ UNESCO WHC India page fetched 2026-11-01 (2021); ✅ owner 14 §13.8A (PDF pp. 108–110).
**Links:** ART-4035, ART-4059, ART-4063 (Chola temples).
**Trap:** Ramappa's 2021 inscription is distinct from Warangal Fort (Tentative List only).
**Mains hook:** Engineering techniques (sandbox foundation) are a conservation and heritage-value argument.
**Back:** *"Twenty-one, Shiva's sandbox temple: Ramappa, Telangana."*

### Flashcard ART-4061 — Santiniketan (2023)
**Front:** When was Santiniketan inscribed and which artist does the owner tie to it?
**Definition (learn):** The Santiniketan site in West Bengal, inscribed 2023.
**Breakdown:**
- State: West Bengal.
- Owner ties Nandalal Bose to Santiniketan (PDF p. 361).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ UNESCO WHC India page fetched 2026-11-01 (2023); ✅ owner 14 §13.8A; 2024 Prelims routed (see ART-4072).
**Links:** ART-4072, ART-4062, ART-4035.
**Trap:** The 2023 date is the WHS inscription, not any earlier founding date for the site.
**Mains hook:** A site linked to an artist shows heritage as cultural landscape, not only architecture.
**Back:** *"Twenty-three, Bose's Santiniketan: the campus, not just the buildings."*

### Flashcard ART-4062 — Sacred Ensembles of the Hoysalas (2023)
**Front:** Which three temples form the Hoysala serial property and what criteria does UNESCO cite?
**Definition (learn):** A serial property of three 12th–13th-century temple complexes in Karnataka, inscribed 2023.
**Breakdown:**
- Channakeshava Temple, Belur.
- Hoysalesvara Temple, Halebidu.
- Keshava Temple, Somanathapura (owner's book spells "Chennakeshava"; UNESCO says Keshava).
- Criteria (i), (ii) and (iv).
- Hoysala features: stellate sanctum; circumambulatory platform; multi-tiered frieze; sculpture over the exterior; legend of Sala and the tiger; artists' signatures.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ UNESCO WHC India page fetched 2026-11-01 (2023); ✅ owner 14 §13.8A (UNESCO property page, 13 Aug 2026).
**Links:** ART-4061, ART-4063, ART-4072.
**Trap:** The book's "Chennakeshava" spelling for Somanathapura differs from UNESCO's "Keshava"; flag, do not silently change.
**Mains hook:** Serial properties are multi-site claims; the stellate sanctum is the diagnostic feature.
**Back:** *"Three Hoysala temples, twenty-three: Belur, Halebidu, Somanathapura."*

### Flashcard ART-4063 — Great Living Chola Temples (1987, extended 2004)
**Front:** What is the Chola WHS and why is 1987 not the only date?
**Definition (learn):** A WHS of Chola temples inscribed 1987 and extended 2004 — one property, two dates, three complexes.
**Breakdown:**
- First inscription 1987; extension 2004 (owner; UNESCO list shows "1987, 2004").
- Three complexes (owner).
- Owner's topic 03 cross-link.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ UNESCO WHC India page fetched 2026-11-01 (1987, 2004); ✅ owner 14 §13.8A(iii).
**Links:** ART-4035, ART-4062, ART-4064.
**Trap:** An extension year is not a first-inscription year; the 2004 date does not mean a new property.
**Mains hook:** Extensions show how WHS status can grow over time.
**Back:** *"Eighty-seven and oh-four: one Chola property, two dates."*

### Flashcard ART-4064 — Pattadakal and the Aihole-Badami-Pattadakal grouping
**Front:** Which of the Aihole, Badami and Pattadakal sites is inscribed?
**Definition (learn):** Only Pattadakal is inscribed (1987); Aihole and Badami appear on the Tentative List.
**Breakdown:**
- Group name on the Tentative List: "Evolution of Temple Architecture — Aihole-Badami-Pattadakal" (owner).
- Inscribed: Pattadakal, 1987.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ UNESCO WHC India page fetched 2026-11-01 (Group of Monuments at Pattadakal, 1987); ✅ owner 14 §13.8A(iv).
**Links:** ART-4063, ART-4065.
**Trap:** Group name on the Tentative List does not mean all three are WHS.
**Mains hook:** Tentative List grouping tests nomination discipline.
**Back:** *"Pattadakal alone in eighty-seven; Aihole and Badami wait."*

### Flashcard ART-4065 — Tentative List is not inscription
**Front:** Does Tentative List status make a site a WHS?
**Definition (learn):** No. Tentative List entries are nomination candidates, not inscriptions.
**Breakdown:**
- Owner examples: Bishnupur, Mattancherry Palace, Mandu, Sri Harimandir Sahib; Aihole, Badami, Thousand Pillar Temple, Warangal Fort, Kakatiya Kala Thoranam.
- Only inscribed sites appear on the WHS list.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §9, §13.8A(iv); ✅ UNESCO list does not show the named Tentative items as inscribed (checked against the 45-site list, 2026-11-01).
**Links:** ART-4064, ART-4035.
**Trap:** A Tentative List entry is a candidate; the answer "Tentative List makes it a WHS" is always wrong.
**Mains hook:** Nomination pipeline is a multi-year process; Tentative status is a step, not an award.
**Back:** *"Tentative is a waiting room, not a badge."*

### Flashcard ART-4066 — Danger List conditionality: Manas and Hampi
**Front:** Which Indian sites were placed on and then removed from the List of World Heritage in Danger?
**Definition (learn):** Manas Wildlife Sanctuary and the Group of Monuments at Hampi were placed on the Danger List and later removed after documented improvement.
**Breakdown:**
- Manas: ethnic unrest (owner).
- Hampi: cable-bridge construction (owner).
- Both removed (owner; PDF pp. 728–729).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §13.8, §13.11; as of 2026-11-01 no Indian site is on the Danger List per owner recheck (⚠️ not re-fetched in list page here).
**Links:** ART-4035, ART-4082 (advanced application), ART-4078.
**Trap:** The Danger List is a conditional status; WHS status is not permanent.
**Mains hook:** Use the Manas and Hampi cases as evidence that recognition creates an obligation, not a finish line.
**Back:** *"Manas and Hampi: listed, then saved, then removed."*

### Flashcard ART-4067 — Khajuraho Group of Monuments (1986)
**Front:** When was Khajuraho inscribed and what conservation issue does the owner link to it?
**Definition (learn):** The Khajuraho Group of Monuments, inscribed 1986, Chandella temples in Madhya Pradesh.
**Breakdown:**
- Inscribed 1986 (✅ UNESCO WHC India page fetched 2026-11-01).
- Owner notes unenclosed platforms (topic 03).
- Matangeshvara is an active worship site (owner §13.9).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ UNESCO WHC India page (1986); ✅ owner 14 §13.7, §13.9.
**Links:** ART-4035, ART-4077.
**Trap:** Khajuraho's WHS status is not a guarantee of protection; unenclosed platforms face visitor pressure.
**Mains hook:** Khajuraho is the textbook case of tourism pressure on open monuments.
**Back:** *"Eighty-six, Chandella stone: Khajuraho's open platforms."*

### Flashcard ART-4068 — AMASR zones are not UNESCO buffer zones
**Front:** Are the AMASR prohibited and regulated areas the same as a WHS buffer zone?
**Definition (learn):** No. AMASR zones come from Indian statute and competent-authority permission; buffer zones are mapped through the nomination and management documents.
**Breakdown:**
- AMASR: 100 m prohibited, a regulated ring (ART-4013).
- Buffer zone: UNESCO nomination/management mapping.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §9, §13.6.
**Links:** ART-4013, ART-4001.
**Trap:** Substituting one boundary for another is the common error.
**Mains hook:** Two different boundary layers must be analysed together.
**Back:** *"Indian rings, UNESCO maps: two boundaries, never one."*

### Flashcard ART-4069 — Inscription does not transfer ownership
**Front:** Does UNESCO inscription transfer ownership of a site to UNESCO?
**Definition (learn):** No. Inscription confers recognition and Convention-based protections; ownership, management and jurisdiction remain with the State Party (India) and its institutions (ASI/AMASR).
**Breakdown:**
- Recognition: international.
- Protection: Convention obligations.
- Ownership and management: India and ASI.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §9, §13.8 sovereignty rule (⚠️ owner-stated inference).
**Links:** ART-4001, ART-4011, ART-4008.
**Trap:** A question claiming UNESCO "owns" a monument is wrong.
**Mains hook:** Recognition is not sovereignty; state responsibility remains.
**Back:** *"Recognised, not repossessed: India keeps the keys."*

### Flashcard ART-4070 — PYQ 2018 GS-I: safeguarding Indian art heritage (10 marks)
**Front:** How should a 10-mark "Safeguarding Indian art heritage as a present need. Discuss" answer be built?
**Definition (learn):** A GS-I Mains demand (2018, 10 marks, 150 words) testing threat diagnosis, instrument matching, a current anchor and the gap.
**Breakdown:**
- Thesis: instrument-fit (protection fails from instrument mismatch).
- Four-category firewall (WHS, ICH, MoW, GI).
- Named threats with mechanisms.
- Instruments: AMASR, akademis, NMM/National Archives, GI, NCF, Adopt a Heritage.
- Dated anchor: Deepavali (10 Dec 2025); Maratha (2025).
- Gap: capacity; verdict.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §13.0, §13.1; PYQ routed by the owner's 2018–2023 ledger.
**Links:** ART-4001, ART-4041, ART-4036, ART-4076.
**Trap:** Listing threats without naming an instrument for each is an incomplete answer.
**Mains hook:** Present need is shown by specific, measurable pressure and a current anchor, not by praising heritage.
**Back:** *"Diagnose, match, date, admit the gap."*

### Flashcard ART-4071 — PYQ 2023 Prelims: colonial-era archaeologists
**Front:** Which PYQ routed in the owner concerns colonial-era archaeologists and what is the answer status?
**Definition (learn):** The 2023 Prelims GS-I Q81 routed by the owner: "Colonial-era archaeologists associated with Indian excavations".
**Breakdown:**
- Q81, GS-I Prelims 2023.
- Official key unavailable locally; no answer letter is inferred here.
- Alexander Cunningham (ASI, 1861; first DG 1871 per Wikipedia) is the founder of the modern ASI (ART-4011).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 (2018–2023 block, Q81); ⚠️ no answer recorded.
**Links:** ART-4011.
**Trap:** Do not infer an answer letter; name the institution (ASI) and founder only as context.
**Mains hook:** Colonial archaeology's institutional legacy shapes the ASI's mandate today.
**Back:** *"Eighty-one, colonial diggers: know the founder, skip the key."*

### Flashcard ART-4072 — PYQ 2024 Prelims: Shantiniketan and Hoysalas (2023)
**Front:** Which 2024 Prelims item concerns the 2023 WHS inscriptions and what does the owner state about its answer?
**Definition (learn):** Q65, GS-I Prelims 2024: UNESCO WHS inscribed in 2023 (Shantiniketan and Hoysalas).
**Breakdown:**
- Q65.
- Official Set-A key available locally; answer not recorded in the owner.
- Both 2023 inscriptions are on the list (ART-4061, ART-4062).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 (2024–2025 block, Q65).
**Links:** ART-4061, ART-4062, ART-4074.
**Trap:** Both Shantiniketan and Hoysalas are 2023; do not mix in Tentative-only sites.
**Mains hook:** A year-specific statement needs its year checked.
**Back:** *"Sixty-five, twenty-three: Santiniketan and Hoysalas together."*

### Flashcard ART-4073 — PYQ 2026 Prelims Q37: Moidams and royal burial
**Front:** What does the 2026 Prelims Q37 test and what is its answer status?
**Definition (learn):** Q37, GS-I Prelims 2026: Tai-Ahom Moidams, royal burial practices and UNESCO status.
**Breakdown:**
- Routed demand: Moidams, royal burial, WHS status.
- Provisional 2026 Set-A key; the owner does not record an answer letter.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 (2026 block, Q37); ⚠️ answer key provisional.
**Links:** ART-4038, ART-4035.
**Trap:** Do not state an answer letter; the provisional key is not confirmed.
**Mains hook:** Use the Moidams case for funerary form and conservation pressures.
**Back:** *"Thirty-seven, Ahom mounds: know the form, check the key."*

### Flashcard ART-4074 — "Latest inclusion" is date-bound
**Front:** How should a "latest inclusion in the ICH list" question be answered?
**Definition (learn):** The answer depends on the question's date. CSE 2024 answer: Garba (2023). Today: Deepavali (2025).
**Breakdown:**
- At the 2024 paper's date, Garba was the latest of Chhau, Durga Puja, Garba and Kumbh Mela.
- Today the latest Indian inclusion is Deepavali.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §13.8B(v) (PDF p. 736); ✅ UNESCO ICH India page (2025 Deepavali; 2024 Nowruz entry on the live page).
**Links:** ART-4041, ART-4056, ART-4072.
**Trap:** Quoting Garba as today's latest Indian ICH element is an error.
**Mains hook:** Read the question date first; cite the list's as-of date.
**Back:** *"Garba for twenty-four, Deepavali for today."*

### Flashcard ART-4075 — Repatriation discipline
**Front:** When may an answer say a specific object was repatriated?
**Definition (learn):** Only when a dated official source names the object, the receiving institution and its provenance.
**Breakdown:**
- Object named.
- Receiving institution named.
- Provenance named.
- Dated source.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §2, §9, §13.11 (rule).
**Links:** ART-4079 (documentation), ART-4009 (antiquities law).
**Trap:** Assuming a repatriation without a dated source is the error to avoid.
**Mains hook:** Repatriation claims need documentary evidence; discuss the legal and evidential route.
**Back:** *"Object, institution, provenance, date: or no repatriation claim."*

### Flashcard ART-4076 — Conservation versus restoration
**Front:** How do conservation and restoration differ in aim, intervention and authenticity risk?
**Definition (learn):** Conservation arrests decay and keeps original fabric; restoration returns an object to a chosen earlier state.
**Breakdown:**
- Conservation: minimum, documented, reversible intervention; low authenticity risk; the object keeps ageing.
- Restoration: substantial, often irreversible; high authenticity risk; a period must be chosen.
- Worked case: Brihadeswara's Rajaraja-period paintings with Nayaka superimposition (owner, topic 07).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §13.9 (⚠️ owner analytical framing).
**Links:** ART-4001, ART-4077.
**Trap:** The two words are not interchangeable; restoration picks a period.
**Mains hook:** Minimum intervention is the defensible default; reconstruction is the exception.
**Back:** *"Conserve the fabric, restore only by choice."*

### Flashcard ART-4077 — Community rights and living heritage
**Front:** Why can conservation not be separated from the community that sustains a monument?
**Definition (learn):** Where a monument is still worshipped or a practice still performed, access, ritual use and livelihood become conservation variables.
**Breakdown:**
- Matangeshvara (Khajuraho): active worship.
- Moidams: continuing ancestor-worship landscape.
- Rights of access and ritual can override a purely fabric-centred regime.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ owner 14 §13.9 analysis.
**Links:** ART-4067, ART-4038, ART-4076.
**Trap:** Do not treat monuments as empty objects; do not assume worship is an obstacle to conservation.
**Mains hook:** A strong answer names the community trade-off.
**Back:** *"Living shrines need living rights."*

### Flashcard ART-4078 — ASI monument count (as-of)
**Front:** How many centrally protected monuments and sites does ASI maintain?
**Definition (learn):** The live ASI figure, time-varying.
**Breakdown:**
- Owner: 3,679 centrally protected monuments and archaeological sites (live page accessed 4 Sep 2026; owner also cites 21 Jul 2026).
- Wikipedia ASI page: "more than 3650" (⚠️ older figure); CAG 2013 audit cited 3,678 structures, 1,655 verified.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §8, §13.8; ✅ Wikipedia ASI fetched 2026-11-01 (3650+; 2013 CAG 3,678). ⚠️ The 3,679 figure's access date is the owner's, not re-fetched.
**Links:** ART-4011, ART-4008.
**Trap:** Do not state the monument count without its date and source.
**Mains hook:** Cite the figure with its access date and note verification gaps (CAG 2013).
**Back:** *"Three thousand six hundred seventy-nine, dated or not at all."*

### Flashcard ART-4079 — Documentation as the least-contested protection
**Front:** Why does documentation protect heritage even when physical protection fails?
**Definition (learn):** Digitisation and cataloguing protect informational value and are the precondition for any provenance or repatriation claim.
**Breakdown:**
- Bodies: NMM, National Archives, IGNCA.
- Risks covered: disaster, neglect, illicit trade.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §13.9, §13.7 (⚠️ analytical).
**Links:** ART-4025, ART-4022, ART-4026, ART-4075.
**Trap:** Digitisation is not conservation of fabric; say it protects information.
**Mains hook:** The least contested instrument is the documentary one.
**Back:** *"Copy before you lose it: documentation is protection."*

### GROUP — Heritage Conservation, Institutions and UNESCO - ADVANCED

### Flashcard ART-4080 — Heritage recognition as a managed trade-off
**Front:** Why is WHS inscription never a cost-free benefit in an advanced answer?
**Definition (learn):** Recognition brings attention, funding and visitors, but also visitor pressure, conservation cost and authenticity trade-offs; every benefit has a corresponding cost.
**Breakdown:**
- Benefit: international attention and preservation funding; cost: visitor footfall on fragile, unenclosed structures.
- Benefit: tourism-driven economy; cost: conservation versus commercial development.
- Benefit: wartime protection (Geneva Convention) per owner; cost: protection is normative, not a guarantee against neglect.
- Benefit: project-management access; cost: requires State Party capacity to absorb it.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner advanced 14 §1–§4 (PDF pp. 725–729); ⚠️ analytical framing.
**Links:** ART-4001, ART-4066, ART-4067, ART-4082.
**Trap:** The owner's benefit list is presented uncritically; do not repeat it without its trade-off.
**Mains hook:** Pair every benefit claim with its trade-off to avoid a one-sided argument.
**Back:** *"Every benefit has a bill: attention costs visitors, status costs capacity."*

### Flashcard ART-4081 — WHS benefits and the sovereignty limit
**Front:** Which benefits does the book list for WHS status and what limit always applies?
**Definition (learn):** The owner lists international attention, tourism, UNESCO restoration funding, UN partnership prestige, global project-management access, partnership facilitation and Geneva Convention wartime protection (PDF p. 728).
**Breakdown:**
- Benefits (owner list): seven items above.
- Limit: ownership, management and jurisdiction stay with India and ASI (ART-4069).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner advanced 14 §3–§4 (PDF pp. 725–729).
**Links:** ART-4069, ART-4080, ART-4008.
**Trap:** Do not say UNESCO funds or manages a site without a named project and source.
**Mains hook:** List the benefits, then state that recognition is conditional and state-managed.
**Back:** *"Seven gains, one limit: India remains the manager."*

### Flashcard ART-4082 — Danger List as a conditional-status tool
**Front:** How does the Danger List show that WHS status is conditional?
**Definition (learn):** A safeguard that moves inadequately protected sites to the List of World Heritage in Danger, and in principle can delist a site if conditions do not improve.
**Breakdown:**
- Placement: inadequate protection or management.
- Removal: documented improvement (Manas, Hampi).
- Delisting in principle if conditions do not improve.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner advanced 14 §2, §4 (PDF pp. 728–729). As of 2026-11-01, no Indian site is on the Danger List per the owner's recheck (⚠️ not re-fetched).
**Links:** ART-4066, ART-4080.
**Trap:** Treating WHS status as permanent is an error.
**Mains hook:** Use the Manas and Hampi removal case as evidence that status is actively managed.
**Back:** *"Listed is a loan, not a gift: Danger List keeps it honest."*

### Flashcard ART-4083 — Constitutional foundation beneath UNESCO claims
**Front:** Which constitutional provisions underlie India's domestic heritage law?
**Definition (learn):** Article 49's State duty (protection from spoliation, removal and export) is the textual basis for ASI's AMASR mandate, with Articles 29 and 51A(f) as the cultural rights and citizen-duty layers.
**Breakdown:**
- Article 49: State duty.
- Article 29: minority culture right.
- Article 51A(f): citizen duty.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner advanced 14 §4 (PDF p. 984).
**Links:** ART-4029, ART-4030, ART-4031, ART-4008.
**Trap:** Doctrine and judicial reading belong to Polity; this file uses the provisions only as the cultural anchor.
**Mains hook:** Close a heritage answer with the legal anchor where the question invites a rights dimension.
**Back:** *"Forty-nine guards stone; twenty-nine guards voice; fifty-one-A-F guards all."*

### Flashcard ART-4084 — Ledger integrity: dated counts, never a stale total
**Front:** How should dynamic heritage counts be cited in a Mains answer?
**Definition (learn):** Every count (WHS, ICH, MoW, ASI monuments, GI goods) must carry its source and access date; inscription years are stable, totals are not.
**Breakdown:**
- Stable facts: inscription years, statute years, founding years, constitutional articles.
- Dynamic facts: WHS total, ICH total, MoW total, ASI monument count, GI count.
- Conflict in owner files: 44 (21 July 2026) versus 45 (4 September 2026); live page gives 45 on 2026-11-01.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner advanced 14 §7, §13; ✅ UNESCO state pages fetched 2026-11-01.
**Links:** ART-4035, ART-4036, ART-4078, ART-4037.
**Trap:** Do not carry the owner's pre-Deepavali ICH total of 15 or the 44 WHS total forward.
**Mains hook:** Close with a dated, source-specific figure rather than a round number.
**Back:** *"Years stay, totals age: date every count."*

### GROUP — Indian Cinema, Film Institutions and Awards - MUST-DO

### Flashcard ART-4085 — Cinema as a composite Indian art form
**Front:** Why is cinema counted among art forms rather than merely as theatre?
**Definition (learn):** Cinema combines story, performance, image, music and editing into a mass-circulated, reproducible medium.
**Breakdown:**
- Story/literature + performance + image and production design + music and sound + editing and montage.
- Circulation → memory, identity and soft power.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §1 (no page citation; owner's composite-art frame).
**Links:** ART-4086, ART-4087, ART-4088, ART-4132.
