**Trap:** Acting alone does not make cinema theatre; camera, editing, recorded sound and reproducibility make a distinct medium.
**Mains hook:** Cinema is "ancient to modern" art because it does what earlier forms did, with reproducibility changing its reach and regulation.
**Back:** *"Story, sound, image, edit, circulate: cinema is composite."*

### Flashcard ART-4086 — Cinematography and editing
**Front:** Define cinematography and editing as cinematic techniques.
**Definition (learn):** Cinematography is the creation of the moving image (camera, lens, lighting, framing, movement); editing is the selection and arrangement of shots for continuity, rhythm, contrast or meaning.
**Breakdown:**
- Cinematography: camera, lens, lighting, framing, movement.
- Editing: continuity, rhythm, contrast, meaning.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §2 (vocabulary).
**Links:** ART-4085, ART-4087, ART-4088.
**Trap:** Do not confuse cinematography (image) with editing (sequence).
**Mains hook:** Use technique terms to show how meaning is made, the "form" dimension of a 20-mark answer.
**Back:** *"Camera makes the image, editor makes the meaning."*

### Flashcard ART-4087 — Mise-en-scene
**Front:** What does mise-en-scene include?
**Definition (learn):** What is arranged within the frame: setting, costume, lighting, performance and composition.
**Breakdown:**
- Setting, costume, lighting, performance, composition.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §2.
**Links:** ART-4086, ART-4085.
**Trap:** Mise-en-scene is the frame's content, not the shot selection (editing).
**Mains hook:** Form analysis of an Indian film uses mise-en-scene to show regional setting and representation.
**Back:** *"Arranged inside the frame: setting, dress, light, actors."*

### Flashcard ART-4088 — Documentary and animation as cinematic forms
**Front:** How do documentary and animation differ from fiction cinema in the owner's vocabulary?
**Definition (learn):** Documentary is non-fiction cinematic form that still involves selection and framing; animation is moving image made from drawn, modelled or computer-generated frames.
**Breakdown:**
- Documentary: non-fiction; not an unmediated record.
- Animation: drawn, modelled, computer-generated.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §2.
**Links:** ART-4085, ART-4086.
**Trap:** A documentary is not a neutral record; selection and framing shape it.
**Mains hook:** Documentary film is evidence of selection, not a raw record.
**Back:** *"Non-fiction still chooses; animation draws the frames."*

### Flashcard ART-4089 — Film certification is not an award
**Front:** Why is certification different from an award?
**Definition (learn):** Certification is a statutory classification for public exhibition; it concerns eligibility, not artistic merit.
**Breakdown:**
- Certification: legal eligibility, age classes.
- Award: recognition of merit by a jury or body.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §2, §6.
**Links:** ART-4094, ART-4105, ART-4131.
**Trap:** A certificate is not a quality award; a "U/A" marker does not praise a film.
**Mains hook:** Separate audience classification from merit recognition in every answer.
**Back:** *"Certified to show, not certified to be great."*

### Flashcard ART-4090 — Film preservation
**Front:** What does film preservation include and why is film fragile?
**Definition (learn):** Conservation, restoration, cataloguing and access for film and related material.
**Breakdown:**
- Conservation, restoration, cataloguing, access.
- Fragility: nitrate and acetate decay, colour fading, obsolete formats (owner analysis).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §2; ⚠️ owner analysis on commercial versus cultural value.
**Links:** ART-4102, ART-4133.
**Trap:** Do not state archive holdings or restoration counts without a dated official source.
**Mains hook:** Commercial value usually decides what is restored; the state must fund cultural value.
**Back:** *"Film rots: catalogue, restore, make accessible."*

### Flashcard ART-4091 — Ministry of Information and Broadcasting (MIB)
**Front:** What role does MIB play in Indian cinema?
**Definition (learn):** The Union ministry that is the policy and administrative anchor for film and broadcasting.
**Breakdown:**
- Anchors: CBFC, FTII, NFAI/NFDC, the awards framework.
- Film bodies sit under MIB (owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §3.
**Links:** ART-4094, ART-4101, ART-4102, ART-4103.
**Trap:** MIB is the ministry; CBFC and FTII are its bodies, not the ministry itself.
**Mains hook:** Film policy is a Union administrative field with federal enforcement in states.
**Back:** *"The ministry holds the bodies: certify, train, archive, develop."*

### Flashcard ART-4092 — Central Board of Film Certification (CBFC): function and founding
**Front:** What is the CBFC's function and what are its founding and renaming dates?
**Definition (learn):** The statutory body that certifies films for public exhibition under the Cinematograph Act, 1952, directly under MIB.
**Breakdown:**
- Function: certification; may require modification or refuse sanction.
- Founding: owner says 1950 as Central Board of Film Censors, renamed under the 1952 Act.
- Wikipedia (fetched 2026-11-01): infobox 15 January 1951; renamed Central Board of Film Certification in 1983.
- Headquarters: Mumbai; nine regional offices (Mumbai, Kolkata, Chennai, Bengaluru, Thiruvananthapuram, Hyderabad, New Delhi, Cuttack, Guwahati) plus Chandigarh facilitation office (owner, MIB).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §8, §11.5A; ✅ Wikipedia CBFC page fetched 2026-11-01. ⚠️ Founding year and rename year conflict.
**Links:** ART-4093, ART-4095, ART-4096, ART-4097.
**Trap:** Owner advanced 15 §11.5 says "eight regional offices"; basic 15 and MIB list nine. The founding date (1950 vs 1951) and rename year (1952 vs 1983) also differ between sources; flag them.
**Mains hook:** Certification is a classification, but its power to cut and refuse gives it a censoring effect.
**Back:** *"Fifty or fifty-one founded, nine offices, Mumbai head: the certifying board."*

### Flashcard ART-4093 — Cinematograph Act, 1952: composition and state role
**Front:** What does the Cinematograph Act, 1952 provide and how is the Board composed?
**Definition (learn):** Central statute for certification and public exhibition, setting up the CBFC and its composition.
**Breakdown:**
- Board: Chairman and not fewer than 12 and not more than 25 members, appointed by the Central Government (owner).
- Grounds: offence to a geographical area, age group, religious denomination or political group.
- Federal split: certification is Union; censorship enforcement in domains is with State governments (owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §8 (PDF p. 575). Wikipedia lists the Board as a chairperson plus 23 members (⚠️ current membership not verified).
**Links:** ART-4092, ART-4094, ART-4097.
**Trap:** Not an awards statute; it governs certification only.
**Mains hook:** A federal split means national film policy is also a state-enforcement question.
**Back:** *"Twelve to twenty-five members, Union-certified, state-enforced."*

### Flashcard ART-4094 — UA7+, UA13+ and UA16+ markers
**Front:** Which certificate markers apply under the amended Act and what do they mean?
**Definition (learn):** Current classification uses U, A and S alongside age-based UA7+, UA13+ and UA16+ markers, which guide parents or guardians.
**Breakdown:**
- U, A, S retained.
- UA7+: parental guidance for children under 7, appropriate above.
- UA13+ and UA16+: parental guidance for children below 13 and 16 respectively (Wikipedia).
- Previous U/A threshold was 12 years (Wikipedia).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §8 (2023 amendment, verified 4 Sep 2026); ✅ Wikipedia CBFC page fetched 2026-11-01 (refinement November 2024). ⚠️ Owner and Wikipedia give different dates for the marker change.
**Links:** ART-4092, ART-4093.
**Trap:** The markers are not award categories; do not describe UA-only as current.
**Mains hook:** Age-band markers show a regulatory shift toward audience classification.
**Back:** *"Seven, thirteen, sixteen: the guardian's markers."*

### Flashcard ART-4095 — Appeal route: section 5C and omitted FCAT (5D)
**Front:** What is the current appeal route under the Cinematograph Act and what happened to FCAT?
**Definition (learn):** Section 5C allows appeal to the High Court; section 5D, which created the Film Certification Appellate Tribunal, was omitted by the Tribunals Reforms Act, 2021.
**Breakdown:**
- 5C: High Court appeal.
- 5D: former FCAT; omitted with retrospective effect from 4 April 2021 (owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §8 (MIB consolidated Act verified 4 Sep 2026); ⚠️ not re-fetched.
**Links:** ART-4092, ART-4093.
**Trap:** Do not describe FCAT as a current tribunal.
**Mains hook:** Tribunal rationalisation changed the appeal route; name the current forum.
**Back:** *"Five-C goes to High Court; five-D tribunal is gone."*

### Flashcard ART-4096 — Cable Television Network (Regulation) Act, 1995 versus CBFC
**Front:** Does CBFC certify television serials and what governs them instead?
**Definition (learn):** CBFC certification is not required for television programmes and serials, which are governed by the content and advertisement codes under the Cable Television Network (Regulation) Act, 1995.
**Breakdown:**
- CBFC: cinema films and certain uploads/imports (owner).
- Cable TV Act 1995: TV programmes and ads.
- Cable TV offences: non-cognisable (owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §8 (PDF p. 577).
**Links:** ART-4092, ART-4100.
**Trap:** Cable TV offences are non-cognisable; Cinematograph exhibition offences are cognisable and non-bailable.
**Mains hook:** Two regimes for two screens: film and cable television.
**Back:** *"Cable has its own code, cinema has the board."*

### Flashcard ART-4097 — Uncertified exhibition: a cognisable, non-bailable offence
**Front:** What offence does exhibiting an uncertified film commit?
**Definition (learn):** Exhibition of an uncertified film is a cognisable and non-bailable offence under the Cinematograph Act, 1952 (owner).
**Breakdown:**
- Scope: all films need a certificate.
- Imported foreign films and dubbed versions need fresh certificates.
- Doordarshan-made films excepted.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §8 (PDF p. 577).
**Links:** ART-4092, ART-4096, ART-4093.
**Trap:** Dubbed films need a fresh certificate; do not treat dubbing as exempt.
**Mains hook:** Language markets need separate certification of dubbed versions.
**Back:** *"No certificate, no screen: cognisable and non-bailable."*

### Flashcard ART-4098 — Certification versus censorship
**Front:** Is the Indian scheme certification or censorship?
**Definition (learn):** Formally certification (classify for audiences); in effect partly censorship because the Board can require excisions and refuse sanction.
**Breakdown:**
- Certification: classify audiences.
- Censorship in effect: excisions, refusal.
- Tension: freedom of expression (Polity owns the doctrine).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §8, §11.2 (⚠️ analytical reading).
**Links:** ART-4093, ART-4089, ART-4131.
**Trap:** Describing the CBFC as purely a "censor" or purely a "certifier" misses the distinction.
**Mains hook:** Argue the distinction holds legally more than practically.
**Back:** *"Classifies by audience, cuts by power: the honest middle."*

### Flashcard ART-4099 — Indian Film Festival (IFFI): festival platform
**Front:** What is IFFI and why is festival selection not a prize?
**Definition (learn):** International Film Festival of India, an international festival platform hosted in India.
**Breakdown:**
- Platform: international, hosted in India.
- Selection or premiere is a status; a prize is a separate outcome.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §3, §4, §11.5. Its founding year is not in the owner files (⚠️).
**Links:** ART-4100, ART-4089, ART-4105.
**Trap:** Selection ≠ prize.
**Mains hook:** Festival visibility extends soft power but is not a measure of cultural value.
**Back:** *"Shown is not won: IFFI is a platform."*

### Flashcard ART-4100 — Festival selection, premiere and prize
**Front:** How do festival selection, premiere and prize differ?
**Definition (learn):** Selection/screening is a programming status; premiere is first screening; prize is a jury award within a festival.
**Breakdown:**
- Selection: programmed.
- Premiere: first screening.
- Prize: jury award (e.g., Cannes, Venice, Berlin).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §4.
**Links:** ART-4099, ART-4101.
**Trap:** Winning the festival's prize is not the same as being selected.
**Mains hook:** Separate status from prize; record each separately.
**Back:** *"Selected, premiered, prized: three different rungs."*

### Flashcard ART-4101 — BAFTA versus Academy Awards versus festival prizes
**Front:** Are BAFTA and the Academy Awards the same institution?
**Definition (learn):** No. BAFTA is the British Academy of Film and Television Arts; the Academy Awards are given by the US Academy of Motion Picture Arts and Sciences; festival prizes are given by Cannes, Venice or Berlin.
**Breakdown:**
- BAFTA: British Academy of Film and Television Arts.
- Academy Awards (Oscars): US Academy of Motion Picture Arts and Sciences.
- Cannes, Venice, Berlin: festival prizes.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §4.
**Links:** ART-4111, ART-4099, ART-4100.
**Trap:** A BAFTA is not an Oscar and not a festival prize.
**Mains hook:** Name the exact award system and category.
**Back:** *"British Academy, US Academy, festival juries: three systems."*

### Flashcard ART-4102 — National Film Archive of India (NFAI)
**Front:** When was NFAI established, where is it, and what happened to its functions?
**Definition (learn):** The national film archive, established 1964 under MIB at Pune for film preservation.
**Breakdown:**
- Established: February 1964 (Wikipedia).
- Location: Pune; regional offices at Bangalore, Calcutta and Thiruvananthapuram (Wikipedia).
- Merged into NFDC: Wikipedia says March 2022 (citing an Indian Express report of 30 March 2022); owner says a 23 December 2020 Government decision (MIB page, ✅ owner) and a "2022 absorption".
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ Wikipedia NFAI fetched 2026-11-01; ✅ owner 15 §3, §11.5A (⚠️ conflicting dates for the merger decision and absorption).
**Links:** ART-4103, ART-4104, ART-4090.
**Trap:** Do not state archive holdings, reel counts or restoration budgets.
**Mains hook:** Specialist preservation mandate versus production-corporation logic is the evaluative line.
**Back:** *"Sixty-four archived, twenty-two merged: NFAI into NFDC."*

### Flashcard ART-4103 — National Film Development Corporation (NFDC)
**Front:** What is NFDC and what was its predecessor?
**Definition (learn):** A public-sector film-development and promotion corporation under MIB, established 1975 per Wikipedia.
**Breakdown:**
- Parent: MIB.
- Predecessors (infobox): Film Finance Corporation and Films Division.
- Consolidation: owner says it absorbed four units (NFAI, Films Division, DFF, CFSI) under the consolidated architecture.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ Wikipedia NFDC fetched 2026-11-01 (1975; MIB); ✅ owner 15 §3, §11.5A. ⚠️ Wikipedia does not confirm the four-unit merger or its dates.
**Links:** ART-4102, ART-4104, ART-4091.
**Trap:** Consolidation came by administrative decision, not by statute; do not say "created" in 2022.
**Mains hook:** Consolidation promises efficiency; the risk is loss of specialist mandates.
**Back:** *"Seventy-five made, consolidated later: NFDC under MIB."*

### Flashcard ART-4104 — Four-unit consolidation into NFDC
**Front:** Which four units did the owner say were merged into NFDC and when?
**Definition (learn):** The owner's MIB-sourced consolidation: NFAI, Films Division, Directorate of Film Festivals and Children's Film Society, India merged into NFDC.
**Breakdown:**
- Units: NFAI, Films Division, DFF, CFSI.
- Decision: 23 December 2020 (owner MIB page).
- Absorption: 2022 (owner, §11.5A).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §3, §11.5A (MIB page rechecked 4 Sep 2026); ⚠️ Wikipedia's NFAI page gives March 2022; the NFDC page did not confirm the merger.
**Links:** ART-4102, ART-4103, ART-4106.
**Trap:** Cite the decision date and the absorption date separately; do not merge them.
**Mains hook:** Institutional merger is a stated efficiency rationale against specialist-archive risk.
**Back:** *"Four units, one corporation: decided 2020, absorbed 2022."*

### Flashcard ART-4105 — National Film Awards
**Front:** What do the National Film Awards recognise?
**Definition (learn):** Indian national recognition across feature, non-feature and cinema-writing categories under the official awards framework.
**Breakdown:**
- Feature films, non-feature films, cinema writing.
- Not a certification.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §3, §4, §11.5.
**Links:** ART-4106, ART-4089.
**Trap:** National award is not certification; the Phalke Award is presented at the National Film Awards.
**Mains hook:** National recognition is a merit measure; state it by category and year.
**Back:** *"Feature, non-feature, writing: the national prizes."*

### Flashcard ART-4106 — Dadasaheb Phalke Award
**Front:** When was the Dadasaheb Phalke Award instituted, who gives it and who is it named for?
**Definition (learn):** India's highest award in cinema, instituted 1969, commemorating Dadasaheb Phalke (1870–1944).
**Breakdown:**
- Instituted 1969 (owner; Wikipedia "first presented 1969").
- Awarding body: Government of India; sponsored and given through the Directorate of Film Festivals (owner; Wikipedia).
- Namesake: Phalke, maker of Raja Harishchandra (1913).
- Prize: Swarna Kamal, shawl and cash (amount conflicts across Wikipedia sections).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §3, §11.4 (PDF p. 977); ✅ Wikipedia fetched 2026-11-01 (1969; Devika Rani first recipient for 1969).
**Links:** ART-4107, ART-4119, ART-4104 (DFF merged).
**Trap:** The awarding body (DFF) was itself merged into NFDC per the owner; attach the current arrangement only with a dated source. The cash prize figure differs within Wikipedia; do not quote it.
**Mains hook:** Recognition of the father of Indian cinema is the highest honour, but its custody is a consolidation question.
**Back:** *"Sixty-nine, Phalke's prize: highest honour in Indian cinema."*

### Flashcard ART-4107 — Dadasaheb Phalke recipients: dated conflict
**Front:** Who received the most recent Phalke Award named in the sources?
**Definition (learn):** Different sources name different recent recipients; the answer depends on year and source.
**Breakdown:**
- Owner (MIB announcement): Mohanlal for award year 2023, 71st National Film Awards.
- Wikipedia: Anant Nag for 2024, 72nd National Film Awards; the page gives a ceremony date of 22 September 2026.
- No 2026 recipient is verified here.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §5; ✅ Wikipedia fetched 2026-11-01 (conflict flagged). ⚠️ Not resolved.
**Links:** ART-4106.
**Trap:** Do not name a recipient from memory; always give the award year and source.
**Mains hook:** Award year and ceremony year are different; name both if asked.
**Back:** *"Mohanlal in the owner's year, Anant Nag in Wikipedia's: check the year."*

### Flashcard ART-4108 — Film and Television Institute of India (FTII)
**Front:** When and where was FTII established, under which ministry and what is its legal status?
**Definition (learn):** India's film-training institute, established 1960, in Pune, under MIB, registered as a society under the Societies Registration Act, 1860.
**Breakdown:**
- Established 1960 (Wikipedia: set up after a Film Enquiry Committee recommendation; courses from 1961).
- Original name Film Institute of India; renamed 1971 (Wikipedia).
- Location: Pune, former Prabhat Film Company grounds.
- Wikipedia infobox: deemed to be university.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ Wikipedia FTII fetched 2026-11-01; 📰 MIB material (owner; rechecked 4 Sep 2026). ⚠️ The local owner PDF does not carry FTII.
**Links:** ART-4091, ART-4102, ART-4103.
**Trap:** FTII is neither regulator nor archive; do not state courses, alumni or controversies.
**Mains hook:** Training reproduces the workforce; keep it distinct from the archive.
**Back:** *"Sixty, Pune, trains the film makers: FTII, not the censor."*

### Flashcard ART-4109 — Five film functions: certify, train, archive, develop, award
**Front:** Which functions does Indian film architecture separate and which bodies carry them?
**Definition (learn):** Certification (CBFC), training (FTII), archiving (NFAI, now within NFDC architecture), development and production (NFDC), festivals (IFFI), and awards (National Film Awards, Phalke).
**Breakdown:**
- Certify: CBFC.
- Train: FTII.
- Archive: NFAI (now consolidated).
- Develop: NFDC.
- Festival: IFFI.
- Award: National Film Awards, Phalke.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §11.5A (function table).
**Links:** ART-4092, ART-4108, ART-4102, ART-4103, ART-4099, ART-4105.
**Trap:** Merging the five jobs into one institution is the substantive error.
**Mains hook:** A "discuss India's film institutions" answer names each job, its body and the 2022 consolidation.
**Back:** *"Certify, train, archive, develop, award: five jobs, five bodies."*

### Flashcard ART-4110 — Indian cinema is not only Hindi cinema
**Front:** Why is "Indian cinema" not equivalent to Hindi cinema?
**Definition (learn):** Indian cinema includes regional, language, documentary, animation and independent traditions; it is administered as many language markets.
**Breakdown:**
- Premsagar (1939) is the owner's first South Indian film (⚠️ unverified).
- CBFC's nine regional offices.
- Fresh certificate for dubbed versions.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §6, §9.
**Links:** ART-4126, ART-4092, ART-4085.
**Trap:** Equating Indian cinema with Bollywood or Hindi cinema is the error.
**Mains hook:** National film policy is also a language and federal question.
**Back:** *"Many markets, one screen: not only Hindi."*

### Flashcard ART-4111 — Boong: BAFTA 2026 Children's & Family Film
**Front:** What did the BAFTA result name Boong as, and which BAFTA ceremony is it?
**Definition (learn):** Boong won the Children's & Family Film category at the British Academy Film Awards, per the owner and Wikipedia.
**Breakdown:**
- Category: Best Children's & Family Film.
- Ceremony: Wikipedia dates it to the 79th BAFTAs (2026); the template in the same page lists it as the 2025 winner.
- Owner: "BAFTA's official 2026 result lists Boong".
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §5 (BAFTA 2026 result, verified 4 Sep 2026); ✅ Wikipedia Boong fetched 2026-11-01 (conflict on year). ⚠️ Year conflict.
**Links:** ART-4112, ART-4113, ART-4101.
**Trap:** Keep category, director and milestone separate; "won a BAFTA" is too broad.
**Mains hook:** Category-specific claims are the only defensible claims.
**Back:** *"Boong, Children and Family: the category, not just the BAFTA."*

### Flashcard ART-4112 — Boong: director, language and release
**Front:** Who directed Boong, in which language and when was it released?
**Definition (learn):** Boong, directed and written by Lakshmipriya Devi, in Manipuri (Meiteilon), premiered at TIFF in 2024 and released in India on 19 September 2025.
**Breakdown:**
- Director: Lakshmipriya Devi (owner; Wikipedia).
- Language: Manipuri (Meiteilon).
- Premiere: TIFF, 2024; Indian release 19 Sep 2025 (Wikipedia).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §5; ✅ Wikipedia Boong fetched 2026-11-01.
**Links:** ART-4111, ART-4110.
**Trap:** Keep director separate from the award; a director is not the award winner.
**Mains hook:** A Manipuri-language film shows regional storytelling in a national-cinema frame.
**Back:** *"Devi's Manipuri film: festival in 2024, cinemas in 2025."*

### Flashcard ART-4113 — "First Indian film to win a BAFTA": the claim trap
**Front:** Can an answer say Boong is "the first Indian film to win a BAFTA"?
**Definition (learn):** Only with the exact category and a dated source. Wikipedia's claim is category-specific and the owner says "first Indian film" claims must be tied to category.
**Breakdown:**
- Safe: "first Indian film to win the BAFTA Children's & Family Film award" (dated).
- Unsafe: "first Indian film to win any BAFTA".
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §5; ⚠️ Wikipedia's "first Indian film" phrasing is not verified against a BAFTA page.
**Links:** ART-4111, ART-4101.
**Trap:** Overgeneralised milestone claims are the examiner's favourite statement-elimination trap.
**Mains hook:** Discipline on category keeps the claim accurate.
**Back:** *"Say the category, or say nothing."*

### Flashcard ART-4114 — Lumière Brothers' 1896 exhibition in Bombay
**Front:** When and where were motion pictures first shown in India?
**Definition (learn):** The Lumière Brothers showed six silent films in Bombay in 1896.
**Breakdown:**
- Six silent films, Bombay, 1896.
- Exhibition, not Indian production.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §7 (PDF pp. 570–571).
**Links:** ART-4115, ART-4117.
**Trap:** The 1896 exhibition is not the first Indian film.
**Mains hook:** Cinema entered India as imported technology and was indigenised within three years.
**Back:** *"Ninety-six, Lumière in Bombay: imported, six silent films."*

### Flashcard ART-4115 — First Indian-shot film, 1897
**Front:** What was the first Indian-shot film and when?
**Definition (learn):** Coconut Fair and Our Indian Empire, 1897, shot in India.
**Breakdown:**
- Indian-shot, 1897.
- Distinct from the first Indian-made feature (1913).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §7, §11.4.
**Links:** ART-4114, ART-4119.
**Trap:** Do not confuse "Indian-shot" with "Indian-made feature".
**Mains hook:** Indigenisation took under three years from import to Indian-shot film.
**Back:** *"Ninety-seven, Coconut Fair shot here."*

### Flashcard ART-4116 — Harishchandra Bhatavdekar ("Save Dada"), 1899
**Front:** Who made the first motion venture by an Indian and when?
**Definition (learn):** Harishchandra Bhatavdekar, known as "Save Dada", made two short films in 1899.
**Breakdown:**
- Two shorts, 1899.
- Not Phalke.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §7 (PDF pp. 570–571).
**Links:** ART-4114, ART-4119.
**Trap:** Do not attribute the first Indian motion venture to Phalke.
**Mains hook:** Early pioneers prove indigenisation predates Phalke.
**Back:** *"Ninety-nine, Save Dada: the first Indian motion venture."*

### Flashcard ART-4117 — First cinema houses: Madras 1900 and Calcutta 1907
**Front:** Which early exhibition houses does the owner name and when?
**Definition (learn):** Major Warwick established the first cinema house in Madras in 1900; Jamshedji Madan the Elphinstone Picture House in Calcutta in 1907.
**Breakdown:**
- Madras, 1900: Major Warwick.
- Calcutta, 1907: Jamshedji Madan, Elphinstone Picture House.
- Exhibition infrastructure came before production.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §7 (PDF pp. 570–571).
**Links:** ART-4114.
**Trap:** Do not conflate Madras (1900) and Calcutta (1907) exhibition milestones.
**Mains hook:** Exhibition preceded production; infrastructure shaped the industry.
**Back:** *"Madras in nineteen hundred, Calcutta in seven."*

### Flashcard ART-4118 — Pundalik (1912): Indo-British collaboration
**Front:** What was the first Indo-British collaboration in Indian cinema?
**Definition (learn):** Pundalik (1912), by N.G. Chitre and R.G. Torney.
**Breakdown:**
- Collaborators: N.G. Chitre and R.G. Torney.
- Year: 1912.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §7 (PDF pp. 571–572).
**Links:** ART-4119, ART-4132.
**Trap:** Pundalik is an Indo-British collaboration, not an indigenous feature.
**Mains hook:** Early collaboration shows a transnational origin of Indian cinema.
**Back:** *"Twelve, Chitre and Torney: the first joint film."*

### Flashcard ART-4119 — Raja Harishchandra (1913): first indigenous silent feature
**Front:** What was Raja Harishchandra and why is Phalke called the father of Indian cinema?
**Definition (learn):** The first indigenous Indian silent feature, made by Dadasaheb Phalke in 1913.
**Breakdown:**
- 1913: first indigenous silent feature.
- Phalke: also credited with Mohini Bhasmasur, Satyavan Savitri and Lanka Dahan (1917 box-office hit).
- Phalke (1870–1944).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §7, §11.4 (PDF pp. 571–572); ✅ Wikipedia Phalke Award page (1913 and namesake).
**Links:** ART-4106, ART-4120, ART-4114.
**Trap:** Raja Harishchandra is not the first exhibition (1896) and not the first box-office hit (Lanka Dahan 1917).
**Mains hook:** Mythology localised the medium before it localised its technology.
**Back:** *"Thirteen, Phalke's Harishchandra: the first home-made feature."*

### Flashcard ART-4120 — Lanka Dahan (1917): first box-office hit
**Front:** Which film is the first box-office hit and why is it not Raja Harishchandra?
**Definition (learn):** Lanka Dahan (1917) by Dadasaheb Phalke, the first box-office hit.
**Breakdown:**
- Box-office hit: 1917.
- Phalke's films: Raja Harishchandra (1913) first feature; Lanka Dahan the hit.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §7, §11.4.
**Links:** ART-4119.
**Trap:** A first feature is not the first hit.
**Mains hook:** Commercial success and historical first are different measures.
**Back:** *"Seventeen, Lanka burns: the first box-office hit."*

### Flashcard ART-4121 — Phalke's companies and the Indian Cinematograph Committee (1927)
**Front:** When was the Indian Cinematograph Committee set up and why?
**Definition (learn):** Founded by the British Raj in 1927 to examine censorship and the "immoral effect" of films.
**Breakdown:**
- 1927: Committee formed.
- Purpose: censorship adequacy.
- Kohinoor Film Company and Phalke's Hindustan Cinema Films Company opened in 1918 (owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §7 (PDF p. 571).
**Links:** ART-4092, ART-4093.
**Trap:** State anxiety about cinema predates independence; do not attribute the 1952 Act's origin to 1927.
**Mains hook:** Regulation's roots are colonial.
**Back:** *"Twenty-seven, the Raj watches cinema."*

### Flashcard ART-4122 — Alam Ara (1931): first talkie
**Front:** What was the first talking film and who produced it?
**Definition (learn):** Alam Ara (1931), directed by Ardeshir Irani, the first Indian talkie.
**Breakdown:**
- 1931: first talking film.
- Also: W.M. Khan, first playback singer with "De de khuda ke naam par" (first recorded song).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §11.4.
**Links:** ART-4123, ART-4118.
**Trap:** The first talkie is not the first colour film; the first recorded song is not the first colour film either.
**Mains hook:** Sound transformed mass reach; name the sound milestone.
**Back:** *"Thirty-one, Alam Ara speaks."*

### Flashcard ART-4123 — Sairandhri (1933): first Indian colour film
**Front:** What was the first Indian colour film and where was it processed?
**Definition (learn):** Sairandhri (1933), Prabhat Film Company; the first Indian colour film, processed in Germany (UFA studios).
**Breakdown:**
- Processing: Germany, UFA studios; Bipack colour printing (Wikipedia).
- Release prints were shown in black and white because colours were garish (Wikipedia).
- Owner: "processed in Germany".
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ Wikipedia Sairandhri (1933 film) fetched 2026-11-01; ✅ owner 15 §11.4.
**Links:** ART-4124.
**Trap:** Sairandhri is "first colour" but not "first indigenously made colour film" — the 1937 Kisan Kanya claim is the paired trap.
**Mains hook:** Milestone wording must say "processed abroad" when relevant.
**Back:** *"Thirty-three, colour from Germany: first Indian colour, shown in black and white."*

### Flashcard ART-4124 — Kisan Kanya (1937): first indigenously made colour film (owner-only)
**Front:** Which film is the first indigenously made Indian colour film?
**Definition (learn):** The owner names Kisan Kanya (1937, Ardeshir Irani) as the first indigenously made colour film.
**Breakdown:**
- Owner: Kisan Kanya, 1937, Ardeshir Irani.
- Contrast: Sairandhri (1933), processed abroad.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §7, §11.4. ⚠️ Not verified by fetched source in this build; the Sairandhri fetch did not mention Kisan Kanya.
**Links:** ART-4123.
**Trap:** Keep "first colour" (1933) and "first indigenously made colour" (1937) separate.
**Mains hook:** Indigenous production is the stronger milestone for self-reliance arguments.
**Back:** *"Thirty-seven, Kisan Kanya: colour made at home."*

### Flashcard ART-4125 — Naujawan (1937): first film without any song (owner-only)
**Front:** Which film is named as the first Indian film without any song?
**Definition (learn):** Naujawan (1937), per the owner.
**Breakdown:**
- Year: 1937.
- Claim: first film without any song.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §7. ⚠️ Not verified in this build.
**Links:** ART-4122.
**Trap:** Claim is owner-stated; do not repeat as settled without a second source.
**Mains hook:** Silent-to-sound contrast shows how song defined Indian commercial film.
**Back:** *"Thirty-seven, no song at all: Naujawan (verify)."*

### Flashcard ART-4126 — Premsagar (1939): first South Indian film (owner-only)
**Front:** Which film does the owner name as the first South Indian film?
**Definition (learn):** Premsagar (1939), K. Subrahmanyam, per the owner.
**Breakdown:**
- Year: 1939.
- Director: K. Subrahmanyam (owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §7. ⚠️ The Wikipedia "Premsagar" page fetched is about a literary figure; the film claim was not confirmed, and earlier South Indian films are not ruled out here.
**Links:** ART-4110.
**Trap:** Do not state "first South Indian film" as settled.
**Mains hook:** Regional film history is multi-centred; name the claim with its source.
**Back:** *"Thirty-nine, Premsagar: first South Indian (owner's claim)."*

### Flashcard ART-4127 — Bulbul-e-Paristan (1926): Fatma Begum
**Front:** Who was the first Indian woman to produce and direct her own film?
**Definition (learn):** Fatma Begum, Bulbul-e-Paristan (1926).
**Breakdown:**
- Year: 1926.
- Role: producer and director.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §7 (PDF pp. 571–572); §11.4.
**Links:** ART-4128.
**Trap:** Frequently omitted in lists of firsts.
**Mains hook:** Women's agency behind the camera is a representation and production question.
**Back:** *"Twenty-six, Fatma directs: first Indian woman producer-director."*

### Flashcard ART-4128 — Parallel cinema (late 1940s)
**Front:** What is parallel cinema and which figures does the owner name?
**Definition (learn):** An alternative to mainstream commercial films from the late 1940s, focused on poverty, social injustice and human suffering, drawing on literature and theatre.
**Breakdown:**
- Named: Satyajit Ray, Ritwik Ghatak, Guru Dutt, Shyam Benegal (owner).
- Reach: narrower than commercial cinema.
- Legacy: art-cinema canon and alternative funding circuit (owner analysis).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §7, §9, §11.6 (PDF pp. 570, 573–574).
**Links:** ART-4129, ART-4130.
**Trap:** Guru Dutt is named as a parallel figure in the owner; do not reduce parallel cinema to four names.
**Mains hook:** Two tracks since the late 1940s: mass entertainment and social realism.
**Back:** *"Late forties, two tracks: mass and the parallel."*

### Flashcard ART-4129 — Women on screen: the owner's periodisation
**Front:** How does the owner periodise women's representation in Indian cinema?
**Definition (learn):** A reading of representation, not social history: silent period (restrictions); 1920–40 emancipation themes; 1960–80 stereotypical; parallel cinema depicting struggle; current cinema depicting career and identity.
**Breakdown:**
- Silent: restrictions on women's lives.
- 1920–40: child marriage, sati abolition, widow remarriage, women's education, workplace equality (V. Shantaram, Dhiren Ganguli, Baburao Painter).
- 1960–80: stereotypical (motherhood, fidelity, sacrifice).
- Parallel: Ray, Ghatak, Dutt, Benegal.
- Current: modern woman balancing career, motherhood, identity.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §9 (PDF pp. 573–574); ⚠️ presented as a reading.
**Links:** ART-4128, ART-4131.
**Trap:** Films are evidence of prevailing norms, not a social census.
**Mains hook:** Representation regressed while social legislation advanced; the direction of change is not linear.
**Back:** *"Restrict, emancipate, stereotype, struggle, balance."*

### Flashcard ART-4130 — Silent cinema's live music
**Front:** How were silent films accompanied in India?
**Definition (learn):** Silent films were accompanied by live music on sarangi, tabla, harmonium and violin.
**Breakdown:**
- Instruments: sarangi, tabla, harmonium, violin.
- Link to the music traditions of topic 08.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §7.
**Links:** ART-4118, ART-4085.
**Trap:** "Silent" films in India were never silent in exhibition.
**Mains hook:** Live music ties early cinema to classical and folk traditions.
**Back:** *"Silent films, live sarangi: never quiet in India."*

### Flashcard ART-4131 — Certification as a censorship tension
**Front:** Why does certification create a censorship tension for Mains?
**Definition (learn):** Formal classification versus effective content control when the Board can require cuts and refuse sanction.
**Breakdown:**
- Formal: audience classification.
- Effective: excisions, refusal, state enforcement.
- Doctrine: Polity owns free speech law.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §8, §10.
**Links:** ART-4098, ART-4093.
**Trap:** Describing the Board as wholly censorship or wholly classification.
**Mains hook:** Give the verdict that distinguishes the two.
**Back:** *"Classify or cut: the Board does both."*

### Flashcard ART-4132 — Film preservation fragility and selection
**Front:** Why does film preservation depend on commercial rather than cultural value?
**Definition (learn):** Film is fragile (nitrate and acetate decay, colour fading, obsolete formats); commercial value usually decides restoration.
**Breakdown:**
- Fragility: nitrate, acetate, colour fading, format obsolescence.
- Selection bias: commercially valuable films restored first.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ owner §9 analysis.
**Links:** ART-4090, ART-4102.
**Trap:** Do not state restoration counts or budgets.
**Mains hook:** Argue that archival selection needs public funding for cultural value.
**Back:** *"Money saves what sells; archives save the rest."*

### Flashcard ART-4133 — Soft power: festival and award reach with its caveat
**Front:** Why is soft power from festivals and awards only partial evidence of cultural value?
**Definition (learn):** Festival selection, international awards and streaming extend reach, but one jury in one category in one year is not a measure of cultural value.
**Breakdown:**
- Reach: festivals, awards, streaming.
- Caveat: one jury, one category, one year.
- Stronger claim: breadth of domestic linguistic production.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §9, §11.7.
**Links:** ART-4099, ART-4101, ART-4111.
**Trap:** An award is not proof of universal superiority.
**Mains hook:** Argue from linguistic breadth, not foreign prizes.
**Back:** *"Prizes travel, plurality stays home."*

### Flashcard ART-4134 — Indian film milestones: separate dates for separate claims
**Front:** How should the dates of firsts be recorded?
**Definition (learn):** Exhibition, Indian-shot short, Indian-made short, indigenous feature, talkie, colour-processing and indigenous-colour milestones must be kept distinct.
**Breakdown:**
- 1896: exhibition.
- 1897: Indian-shot film.
- 1913: indigenous feature.
- 1931: talkie.
- 1933: colour processed abroad.
- 1937: indigenous colour.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §11.4, §11.8.
**Links:** ART-4114–ART-4124.
**Trap:** Merging the dates into one "first" misleads.
**Mains hook:** Milestones answer different questions; record each.
**Back:** *"Ninety-six, ninety-seven, thirteen, thirty-one, thirty-three, thirty-seven."*

### Flashcard ART-4135 — Entertainment tax on cinema (Calcutta 1922, Bombay 1923)
**Front:** When was entertainment tax imposed on cinema in Calcutta and Bombay?
**Definition (learn):** Entertainment tax was imposed in Calcutta in 1922 and Bombay in 1923 (owner).
**Breakdown:**
- Calcutta: 1922.
- Bombay: 1923.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §7 (PDF pp. 571–572); ⚠️ not verified by a second source.
**Links:** ART-4117.
**Trap:** Do not confuse entertainment tax with certification fees.
**Mains hook:** Fiscal treatment of cinema shows its economic status.
**Back:** *"Twenty-two Calcutta, twenty-three Bombay: cinema taxed."*

### Flashcard ART-4136 — PYQ 2026 Prelims Q85: Boong and the milestone
**Front:** What does the 2026 Prelims Q85 test?
**Definition (learn):** Film Boong, BAFTA recognition, director and Indian cinema milestone as separate statements.
**Breakdown:**
- Q85, GS-I Prelims 2026.
- Statements: award, category, director, milestone.
- Provisional 2026 Set-A key; no answer letter recorded.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §10 (2026 block); ⚠️ key provisional.
**Links:** ART-4111, ART-4112, ART-4113.
**Trap:** "Won a BAFTA" is too broad for statement elimination.
**Mains hook:** Keep award, category and director separate in every statement.
**Back:** *"Eighty-five, Boong's statements: award, category, director, milestone."*

### Flashcard ART-4137 — Mains routes for cinema (no direct GS-I PYQ)
**Front:** Which Mains routes are realistic for cinema given no direct GS-I question in the audited 2024–2025 papers?
**Definition (learn):** The owner names three routes: cinema as modern art form, cinema and social change or representation, and certification versus freedom of expression.
**Breakdown:**
- Route 1: art form ("ancient to modern times").
- Route 2: social change or representation.
- Route 3: certification versus expression (Polity owns the doctrine).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §10 (⚠️ owner analysis).
**Links:** ART-4085, ART-4098, ART-4128, ART-4129.
**Trap:** Do not claim a direct Mains PYQ on cinema; the owner states none was found.
**Mains hook:** Use the three routes for an unknown cinema question.
**Back:** *"Art, society, freedom: three cinema routes."*

### GROUP — Indian Cinema, Film Institutions and Awards - ADVANCED

### Flashcard ART-4138 — Cinema as cultural memory and mass medium
**Front:** Why is cinema simultaneously an art form, an industry, an archive and a mass medium?
**Definition (learn):** Cinema combines form, political economy, social memory and mass reach; a strong answer addresses all four without treating box office as the measure of importance.
**Breakdown:**
- Art form: camera, editing, sound.
- Industry: finance and distribution.
- Archive: social memory.
- Mass medium: reach.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 advanced §1 (⚠️ analytical).
**Links:** ART-4085, ART-4090, ART-4139.
**Trap:** Box office is not cultural importance.
**Mains hook:** Combine form with institutions in the answer.
**Back:** *"Art, industry, archive, medium: four lenses, no box-office verdict."*

### Flashcard ART-4139 — Six-dimension analytical matrix for cinema
**Front:** What six dimensions does the advanced matrix use for Indian cinema?
**Definition (learn):** Form, representation, political economy, regulation, preservation and soft power.
**Breakdown:**
- Form: camera, editing, sound, performance.
- Representation: language, region, gender, caste, tribe, borderland.
- Political economy: finance, distribution, screens and platforms.
- Regulation: certification, age suitability, public order.
- Preservation: restoration, cataloguing, access.
- Soft power: festivals and awards.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 advanced §2.
**Links:** ART-4138, ART-4092, ART-4090, ART-4099.
**Trap:** Do not analyse only one dimension.
**Mains hook:** Use the matrix to structure a 20-mark answer.
**Back:** *"Form, who, money, rules, memory, reach."*

### Flashcard ART-4140 — Boong as a regional case study
**Front:** Why is Boong a case study for regional and Northeast storytelling?
**Definition (learn):** Global circulation of a Manipuri-language film widens the cultural map of Indian cinema beyond the Hindi-centric image.
**Breakdown:**
- Language: Manipuri.
- Region: Northeast India.
- Point: circulation, not merely the award.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 advanced §3 (⚠️ analytical).
**Links:** ART-4111, ART-4112, ART-4110.
**Trap:** Do not claim an award proves cultural superiority.
**Mains hook:** Use Boong to show plurality, with the caveat about recognition.
**Back:** *"Manipuri on a world stage: the map widens."*

### Flashcard ART-4141 — Mains tensions in Indian cinema
**Front:** Which five tensions does the advanced file list for Mains answers?
**Definition (learn):** Artistic freedom versus certification; national canon versus regional plurality; commercial preservation versus fragile archives; international recognition versus external validation; streaming access versus platform concentration.
**Breakdown:**
- Freedom versus age classification.
- Canon versus plurality.
- Commercial incentive versus archival heritage.
- Recognition versus dependence.
- Access versus concentration.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 advanced §4.
**Links:** ART-4098, ART-4090, ART-4133.
**Trap:** Name a tension and its counter, not just the tension.
**Mains hook:** Use one tension per paragraph with a concrete example.
**Back:** *"Freedom, canon, archive, recognition, platform: five tensions."*

### Flashcard ART-4142 — International award is not universal superiority
**Front:** Why does an international award not establish universal cultural superiority?
**Definition (learn):** It records recognition by a particular institution, category and jury in a particular year; cultural significance needs wider analysis of form, context and reception.
**Breakdown:**
- Institution, category, jury, year.
- Form, context, reception.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 advanced §5.
**Links:** ART-4101, ART-4133, ART-4111.
**Trap:** Universal superiority claim is the advanced trap.
**Mains hook:** State the award's scope precisely before drawing a cultural conclusion.
**Back:** *"One jury, one category, one year: not superiority."*
